# Progress Tracker

Update this file after every completed feature. Any AI agent reading this should immediately know what is done, what is in progress, and what is next.

## Feature 38 — Premium Content Gating (Phase 9, in progress)

Built as a **stack of 9 per-stage branches/PRs**, not one branch — the feature touches nearly every gated surface in the app (Learn, Practice, Playground, all of Interview Prep, plus the real `/pricing` page), so reviewing it as one giant diff wasn't practical. Each stage branches off the *previous stage's tip*, not `develop` (which won't have earlier stages until they merge): schema (PR #119) → learn (#120) → practice (#121) → playground (#122) → ff-collections (#123) → ff-system-design (#124) → playbook (#125) → study-plans-company-guides (#126) → pricing (#127). All 9 stages were already built and pushed before this merge pass started. **Working the stack bottom-up now: each stage gets its CodeRabbit findings addressed and a full anon/free/premium regression pass before merging, one stage at a time, in order** — no `develop`-sync work happens on any of them until the user explicitly confirms each merge.

**Stage 1 (PR #119, `feature/38-premium-content-gating-schema`) — schema + shared infra.** `challenges.parentChallengeId` (self-ref FK, for a future "harder variant" content pairing — no variant content is authored against it yet) and `uiBattleChallenges.isSolutionFree` (flagship free-solution flag, wired up for real in Stage 4). Generalized the old Build-tab-only `BuildPremiumLocked` into a shared `PremiumLocked` component (used by `ConceptBuild`/`ConceptChallenge` here, reused across every locked surface later stages add) and added `features/practice`'s own `getIsPremiumUser` (matches the per-feature-duplicated pattern already used by learn/interview-prep/playground; unused by any live code path on this branch — Practice's own gating is Stage 3's job). This stage is a pure refactor plus additive schema on its own: no challenge/concept is premium-locked *because of* this stage's own code — the `PremiumLocked` swap only changes visuals for the one concept (`aria-live-regions`) that already carried a stray `isPremium: true` flag from later-stage seed data sharing the same dev DB.

**CodeRabbit review (2026-08-07):** 1 finding, Minor. `PremiumLocked.tsx`'s comment on `PRICING_PATH` asserted `/pricing` is "a real page as of this feature (Stage 9)" — true of the finished stack, but misleading on this PR's own diff, since `/pricing` doesn't exist until Stage 9 merges and every CTA on this branch 404s today. Fixed: reworded to describe `/pricing` as the future destination, not an existing route. Committed (`4a371a3`) and pushed.

**Regression pass (2026-08-07), anon/free/premium, both cookies verified live against the DB first:** DB migration confirmed applied correctly (both columns present with correct types/defaults/FK/index, no orphaned data). Clean `.next` wipe + dev restart, `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both clean project-wide, all 38 known routes warm. Build and Challenge tabs tested against real content — a free concept (`event-delegation-bubbling-capturing`) and a premium concept (`aria-live-regions`), each with both a challenge and a build project already live in the DB — across all 3 viewer states: free concept renders the full workspace for every viewer, premium concept correctly shows the `PremiumLocked` wall for anon/free and the full workspace for premium, zero console errors in any combination. Two Playwright test-script bugs caught and fixed along the way, not app bugs: a `text=Run` locator matching "JavaScript Runtime" in the sidebar instead of the `Run Tests` button (fixed by scoping to the tab panel with exact button text), and a click-before-hydration race on the premium Challenge tab's heavier bundle silently leaving the Understand tab's content on screen (fixed by polling for the clicked tab to actually become `aria-selected`, not a fixed sleep). Full route sweep: 30 routes × 3 viewer states (90 checks) — 85 passed immediately, 5 timed out past the script's 45s limit during a burst of first-time Turbopack compiles (`/leaderboard`, `/dashboard`, `/settings`, `/settings/profile` for free; `/interview-prep` for premium); cross-checked against the dev server log, which showed each one completing successfully seconds after the client-side timeout (this environment's documented up-to-100s+ cold-compile behavior, not a hang) — all 5 re-ran clean (3–14s, zero console errors) once the server was warm. **Merged to `develop` via PR #119 (merge commit `717e392`)**, branch `feature/38-premium-content-gating-schema` kept, not deleted.

**Stage 2 (PR #120, `feature/38-premium-content-gating-learn`) — Learn concept-level gating.** `[category]/[slug]/page.tsx` now computes one concept-level `isPremiumLocked` (`concept.isPremium && !isPremiumUser`) instead of three separate per-item checks on `challenge.isPremium`/`projectBrief.isPremium`/`interviewQuestions[].isPremium` — Understand never locks (the SEO/GEO crawl story and the product's stated differentiator, the simulator, both depend on every concept staying browsable). Simulate gains the same `isPremiumLocked` branch Build/Challenge already had. **Interview originally shipped with a per-question teaser (question text visible, only the answer locked), then was corrected mid-PR** (`e6bf997`, direct user feedback: a visible question alone is enough to Google the answer elsewhere, defeating the gate) **to the same full `PremiumLocked` wall Simulate/Challenge/Build use** — `page.tsx` sends an empty `questions` array whenever the concept is locked, so there's no real question text in the payload to leak via view-source either; `QuestionCard`'s own per-question lock UI is now unused by this tab (still required by its props since FF Collections, Stage 5, uses real per-item locking there). `scripts/seed/concepts.ts` reflagged so every category lands near 60% free / 40% premium (previous flags were leftover from an earlier, explicitly-rejected model — system-design was 100% premium, javascript-runtime was 0%); all 4 concepts with a real built simulator (event-loop, react-rendering, browser-rendering-pipeline, css-specificity) are pinned free so the product's differentiator stays visible regardless of the difficulty-based selection. **This branch was originally stacked on Stage 1's branch tip (per PR #120's own description, "retarget to develop once #119 merges"); since #119 merged, cleanly rebased onto post-Stage-1 `develop` (no conflicts — Stage 2's own commits never touched `PremiumLocked.tsx` or this file) and force-pushed. PR #120 needs retargeting from `feature/38-premium-content-gating-schema` to `develop` on GitHub before merging.**

**CodeRabbit review (2026-08-07):** 2 findings. **Major, Security:** `page.tsx`'s `clientChallenge` only cleared `solutionCode` for a locked concept, leaving `description`/`starterCode`/`testCases`/`hints` intact in the RSC payload — `ConceptChallenge`'s locked branch never reads them, but React serializes every prop regardless of which branch renders (the same bug class this feature has hit repeatedly). Fixed by redacting all five fields, matching `clientProjectBrief`'s already-correct pattern in the same file. **Minor, Functional:** `CategoryAccordionItem.tsx`'s sidebar premium icon was suppressed for the currently-active concept via a stray `!isActive` check, hiding premium status exactly where a user would look for it. Fixed by removing the exclusion. Both verified live, not just by inspection: a leak probe using real DB content (the `aria-live-regions` challenge's actual description/starterCode/hints/testCases text) confirmed all four fields absent from the served bytes for anon and free, present for premium; a fresh Playwright pass confirmed the sidebar now shows the correct lock/unlock icon on the active concept for both free and premium. Committed (`d5a03e5`, post-rebase) and pushed. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean project-wide after both the fix and the rebase. **Merged to `develop` via PR #120 (merge commit `9a64137`)**, branch `feature/38-premium-content-gating-learn` kept, not deleted.

**Combined Stage 1+2 regression pass (2026-08-07), on merged `develop`, anon/free/premium:** both cookies re-verified live. Clean `.next` wipe + restart, `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean, all 38 routes warm. Leak probe (33 checks, real DB content across Challenge/Interview/Build's gated fields) — all absent for anon/free, all present for premium; Understand always present regardless of lock state. 4-tab functional check (free concept + locked concept × 3 viewers, 6 combinations) — all pass; one Playwright flake surfaced and resolved (a single `isVisible()` sample landing mid-`AnimatePresence`-transition during rapid sequential tab clicks — a different tab failed on each of two runs, the signature of a timing race rather than a real bug, confirmed by switching to polling). Sidebar accordion icons (10 checks, 5 concepts × free/premium) all matched DB truth exactly. Full route sweep: 33 routes × 3 viewers (102 checks) — 94 passed immediately, 8 timed out past the script's limit during a burst of cold compiles, all 8 confirmed genuine (dev log showed real 200s) and re-ran clean once warm. **No regressions found; no code changes needed.** Also caught and fixed a documentation-only error during this pass (see Stage 2's Interview-tab correction above) via `docs/120-progress-tracker-correction`, **merged via PR #129 (merge commit `770235d`)**.

**Stage 3 (PR #121, `feature/38-premium-content-gating-practice`) — Practice premium gating.** Company tags redacted server-side for every Practice challenge (list + detail) regardless of that challenge's own `isPremium` — never sent to a non-premium client. Company filter dropdown replaced with a locked trigger + upsell dialog (linking to `/pricing`) for non-premium viewers, instead of an empty dropdown or a bespoke pricing modal. Challenge detail page: real lock now — `starterCode`/`solutionCode`/`testCases`/`hints`/`description` all stripped, and the whole challenge object is never even passed to a client component when locked (`PremiumLocked` renders instead of `ChallengeEditorWorkspace`; header/breadcrumb/nav stay visible). Skips fetching discussion posts entirely for a locked challenge — `challenge_discussion_posts` has no spoiler gate by design (a pre-Feature-38 decision) and can carry full shared solution code, which would otherwise leak a premium challenge's solution through an unrelated path. Fixed `isAccessibleForFree` in the challenge detail page's `LearningResource` JSON-LD (was hardcoded `true`). Reflagged 362 standalone Practice challenges across css/typescript/react/systemDesign/javascriptRuntime to ~60/40 free/premium per category via a small script, using company-tag count as an automated proxy for "recognized" (more companies attributed = more likely a well-known, frequently-asked problem = stays free). Also added the shared `PremiumBadge` component (open/closed lock state, matches `PremiumLocked`'s icon-only variant) — wired into `ChallengeListRow` now, remaining call sites pick it up as their own stages touch that code (Playground next).

**This branch was originally stacked on Stage 2's pre-rebase commits (branched before Stage 2's CodeRabbit fix and rebase existed); retargeting PR #121 to `develop` surfaced a real merge conflict** — Stage 2's rebase (documented above) rewrote its commit SHAs when it merged, orphaning Stage 3's shared ancestry. Diagnosed with a local dry-run merge before touching anything: the conflict was confined to a single comment block in `page.tsx` (Stage 3's copy of Stage 2's pre-CodeRabbit-fix comment vs. `develop`'s post-fix version) — git auto-merged everything else, since `git rebase origin/develop`'s patch-id matching recognized Stage 2's changes were already present upstream and skipped replaying them entirely rather than requiring manual resolution. Rebased cleanly, force-pushed; verified via an empty diff that Stage 3's own files (the 5 seed files, the practice pages, `ChallengeListRow`/`ChallengeListClient`, `PremiumBadge.tsx`) are byte-identical before and after. **Expected to recur once per remaining stage (4 → 9)** as each one gets its own rebase-onto-`develop` treatment right before its CodeRabbit review — not a growing problem, since git's patch-id matching has handled the bulk of it automatically both times so far.

**CodeRabbit review (2026-08-07):** 3 findings. **Major, Security (Quick win):** `generateMetadata` never checked lock state — always built the real description/OG/Twitter meta from `challenge.description` regardless of viewer, so an anonymous request's `<meta>` tags carried real premium content even though the rendered page showed the `PremiumLocked` wall (the same "generateMetadata never gates" bug class already known from Playground). Fixed by recomputing `isChallengeLocked` inside `generateMetadata` (separate function scope, doesn't inherit the page body's computation) and falling back to a generic description with `robots: { index: false, follow: false }` when locked. **Minor, Functional (Quick win):** `isAccessibleForFree` in the `LearningResource` JSON-LD was derived from the viewer-specific `isChallengeLocked` instead of the challenge's own price, so a premium viewer on a premium challenge incorrectly reported it as free to crawlers. Fixed to `!challenge.isPremium`. **Major, Heavy lift — skipped, with reason:** a refactor suggestion to move the premium entitlement check, lock computation, and content redaction out of the route into `src/features/practice/`, citing `AGENTS.md`'s feature-first rule. Checked against precedent first: Learn's already-merged, already-CodeRabbit-reviewed `page.tsx` (Stage 1+2) keeps the identical shape of logic directly in the route file and was never flagged for it across two separate reviews. Applying this now would diverge from that established, working pattern for a pure code-organization preference (not a bug) at real cost — CodeRabbit's own "Heavy lift" label — which the instructions explicitly asked to avoid ("don't make unnecessary changes, only touch the relevant files"). Both real fixes verified live against a real premium standalone challenge (`three-column-feed-layout`) and a real free one (`center-element-vertically`): generic description + `noindex` for anon/free on the premium challenge, real description + `index` for premium, `isAccessibleForFree: false` for the premium challenge regardless of viewer, free challenge unaffected in every state. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean. Committed (`f4fe0ee`) and pushed.

**Pre-merge regression pass (2026-08-07), anon/free/premium, both cookies verified live:** clean `.next` wipe + restart, `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean, all 38 routes warm. Leak probe against 2 real premium standalone challenges — `three-column-feed-layout` (real company tags "Meta"/"Amazon") and `improve-full-name-formatter` (a real discussion post) — confirmed description/starterCode/solutionCode/hints/testCases/companies/discussion-post-body all absent for anon/free, present for premium; one false alarm caught and dismissed along the way (a "Meta" fragment matching Next.js's own internal `MetadataBoundary`/`Next.Metadata` strings, not the company tag — confirmed by dumping exact byte offsets, then fixed with a disambiguated JSON-string fragment). List page company redaction confirmed the same way. Company filter UI (Playwright): locked trigger + upsell dialog with a working `/pricing` link for anon/free, real dropdown for premium, zero console errors; `PremiumBadge` correctly shows `Lock`/`LockOpen` across all 15 premium challenges in a sampled category. Stage 1+2 surfaces re-probed on `aria-live-regions` to confirm no regression from Stage 3's changes (none — expected, since Stage 3 never touches Learn files, but verified rather than assumed). Full route sweep: 35 routes × 3 viewers (108 checks) — 103 passed immediately, 5 timed out past the script's limit during a cold-compile burst, all 5 confirmed genuine (dev log showed real 200s, one taking 30.9s) and re-ran clean once warm. **No regressions found; no code changes needed.** **Merged to `develop` via PR #121 (merge commit `1d3a162`)**, branch `feature/38-premium-content-gating-practice` kept, not deleted.

**Stage 4 (PR #122, `feature/38-premium-content-gating-playground`) — Playground UI Battle gating.** The battle editor now has a real attempt-gate: `challenge.isPremium` was cosmetic-only before (the editor rendered regardless), now a locked challenge shows `PremiumLocked` instead of `BattleEditorWorkspace`. Solution visibility gains a second, independent unlock path — `isPremiumUser || challenge.isSolutionFree` — layered on top of the challenge-level lock (Stage 1's `isSolutionFree` column, wired up for real here); the real solution HTML/CSS/JS is stripped before the client payload is built, same "never client-side only" pattern as Learn/Practice. `isPremiumUser` threaded through both battle list surfaces (the dedicated list page and the Playground hub's preview row); both swapped their old hand-rolled lock badge for the shared `PremiumBadge` from Stage 3. Experiments and Roadmaps needed no changes — already matched the agreed model (both fully free). **Content reality check, stated honestly rather than glossed over:** only 2 UI Battle challenges exist today, both Easy — the difficulty-driven rule (Easy=free, Medium/Hard=premium) currently locks nothing in practice, since no Medium/Hard content exists yet. One of the two (`login-card-recreate`) is flagged `isSolutionFree` so that mechanism is genuinely exercised; the other (`profile-card-recreate`) gates its solution normally. No fake premium content was invented just to make the feature look more exercised than it is.

**Branched off Stage 2/3's pre-rebase commits (same structural cause as Stage 3's own conflict, documented above) — retargeting PR #122 to `develop` surfaced the identical class of conflict.** Rebased onto post-Stage-3 `develop`: `git rebase`'s patch-id matching recognized all 4 already-applied commits (`590f7bf`/`8c1621f`/`ac3d463`/`897aab1`) and skipped replaying them entirely — zero manual conflict resolution, and confirmed via `git merge origin/develop` returning "Already up to date" (the branch doesn't just merge cleanly, it already contains all of `develop`'s history). Verified via empty diff that Stage 4's own 9 files are byte-identical before and after. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean, force-pushed. PR #122 now shows exactly 1 commit against `develop`.

**Pre-merge regression pass (2026-08-08), anon/free/premium, both cookies verified live:** clean `.next` wipe + restart, `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean, all 38 routes warm. Login-gate redirect confirmed correct (`/login?callbackURL=/playground/battles/[slug]`, unencoded per the app's own `redirect()` call — an early test-script assumption that it should be URL-encoded was itself the bug, caught and fixed before trusting the result). Solution-gating leak probe on the 2 real battles, real content: `login-card-recreate` (`isSolutionFree: true`) shows its real solution to both free and premium, as designed; `profile-card-recreate` (`isSolutionFree: false`) correctly hides it from free, shows it to premium. List/hub pages: 0 `PremiumBadge` lock icons rendered for any viewer, matching reality (no battle is actually premium today). **One real bug found and fixed, not by inspection but by live-testing a path the original PR couldn't reach** (its own description notes it had no valid authenticated session to verify the locked/unlocked editor states): with the user's explicit approval, temporarily flipped `profile-card-recreate` to `isPremium: true` in the dev DB. The page body correctly showed the `PremiumLocked` wall and never leaked the solution — but `generateMetadata` never checked lock state at all, shipping the real description into `<meta>`/OG/Twitter tags for a free viewer regardless (identical bug class to Stage 3's own `generateMetadata` finding). Fixed by recomputing `isChallengeLocked` independently inside `generateMetadata` and falling back to a generic description + `robots: noindex` when locked; re-verified the fix live across anon/free/premium, then reverted the DB flip and confirmed via a fresh query that both battles are back to their real, original state. Stage 1–3 surfaces re-probed on `aria-live-regions` and `three-column-feed-layout` to confirm no regression (none). Full route sweep: 32 routes × 3 viewers (96 checks) — first pass hit 20 timeouts concentrated in the anon viewer's run, unusually high; cross-checked `dev.log` and confirmed every one completed with a real 200 server-side (one `get-session` call took 107s) — genuine resource buildup from a dev server that had been running continuously through many consecutive test cycles and DB flips that session, not a code defect. Did a full clean restart + re-warm rather than just trusting the log, then re-ran the entire sweep: down to the usual 4 (the same recurring `/leaderboard`/`/dashboard`/`/settings`/`/settings/profile` cold-compile cluster seen in every prior stage's pass), all 4 confirmed clean on retry. **Fix committed (`495f3d9`, title amended once — a copy-paste artifact from Stage 3's commit message, user-approved fix since unpushed) and pushed.** CodeRabbit stayed rate-limited (never posted a single finding — confirmed via the API, zero review comments) all the way through to merge; nothing was skipped as a result, just nothing to review. **Merged to `develop` via PR #122 (merge commit `e7e6f8f`)**, branch `feature/38-premium-content-gating-playground` kept, not deleted.

**Stage 5 (PR #123, `feature/38-premium-content-gating-ff-collections`) — FF Collections (Interview Prep) premium gating.** All 3 collections (`ff-javascript`, `ff-react`, `ff-nextjs`) land at exactly 40% premium overall, using the same company-tag-count "recognized" proxy as Stage 3's Practice reflagging; the virtual `ff-75` collection (`isFf75=true`, 75 rows spanning all 3 real collections) is fully premium, and its rows count against each real collection's 40% quota rather than stacking a separate quota on top. Question detail page (`[collection]/[slug]/page.tsx`) gets a real lock for the first time — a full `PremiumLocked` wall for a locked question, not a per-question teaser (the per-item teaser pattern was never actually live before this stage, since `isPremium` didn't exist on `collection_questions` until now); same "question text alone is Google-able" reasoning already applied to Learn's Interview tab in Stage 2. `generateMetadata` independently recomputes lock state and falls back to a generic title/description + `robots: { index: false, follow: false }` for a locked question, instead of unconditionally indexing the real question/answer. List page (`[collection]/page.tsx`) gets the shared `PremiumBadge` per row (Stage 3's component) and a blurred-placeholder teaser row for locked questions; the FAQPage JSON-LD now excludes premium questions entirely, since their own pages are `noindex`. Fixed `isAccessibleForFree` (was hardcoded `true`).

**Branched off Stage 4's pre-rebase commits (identical structural cause as Stage 3 and Stage 4's own conflicts, documented above) — retargeting PR #123 to `develop` surfaced the same class of conflict.** Rebased onto post-Stage-4 `develop`: `git rebase`'s patch-id matching recognized all 5 already-applied commits (`590f7bf`/`8c1621f`/`ac3d463`/`897aab1`/`8507248`) and skipped replaying them entirely — zero manual conflict resolution needed, confirmed via a local dry-run `git merge --no-commit --no-ff` into `develop` completing with "Automatic merge went well" (zero conflicts) and `develop` verified as an ancestor of the rebased branch's tip. Force-pushed; PR #123 now shows exactly 2 commits against `develop`.

**CodeRabbit review (2026-08-08):** 2 findings, both confirmed real against current code and fixed. **Critical, Security & Privacy:** the question detail page's prev/next nav (`CollectionQuestionPrevNextNav`) received adjacent-question data from `getAdjacentCollectionQuestions`, which carried no `isPremium` field — a locked neighbor's real question title still reached the client unconditionally via the nav button's `title`/`aria-label` attributes, defeating the just-added lock wall for that neighbor (same "full text leaks via a UI affordance the lock branch doesn't gate" bug class as Stage 2's Interview-tab correction). Fixed: added `isPremium` to `CollectionQuestionNavItem` and populated it in `getAdjacentCollectionQuestions`; threaded a new `isPremiumUser` prop through `CollectionQuestionPrevNextNav`/`NavButton`; a locked neighbor's title is now replaced with the shared `BLURRED_QUESTION_PLACEHOLDER` (moved from the list page into `queries.ts` as a single exported source of truth, so the list and the nav tooltip can never drift onto different placeholder text). **Major, Data Integrity:** the list page's FAQPage JSON-LD set `acceptedAnswer.text` to the question's page URL instead of real answer text, contradicting the file's own comment ("Answers are truncated to a clean plain-text summary") and disqualifying the markup from FAQ rich results. Fixed by adding a new `getCollectionQuestionFaqItems` query (deliberately a separate type from `CollectionQuestionListItem`/`ClientQuestionItem`, which are serialized into `CollectionQuestionListClient`'s props — mixing a real `answer` field into that shared type would have leaked every premium question's full answer to a non-premium client's page props while fixing this) and using `toPlainTextSummary(q.answer)` for the JSON-LD text, still filtered to free questions only. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both clean project-wide after the fix. Pre-merge regression pass (anon/free/premium) still pending before merge.

**Also built, not a `build-plan.md` feature — see the Feature 55 entry below:** Settings overlay resized to Claude.ai-like proportions, plus real Feedback/Changelog/View-Progress pages replacing three long-dead `LearnSidebar` links. **Same branch, a full pre-commit regression pass then found and fixed 5 unrelated real bugs** — a `callbackURL`-dropping redirect bug (`proxy.ts` + 3 client components), a `useUser.ts` hydration-mismatch regression, missing `priority` on 4 above-the-fold gallery images, and — the significant one — `AppNavbar`'s account-menu avatar rendering completely off-screen at common laptop widths (1024–1366px), fixed alongside a compact search-trigger redesign. **Merged to `develop` via PR #109 (merge commit `84fbfbe`)**, branch `feature/55-settings-overlay-feedback-changelog-progress` (kept, not deleted, per this project's never-delete-a-merged-branch convention). See the Feature 55 entry below for the full narrative.

**Also built, Phase 13 extra — see the Feature 56 entry below:** Settings → Profile is now a real edit form (full name, username, bio, avatar URL), not just a display-only streak heatmap (moved to Dashboard, its other real home). **Merged to `develop` via PR #111 (merge commit `c9a283f`)**, branch `feature/56-settings-profile-edit` kept, not deleted.

**Also built, Phase 13 extra — see the Feature 57 entry below:** Settings → Account — real connected-provider list (Google/GitHub) with connect/disconnect, and account deletion with type-to-confirm. Also fixed two real pre-existing bugs (dating back to the modal's original build) surfaced only once a second section existed to navigate between: switching sections visibly flashed the whole overlay, and closing after a switch landed back on the other section instead of the page behind the modal. Also, unrelated to Feature 57 itself but shipped on the same branch: a caching-strategy audit that found and fixed a real gap (`getLeaderboardEntries` was re-querying on every visit despite being identical for every viewer). **Merged to `develop` via PR #113 (merge commit `e07932d`)**, branch `feature/57-settings-account` kept, not deleted.

**Also built, Phase 13 extra — see the Feature 58 entry below:** Settings → Security — a real Active Sessions list (device/browser label, IP, last-active time, current-device tag) with per-session revoke and "sign out of all other devices." Ran the `architect` skill before building; surfaced and resolved one real architectural decision up front (Better Auth's `listSessions()` requires a fresh, <1-day-old session by default, which would wall out most returning users just for *viewing* their sessions — the list is read directly from the `session` table instead, keeping the freshness gate only on the actual mutations). A second real finding turned up during testing, not planning: `authClient.revokeOtherSessions()` only revokes what Better Auth's Redis session index knows about, not what this page's Postgres-sourced list shows — fixed by revoking each listed session's own token individually. Built, typechecked, linted, and tested against the live account (including two disposable DB-inserted fake sessions, cleaned up after). A senior-engineer code review before commit caught and fixed two real race conditions (no serialization between concurrent revoke actions; a partial-failure path in "sign out of all other devices" that skipped `router.refresh()` even when some revokes had genuinely succeeded). Live two-real-browser testing then surfaced a genuine security gap — a revoked session stayed valid for minutes via Better Auth's cookie cache — fixed by shrinking `auth.ts`'s `cookieCache.maxAge` from 5 minutes to 30 seconds, verified via a real revoke-and-recheck test. A full pre-merge regression pass across every route in the app (not just this feature) followed and found no additional code defects — see the dedicated regression-pass entry below. A CodeRabbit review on the pushed PR then found one more real gap — raw session tokens reaching the client — fixed by identifying sessions by `id` everywhere and moving the actual revoke logic server-side into two new routes (`app/api/settings/security/revoke(-others)`); see the dedicated CodeRabbit-fix entry below for the full trail, including a stale duplicate finding correctly identified and left unapplied (replying on the PR thread explaining why). **Merged to `develop` via PR #115 (merge commit `be47452`)**, branch `feature/58-settings-security` kept, not deleted.

**Last completed:** 37 Streak System Polish — ran the `architect` skill before building; 3 decisions confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (the 30-day heat map is graded by daily XP into 3 intensity bands — 0 / 1–24 / 25+ — rather than a flat binary lit/unlit cell; the 7/30/100-day milestone confetti fires on the Learn page banner, deduped via a `localStorage` key so it doesn't replay on refresh, rather than on the settings/profile page; the Learn page streak banner only shows once `streakCurrent >= 2`, not from day 1). No schema changes — every value comes from `profiles` (`streak_current`, `streak_longest`, `streak_last_activity`) and `xp_events` (`created_at`, `xp_amount`), both already written by the existing streak system (`applyProgressUpdate.ts`, Feature 27). Real gap found during planning: `build-plan.md` calls for the calendar on "profile/settings," but neither route existed yet — `UserDropdown.tsx`'s Profile/Settings items (Feature 17) already linked to `/settings/profile`/`/settings` and `proxy.ts` already login-gated both (Feature 14). First attempt built `/settings/profile` as the real page and `/settings` as a `redirect()` to it — **corrected after real user feedback** (see below): the streak calendar now lives directly at `/settings` (no `/settings/profile` route at all), and both dropdown items point there. There's still only one real settings page — a second, distinct `/settings` (Account info, preferences, etc.) is explicitly deferred to a future feature, not invented now.

**Two post-build fixes, same branch, both from direct user feedback against real screenshots:** (1) **Heat map visual bug** — the grid's implicit `auto`-sized columns had nothing constraining their width, and since Tailwind/CSS grid's default `justify-content: normal` behaves like `stretch` for non-flexible auto tracks when there's leftover container space, the weekly columns stretched apart across the full card width instead of packing tightly — fixed with `auto-cols-max justify-start` on the grid container. Separately, `rounded-sm` resolves to 6px in this project's token scale (`ui-tokens.md`), which reads as a near-circle on a 14px cell — replaced with an explicit `rounded-[2px]` on both the cells and the legend swatches for a small-square, GitHub-contribution-graph look. (2) **`/settings` vs `/settings/profile` being two different pages, one a redirect** — user flagged this as wrong on sight. Asked what should differentiate them via `AskUserQuestion`; user had no spec for `/settings` yet, so rather than invent Account/preferences content, collapsed both dropdown items to the one real page (now at `/settings`), deleted the `/settings/profile` route and its `warmup-dev.mjs` entry, and left a comment on `UserDropdown.tsx` marking this as temporary pending a real Settings-scope decision. Re-verified after both fixes: `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean, `warmup-dev.mjs` 33/33 green (one transient 500 on `/learn` during the first re-run, confirmed transient by an immediate clean retry — same class of flake already documented elsewhere in this file), and the heat map re-screenshotted via the same temporary preview-page + Playwright approach — small square cells, no stretched gaps, correct in both themes.

**Settings rebuilt again, same branch, this time as a real modal — user showed a Claude Code screenshot of its own Settings overlay (URL updates to `/settings/general`, background page stays visible/dimmed) and asked for the same pattern, with a sidebar and a Profile section for now.** Implemented with Next.js's actual documented pattern for this (Parallel + Intercepting Routes — read from `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` per `AGENTS.md`'s "this is not the Next.js you know" rule before writing anything): a new `@settingsModal` slot on `app/(app)/layout.tsx`, `app/(app)/@settingsModal/(.)settings/[section]/page.tsx` intercepts client-side navigation to `/settings/[section]` and renders `SettingsModal` (a `Dialog`-based overlay) over whatever page was open; a hard navigation/refresh to the same URL instead renders `app/(app)/settings/[section]/page.tsx`, the real full page (`SettingsShell` — sidebar + content, no dialog chrome). `app/(app)/settings/page.tsx` redirects to `/settings/profile`, the only real section today (`features/settings/lib/sections.ts`, `SETTINGS_SECTIONS = ["profile"]` — extensible for Billing etc. once those exist, per explicit user instruction not to invent them yet). Both `UserDropdown.tsx` items were later repointed (see the Dashboard entry below) but Settings itself still opens straight to `/settings/profile`. **Mechanism verified with an isolated, no-login-required test harness** (`@previewModalTest` slot + throwaway trigger/target pages, all deleted after) rather than assumed from documentation alone: confirmed via Playwright that a client-side (soft) navigation masks the URL and overlays the modal over the still-visible trigger page, a hard navigation/refresh renders the real full page with no modal chrome, and — the real risk area — `redirect()` inside the intercepted route (the auth gate, when logged out) correctly breaks out of the interception and lands on `/login` rather than hanging or erroring.

**Real dev-server instability hit hard during this session, unrelated to the code itself** — the Windows dev server repeatedly died or hung for 10+ minutes with zero compile progress after structural route changes (adding parallel-route slots while already running), on a machine `next dev` itself flagged with "Slow filesystem detected... consider moving `.next/dev` to a local folder." Recovered each time via: killing orphaned Node processes left behind when a stopped background task didn't release its port, a full `.next` wipe + cold restart, and ultimately falling back to a full `npm run build` (a one-shot, deterministic compile) to get environment-independent confirmation that every route — including `/dashboard`, `/settings`, `/settings/[section]`, and the intercepted `/(.)settings/[section]` — compiles cleanly (136/136 static pages, zero errors) when the dev server itself wasn't cooperating.

**Then a new Dashboard page, same branch, per a second reference screenshot (an AlgoMaster-style learner dashboard) and explicit instruction to rename the "Profile" dropdown item to "Dashboard" and keep Settings parked as-is.** `UserDropdown.tsx`: "Profile" → "Dashboard" (`/dashboard`, new); "Settings" still → `/settings/profile` (unchanged). New `/dashboard` page and `features/dashboard/` built entirely from data that already existed — no schema changes: `getDashboardProfileStats`/`getWeeklyDashboardStats`/`getTotalUserCount`/`hasAnyLearningProgress` (`features/dashboard/lib/queries.ts`) reuse `profiles`, and Feature 36's `getUserRank`/`getLeaderboardEntries` directly rather than duplicating weekly-rank logic. `getDailyActivity` (`features/streak/lib/queries.ts`) was generalized to take a `days` parameter (was hardcoded to 30) so the Dashboard's 6-month activity graph reuses the exact same query as the Settings Profile section's 30-day one, just with a wider window; `intensityClass` was pulled out to a shared `features/streak/lib/intensity.ts` for the same reason. A `level` system is genuinely new — no such concept existed anywhere before this — `features/dashboard/lib/level.ts` defines a simple, transparent, explicitly-not-final formula (`level = floor(xp / 50) + 1`, 50 XP per level) rather than guessing at a real curve. **Deliberately not built: "Achievements" (0/8 badges) and "New releases,"** both visible in the reference screenshot — neither has any schema, content, or definition in this codebase, and inventing either would break the project's own established "honest empty state, don't fake data" precedent (Interview Prep's Review Queue, Practice Hub's old "Continue where you left off" card, etc.). Flagged explicitly rather than silently omitted. `StartLearningCard` (the "Start your first course" empty state) is shown only when `hasAnyLearningProgress` is false, and disappears entirely once real progress exists — no fake "continue where you left off" targeting logic was built to replace it. **Verified via the same production-build fallback** (dev server was unusable at the time) plus a temporary synthetic-data preview page + Playwright screenshots in both themes (deleted after): caught and fixed two real bugs this way — the 6-month heatmap's month labels collided into unreadable text ("JanFeb") whenever the window started mid-month, and the "lessons completed" stat tile's label truncated to "lessons compl…" (fixed by letting it wrap to two lines instead of forcing `truncate`).

**Two more rounds of fixes, same branch, both from real user feedback against the actual running Dashboard:** (1) the activity heatmap used `StreakHeatMap`'s fixed-size GitHub-style cells, which left a large dead gap on the right of the much wider Dashboard card instead of filling it — switched to `flex-1` columns + `aspect-square` cells so it stretches to the card's full width at any size. (2) The first attempt at fixing the month-label collision (a minimum-column-gap rule that skipped a label if it sat too close to the previous one) turned out to be a worse bug than the one it fixed: it silently dropped real months from the calendar (e.g. February disappearing entirely, making January's block look like it spanned nearly two months' worth of days) — caught from a real screenshot, not just theorized. Correctness over cosmetics: removed the skip-rule entirely so every month always gets a label, and instead capped each label to its own column's width (`overflow-hidden`/`text-ellipsis`) so a too-narrow column clips its label ("Ja") instead of letting two adjacent labels overlap — no month is ever silently hidden again.

**Full code-quality/security review pass, same branch, on explicit user request** ("check security, data handling, code quality... fix and retest before committing"). Read every new/changed file (25 new, 8 modified). Security/data-handling: every new query is correctly scoped by `userId` (`eq(profiles.id, userId)` / `eq(xpEvents.userId, userId)` / etc.), no raw string concatenation in the two new raw-SQL `sql` templates (both parameterized via Drizzle's tagged-template, column references only), no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` anywhere (all user-facing text — display names, quotes — goes through normal JSX text interpolation, which auto-escapes), the `section` route param is validated against a fixed allowlist (`isSettingsSection`) before any use, and no new write/POST routes were added (so no rate-limiting gap). No `any` types, no leftover `console.log`/`TODO` markers, no hardcoded colors anywhere in the new code (all through CSS-variable-backed tokens). **Two real code-quality findings, both fixed:** `app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx` had the same `<LevelCard ... />` JSX (7 identical props) written out twice across a ternary branch — extracted to a single `levelCard` variable rendered in both branches; `LeaderboardPreviewCard.tsx` called `cn()` on a single static class string with no conditional logic to merge — dead indirection, replaced with a plain string. Re-verified after both fixes: `tsc --noEmit` clean, a full project-wide `eslint .` clean (zero errors/warnings), and a second full `npm run build` succeeded (136/136 static pages, same route list as before, zero errors) — confirming the fixes didn't regress anything.

**One more real bug, same branch, caught from a real screenshot of the live Dashboard (not synthetic data this time):** the 6-month activity heat map's flat 183-day rolling window almost always starts mid-month — for a user viewing it on July 28, that's January 27, meaning the window actually touched **7** different calendar months (a partial January plus Feb–Jul) while the card's own text said "last 6 months." Worse, because columns are 7-day weeks continuous across month boundaries, the number of columns between one month's label and the next varied for reasons invisible to anyone without a GitHub-contribution-graph mental model, reading as arbitrary ("why does March get 5 columns and April only 4?"). Fixed properly, not patched: new `features/dashboard/lib/dateWindow.ts` — `getCalendarWindowDays(monthsBack)` computes the exact day count from the 1st of the UTC calendar month `monthsBack − 1` months ago through today, so the window always starts exactly on a month boundary (no more phantom 7th month). `DashboardActivityHeatmap.tsx` was restructured to match: `buildMonthGroups()` splits the activity list by real calendar month (comparing each date's `"YYYY-MM"` prefix) and pads **each month independently** to a whole number of weeks (its own leading weekday offset, its own trailing padding) — rendered as 6 distinct mini-calendars side by side with a visible gap between them, each with its own label, instead of one continuous unlabeled-boundary strip. A "short" month like February now visibly has fewer boxes for the same reason a real February wall-calendar page does, not for an unexplained reason. Verified via a temporary synthetic-data preview page (deleted after): `getCalendarWindowDays(6)` on the actual system date produced `first date = 2026-02-01`, exactly 6 labels (Feb–Jul, no January), confirmed in both a wide and a narrow card. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on the changed files.

**Correction to the previous entry, same branch, caught immediately from screenshots of the actual rendered card next to a real github.com contribution graph:** the per-calendar-month mini-grid redesign above was itself wrong — it split the heat map into 6 visually separate boxes with gaps between them, which isn't how GitHub's own graph looks and read as a second broken design rather than a fix. Reverted to a single continuous grid: `buildMonthGroups()` was replaced with `buildWeeks()` (lays the whole window out as one unbroken run of Sunday-to-Saturday week columns; only the partial week at each end — before the window start or after today — gets null-padded cells) and `buildMonthLabels()` (labels a column only when it contains that month's 1st, matching where GitHub's own graph switches labels). `getCalendarWindowDays()` from the previous fix was correct and untouched — the window-boundary math was never the problem, only the rendering was. Box-count differences between months are now explained the same way GitHub explains them: real calendar days in one continuous grid, not an invented per-month boundary. Verified via a temporary synthetic-data preview page (deleted after) with the real `getCalendarWindowDays(6)` window (Feb 1 – Jul 28) and Playwright screenshots in both themes: one unbroken grid, correct month labels (Feb–Jul) at each month's first column, no gaps, no separators. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.

**One more polish item, same branch, caught from a screenshot of the live Dashboard:** hovering a heat map cell showed the browser's own unstyled system tooltip (native `title` attribute) — looked out of place next to a designed card. Fixed with a new shared `features/streak/components/HeatmapCell.tsx`, a small client component wrapping the project's existing (until now unused) Base-UI-backed `Tooltip`/`TooltipTrigger`/`TooltipContent` (`components/ui/tooltip.tsx`, `TooltipProvider` already mounted in the root layout) — both `DashboardActivityHeatmap.tsx` and `StreakHeatMap.tsx` (Settings > Profile's 30-day view, which had the identical bug) now render through it instead of a raw `<div title=...>`. Verified via a temporary synthetic-data preview page + Playwright hover simulation (deleted after): a styled dark tooltip with an arrow now appears on hover, matching the rest of the app's tooltip styling. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on all four changed files.

**One more polish item, same branch, caught from a screenshot of the live Dashboard:** the greeting ("Good afternoon, ...") was computed from `getUTCHours()`, so it could read "Good afternoon" while it was actually evening for the visitor — the date line intentionally stays UTC-based (documented architecture decision, no per-user timezone system exists), but a greeting has no such invariant to protect, so this was a real bug, not a design tradeoff. Fixed with new `features/dashboard/lib/greeting.ts` (`greetingForHour(hour)`, the pure morning/afternoon/evening logic, shared) and a new client component `features/dashboard/components/GreetingLine.tsx` using the same `useSyncExternalStore` split as `useSafeReducedMotion.ts`: `getServerSnapshot` renders the UTC-based guess (avoids a hydration mismatch), `getSnapshot` swaps in the greeting for the visitor's real local hour immediately after hydration — no naive `useState`+`useEffect` flag, same reasoning as that existing hook. `DashboardHeader.tsx` now renders `<GreetingLine displayName={...} />` in place of the old inline `getGreeting()`. Verified via a temporary preview page + Playwright (deleted after): rendered "Good evening" matching the test machine's actual local hour (23), zero console errors/warnings (confirming no hydration mismatch). `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.

**A real bug, not a Dashboard one this time — found via a user repro on a Learn concept page:** opening Settings from the navbar dropdown worked fine, but opening it from `LearnSidebar`'s bottom-of-sidebar "Settings" link produced a cascade of React/DOM crashes (`Cannot perform a React state update on a component that hasn't mounted yet`, `Base UI: Cannot call an event handler while rendering`, `Failed to execute 'removeChild' on 'Node': the node to be removed is not a child of this node`). Root cause: the navbar dropdown's link goes straight to `/settings/profile` (`router.push("/settings/profile")`, matching the `(.)settings/[section]` intercepting-route pattern exactly), but `LearnSidebar.tsx` linked to bare `/settings`, which doesn't match that pattern and has to server-`redirect()` to `/settings/profile` — that redirect firing mid-client-navigation is what corrupted the parallel-route (`@settingsModal`) reconciliation and crashed the DOM. Fixed by changing `LearnSidebar.tsx`'s Settings link to `href="/settings/profile"` directly, matching the already-working dropdown pattern (grepped the whole `src/` tree for any other bare `href="/settings"` — this was the only one). Verified via Playwright: the fixed link now fires a single direct RSC request to `/settings/profile` with zero console errors — byte-for-byte the same network/console behavior as the navbar dropdown's link, confirmed side by side. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.

**CodeRabbit review on PR #107, all 6 findings verified against current code and confirmed real, all fixed:** (1) `GreetingLine.tsx`'s `subscribe` was a true no-op — a tab left open across an hour boundary would never re-render, freezing on whatever greeting was current at mount; now schedules a callback right at the next hour boundary and reschedules itself. (2) `LevelCard.tsx`'s XP progress bar (`role="progressbar"`) had no accessible name; added `aria-label="Level progress"`. (3) `SettingsShell.tsx`/`SettingsModal.tsx` used a fixed `flex` row with no responsive fallback — a 375px-wide sidebar+content squeeze was a real mobile bug; both now stack sidebar-above-content below `md` (`SettingsModal`'s divider switches from `border-r` to `border-b` to match), restoring the row layout at `md`. (4) `HeatmapCell`/`StreakHeatMap` (and, by the same underlying bug, `DashboardActivityHeatmap` — not named in the review but sharing the identical pattern): the tooltip's per-day detail only surfaced on hover/focus, and the parent grid's `role="img"` flattened every child cell into one opaque image for screen readers — `HeatmapCell` now carries its own `aria-label`/`role="listitem"`, both grids switched from `role="img"` to `role="list"`. (5) `MilestoneCelebration`'s dedup key (`userId:streakCurrent`) had no date component, so reaching a milestone once permanently suppressed celebrating that same threshold again after a later streak break and rebuild — `streakLastActivity` is now threaded through as a prop and included in the key. (6) Docs (`progress-tracker.md`, `ui-registry.md`) still said "PR not yet opened" — updated to PR #107. Verified: full-project `eslint .`/`tsc --noEmit` clean, hour-boundary timer math sanity-checked in isolation (always lands exactly on the next hour, including across midnight), Playwright-confirmed the mobile (375px) and desktop (1200px) responsive stacking for both Settings surfaces and the new `role="list"`/`role="listitem"` accessibility tree (per-cell `aria-label` like `"2026-06-29 — 50 XP"` now present regardless of hover state), and a full `npm run build` (136/136 static pages, zero errors).

New `features/streak/lib/queries.ts` — `getStreakSummary(userId)` (`cache()`-wrapped read of the 3 profile columns) and `getDailyActivity(userId)` (groups `xp_events` by UTC calendar day over the last 30 days via a raw `(created_at at time zone 'utc')::date::text` SQL cast — this codebase's second raw-SQL-in-Drizzle query after Feature 36's `rank()` — zero-filled so the heat map never has to backfill gaps itself). `features/streak/lib/milestones.ts` — `isMilestoneDay()` is true only when `streakLastActivity === todayUtc()` (reused from `applyProgressUpdate.ts`) and `streakCurrent` is one of the new `STREAK_MILESTONES = [7, 30, 100]` (`lib/constants.ts`); it naturally stops firing once the UTC day rolls over, no "acknowledged" flag needed.

`StreakHeatMap` (`features/streak/components/`) is a Server Component — 30 cells in a `grid-flow-col grid-rows-7` layout aligned to real UTC weekdays (GitHub-contribution-graph convention), 3-level `bg-border`/`bg-streak-light`/`bg-streak` coloring, current/longest streak stats above, a Less→More legend. `StreakBanner` is presentational only; `StreakBannerSection` (also server) is the single place deciding whether the banner (and its confetti) render at all, so the two can't drift out of sync. `MilestoneCelebration` reuses Feature 53's `Celebration` burst component but deliberately avoids a naive `useState`+`useEffect` "has fired" flag — that trips the `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule and is the wrong tool for syncing with an external mutable source (`localStorage`), same reasoning already documented on `useSafeReducedMotion.ts` — instead reads the dedup flag via `useSyncExternalStore` (a no-op `subscribe`, `getServerSnapshot` always `false` to avoid a hydration mismatch) and marks it seen via a plain `useEffect` with no `setState` call.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` (targeted new-file sweep) both clean; `warmup-dev.mjs`'s `ROUTES` list gained `/settings` and `/settings/profile` (both new login-gated routes warmed cleanly, 34/34 routes green, redirecting to `/login` when logged out exactly like `/leaderboard`). **Visual verification worked around this environment's standing inability to drive a real OAuth session** — rather than skip it, a temporary, non-routed preview page (`dev-preview-streak-37/`; the first attempt at `_dev-preview-streak-37/` 404'd because Next.js treats any `_`-prefixed folder as private/non-routable, not a Turbopack bug) rendered the real `StreakHeatMap`/`StreakBannerSection` components with synthetic data, screenshotted via Playwright (`C:\tmp_pw`) in both themes, then deleted before commit. Confirmed: the banner correctly stays hidden below `streakCurrent` 2, the heat map's 3 color bands land exactly where the synthetic data pattern predicts, and the confetti burst fires and animates when `isMilestoneDay` is true — in both light and dark mode. **Not yet done, as of this entry (now superseded — PR #107 opened, went through a CodeRabbit round, and merged; see the entries above):** PR not opened, no CodeRabbit round yet.

**Previously completed:** 36 Leaderboard Page — ran the `architect` skill before building; 3 decisions confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (This Week/This Month use UTC calendar boundaries — Monday-start week, 1st-of-month — same day-boundary convention the streak system already uses, not a rolling trailing window; ranking is a real DB-side query rather than fetch-all-and-sort-in-memory, since a leaderboard is exactly the feature where user growth matters most; Concepts Completed/Streak stay fixed all-time snapshots regardless of the active range tab, only Rank/XP move). No new schema — every column build-plan.md asks for already existed on `profiles`/`xp_events`/`user_concept_progress`. `/leaderboard` was already login-gated by `proxy.ts`'s matcher from Feature 14, but that matcher only checks cookie *presence*, not a real session — so "Your Rank" always rendering with no logged-out branch relies on the page's own defensive `getCachedSession()` + redirect (same precedent as `/interview-prep/review`), not the matcher alone. New `features/leaderboard/lib/queries.ts` — **this codebase's first use of a raw SQL window function inside a Drizzle query** (`rank() over (order by ... desc)`, cast `::int` since Postgres returns `bigint`/`numeric` for `rank()`/`sum()` and node-pg would otherwise hand back a string): `getLeaderboardEntries(range)` ranks directly off `profiles.xp` for All Time, or off a live `SUM(xp_events.xp_amount)` grouped by user for Week/Month (users with zero events in the window simply don't appear — there's no value padding the table with tied-at-zero rows), capped at the top 100. `getUserRank(userId, range)` is a separate, lightweight `COUNT(*) + 1` query (a Drizzle subquery via `.as()` for the week/month case) so the logged-in user's real rank is correct even outside the top 100, without fetching every profile. Both use `rank()`, not `row_number()`, so tied scores share a rank — matching the `COUNT+1` formula's own tie semantics. Concepts Completed is a second, targeted query scoped to just the ids already on the page (`inArray`), not a join against the ranking query, to avoid a fan-out that would corrupt the XP sum. `LeaderboardRangeTabs` is a plain Server Component using `<Link href="/leaderboard?range=...">` — no client JS at all, since switching tabs is a real navigation that re-runs the ranked query server-side, not client-side state (deliberately simpler than the `FilterPillGroup` client pattern Practice/FF Collections use, since those filter already-fetched data in place). Podium (`LeaderboardPodium`, top 3 only when ≥3 ranked entries exist) uses the existing `--color-xp` token for the #1 slot rather than inventing gold/silver/bronze hardcoded colors. **Verified beyond the usual `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` (all clean) and a live dev-server route sweep (all 3 `?range=` params correctly 307-redirect to `/login` unauthenticated, confirming the route resolves and the auth gate works, not a Turbopack 404):** a disposable standalone script (same precedent as Feature 32's SM-2 spot-check) ran the actual ranking/rank-lookup SQL against the real dev DB with 2 real profiles — confirmed All Time ranks by total XP (870 vs 0), Week correctly showed only the 1 user with activity in the current calendar week (15 XP, excluding the 0-XP user entirely from that table), Month showed that same user's 870 XP (all earned this calendar month), and `getUserRank` correctly returned rank #2 for the 0-XP user in every range — including Week, where that user doesn't appear in the top-entries list at all, proving the two queries are consistent with each other. Script deleted after. **Authenticated page render confirmed via real user-supplied screenshots (this environment still can't drive an OAuth session itself)** across all 3 range tabs, logged in as the real "Superpower India" profile: All Time correctly shows both real profiles (#1 870 XP, #2 0 XP); This Week and This Month both correctly show only the 1 profile with real activity in that window, matching the standalone-script check exactly; the current user's own row is highlighted (`bg-accent-muted`) in every view since they're rank #1; the Premium pill renders under the premium profile's name; and — as designed — no podium renders with only 2 total ranked profiles, going straight to the table starting at rank 1, since the podium only activates at ≥3 entries. No visual bugs found.

**CodeRabbit review round 1 on the pushed branch, 3 findings addressed, 0 skipped:** (1) added migration `0026_leaderboard_query_indexes.sql` — `profiles_xp_idx` on `profiles.xp` and a composite `xp_events_user_id_created_at_idx` on `(user_id, created_at)` — this feature is the first thing in the codebase to actually `SELECT` from `xp_events` (every other consumer only inserts into it), so neither index existed before; generated via `drizzle-kit generate` and applied to the dev DB via `drizzle-kit migrate`, confirmed clean; (2) `LeaderboardTable.tsx`'s CSS-grid rows had no table semantics for assistive tech at all — added `role="table"`/`row`/`columnheader`/`cell` across the container and every row, same fix shape as any div-based grid standing in for a real `<table>`; (3) corrected the "every visitor has a session" phrasing in this file and `ui-registry.md` — `proxy.ts`'s matcher is only a cookie-presence check, not a real guarantee; the actual guarantee is the page's own `getCachedSession()` + redirect. Re-verified after the fixes: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint .` (full project, not just new files) clean, full `next build` clean.

**CodeRabbit review round 2, 1 finding fixed, 1 skipped with a verified reason:** (1) fixed — `LeaderboardTable`'s fixed columns (3rem+5rem+4rem+4rem) plus 4 `gap-3` gaps plus `px-4` padding already total ~19rem before the name column gets any room, exceeding a 320px viewport; the outer `overflow-hidden` was silently clipping the rightmost columns (including "Done") instead of scrolling to them. Fixed by wrapping both the header row and the data-row list in a new `overflow-x-auto` div and giving both row templates a matching `min-w-[30rem]`, so columns/gaps stay aligned while scrolling instead of independently compressing; the outer `role="table"` div keeps `overflow-hidden` only to clip its rounded corners. (2) **skipped, not applied blindly:** CodeRabbit suggested `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` for migration 0026's two new indexes, to avoid a regular `CREATE INDEX`'s write-locking during deployment — checked against this project's actual migration runner before touching anything, not assumed: `drizzle-orm/pg-core/dialect.js`'s `migrate()` wraps every pending migration in one `session.transaction(...)`, and Postgres hard-errors if `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` runs inside a transaction block. Applying the suggestion as written would have broken `npx drizzle-kit migrate` outright the next time it ran (not just been suboptimal) — for this migration and every other one batched with it. A correct fix would mean building a net-new non-transactional migration/deployment path with no precedent across the other 26 migrations in this repo — disproportionate for a pre-launch app with a two-row `profiles` table and no live production write traffic yet. Deferred for whenever real production traffic and a proper zero-downtime migration process are actually needed. Re-verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean on `features/leaderboard` (full-project sweep at the time still showed one pre-existing, unrelated `useFlowConnectors.ts` finding — fixed separately afterward, see below). **Merged to `develop` via PR #102 (merge commit `61fe3df`)**, branch `feature/36-leaderboard-page`, commits `a06c9a7` (full build) + `d2f335c` (real-screenshot verification note) + `710e085` (CodeRabbit round 1) + `d7b286a` (CodeRabbit round 2).

**Follow-up fix, separate branch:** the one pre-existing `useFlowConnectors.ts` `prefer-const` finding (`raf` declared with `let` but never reassigned — a Feature 35 leftover, unrelated to Feature 36) was fixed on its own branch rather than folded into the Leaderboard PR. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` both fully clean project-wide afterward (zero errors, zero warnings); a production build's first run hit an unrelated transient DB connection timeout while prerendering `/interview-prep/study-plans/1-week` (a page this change never touches), confirmed transient by an immediate clean retry. **Merged to `develop` via PR #104**, branch `fix/useflowconnectors-prefer-const`, commit `530e929`.

**Known gap, deferred — API routes don't fully follow `error-handling.md`'s own invariants:** that doc states "every async function that calls an external service is wrapped in try/catch" and shows the canonical shape as one try/catch around the whole route body. In practice, every write route that does a pre-write `SELECT` check first — `roadmap-progress`, `interview-rating`, `interview-review-rating`, `progress`, `practice/discussion`, `practice/submit` — only wraps the final write step, leaving the read(s)/auth/rate-limit calls before it unguarded (an uncaught error there returns Next.js's generic error response instead of this app's `{error}` JSON shape). `settings/profile` (Feature 56) briefly joined this list when CodeRabbit flagged the same gap on that PR, but was then brought into compliance with `error-handling.md`'s canonical shape per a new standing instruction ("use try/catch for error handling from now on") — see the Feature 56 entry above; it's the reference example the remaining 6 routes should eventually match. Separately, `error-handling.md` also documents a `lib/toast.ts` + toast-notification system (wrapping e.g. `sonner`) that was never actually built — no toast library is installed, and every optimistic-update component (`RoadmapDetailView`, `CollectionQuestionListClient`) silently reverts on a failed save with no user-facing message, contradicting the doc's own "never swallow errors silently" rule. Surfaced during a CodeRabbit review of Feature 35's `roadmap-progress` route (see that entry below) — not fixed reactively there, since patching one route while its siblings keep the identical gap doesn't actually close the gap. **Deferred as a standalone pass**, whenever it's prioritized: (1) wrap the remaining 6 routes' reads/auth/rate-limit calls consistently, following `settings/profile`'s now-compliant shape, (2) actually build the toast system and wire it into the optimistic-update components that currently fail silently.
**Previously completed:** 54 Experiments Gallery — the second and final Phase 12 sub-section. Ran the `architect` skill first; 3 decisions confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (pilot = Particle Cursor Trail — vanilla canvas, zero deps, no camera/mic permission; thumbnails = real Playwright screenshots of the running demo, doubling as the page's og:image; View Code reads the experiment's real source files off disk, never a hand-maintained copy that could drift). Experiments are first-party, in-repo interactive components — **no DB table, no query layer, no iframe sandbox** (first-party code, not user-submitted; per build-plan.md). A server-safe metadata registry (`features/playground/experiments/registry.ts`: slug/title/description/tags/thumbnail + real on-disk source-file paths) is the source of truth for both the gallery cards and the detail page; the runnable component is mapped separately in `clientRegistry.tsx` because `next/dynamic({ ssr: false })` is only legal inside a Client Component (these demos are canvas/rAF-driven, client-only) and `registry.ts` must stay importable from the RSC list/detail pages. `lib/experimentSource.ts` reads the real source files (traversal-guarded to the experiments dir, same guard as `systemDesignGuides.ts`/`lib/mdx.ts`) so the View Code panel shows exactly what runs. New routes: `/playground/experiments` (grid of `ExperimentListCard`s from the registry, neal.fun-style) and `/playground/experiments/[slug]` (RSC — `generateStaticParams`/`generateMetadata` with canonical + OG/Twitter image wired to the thumbnail via a request-derived absolute URL, `notFound()` for unknown slugs; **not** login-gated, unlike the Battles editor — experiments are publicly viewable with no personalization). Client `ExperimentWorkspace` **(this original detail-page layout was redesigned the same session — see the "Same-session redesign" note below for the current full-bleed design; the description in this sentence is kept only for history)** = breadcrumb + title/tags + `ExperimentShareRow` (X/Facebook/Instagram, built locally rather than importing Practice's `ShareButtons` — features never import other features) + a View Code toggle, over an `ExperimentStage` (renders the dynamic demo, native Fullscreen API with a CSS fixed-overlay fallback, persistent bottom-center `frontendforever.dev` watermark link) and an `ExperimentCodeView` (read-only source; reuses the shared Monaco `CodeEditor` in a new **additive** `readOnly` mode rather than a hand-rolled `<pre>` — a `.tsx` file needs real language-aware highlighting). `ExperimentCard` (the hub's compact scroll-row card) extended with a real thumbnail + width lifted to callers (same move Feature 53 did to `BattleCard`); `ExperimentListCard` added as its richer grid sibling (BattleCard/BattleListCard precedent). The Playground hub's Experiments preview row converted from Feature 52's static `EXPERIMENT_PREVIEW_ITEMS` teaser to real registry data (same hub-preview-to-real conversion Features 50/51/53 did once their feature shipped) — `previewData.ts` deleted, nothing else imported it. One pilot experiment (`particle-cursor-trail` — a canvas particle trail that chases the cursor, brand colors resolved from `--color-accent`/`--color-premium` via `getComputedStyle` since canvas fillStyle can't consume CSS vars) proves the system end-to-end; remaining experiments are a separate user-supplied content pass, same pilot-then-full-run precedent as Features 49/53. Thumbnail screenshotted via the `C:\tmp_pw` Playwright setup, `warmup-dev.mjs` `ROUTES` gained both new routes. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; a full production `next build` (both new routes compile, 91 routes total); a live dev-server sweep — `/playground`, `/playground/experiments`, and the pilot detail route all clean 200s, and the running pilot demo captured with zero console/page errors via Playwright (which is also what produced the thumbnail); grepped every new file for hardcoded colors — the only hit is the canvas fillStyle fallback hex (documented, unreachable in practice, mirrors Feature 49's `RoughDiagram` precedent). **Not verified — same standing limitation as Features 29–53 in this environment:** the actual feel of the running demo, the Fullscreen toggle, and the Share dialog need the user's own click-through before merge. Branch `feature/54-experiments-gallery` off `develop`.

**Same-session redesign of the detail page, driven by a real user screenshot** — the first build's detail page was a boxed content page (breadcrumb + back button + title + description + tag chips + a full X/Facebook/Instagram share row, with the demo in an `aspect-[16/10]` card below). The user asked to strip all of that: **remove the breadcrumb/back-button, title, description, and tag chips entirely; replace the 3-icon share row with a single "Share on X" button matching the UI Battles editor's `BattleShareButton`; make the canvas fill the whole screen below the navbar; and make View Code a premium feature.** Rebuilt to match: `ExperimentWorkspace` is now a full-bleed `h-[calc(100dvh-3.5rem)]` canvas (same viewport-minus-navbar calc as the Battles editor) with only a minimal floating top-right toolbar (Share on X + View Code + Fullscreen, all translucent-backdrop overlay buttons) and the persistent bottom-center watermark — no other chrome. `ExperimentStage` reduced to just rendering the dynamic demo (all chrome/fullscreen lifted up to the workspace). `ExperimentShareRow` (X/Facebook/Instagram) deleted, replaced by `ExperimentShareButton` (X-only, local sibling of `BattleShareButton`). **View Code is now genuinely premium-gated, enforced server-side** — the detail page fetches the viewer's session + `getIsPremiumUser` and only calls `readExperimentSources` for a premium viewer (a non-premium/logged-out viewer's props carry an empty `sources` + `isCodeLocked: true`, so the real source is never serialized to a non-premium client) — same real-strip pattern as the Battles solution, and it surfaces through the same `Dialog` primitive: a locked "Upgrade to Premium" note for non-premium viewers, the read-only Monaco source view for premium ones. The SEO `generateMetadata` (title/description/OG) is untouched — only the visible page body chrome was removed. Re-verified live via Playwright (logged-out): full-bleed canvas renders and runs with the particle trail, the minimal toolbar shows the lock-icon View Code button, the dialog shows the premium-locked state, zero console/page errors. `tsc`/`eslint` clean.

**Thumbnail redesign, same session (user flagged the card looked empty):** the literal demo screenshot (faint particles on a white canvas) read as "nothing there" at card size, so it was replaced with a designed **neal.fun-style particle-art cover** — a glowing teal-headed comet with a violet-tinted tail on a dark gradient, generated via a standalone canvas HTML + Playwright (`C:\tmp_pw`), used for both the gallery grid and hub-row thumbnail and the page's og:image. Verified in both cards. **A dev-only gotcha surfaced and is documented for future image swaps:** after overwriting `public/experiments/particle-cursor-trail.png`, the hub card kept showing the old image while the list card showed the new one — Next's dev image optimizer had cached a stale small-size (`w=640`) variant that survived targeted `.next/cache/images` deletes (Windows file-locking, same family of dev-server quirk as the Turbopack notes in `AGENTS.md`); only a full `.next` wipe cleared it. Production builds regenerate every variant, so this never affects the deployed asset — but locally, after swapping an image, stop the dev server and delete the whole `.next`, not just `.next/cache/images`.

**CodeRabbit review addressed (4 findings) before merge:** (1) removed the hardcoded `#14b8a6` fallback in `ParticleCursorTrail` — particle colors now come only from `--color-accent`/`--color-premium`, and `spawn()` skips if neither resolves (no invented color); (2) made the particle animation frame-rate independent — movement/gravity/decay scale by a clamped delta-time so 120Hz displays and slow frames match the authored 60Hz; (3) replaced the detail page's local `getBaseUrl` duplicate with the shared `lib/seo.ts` helper; (4) marked this entry's original detail-page description as superseded by the redesign above. **One finding skipped with reason:** CodeRabbit suggested deriving `getBaseUrl` from a validated site-URL config, but no such config exists in this project by design (no `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`, per `AGENTS.md`) — the shared request-derived `lib/seo.ts` helper is the canonical approach, already used by Practice's and the Battles editor's pages. Re-verified after the fixes: `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` all clean, demo still runs error-free with both brand colors. **Merged to `develop` via PR #97 (merge commit `9371b33`)**, branch `feature/54-experiments-gallery`, commits `4c6d2b0` (build) + `6bd154f` (full-bleed detail + premium View Code) + `874650a` (neal.fun thumbnail) + `98aa4f7` (CodeRabbit fixes). Phase 12 — Playground is now fully complete.

**Previously completed:** 53 UI Battles — List + Editor. Full 3-column IDE-style editor (collapsible file tree, Monaco, a live compare pane with Diff/Slide & Compare modes), a real premium-gated official solution (genuinely stripped server-side, not the cosmetic pill used elsewhere in Playground), X share with a real OG image, and both `BattleCard`/`BattleListCard` polished across two rounds of visual feedback. Two real pilot challenges ("Login Card", "Profile Card") prove the system end-to-end, including genuine cross-battle navigation. Built across **six same-session follow-up rounds** driven by direct user feedback against the running build — full narrative (including two real architecture reversals: the target reference became a live iframe not just a static screenshot, and a `ResizeObserver`-based letterbox that was solving a problem that didn't actually exist got ripped out for a plain full-fill layout) — **plus two rounds of CodeRabbit PR-review fixes and a full browser-based login/logged-out regression pass (21/21 + an 8/8 cross-battle isolation check), all using a Better-Auth `testUtils`-minted session since no OAuth is automatable here** — preserved further down this file, starting at "**Last completed:** 53 UI Battles — List + Editor." **Merged to `develop` via PR #95 (merge commit `a7a4990`)**, branch `feature/53-ui-battles`.

**55 Settings Overlay Resize + Feedback/Changelog/Progress Pages — not a `build-plan.md` feature, requested directly by the user as "extras," explicitly numbered 55 (not 38, which is already Phase 9's Premium Content Gating) continuing the append-only numbering Phase 12 established.** Ran the `architect` skill first; scope was narrowed twice via `AskUserQuestion` — first confirming this shouldn't collide with the real Feature 38, then confirming the round covered the full original list minus `/pricing`, a Billing section, an Upgrade nav item, and a free/premium badge (all explicitly dropped, since none of those have real backing data yet — no Stripe/billing code exists anywhere in this repo, and inventing fake payment/plan UI would break this project's own "no fake data" precedent), and a final decision that the Feedback mechanism should be a plain `mailto:` link, not a new DB table (simpler, zero new schema, still genuinely functional). **Settings overlay resize:** `SettingsModal.tsx` grew from `max-w-2xl` (672px, no fixed height) to `max-w-4xl` (896px) with a fixed `h-160` (640px) and a wider `md:w-56` sidebar, matching the reference Claude.ai settings screenshot's proportions much more closely; `SettingsShell.tsx` (the full-page fallback) got the same `md:w-56` sidebar width for consistency between the two surfaces. **Feedback** (`app/(app)/feedback/page.tsx`): a simple centered page with a `mailto:` button + the address as plain text, addressed to the real site owner's own inbox rather than a fake/unmonitored placeholder domain (this project has no configured domain at all, per `AGENTS.md`'s "no `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`" rule) — genuinely functional, not just decorative. **Changelog** (`app/(app)/changelog/page.tsx` + `features/changelog/lib/entries.ts`): real, dated entries traced from actual `git log --merges` output (e.g. PR #107 on 2026-07-29 down through PR #1 on 2026-06-17), rewritten in user-facing language rather than this file's own internal dev-log tone — internal-only merges (auth/DB/Redis setup, data-layer hardening, docs/chore syncs) deliberately left out since there's nothing user-visible to announce for those. **View Progress** (`app/(app)/learn/stats/page.tsx`, nests under `learn/layout.tsx` so `LearnSidebar` renders automatically): a real page behind the "View learning stats" link, which had been dead since Feature 20 — total XP/current streak/longest streak (reusing `getDashboardProfileStats`, no new query) plus a per-category completion breakdown (reusing `getCategorySummaries`, the same query `LearnSidebar`'s own accordion already uses) with the identical `ICON_BG`/`ICON_TEXT`-by-`colorKey` mapping pattern `CategoryAccordionItem.tsx`/`CategoryCard.tsx` each already duplicate locally — matching that existing (imperfect but consistent) precedent rather than introducing a new shared abstraction the codebase doesn't already have. Own defensive session check + redirect to `/login`, since it nests under the publicly-browsable `/learn` (not in `proxy.ts`'s matcher) but is meaningless without an account. **Nav wiring:** `LearnSidebar.tsx`'s three links (`/feedback`, `/changelog`, `/learn/stats`) needed zero code changes — they'd been dead only because their target pages didn't exist yet. `UserDropdown.tsx` gained two new items (Feedback, Changelog) alongside the existing Dashboard/Settings, since it had neither before. `warmup-dev.mjs`'s `ROUTES` gained all three new routes. Verified: full-project `eslint .`/`tsc --noEmit` clean (after clearing a stale `.next/dev/types` cache that was throwing unrelated generated-file syntax errors — confirmed not a real code issue by deleting `.next` and rerunning clean), a temporary synthetic-data preview page + Playwright screenshots (deleted after) confirmed the resized modal's new proportions, the Feedback/Changelog pages' real content, and the View Progress layout with fake category data, zero console errors throughout, and a full `npm run build`. Branch `feature/55-settings-overlay-feedback-changelog-progress` off `develop`.

**Two follow-ups, same branch, from direct user feedback:** (1) the Feedback page's address changed from the site owner's personal inbox to `feedback@frontendforever.dev`, per explicit instruction. (2) **A real, previously-undiscovered bug, found via user repro on the actual public landing page (not a Dashboard/Learn page this time):** clicking Settings from the navbar dropdown while on the marketing homepage (`/`) rendered the Settings modal's backdrop over a completely blank body — not the landing page, blurred or otherwise.
