Root cause, confirmed against Next's own docs (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../parallel-routes.md`) before touching anything, per `AGENTS.md`'s "read the docs for this Next version" rule: `(app)/layout.tsx` has no `default.tsx` for its implicit `children` slot, which the docs state is required "to render a fallback for `children` when Next.js cannot recover the active state of the parent page." The Settings modal (`@settingsModal`'s `(.)settings/[section]` interceptor) had only ever been triggered from pages already inside `(app)`'s layout tree, where `children`'s previous page is always recoverable — the marketing homepage lives in a completely separate, sibling route group (`(main)`, its own `Navbar`/`Footer`, no shared layout with `(app)` below the root), so a soft navigation into `/settings/profile` from there is `(app)/layout.tsx`'s **very first mount**, and `children` has no state to recover and no default to fall back to. Confirmed empirically before fixing anything: a disposable repro (temporary auth bypasses on both settings routes + `proxy.ts`'s matcher, a throwaway link on the real homepage, all reverted after) showed the modal's backdrop over `(app)`'s own (wrong, logged-in) navbar with a flat blank body — not a rendering glitch, a structurally blank `children` slot, exactly as the docs predict. **Fixed at the source of the ambiguity, not by patching the symptom:** added a `hardNavigateSettings` prop to the shared `UserDropdown.tsx` (default `false`) — when `true`, the Settings item does a real `window.location.assign("/settings/profile")` instead of `router.push`, forcing the hard-navigation code path the docs say "should render the entire page instead of the modal, no interception." Only `Navbar.tsx` (the marketing `(main)` navbar, both its desktop and mobile render sites) passes `hardNavigateSettings`; `AppNavbar.tsx` (used only inside `(app)`, where the previous-page state is always recoverable) is untouched and keeps the soft-nav modal UX. Re-verified with the same repro harness: the fixed hard-navigation link now lands on the real, fully-populated `SettingsShell` page (sidebar + Profile content), zero blank body, zero console errors, harness fully reverted after. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` clean, full `npm run build` clean.

**First attempt at a real overlay from the landing page — tried and reverted, wrong architecture, not a dead end:** mirroring `(app)`'s exact `@settingsModal`/`(.)settings/[section]` slot + interceptor into `(main)/layout.tsx` too (own `default.tsx`, own intercepted route, `(main)/layout.tsx` rendering the new `{settingsModal}` slot alongside `{children}`) — the idea being that if `(main)` had its own interceptor, the previous-page-recovery problem above wouldn't apply. **Confirmed empirically not to work:** the dev server log showed the soft-navigation's RSC fetch itself 404ing (`GET /settings/profile 404`), which then fell back to a real hard navigation anyway — **two separate interceptors for the identical target route, defined in two different route groups, is what actually breaks**, not the cross-tree navigation itself. Reverted cleanly and restored the `hardNavigateSettings` fallback from above as a stopgap.

**Second attempt, the actually-correct fix, per user follow-up ("can we fix it or is it not fixable?") pushing for a real answer instead of accepting the tradeoff:** the fix isn't a second interceptor — it's **one** interceptor, moved to the layout both route groups actually share. `(main)` and `(app)` are siblings directly under the true root (`app/layout.tsx`); the previous attempt put a slot in each sibling instead of the one shared ancestor. Moved `@settingsModal` (both `default.tsx` and the intercepted `(.)settings/[section]/page.tsx`) from `app/(app)/@settingsModal/` to `app/@settingsModal/` — the root — and updated `app/layout.tsx` to render `{settingsModal}` alongside `{children}` (removed the now-redundant `settingsModal` prop/slot from `app/(app)/layout.tsx`, since the single root-level slot covers it now). Fully reverted the `hardNavigateSettings` workaround from `UserDropdown.tsx`/`Navbar.tsx` — no longer needed, plain `router.push` now works correctly from both layout trees. **Confirmed empirically, both directions:** a disposable repro (temporary auth bypasses, throwaway links on the real homepage and the real `/learn` page, all reverted after) showed, from the landing page, the URL updating to `/settings/profile`, the modal rendering, and — critically — the actual landing page (real Navbar, hero, simulator section, testimonials, footer) genuinely visible and blurred behind it, not blank; from `/learn` (an `(app)` page), the identical correct overlay behavior it already had, unaffected by the slot's move. Zero console errors either direction. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` clean, full `npm run build` clean. **Net result: the true overlay-over-any-page experience works everywhere now — the earlier conclusion that this was an unfixable platform limitation was wrong; the first attempt had just picked the wrong two-slots architecture instead of the one-shared-slot one.**

**Full senior-engineer-style regression pass, same branch, on explicit direct request before committing ("test the app thoroughly... check security, code quality... fix and retest") — five real bugs found, all fixed, none of them Feature 55's own code.** Static sweep first: full route inventory (39 pages + 9 API routes), `eslint`/`tsc --noEmit`/`next build` all clean; every `/api/*` route re-read against the auth → rate-limit → validate → ownership-scoped-write shape (all consistent, no gaps); no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` on user content anywhere; `getPlaybookChapter`'s path-resolution confirmed properly traversal-guarded.

- **`proxy.ts`'s middleware redirect dropped `callbackURL`.** Every per-page defensive redirect (`/settings/[section]`, `/dashboard`, `/leaderboard`, etc.) already appends `?callbackURL=...` so `/login` sends the user back where they came from — but `proxy.ts`'s own cookie-presence-check redirect (the one that actually fires *first*, for the exact routes in its matcher: `/leaderboard`, `/settings/:path*`, `/profile/:path*`, `/dashboard`) sent a bare `/login` instead, silently dropping the user onto `/login`'s default (`/learn`) after signing in. Caught via a Playwright regression sweep, not by inspection — confirmed via `/login` vs `/login?callbackURL=...` in the redirected URL. Fixed by setting `loginUrl.searchParams.set("callbackURL", request.nextUrl.pathname + request.nextUrl.search)` before redirecting. Same gap, same fix shape, found independently in 3 client components with their own logged-out-click handlers (`MarkUnderstoodButton.tsx`, `MarkAsReadButton.tsx`, `CollectionQuestionListClient.tsx`) — one of them (`MarkUnderstoodButton`) had a comment literally promising "sent to sign in, then back to this page" that the code never actually did; all three now build the redirect off `window.location.pathname`.
- **A real hydration mismatch, only surfaced by a genuine GitHub OAuth login (this environment can't drive OAuth itself — the user logged in for real and reported the console error).** `useUser.ts`'s `authClient` session store is a module-level nanostore singleton, shared across every client-side navigation in a tab — it can carry a stale or still-settling value (left over from before the page loaded, or a fetch aborted by a newer one — the `AbortError: signal is aborted without reason` in the reported console log) independent of what the server actually rendered. The hook trusted that store's `isPending` flag on the very first client render; when it happened to already read `false`/no-session while SSR had a fresh real session, the server's `UserDropdown` button and the client's first-paint `Login` link disagreed — React discarded and regenerated the subtree, logging the mismatch (plus an incidental "script tag" warning from Next's own dev-overlay internals getting swept into the same forced re-render). **Notable: this exact bug, with this exact root cause, had already been diagnosed and fixed once before** (2026-06-25, `user = session?.user ?? initialUser ?? null` — see the env-tls-interception memory) and had regressed back to the vulnerable `isPending ? initialUser : null` form by the time this pass reviewed the file — worth remembering this fix is easy to accidentally revert. Fixed with a stronger version of the same idea: a `hasMounted` state (flipped by a mount-only effect) forces the first client render to always equal `initialUser`, full stop, regardless of the shared store's state; only after mount does it switch to trusting the live session. Re-verified clean (zero console errors) across a full authenticated Playwright pass, not just the logged-out paths available before.
- **4 gallery cards (`BattleCard`, `BattleListCard`, `ExperimentCard`, `ExperimentListCard`) lazy-loaded their first, above-the-fold image** — Next's own dev warning flagged `/playground/battles/login-card-recreate.png` as the page's Largest Contentful Paint element with no `priority`. Fixed with `next/image`'s actual recommended mechanism (not the browser's literal `loading="eager"` suggestion — `priority` also sets `fetchpriority="high"` and injects a real preload link): each card gained an optional `priority` prop, and each list's render site (`/playground`, `/playground/battles`, `/playground/experiments`) passes `priority={index === 0}` — only the genuinely-first, above-the-fold item in each grid, not every image.
- **The big one — the account-menu avatar was completely unreachable from any `(app)` page at common laptop widths, found via real authenticated Playwright testing.** The user shared their own Better Auth session cookie (`better-auth.session_token` — deliberately *not* their real Google/GitHub password, confirmed as a genuinely valid, non-expired row in the `session` table before use, deleted from disk after every test round, session never signed out) so Settings/Feedback/Changelog/Sign-Out could be tested with a real logged-in identity instead of just the logged-out paths this environment can normally reach. `AppNavbar.tsx`'s account-menu avatar rendered off-viewport — genuinely unclickable — at 1024/1152/1280/1366px widths (1366×768 being the single most common laptop resolution). Two compounding causes, both fixed: (1) the old search trigger's inner `<span className="flex-1 ...">` had no truncation, so its placeholder text wrapped across ~4 lines instead of ellipsizing, ballooning the button past its own `w-52` allotment (`min-w-0 truncate` added); (2) even with that fixed, the nav links (606px) plus the stats/bell/avatar cluster (285px) alone already filled the exact `lg:` (1024px) breakpoint with zero slack — the search box, at any width, was the marginal straw. First fix: deferred the (still functionally inert — no `onClick` exists yet) search trigger to `2xl:` and tightened the row's `gap-5`→`gap-3 xl:gap-5` plus the nav block's `ml-10`→`ml-4 xl:ml-10`; confirmed zero overflow from 1024px through 1920px via a real-viewport sweep. **Then, per a user-shared reference screenshot** (AlgoMaster's compact icon+shortcut-badge search trigger that expands to a full command-palette overlay on click — that overlay is explicit future work, not built now) **and the user's direct ask for an opinion on the idea:** redesigned the trigger from a 288px labeled box to a 64px icon+`⌘K` pill, small enough to restore visibility starting at `xl:` (1280px) instead of `2xl:` (1536px) — the narrow 1024–1279px band is now the only range without it, since nav+stats+avatar alone already fill that width with no room to spare. Both stages verified against a real authenticated session across the same width sweep (1024/1152/1280/1366/1440/1536/1920px), plus a final screenshot confirming the visual result.
- **Full re-verification after all five fixes:** `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .` clean; a full authenticated Playwright pass (9/9 checks, zero console errors) covering the Settings overlay from both the landing page and an `(app)` page, a hard refresh on `/settings/profile`, Dashboard, `/learn/stats`, and the dropdown's Feedback/Changelog links — including the one check (opening Settings from `/learn`) that the `AppNavbar` overflow bug had been silently blocking. Session cookie deleted from disk after every round; never committed anywhere.

**Pushed and opened as PR #109. CodeRabbit review, 4 findings, all verified against current code and confirmed real — none skipped.** (1) `playground/page.tsx`'s `experiments.map` was missing `priority={index === 0}` on `ExperimentCard`, inconsistent with the identical `battles.map` right above it — added. (2) The 3 client-component `callbackURL` redirects (`CollectionQuestionListClient`, `MarkAsReadButton`, `MarkUnderstoodButton`) built the redirect from `window.location.pathname` alone, dropping any query string — all three now include `window.location.search` too, matching `proxy.ts`'s own convention. (3) `useUser.ts`'s `useState`+`useEffect` mount flag — **confirmed as a genuinely failing lint error** (`npx eslint src/hooks/useUser.ts` failed before the fix, not just a style nitpick) — rewritten to the same `useSyncExternalStore` pattern this codebase already uses for exactly this problem (`useSafeReducedMotion.ts`): `getServerSnapshot` always `false`, `getSnapshot` always `true`, so the first client render still matches SSR with no manual `setState` call. (4) *Nitpick, also confirmed real* — `next/image`'s `priority` prop is marked `@deprecated` in the installed Next.js 16.2.9's own type definitions, in favor of a functionally-identical `preload` prop; all 4 gallery cards (`BattleCard`, `BattleListCard`, `ExperimentCard`, `ExperimentListCard`) now pass `preload={priority}` to the underlying `<Image>`, keeping each component's own public `priority` prop name unchanged. Re-verified with the same full test suite as before (fresh `eslint`/`tsc --noEmit`/`next build`, 38/38 warmed routes, a 16/16-check Playwright pass, and the `AppNavbar` width sweep re-confirmed clean at all 7 widths) — zero regressions.

**Re-tested once more in full before merging, per explicit request, with a freshly-provided session cookie** — 23/23 Playwright checks (expanded to verify all 3 client components' `callbackURL` query-string preservation individually, not just one), zero console errors, `AppNavbar` sweep still clean at all 7 widths, fresh `eslint`/`tsc --noEmit`/`next build` all clean. **Merged to `develop` via PR #109 (merge commit `84fbfbe`)**, branch `feature/55-settings-overlay-feedback-changelog-progress` kept, not deleted.

**56 Settings — Profile Edit Form, first of the 4 Phase 13 extras from the Pre-Feature-56 decision entry below.** Ran the `architect` skill first; scope (username/full-name/bio length rules, avatar-as-URL not upload, type-to-confirm deferred to Feature 57's delete-account, lightweight manual UA parsing deferred to Feature 58) had already been resolved in that planning pass. New `POST /api/settings/profile` (auth → rate-limit → validate → write, same shape as `/api/progress`), `features/settings/lib/validateProfileInput.ts` (shared client/server validation), `features/settings/lib/queries.ts` (`getProfileForEdit`), `features/settings/components/ProfileEditForm.tsx`. `SettingsSectionContent.tsx`'s Profile branch now renders the form instead of `StreakHeatMap` — the heatmap wasn't deleted, it already lives on `/dashboard` (confirmed via `Grep` before removing anything) and stayed there untouched. Real username conflicts reuse `provisionProfile.ts`'s unique-violation-catch pattern (`profiles_username_unique`), surfaced as a proper 409 with a field-level message instead of a raw Postgres error; a new `getPostgresErrorConstraint` was added to `lib/dbErrors.ts` for this (small, safe extraction, no behavior change to the existing `getPostgresErrorCode`).
- **A real architecture bug found during testing, not just a checkbox pass:** the first version mirrored the edited name/avatar onto Better-Auth's own `user` table (read by the Navbar/`UserDropdown`/Dashboard greeting — `profiles.fullName`/`avatarUrl` are a separate record, only read by Leaderboard/Discussion) via a server-side `auth.api.updateUser()` call inside the route. That updated the DB row but never touched the Navbar, because Better-Auth's client only triggers its automatic reactive session-store refetch when its own `authClient` fetch wrapper sees the `/update-user` response — a server-side call is invisible to it (confirmed directly in `better-auth`'s own client source, `session-refresh.mjs`/`config.mjs`). Fixed by moving the mirror call to the client (`ProfileEditForm` calls `authClient.updateUser({ name, image })` itself, right after the `profiles` write succeeds) — the `profiles` row stays the authoritative, error-surfacing write; the client-side mirror is best-effort and only responsible for keeping the live session in sync.
- Verified via two full authenticated Playwright passes (fresh session cookies, deleted after each round), not just `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` (all clean): client-side validation (invalid username/empty name/bad URL all blocked before any network call), a **real** username conflict against a second genuine account (`aman-kumar` ↔ `superpower-india`, not a synthetic case) correctly rejected with 409 + the right message and zero data corruption, a valid save persisting across a hard reload, the Navbar dropdown reflecting the new name **immediately** post-save (confirming the client-mirror fix), both Settings surfaces (the intercepted overlay modal and the full-page shell) rendering the form correctly, the Dashboard's real heatmap unaffected, and a 9-route regression sweep (`/learn`, `/practice`, `/interview-prep`, `/leaderboard`, `/playground`, `/roadmaps`, `/changelog`, `/feedback`, `/learn/stats`) plus the theme toggle (`UserDropdown.tsx` picked up an unrelated stale-comment fix this session) all still clean. Test account reverted to its original state after every round.
- **CodeRabbit review, 4 findings verified against current code, 3 fixed, 1 skipped with reason:** (1) `validateAvatarUrl` had no max length — a syntactically-valid but arbitrarily long URL string would pass validation and get written straight to a `text` column; added a 2048-char cap. (2) No payload-size guard before `req.json()` — added a `Content-Length`-based 413 check ahead of parsing (a legitimate request here is always tiny: 4 short strings). (3) `ProfileEditForm` validated/submitted raw, untrimmed field state while the route's own `readField` already trims — a real false-rejection bug (e.g. a valid 78-char name with a few trailing spaces reads as 83 raw chars and gets client-blocked as "too long," even though the server would have happily stored the trimmed 78-char version); fixed by trimming all 4 fields once, at submit time, before validating/submitting/mirroring. (4) *Nitpick, also fixed* — wired `aria-describedby` from each of the 4 inputs to its own error paragraph's `id`, set only when that field has an error. **Skipped: wrapping `auth.api.getSession`/`ratelimit.limit` in try/catch.** Real concern, but not unique to this route — it's the exact same shape as the standing "Known gap, deferred" note above (`roadmap-progress`, `interview-rating`, `interview-review-rating`, `progress`, `practice/discussion`, `practice/submit` all have the identical unguarded-external-call gap); that note explicitly says patching one route in isolation doesn't close it. Added this route to that note's list instead of a one-off fix. Re-verified: `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on all 3 changed files.
- **Follow-up, same PR, per a new standing instruction ("use try/catch for error handling from now on so the app has production-level code"):** scoped explicitly to going forward, not retroactively — the other 6 routes in the "Known gap, deferred" note stay as documented, this route is the one still-open exception since its own PR review is what raised it. Restructured to `error-handling.md`'s canonical single-try/catch-per-route shape: the whole handler body (auth, rate-limit, size guard, parse/validate, write) now runs inside one outer `try`, with the inner `try/catch` around the `profiles` write narrowed to just its one special case (the username-conflict 409) and rethrowing anything else to the outer catch-all, which logs safely (`getPostgresErrorCode`, never the raw error) and returns a generic 500 — matching the doc's own example almost exactly. Re-verified live via direct requests covering all 5 response paths (valid save 200, validation error 400, real username conflict 409, oversized body 413, unauthenticated 401) — all unchanged; `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.
- **Full regression pass, one more time, after all of the above, with a fresh session cookie:** 23/23 real checks — client-side validation (including the new avatar-length cap), `aria-describedby` wiring, the trim fix, the real username conflict still correctly 409ing after the try/catch restructure, valid save + immediate Navbar sync, persistence across reload, account revert, the Settings overlay modal, Dashboard's heatmap unaffected, and a 9-route sweep. A handful of apparent failures on the first couple of runs were individually root-caused, not dismissed: this dev environment's own Turbopack recompile/cold-DB-pool latency (documented elsewhere in this file), one test assertion that didn't account for the `bio` field's own `maxLength={280}` clamp, and a fake test-only avatar domain correctly failing to resolve. **Merged to `develop` via PR #111 (merge commit `c9a283f`)**, branch `feature/56-settings-profile-edit` kept, not deleted.

**57 Settings — Account (Connected Providers + Delete), second of the 4 Phase 13 extras from the Pre-Feature-56 decision entry below.** Ran the `architect` skill first; before writing any code, read Better Auth's actual source (`account.mjs`, `update-user.mjs`), not just its docs, since some of this feature's real constraints only show up there: (1) `user.deleteUser` is disabled by default — enabled it in `auth/server.ts`; (2) **both unlink and delete require a "fresh" session** (created within `session.freshAge`, default 1 day) unless email verification is configured — this project has no email-sending infra (same reason Feedback is a plain `mailto:` link), so both rely on the framework's built-in freshness check, with a friendly "please sign out and back in" fallback on `SESSION_NOT_FRESH`/`SESSION_EXPIRED` rather than building a verification-email flow; (3) unlinking down to zero providers is **already blocked by Better Auth itself** (`FAILED_TO_UNLINK_LAST_ACCOUNT`) — no extra guard code needed, just surfacing the message; (4) `account.accountLinking.allowDifferentEmails` defaults to `false`, which would silently break linking a second provider for most real users (Google/GitHub emails commonly differ) — turned on. No custom API route was needed at all — every action (`link/unlinkAccount`, `deleteUser`) is a direct `authClient` call, same precedent as `UserDropdown`'s `authClient.signOut()`, since this feature operates entirely on Better Auth's own tables, not `profiles`. New `features/settings/lib/queries.ts` → `getConnectedAccounts()` (wraps `auth.api.listUserAccounts`, Better Auth's actual server-side name — the client alias `listAccounts` is friendlier but doesn't exist server-side), `AccountSection.tsx` + `DeleteAccountDialog.tsx` (split for the 200-line file limit) + `authErrorMessages.ts` (shared fresh-session error mapping). Delete confirmation is type-to-confirm (exact username match), same friction level as GitHub's repo-delete flow. **`GoogleIcon`/`GithubIcon` moved from `features/auth/components/` to `components/shared/`** — `features/settings` needed them too, and "features never import from other features" is a hard invariant (`architecture.md`); `OAuthButton.tsx`'s imports updated, nothing else referenced the old path.
- **A real, user-reported bug, found only after the feature was live: switching between Account and Profile inside the Settings overlay made the whole modal visibly disappear and reappear.** Root-caused to a structural issue that had existed since the modal was first built (Feature 37) but was invisible until this feature added a second section to navigate *between*: the intercepted route's `page.tsx` alone owned both the `<Dialog>` (via `SettingsModal`) and the section content, and Next.js fully unmounts/remounts a page component when its own dynamic segment's param changes — tearing down and rebuilding the Dialog on every section switch, replaying its open/close animation. **First fix attempt — a `layout.tsx` scoped to `[section]` itself — did not work**, confirmed empirically with a true (`useEffect` + empty deps) mount/unmount lifecycle log, not just visually: Next.js treats a layout scoped to the exact segment whose param is changing as part of "that segment," so it still remounted. **Second attempt, the correct one:** moved the layout one level *above* `[section]` (`(.)settings/layout.tsx`, not `(.)settings/[section]/layout.tsx`) — a genuinely stable ancestor regardless of section value. This meant the layout could no longer receive `section` as a param at all (confirmed directly via Next's generated typed-routes during a clean production build, not assumed from docs — a layout above a dynamic segment resolves that segment's params as `{}`, not `{ section: string }`; the first version of this fix shipped with the wrong assumption and would have 404'd every settings-modal open). Fixed by having `SettingsModal` (already a Client Component) derive `activeSection` from `usePathname()` instead of a prop — the layout now does no async work at all, deferring all section/session validation to `page.tsx`, which still fully guards the route. Verified two independent ways: a true mount/unmount lifecycle log showed zero remounts across a Profile→Account→Profile switch (down from 2 remounts before the fix), and a separate DOM-node-identity check confirmed the actual `<Dialog>` element is the same node throughout. Also hit and correctly ruled out as unrelated dev-server noise along the way: a transient `useThemeContext must be used within a ThemeProvider` 500 (cleared on retry, `ThemeProvider` untouched by this diff) and a stale `.next/dev/types` route-type mismatch after adding the first (later-reverted) layout file (cleared by a full `.next` wipe + rebuild, same known-gotcha class already documented elsewhere in this file). `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` all clean throughout.
- **Second real, user-reported bug, found immediately after the remount fix above: closing the modal after switching sections landed back on the *other* section instead of the page behind the modal.** E.g. open on Profile → switch to Account → click Close → briefly flips back to Profile (still open) instead of closing. Root cause: `SettingsSidebar`'s section links were plain `next/link` `<Link href>`, which **pushes** a new history entry on every click — so opening Settings (`router.push` from `UserDropdown`) plus switching to Account each added their own entry (`[..., /learn, /settings/profile, /settings/account]`). The Dialog's close handler is a single `router.back()` (Feature 37's own convention, so the URL and modal never drift apart) — one `back()` only pops the most recent entry, landing on the previously-visited section's URL, not the real underlying page. Fixed by adding a `replace?: boolean` prop to `SettingsSidebar` (default `false`, so the full-page `SettingsShell`'s normal browsing history is untouched) and passing `replace` from `SettingsModal` only — section switches inside the modal now replace the current history entry instead of pushing a new one, so no matter how many sections get visited, exactly one entry sits between the modal and the page behind it, and one `back()` always closes it correctly. Verified with a real authenticated Playwright pass (fresh session cookie, deleted after): switch once then close (lands on the real underlying page, not the other section), switch through 3 sections then close (same), close with zero switches (no regression), and switching sections still renders the right content both ways. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Unrelated tangent, same branch, from a direct user question ("why does Settings feel slow?") that led to a full caching-strategy audit.** Diagnosed the Settings slowness itself as this dev machine's known TLS-interception DB latency (`env-tls-interception-supabase`, ~1.8–2.5s per DB-touching request), not a code bug — declined the offered `loading.tsx` skeleton fix. The follow-up "are we caching well overall?" question got a full evidence-based audit (34 `cache()` call sites, 8 `unstable_cache` sites, Redis/Upstash scope, DB pool config) before any change: confirmed `getLeaderboardEntries` (`features/leaderboard/lib/queries.ts`) was a real gap — public, non-user-scoped ranking data using only request-scoped `cache()`, re-querying on every single visit — and fixed it with `unstable_cache` (`{ tags: ["leaderboard"], revalidate: 60 }`, time-based rather than the content-reseed `"concepts"` tag other catalogs use, since this data changes from live XP events, not a reseed). A second suspected gap (interview-prep's `getCollectionQuestionList`/`getCollectionQuestionBySlug`) turned out to be a false positive on closer reading — its underlying catalog is already `unstable_cache`-wrapped exactly like Practice's/Learn's, and the outer functions are correctly `cache()`-only because they merge in per-user completion status; left untouched rather than force an unneeded (and potentially leaky) change. Verified for real, not just by inspection: a temporary diagnostic log inside the cached function, a clean dev-server restart to capture output, and a real authenticated request pair against `/leaderboard?range=all-time` — first request logged the query and took 4.1s, the immediate second did not log a new query run and returned in 1.5s, with identical rendered XP figures across both. Also hit and correctly fixed, not worked around: a stale `.next/dev/types` mismatch from the build/dev-server churn (cleared via the established `.next` wipe + rebuild). Documented the general rule (`cache()` for anything `userId`/session-scoped, `unstable_cache` + content tag for reseed-driven catalogs, `unstable_cache` + short `revalidate` for live shared aggregates) in a new "Caching Strategy" section in `architecture.md`, so future features don't have to rediscover this ad hoc. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint`/`next build` all clean.
- **CodeRabbit review on PR #113, 3 findings, all verified against real code/APIs before fixing:** (1) confirmed via GitHub's own API that PR #113 was real and open (not assumed) — synced `progress-tracker.md`'s stale "PR not yet opened" notes. (2)/(3) **A real gap, confirmed by reading Better Auth's client source, not just the app code:** `@better-fetch/fetch`'s core `await fetch(...)` call is not wrapped in try/catch, so a genuine network failure (plausible on this dev machine's documented flaky connection) throws instead of resolving to `{error}` — `DeleteAccountDialog.handleDelete` and `AccountSection.handleDisconnect` both lacked try/catch around their `authClient` calls, meaning a rejected request would skip the busy-state reset and leave the button permanently stuck disabled. Fixed both with try/catch/finally, matching the shape `handleConnect` already used correctly in the same file. Verified live, not just by inspection: intercepted and force-failed the real `/api/auth/unlink-account` and `/api/auth/delete-user` requests via Playwright route interception (with a realistic delay to make the pending state observable) — confirmed the button disables during the request and re-enables after the simulated failure, the error message shows, and — critically — **neither the real provider connection nor the real account was ever touched**, since the request was intercepted before reaching the server.
- **Two full authenticated regression rounds, same branch, before merging, per explicit user request ("test thoroughly... no bug in the code") — 26 then 41 real checks, every apparent failure individually root-caused, not dismissed or blindly trusted.** Round 1: 24/26 passed; the 2 "failures" were a test-script timing race (an instantly-aborted request left no observable window for the button's pending state — re-tested with a realistic delay and confirmed the fix works correctly) and a bad assertion (checked for the literal text "Dashboard," which the page never renders, even though its actual content — XP, streak, leaderboard preview — was correct). Round 2 (expanded coverage: hard-navigation `/settings/[section]` outside the modal, Delete dialog Cancel behavior, the type-to-confirm gate rejecting empty/wrong input): 40/41 passed; the 1 failure was `page.getByRole("dialog").last()` being a dynamic locator that silently re-resolved from the closed confirm dialog to the still-open (correctly so) outer Settings modal — re-verified with content-specific selectors and confirmed Cancel closes only the confirmation dialog, leaves the Settings modal open, with no navigation. Every real assertion passed both rounds; your account/connected providers were never actually modified in any test. **Merged to `develop` via PR #113 (merge commit `e07932d`)**, branch `feature/57-settings-account` kept, not deleted.

---

### Previously completed — Feature 51, for reference

**Last completed:** 51 Lightning Prep (Study Plans + Company Guides) — the final feature of Phase 6. Ran the `architect` skill before building; 3 decisions confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` after digging into the real seed data first (found only 12 of the target 32 companies have any real company-tagged content today): (1) ship the full 32-company grid now with honest zero-counts for the other 20, same "no faked data" precedent as every other list in this codebase, rather than shrinking the launch grid; (2) every company badge is a flat monochrome initials tile, including the 6 companies that already have real brand SVGs elsewhere (`CompanyGuidesPreview`, the homepage strip) — a 32-card grid mixing 6 branded logos with 26 plain tiles would read as inconsistent, and matches this section's existing "deliberately monochrome" `COLLECTION_META`/`PLAYBOOK_META` precedent; (3) `study_plan_items` gets no new completion-tracking schema — a plan's checkmarks are derived live from each item's own existing progress table (concept → `user_concept_progress.fullyCompleted`, playbook chapter → `playbook_reads`) where one exists, `null` (no checkmark) everywhere else, rather than building a redundant tracking table for content whose real completion state already lives elsewhere.

**Company Guides** sourced from `collection_questions` (Feature 31's real 299-question FF Collections content) + `challenges` (Practice's real, company-tagged standalone content) — not `interview_questions`, which `build-plan.md`'s original spec text names but which predates Feature 31's dedicated schema and today only feeds Learn's per-concept Interview tab; confirmed by grepping the actual seed data, not assumed. New `features/interview-prep/lib/companies.ts` (`COMPANIES`, 32 `{name, slug}` entries) and `getCompanyGuideSummaries()`/`getCompanyGuideDetail()` (`features/interview-prep/lib/queries.ts`) — both source tables load in full and aggregate in memory (low hundreds of rows each), same "load it all, filter in memory" approach as `getCollectionQuestionCatalog`/Practice's `getPracticeCatalog`, avoiding a raw `unnest()` query. No new schema at all for this half of the feature. Routes: `/interview-prep/company-guides` (32-card grid) and `/interview-prep/company-guides/[company]` (Interview Questions + Practice Challenges sections, honest "Coming soon" empty state for the 20 companies with no content yet).

**Study Plans** are real DB rows, not filesystem MDX like Playbook/System Design — new `study_plans` + `study_plan_items` tables (migration `0016_wild_karen_page.sql`), since a plan's content is a curated sequence of links + labels, not long-form prose. `study_plan_items` has no foreign key to the content it points at (a day can bundle a whole collection, e.g. "FF 75 rapid review," not one question, and Playbook/System Design content is filesystem-only with no row to reference) — instead a hand-authored `{groupLabel, itemType, refId?, href, title, description}` row per item, where `itemType`/`refId` (a parseable slug composite) exist only so the detail page can look up a live completion checkmark from that content's own real progress table (see decision 3 above). New `features/interview-prep/lib/studyPlanQueries.ts` (`getStudyPlans()`, `getStudyPlanBySlug()` + the live-completion derivation). Routes: `/interview-prep/study-plans` (3 cards) and `/interview-prep/study-plans/[slug]` (itinerary grouped by `groupLabel`, e.g. "Day 1"–"Day 7" for the 1-Week plan, "Month 1 · Week 1"–"Month 3 · Week 12" for the 3-Month plan). All 3 plans' full content was written this session (55 items total: 11 for 1-Week, 11 for 1-Month, 33 for 3-Months), every `href` a real, verified route — concept slugs/categories checked against `scripts/seed/concepts.ts`, playbook chapter slugs against the actual chapter list, Practice category slugs against `PRACTICE_CATEGORIES` — seeded via a new `scripts/seed/studyPlans.ts` (delete-and-reinsert per plan on re-run, since items have no stable per-row key of their own to upsert against).

**3-Months plan rebuilt on the same pattern, same branch, after a full critique-then-tweak cycle** — the user asked for a plan, then explicitly asked for a critical senior-curriculum-designer self-review before building anything. That review caught one real bug by checking actual data rather than eyeballing the structure: FF75's curated questions are heavily front-loaded inside each real collection's own ordering (confirmed via query — 81%/88%/**100%** of the FF75-flagged questions in ff-javascript/ff-react/ff-nextjs sit inside each collection's first 40 positions), so an earlier draft's "Month 3 deep-dive `Q1–40`" would have silently repeated almost the entire FF75 pass as "new" content. The user's own follow-up fix removed FF75 from this plan entirely ("it's a 1-2 week revision collection, not a 3-month depth plan") and bumped `hoursCommitment` from `~4 hrs/week` to **`~2 hrs/day`** (roughly 3x the weekly budget), which eliminated the bug by construction: the plan now walks all 3 real FF collections (JavaScript 101, React 99, Next.js 99 — 299 total) straight through exactly once with zero repeats, finishing by end of Month 2, instead of a curated-subset-then-deep-dive that could overlap itself. Final structure (88 items): **Month 1** — JavaScript and React each get a dedicated core-then-advanced 2-week pass (JS: Hoisting/Closures/Callbacks/Event Loop/Promises, then this-Binding/Prototypal-Inheritance/Array-Methods/Debounce-Throttle; React: JSX-VDOM/useState-useEffect/Forms/Context-API/Rendering/State-Management, then useRef/Composition/Custom-Hooks/Render-Performance) — deliberately double the calendar time every other category gets, since JS/React dominate real interview loops and the original draft didn't actually use its extra runway on them. **Month 2** — Browser Internals, CSS, TypeScript, Accessibility+Performance (combined, neither has a Practice category) — one week each, with the JS/React/Next.js collections finishing exactly once across these weeks (`Q41–80`→`Q81–101` etc., no overlap with Month 1's `Q1–40`). **Month 3** — 3 System-Design Learn concepts spread one/week alongside the guides (rather than crammed into one 9-item week), a 6-chapter Playbook sweep across 5 of 6 playbooks, Company Guide, Final Review. 24 of 28 System Design guides used (2/week × 12, unavoidably overlapping with 1-Week's 7 and 1-Month's 12 picks — 19 of 28 guides were already spoken for, confirmed via inventory count, not a fixable flaw without authoring new guide content). Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean; DB-level check confirmed all 40 concept `refId`s resolve to real concepts (0 broken), all 6 playbook refIds real, and a direct query confirmed **zero** `ff-75` href references anywhere in the plan; live fetch of `/interview-prep/study-plans/3-months` confirmed 88 `ItemList` JSON-LD positions, starting at "Learn: Hoisting & the Temporal Dead Zone" and ending at "Final spaced-repetition review."

**1-Month plan rebuilt on the same `rangeLabel` pattern, same branch, on explicit user request** ("do a similar realistic plan for 1 Month") — went from 11 whole-collection-link items to 37, restructured from `groupLabel`s "Week 1"–"Week 4" (was already weekly, just thin). Weekly not daily granularity, deliberately — the point of 4 weeks over 1 is room for content 1-Week never touches, not the same daily cadence stretched out. Week 1 (JavaScript foundations): 5 Learn concepts named by the user (Hoisting, Closures, Callbacks, Event Loop, Promises/Async-await) + FF 75 (`Solve Q1 – Q20`) + FF JavaScript deep dive collection (101 questions, `Solve Q1 – Q20`) + JS coding reps (`Solve Q1 – Q10`) + 3 easy System Design guides. Week 2 (React): 6 Learn concepts named by the user (JSX/Virtual DOM, useState/useEffect, Forms, Context API, React Rendering, State Management) + FF 75 (`Solve Q21 – Q40`) + FF React deep dive (99 questions) + React coding reps + 3 medium/hard System Design guides. Week 3 (breadth): FF 75 (`Solve Q41 – Q60`) + FF Next.js deep dive (99 questions) + TypeScript coding reps + 3 System Design guides — CSS coding reps deliberately excluded per explicit user instruction. Week 4 (interview mode): FF 75 closes out at `Solve Q61 – Q75` (all 75 covered by month's end) + the kept STAR-method/resume-structure playbook chapters + 3 harder System Design guides + Company Guide + Final review. **12 System Design guides total (3/week × 4)** — the user flagged that the original 1-Month draft had zero individual System Design questions across all 4 weeks (only a single "browse the guides" link in Week 3); fixed by picking 12 real guides from the 28-guide inventory (deliberately distinct from the 7 already used in 1-Week, for broader coverage across the two plans), easy-to-hard within and across weeks (Week 1 all `easy`, Week 4 mostly `hard`) — same "real guide, direct link, no invented content" approach as 1-Week's picks. Plan-level `description` rewritten to state an explicit weekly "Goal:" per week, addressing the earlier-raised (and until now unresolved) request for stated goals per plan. Re-verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, re-seeded (37 items confirmed), live HTML fetch of `/interview-prep/study-plans/1-month` confirmed all 11 concept titles, all 12 System Design guide links, zero CSS Practice links, and all 8 `rangeLabel` values render correctly.

**Post-ship redesign of the 1-Week plan, same branch, prompted by real user feedback:** the original 1-Week plan (and 1-Month/3-Months) linked whole collections/categories as single items — e.g. "FF 75 rapid review" pointing at all 75 questions as one Day 1 checkbox — which reads as complete but actually asks for 75-100+ questions in a day, not realistic. A first revision attempt went to the opposite extreme (one row per individual question, 87-147 items per plan) and was explicitly reverted by the user (`git reset --hard` back to the original commit, including a live-DB CHECK-constraint/migration-journal desync fix since `drizzle-kit generate` diffs against local snapshot history, not the live DB). The user then designed the real fix themselves: keep one card per collection/category per day (low item count) but add a `rangeLabel` field — rendered as a premium-style "★ Q.01–Q.10" pill (`bg-premium-light`/`text-premium`, `StudyPlanItemRow.tsx`) — marking the subset to actually work through that day. New nullable `range_label` column on `study_plan_items` (migration `0017_jittery_proemial_gods.sql`). Rebuilt only the 1-Week plan on this pattern (33 items): Days 1-4 each get FF 75 (10/day, `Q.01–Q.10` through `Q.31–Q.40`), JS coding reps (5/day), React coding reps (5/day, note: Practice's React category only has 38 challenges total, so this uses 35 of them — tight ceiling for any future plan reusing the same category), and one hand-picked System Design guide; Day 7 extends the FF75 range to `Q.61–Q.75` (15) to close out the full 75 instead of leaving 5 uncovered. The 7 System Design picks (`search-autocomplete`, `reusable-modal-dialog-system`, `infinite-scroll-feed`, `photo-sharing-feed`, `social-news-feed`, `realtime-messaging-client`, `video-streaming-player`) were chosen via live `WebSearch` against real "most commonly asked frontend system design" sources, not guessed, then matched against the real 28-guide inventory — ordered easy-to-hard across the week. Days 5-7 keep the extra Playbook-chapter/Company-Guide/Review items from the original build per explicit instruction. 1-Month and 3-Months were deliberately left on the old whole-collection pattern — the user confirmed only the 1-Week redesign so far; extending the pattern to the other plans (plus the previously-requested 6-Month plan and explicit "Goal:" statements) remains open.

**Both `isPremium: true`** (Study Plans per-plan, Company Guides section-wide) per `project-overview.md`'s monetization doc, rendered as a `bg-premium-light`/`text-premium` pill — no blur/lock UI, matching Feature 49's own precedent (FF System Design ships "mostly premium" per the doc but fully open); real enforcement is explicitly Feature 38's job per `build-plan.md`.

**Get Started page converted from static teasers to real data** (same conversion Feature 50 did for `PlaybookPreview`): `StudyPlansPreview` now reads `getStudyPlans()` instead of 3 hardcoded cards; `CompanyGuidesPreview` keeps its existing 6 real logo components as-is (predates the monochrome-tile decision, left alone rather than restyled) but now shows a real aggregate question count across all 32 companies instead of no count at all.

**One real bug caught during live verification, not by lint/typecheck:** `getCompanyGuideSummaries()`'s per-company aggregation initially returned `Map<string, number>` from inside an `unstable_cache`-wrapped function — `unstable_cache` round-trips its return value through JSON to store it, which silently collapses a `Map` to `{}` on retrieval, throwing `TypeError: questionCounts.get is not a function` the moment a cached value came back deserialized (first hit built the cache fine since it was still the in-memory object; the crash only showed up reading the *cached* copy). Same class of mistake this file's own `getCollectionQuestionCounts` had already avoided by returning a plain `Record` instead of a `Map` — missed the precedent on the first pass, caught via a live curl route sweep (500 on `/interview-prep/company-guides`), fixed by switching both `questionCounts`/`challengeCounts` to `Record<string, number>`, re-verified with a clean 200 afterward.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean throughout; a full production `next build` (126 static pages, no errors); a live dev-server route sweep — company-guides list, 3 company detail pages (Google — real ~167-question aggregate; OpenAI — honest empty state; a 404 for an unknown company slug), study-plans list, all 3 detail pages, a 404 for an unknown plan slug, and the Get Started page — all returned the expected status codes, with real content spot-checked in the raw HTML (correct per-day/per-week grouping and item counts on the 3-Month plan, real aggregate counts on company pages). No hardcoded colors — grepped every new file for raw Tailwind palette classes/hex values, clean. **Not verified:** dark mode and responsive layout only checked by reading the token classes used (all token-based, no hardcoded colors) — no browser automation tool available in this environment to visually confirm, same standing limitation documented on Features 30/31/49/50. **Merged to `develop` via PR #89 (merge commit `5d9aa54`)**, branch `feature/51-lightning-prep`, commits `32ee394` (original build) + `d581e54` (Study Plans range-tag rebuild) + `c350b54` (CodeRabbit fix pass).

**Follow-up quality-audit pass on Feature 50, same session:** the user asked for a rating/comparison of the Playbook content against the best competing material already online, before merging. 5 parallel research agents (one per playbook, launched via the `Agent` tool, run in background) each read every chapter of one playbook and cross-checked it against real sources (GreatFrontEnd, Tech Interview Handbook, AlgoMaster, Hello Interview, Teal, current React/web-platform documentation) before rating and flagging gaps — findings synthesized (not just relayed) into a single report, published as an Artifact for the user rather than dumped as chat text given the volume (32 chapters' worth of findings). Result: average 7.4/10, zero factual errors found anywhere (including all 5 React code snippets, verified against current React 18/19 behavior), and one recurring cross-playbook pattern independently flagged by 3 of 5 reviewers — conceptually correct writing that stopped one level short of the concrete number/named framework/exact term competing sources use to make advice rehearsable. The top-10 prioritized fix list was then applied directly to the content, in order: (1) fixed a frontmatter/body inconsistency (`a-repeatable-framework-for-frontend-system-design.mdx` claimed "five stages," body had seven) and added the RADIO-inspired time-budget percentages the audit called for in the same edit; (2) fixed a garbled sentence in `culture-fit-questions-decoded.mdx`; (3) added a **React Compiler** section to `talking-about-performance-like-a-senior-engineer.mdx` — the single highest-priority finding across the whole audit, since the chapter's original framing (manual `memo`/`useMemo`/`useCallback` as the senior move) reads as close to backwards for a compiler-enabled codebase now that React Compiler is stable; (4) added `useEffectEvent` (React 19.2+) to the gotchas chapter; (5) rewrote `common-interview-mistakes.mdx` with 3 new genuinely frontend-specific sections (UI-round keyboard/semantic-HTML failures, skipped loading/empty/error states, framework-magic-without-platform-understanding) — the original 6 mistakes were all generic to any SWE interview despite the chapter's title; (6) added ATS-parsing mechanics across the Resume playbook's tailoring and structure chapters — previously absent from the entire playbook despite being, per most competing sources, the primary reason a resume gets filtered before a human ever reads it; (7) added a CSR/SSR/SSG rendering-strategy section to the System Design playbook's data-fetching chapter — total absence across all 6 chapters was the audit's top System Design finding; (8) added Core Web Vitals (LCP/INP/CLS) terminology plus real DOM-node figures pulled forward from the linked System Design deep-dive guides (200–400 mounted rows, not just "hundreds to thousands"); (9) added an explicit 60%-Action/under-20%-Situation+Task ratio to the STAR chapter, replacing the vague "the majority"; (10) added a concrete 10–15%-above-offer negotiation heuristic plus "negotiate through the recruiter, not the hiring manager" to the negotiation chapter. **A third real bug was self-caught during this fix pass, not by the audit**: one of the new mistakes-chapter sections used a literal `<button>` in prose outside a code fence — the exact same MDX-parses-`<`-as-JSX trap already documented from the original pilot build — caught by rerunning the same grep-based sweep before it ever reached a dev server, fixed by rewording rather than escaping. **Previously completed:** 49 FF System Design — AlgoMaster-Style Practice — **now fully complete, all 27 topics authored (28 guides total with the pilot).** Split out from Feature 31 since this collection's content model (long-form guides with diagrams) doesn't fit the other 4 collections' short-Q&A shape. Sourced all 16 "Applications" + 6 "UI Components" + 5 "Techniques/Topics" entries from that repo's README (skipping its meta "RADIO Framework" article and its literal template section, neither of which is a real topic), each renamed and researched independently (a `WebSearch` pass per topic grounding the guide's Constraints/Deep-Dives sections in real, cited engineering sources — Meta/Figma/Spotify/Uber/Airbnb/W3C ARIA APG/Floating UI, etc.) and authored as a full 8-section guide. Content lives as MDX in `content/interview-prep/system-design/[slug].mdx`, read via `lib/systemDesignGuides.ts` (mirrors `lib/mdx.ts`'s path-traversal-safe pattern) — no schema/DB table, matching the original spec. Routes: `/interview-prep/ff-system-design` (list, grouped dynamically by each guide's `section` frontmatter) and `/interview-prep/ff-system-design/[slug]` (guide page — `MDXRemote`, `TechArticle`/`mainEntity` JSON-LD, answer-first summary, prev/next nav). **`rough.js` diagram primitives** (`features/interview-prep/components/diagrams/rough/`) — `drawBox`/`drawArrow` helpers, a `RoughDiagram` client-component canvas wrapper resolving this app's real theme colors via `getComputedStyle` at draw time and redrawing on theme toggle via a `MutationObserver` on `<html>`'s `class` attribute (roughjs bakes literal colors into generated SVG, unlike the plain hand-authored diagrams elsewhere which use `var(--color-*)` directly). The pilot got one fully hand-tuned bespoke diagram (`InfiniteScrollArchitectureDiagram`); given 27 more guides needing one each, built a second, generic **`FlowDiagram`** primitive instead of hand-tuning 27 more — a data-driven component that auto-lays-out a guide's own Component-Architecture nodes/edges (`systemDesignDiagramData.ts`, one spec per guide, node labels and edges pulled directly from each guide's own prose) into a grid with rough.js-sketched boxes/arrows, a deliberate scope tradeoff given the run's size, still real rough.js output just generated from data rather than pixel-positioned by hand. **Promoted `mdxComponents.tsx`** from `features/learn/components/concept/` to `components/shared/` on its second consumer, adding `table`/`thead`/`th`/`td` renderers for these guides' comparison tables. **Two real bugs caught during verification:** (1) the pilot diagram's wrapper component was missing `"use client"`, so passing a `draw` function prop to the client-only `RoughDiagram` crossed an RSC serialization boundary (`Error: Functions cannot be passed directly to Client Components`) — fixed by marking the wrapper `"use client"`, since the whole diagram only ever runs client-side; (2) after shipping the pilot, the Get Started page's row still showed "Coming soon" and the collection's label was "FF System Design" (renamed to **"FF Frontend System Design"** per explicit request, everywhere it's user-facing — `COLLECTION_META`, `navConfig.ts`, the homepage's `FeatureHighlightsSection`/`PlatformGraph`/testimonial copy) — the "Coming soon" bug was `getCollectionSummaries()` never having an `ff-system-design` entry at all (this feature deliberately has no DB table); fixed by sourcing a 5th summary entry from `getAllSystemDesignGuides().length` (the filesystem, not a query). Verified across the whole run: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; every one of the 28 guide pages plus the list page returned a clean 200 with no server-side errors logged (spot-checked content/diagram presence across a representative sample from every batch); Get Started page shows "28 questions" with a real (honestly-zero) progress bar; a Learn concept page re-checked to confirm the `mdxComponents` promotion didn't regress its original consumer. Branch `feat/49-ff-system-design` off `develop`, merged to `develop` via PR #85 (merge commit `73be4b0`) — see Current Status below for the full commit/merge history.

**Also from this Feature 49 session, but a real writing-quality caveat worth flagging:** every guide's technical claims were grounded via live `WebSearch` results (real case studies, real ARIA/W3C specs, real engineering blog posts, cited by URL at the end of each guide) rather than invented — but none of this was independently fact-checked against the cited sources beyond reading the search engine's own summaries, and no human/domain-expert review has happened yet. Treat the content as a strong, well-researched first draft, not a guaranteed-error-free final version, until someone reviews it directly.

**Follow-up quality pass, same session — self-critiqued as a "senior system design architect" review, then fixed all three gaps found across all 28 guides:** (1) zero internal links between guides despite constant cross-references by name in prose — added real markdown links wherever one guide's prose named another (mapped via `grep` across every `.mdx` file first, not guessed); (2) zero markdown comparison tables despite the shared `mdxComponents` table renderer existing specifically for this content — converted the most naturally tabular Deep Dive/Tradeoffs discussion in every guide (OT vs. CRDT, P2P vs. SFU vs. MCU, LWW vs. CRDT, native `<dialog>` vs. custom, etc.) into a real table; (3) no back-of-envelope quantitative estimation — added one worked numeric estimate per guide, scaled to what actually matters at that guide's scope (bandwidth/storage/request-rate math for the "system"-scale guides; a concrete DOM/viewport-count threshold for the small "component"-scale guides like Dropdown Menu/Star Widget). **A real, more serious bug caught during this pass's own verification, not before:** none of the new tables were rendering as `<table>` at all — raw MDX (via `next-mdx-remote/rsc`) doesn't parse GFM pipe-table syntax without the `remark-gfm` plugin, which this codebase had never installed (the platform's *other* markdown renderer, `components/shared/Markdown.tsx`, has its own hand-rolled GFM table support — a completely separate code path from `MDXRemote`, so its existing table support didn't apply here). Fixed by installing `remark-gfm` and passing `options={{ mdxOptions: { remarkPlugins: [remarkGfm] } }}` to the guide page's `<MDXRemote>` call — confirmed live afterward that tables render as real `<table>`/`<thead>` elements and internal links resolve correctly. Re-verified: `tsc`/`eslint` clean; spot-checked ~10 guides across every batch live, all clean 200s, tables/links/diagrams all confirmed present in the rendered HTML.

**Second, heavier follow-up pass, same session — the two gaps explicitly deferred from the critique above (sequence diagrams, code snippets), now closed:** (1) **`SequenceDiagram`**, a new generic rough.js primitive (`features/interview-prep/components/diagrams/rough/SequenceDiagram.tsx`) alongside `FlowDiagram` — actors as lifelines, messages stack top-to-bottom in the order given, `dashed: true` renders a response/ack in muted color instead of accent so request/response pairing reads at a glance, self-messages (`from === to`) draw a small loop-back arrow for an actor's own internal step (e.g. "recompute dependents," "interpolate marker"). Needed one new shared primitive, `drawLine` (`roughPrimitives.ts`) — a plain sketched line with no arrowhead, for lifelines. Data lives in `systemDesignSequenceData.ts` (`SYSTEM_DESIGN_SEQUENCES`, one `SequenceSpec` per slug), wired into `[slug]/page.tsx` via the exact same per-slug-registry pattern `ArchitectureDiagram` already uses, registered as a second MDX component (`SequenceDiagram`) alongside it. Added to the 8 guides where a genuine time-ordered protocol was already described in prose — every message in every spec was pulled directly from that guide's own Data Contract/Deep Dives text, not invented: `realtime-messaging-client` (send→ack→deliver→read→reconnect-backfill), `browser-video-conferencing` (SFU offer/answer/ICE signaling then publish/forward), `multiplayer-document-editor` (two concurrent OT inserts transformed and broadcast), `collaborative-spreadsheet-engine` (remote `cell_update` triggering recalculation, both self-loops), `collaborative-canvas-design-tool` (concurrent property-level LWW edits + a fractional-index layer insert), `offline-first-sync-engine` (local write/log, then batched reconnect sync), `offline-capable-email-client` (queued send replay, then a second device pulling read-state on its own next sync — deliberately no direct device-to-device message, matching the guide's own "independent Action Queue drainer" model), `realtime-ridesharing-map` (two location updates with the interpolation step shown as the rider's own self-loop). (2) **Code snippets** — a short, concrete TypeScript block (rendered via the existing `pre`/`code` styling in `mdxComponents.tsx`, no new component needed) added to 10 guides where the prose already described one specific concrete algorithm, closing that gap without forcing every one of the 28 guides to have one: fractional-index midpoint generator (`collaborative-canvas-design-tool`), `Intl.PluralRules` category resolution against an ICU-style template (`i18n-l10n-architecture`), debounce+`AbortController` pairing (`search-autocomplete`), roving-tabindex arrow-key handler (`accessible-dropdown-menu`), greedy shortest-column masonry packing (`masonry-discovery-feed`), ABR rendition-selection heuristic (`video-streaming-player`), a Tab/Shift+Tab focus-trap handler (`reusable-modal-dialog-system`), Floating UI's `flip`/`shift`/`size` middleware pipeline (`positioning-aware-tooltip-popover`), one OT transform-function entry (`multiplayer-document-editor`), and a lerp-based position interpolator (`realtime-ridesharing-map`). Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; fresh dev server (`.next` cleared) — every one of the 15 touched guides plus 4 untouched guides spot-checked, all clean 200s; confirmed the sequence-diagram `aria-label` and SVG actually render (not just compile) via a direct HTML check on one guide, and confirmed figure/pre-block counts matched expectations across every touched guide (2 figures for the 8 sequence-diagram guides, 2 `pre` blocks for the 10 code-snippet guides).

**CodeRabbit review on the PR caught 23 real issues, fixed in a follow-up commit before merge.** Genuine correctness bugs across both code and content: `transformInsert` (OT) had no tie-breaker for equal-position concurrent inserts, so the two transform directions could converge on different final text — verified by hand-tracing both orderings before fixing; the fractional-indexing `generateKeyBetween` snippet silently returned an out-of-bounds key for prefix-relationship inputs like `"a"`/`"a0"` (now throws explicitly instead of returning a wrong answer, since a real fix would need production-grade fractional-indexing machinery out of scope for an illustrative snippet); `getAllSystemDesignGuides()`'s sort ordered by `orderIndex` alone, which only resets 1–N *per section*, silently scrambling Prev/Next navigation order across section boundaries — fixed with an explicit `SECTION_ORDER` array, verified live afterward that Prev/Next now reads in the intended order; the search-autocomplete debounce/abort snippet had the exact stale-response race condition its own prose claims to solve; `FlowDiagram`'s arrow endpoints used a fixed per-axis offset instead of a true ray-rectangle intersection, so diagonal connector arrows didn't clip to the actual box edge; the pilot diagram's fetch-trigger arrow visually cut straight through the Cache box. Several content-accuracy fixes too (dependency-graph edge direction reversed from what its own traversal needs, missing idempotency/props-validation/final-revalidation guidance across half a dozen guides, an outdated "Firefox-only" masonry claim corrected via live `WebSearch` against WebKit's own blog). **3 findings skipped with reasons, not silently ignored:** moving page-level diagram-registry logic and `systemDesignGuides.ts` into feature folders was skipped because both contradict precedent already established elsewhere in this exact codebase (Feature 31's own `DIAGRAM_BY_SLUG` lives in the page file too; `systemDesignGuides.ts` is explicitly modeled on `lib/mdx.ts`, which lives in the same `src/lib/` location) — verified by reading those files, not assumed; a suggestion to make `RoughDiagram`'s CSS-variable read throw instead of falling back was skipped since the fallback is unreachable in practice (confirmed via grep across 39 files sharing the same tokens) and throwing would turn a decorative diagram into a crash risk for no real gain. Fixed in commit `966c072`. **Merged to `develop` via PR #85 (merge commit `73be4b0`)**, branch `feat/49-ff-system-design`, commits `d09f19d` (full build) + `966c072` (the CodeRabbit fix pass).

Worth revisiting with real browser devtools before trying again, rather than another blind attempt. **A second real bug caught from user screenshots, same session:** hovering any card (`hover:-translate-y-0.5`) cut off its top edge — `SCROLL_ROW_CLASS` had `pb-2` but no top padding, and `overflow-x-auto` forces the container's `overflow-y` to a non-`visible` computed value too (per the CSS overflow spec, whichever axis isn't `visible` pulls the other one out of `visible` as well), so the row clips vertically at its own edges with zero headroom above the cards. Fixed by changing `pb-2` to `py-2`. **A third issue, same session (content-length, not a code bug):** the Experiments row showed no edge fade/cutoff at all on the right, inconsistent with the Battles row — because Battles has 5 teaser cards (`w-56` each) that overflow the container and Experiments only had 4 (`w-64` each) that fit exactly within it, so there was nothing to fade. First attempted by adding a 5th teaser item (`ascii-webcam-filter`, "ASCII Webcam Filter") to `EXPERIMENT_PREVIEW_ITEMS` — **didn't actually fix it, caught from a follow-up screenshot still showing no 5th card at all.** Real root cause: `ExperimentCard` was `w-64` (256px) vs `BattleCard`'s `w-56` (224px); at this layout's actual available row width, 4 `w-64` cards + gaps summed to within exactly one `gap-4` (16px) of the container's edge, so the 5th card's leading edge landed precisely at the clip boundary — 0px visible, not partially faded, just entirely clipped. A coincidence of that specific pixel arithmetic, not something a mask/overflow tweak could fix. Fixed by changing `ExperimentCard` to `w-56` to match `BattleCard` exactly — same card width means both rows now share identical overflow arithmetic, so the peek behaves identically (and predictably) rather than depending on a per-width, per-item-count coincidence. Verified live: both card types render at `w-56` in the actual HTML. **Both preview rows are static teaser data** (`features/playground/lib/previewData.ts`), not real queries — even though `ui_battle_challenges` is a real table, it's real-but-guaranteed-empty until Feature 53 seeds a pilot challenge, and Experiments has no schema at all yet (first-party code + a config registry, per the architect session). Same precedent as Feature 30's original `PlaybookPreview`/`StudyPlansPreview`/`CompanyGuidesPreview`, which started as static teasers and were converted to real queries once their own features shipped (Features 50/51) — Battles' and Experiments' preview rows get the same treatment once 53/54 land. **Same-session follow-up round on Feature 53, driven entirely by direct user feedback against the plan/build above — 6 issues raised, all addressed before any further code was written for the next feature:**

1. **Full-page IDE layout.** `BattleEditorWorkspace` rebuilt from a boxed `max-w-screen-2xl` content page into a true full-bleed workspace — `h-[calc(100dvh-3.5rem)]` (same calc pattern `LearnSidebar`/`InterviewPrepSidebar` already use for full-height columns below `AppNavbar`'s `h-14`), a compact single-strip header (breadcrumb + title + difficulty + truncated description + Solution button, all one row), and the 3-column workspace filling the remaining height edge to edge, no outer `mx-auto`/`max-w` container. Confirmed with the user first: widening only the editor doesn't touch the compare pane's own size — they're independent flex columns, not a shared ratio, so making the editor narrower doesn't make the pane narrower too (the pane is deliberately the *bigger* one, see next point).
2. **Editor narrower, compare pane bigger — by direct request** (`lg:flex-[2]` editor vs `lg:flex-[3]` pane, excluding the file-tree column). `CodeEditor`'s `height` prop widened from `number` to `number | string` (the underlying `@monaco-editor/react` `Editor` already supported both; this app's wrapper had just never needed a string before) so it can take `height="100%"` and actually fill its column — verified this is a safe widening, not a behavior change, for every existing numeric-height caller (Learn/Practice/Build): a CSS percentage height only resolves against an ancestor with a *definite* height, and none of those callers' ancestors have one, so `h-full` added to `CodeEditor`'s own wrapper div computes to a no-op `auto` there — it only takes effect in this feature's new `calc(100dvh-...)`-rooted column.
3. **One tag naming what's showing, swapping with the active mode** (confirmed via the user's own wording, "one tag swaps depending on which mode is active") — a small pill in `ComparePane`'s top-left corner reads "Output" in Diff mode, "Target UI" in Slide & Compare mode. Simpler than the two-tags-always-visible alternative floated during planning.
4. **Large/tall target UIs (a full page, a grid section) not fitting a small fixed pane — deliberately deferred, not solved algorithmically.** The user asked to see the current behavior first rather than commit to a sizing strategy up front. Built the one piece that was already agreed on regardless of the eventual answer: a **fullscreen compare mode** — a `Maximize2`/`Minimize2` toggle in `ComparePane`'s own toolbar, lifted to `BattleEditorWorkspace` (`isPaneFullscreen` state) so toggling it hides the file tree and editor entirely and lets the pane fill the whole workspace area. Whether this alone is enough for genuinely large targets, or a zoom/scroll mechanism is also needed, is explicitly left open until the user has real content to test it against.
5. **Slider drag "friction" — root-caused, not just patched.** The original implementation tracked dragging via a `window`-level `pointermove` listener; the compare pane has two real iframes in the drag path, and an iframe is a separate document — the moment the cursor crossed onto one mid-drag, its pointer events fired inside *that* document instead of bubbling to the parent's `window` listener, which is exactly what stuttering/"lots of friction" while dragging looks like. Fixed with the browser-native mechanism for exactly this problem: `setPointerCapture()` on the handle itself at `pointerdown`, which routes every subsequent event for that pointer to the handle regardless of what's under the cursor (iframes included) until `pointerup` releases it — no `window` listeners, no `useEffect`, simpler than the original code, not just smoother.
6. **Collapsible file tree.** `FileTabs` gained a `collapsed`/`onToggleCollapsed`-driven state (owned by `BattleEditorWorkspace`): expanded is the original `w-40` labeled list; collapsed shrinks to a `w-11` icon-only strip (`FileCode2` per file, native `title` tooltip + `sr-only` label for the name), giving the width back to the editor and compare pane.

**Plus one new feature, not a fix — an official, genuinely premium-gated solution**, per explicit request ("I want the specific solution for this," i.e. real enforcement now, not the cosmetic-pill-only pattern every other Playground/Study-Plan/Company-Guide premium flag uses pending Feature 38). Real, not cosmetic, because the alternative would have been security theater: **new `solutionHtml`/`solutionCss`/`solutionJs` columns** (migration `0021_add_ui_battle_solution.sql`) — deliberately a *separate* asset from `targetHtml`/`targetCss`/`targetJs`, not a re-gate of the same content, since the target is unavoidably shipped to every viewer already (it's the always-visible live comparison pane) and "locking" it would gate nothing real. `getIsPremiumUser(userId)` added to `features/playground/lib/queries.ts` — duplicated locally rather than imported, same precedent already established between `features/learn` and `features/interview-prep`'s own copies of the identical function (features never import other features). The editor route (`[slug]/page.tsx`) now fetches the viewer's real premium status alongside the challenge and strips the 3 solution fields to `""` (plus an `isSolutionLocked` flag) *before* they're ever serialized into the client component's props when the viewer isn't premium — same "never client-side only" pattern as `ConceptInterview`/`QuestionCard`'s answer redaction. New `BattleSolutionPanel` (locked dashed note vs. reveal button vs. a small 3-file read-only code viewer) — a deliberate sibling of the shared `SolutionPanel`, not a reuse of it: `SolutionPanel`'s contract is one `solutionCode` string, correct for every existing Practice/Build challenge (always one file); a Battle solution is genuinely 3 files, a different shape, not a config difference. Surfaced via the app's first real `Dialog` usage (the shadcn/base-ui primitive installed since Feature 00 had zero consumers until now) — controlled `open`/`onOpenChange`, triggered by a header button next to the description.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean across the full follow-up round; a clean production `next build`; a live dev-server re-check of both routes (list page 200 with real content, detail route still 307-redirecting unauthenticated visitors correctly) with no server errors in the log; grepped every touched file for hardcoded colors, clean. **Not verified — no browser automation tool with a real login session available in this environment, same standing limitation documented on Features 29–31/49–52:** the actual visual layout proportions, drag smoothness by feel, the collapse/expand toggle's look, and the Solution dialog's rendering all need a real click-through by the user before merge.

**Second same-session follow-up round on Feature 53 — the user actually clicked through the first round's build and sent a real screenshot, raising 6 more issues, all fixed:**

1. **Compare-pane copy button.** `BattleSolutionPanel` gained a `Copy`/`Check` icon button in its file-tab header row, copying whichever of the 3 solution files is currently active via `navigator.clipboard.writeText` — one button, not three, since only one file is ever visible at a time; a 2s "Copied" confirmation swap, same pattern as `ShareButtons`' Instagram-caption-copy precedent.
2. **Compare box wasn't actually aligning with its own toolbar — a real sizing bug, not a polish request.** The screenshot showed the "Output"/target box stopping well short of where the fullscreen button sat. Root cause: the box was sized `h-full` + `aspect-ratio` + `max-w-full`, which is *height*-driven — for the pilot's portrait 400×480 target, the computed width was narrower than the column, so the box centered itself with dead space on both sides instead of reaching the column's edges the toolbar row (Diff/Slide group, fullscreen button) already spans full-width. **This is also why fullscreen mode (issue 6 below) wasn't visibly doing anything** — same box, same fixed computed size, regardless of how much extra room fullscreen actually freed up.
3. **Fixed with a proper two-layer measured letterbox, not another CSS tweak.** Tried the pure-CSS "aspect-ratio + max-width + max-height, no explicit size" trick first (the standard `object-fit: contain`-equivalent for a plain div) — doesn't actually work here: that trick only resolves against an element's own intrinsic content size, and every child of the compare box (both iframes, the mode tag, the slider handle) is `position: absolute`, which removes them from flow entirely, leaving the box with *zero* intrinsic size to derive anything from. Replaced with `ResizeObserver` on the outer frame (which is always exactly `w-full h-full` of its column, so *it* is what genuinely aligns with the toolbar) computing the largest width/height that both fits the frame and preserves the target's aspect ratio — the same letterbox math a video player uses — applied as explicit pixel `width`/`height` on the inner box. Runs in `useLayoutEffect` (not a plain `useEffect`) so the box goes straight to its measured size instead of flashing at 0×0 for a frame.
4. **This same fix is what makes fullscreen mode actually do something now.** Toggling fullscreen gives the outer frame the *entire* workspace area instead of a `flex-[3]` column; the `ResizeObserver` picks that up and the letterbox math recomputes against the much bigger frame, so the rendered target/output genuinely grows — for the pilot's portrait card this already reads as dramatically bigger in fullscreen. Whether that alone is enough for a genuinely large target (a full "About Us" page, a grid section) once real content exists is still the open question from the first round — not something this fix claims to solve on its own, but it does mean fullscreen is no longer a no-op.
5. **Diff mode's ghost was clashing with dynamic content, not just "a bit strong."** The user's own repro: clicking "Sign In" changes the *output*'s button label, but the *target* ghost on top never changes (it's a frozen reference render) — at the original 50% opacity, "Sign In" (ghost) visibly double-exposed against "Signed in ✓" (real), reading as garbled overlapping text. Dropped to `opacity: 0.22` — enough to align by eye, not enough to fight with content that's actively changing underneath it.
6. **Slider still laggy sometimes, even after the Pointer Capture fix — a second, different cause, not the same bug again.** Pointer Capture fixed *dropped* events (the iframe swallowing them); this was *too many* events — a fast mouse can fire `pointermove` well above 60/sec, and each one was applying its own `setSlidePos` synchronously, forcing React to re-render and the browser to repaint two live iframes' `clip-path` more often than it could keep up with. Fixed by coalescing into at most one state update per animation frame (`requestAnimationFrame`-batched — a ref holds the latest raw pointer position, only the last one per frame actually gets applied), plus a `will-change: clip-path`/`opacity` hint on the target iframe so the browser can optimize compositing during the drag. Both causes needed fixing independently; neither alone fully explained "sometimes fine, sometimes laggy."
7. **New: an X share button** (`BattleShareButton.tsx`) — X-only (not the full X/Facebook/Instagram row `ShareButtons` uses on Practice's Editor page, which wasn't asked for here), a pre-filled `x.com/intent/tweet` with copy written as a dare ("Think you can recreate this pixel-for-pixel?") rather than a flat announcement, explicitly naming frontendforever.dev in the text as the brand mention (no per-battle OG image exists yet for a visual watermark — flagged as a real option if a richer link-preview card is wanted later, same machinery Feature 29's `opengraph-image.tsx` already proves out). Placed in the header next to the Solution button.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; a clean production `next build`; grepped every touched file for hardcoded colors, clean. **Still not verified for the same standing reason** (no browser automation with a real login session in this environment) — the letterbox sizing, drag smoothness, ghost opacity read, copy button, and share button all need the user's own click-through.

**Third same-session follow-up round on Feature 53 — the user's next screenshot showed the round-2 letterbox fix hadn't actually solved the real problem, and a genuine misunderstanding on this end got corrected mid-round before any more code was written:**
