1. **The `ResizeObserver`-measured letterbox from round 2 was over-engineered and solving the wrong problem — ripped out entirely, not patched again.** The user's real ask (after two rounds of back-and-forth to actually pin it down): the compare window should fill 100% of its available space, edge to edge, with no empty margin — full stop. The apparent tension with "match the design's original pixel dimensions" that prompted an `AskUserQuestion` about cropping trade-offs turned out to be a false one: **a recreated UI's rendered size is governed by its own CSS** (fixed widths, centering, `min-height: 100vh`, etc.), not by how big the surrounding iframe box is. Giving the iframe more room doesn't stretch, scale, or crop the card — the card (`width: 320px`, flex-centered) just stays exactly its authored size and re-centers, precisely like a real browser tab. So there's no fit-vs-crop trade-off to make at all: both iframes are now a plain, unconditional `w-full h-full` of the frame — no `aspect-ratio`, no `ResizeObserver`, no scale transforms, no `boxSize` state, no `targetWidth`/`targetHeight` props on `ComparePane` anymore (dropped from its `Props` type and both call sites in `BattleEditorWorkspace` — no longer needed there at all). This is also the more correct general answer for future landscape-shaped challenges (a page section, a grid) — they'll reflow into the bigger canvas exactly like a real page would, not fight a pixel-locked frame.
2. **Toolbar restructured into a real overlay, not a sibling row.** `ComparePane`'s root is now a single relatively-positioned frame (`h-full w-full`, border, rounded) — both iframes fill it completely (`absolute inset-0`), and the Diff/Slide toggle + fullscreen button sit in an `absolute inset-x-0 top-0` bar (`bg-background/80 backdrop-blur-sm`, `z-30`) *inside* the frame's own top edge, per direct request ("the position of diff/slide/fullscreen button is correct, I don't want to move that, but it should be inside the window canvas"). The mode tag ("Output"/"Target UI") moved from top-left to bottom-left to stay clear of the new toolbar overlay. The slider's drag-position math (`getBoundingClientRect()`) now reads off the frame itself (the old separate `containerRef` for an inner letterbox box no longer exists — one ref, `frameRef`, used for both the frame's bounds and the drag math).
3. **X share now actually shares the challenge image.** The editor page's `generateMetadata` had no `openGraph`/`twitter` image at all — X (and Facebook) unfurl whatever a page declares, so a shared link previewed with no image. Wired `openGraph.images`/`twitter.images` to the challenge's own `targetImageUrl` (the same PNG already serving the list-page thumbnail — no new image-generation route needed, unlike Practice's dynamic `opengraph-image.tsx`, since a real static asset already exists per challenge) with an absolute URL derived via `headers()`, same pattern as Practice's Editor page.

**Process note worth keeping:** this round only converged after an `AskUserQuestion` about a cropping trade-off that the user correctly flagged as based on a wrong premise ("i think you did not get it, let me describe the point 1 again") — the fix was to actually re-ask for the description rather than guess again, which surfaced the real, much simpler answer. Worth remembering before reaching for a technically-impressive solution (measured letterboxing, `ResizeObserver`, scale-to-cover math) when a plain CSS default might already be correct once the actual constraint is understood.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; a clean production `next build`; grepped every touched file for hardcoded colors, clean. **Still not verified for the same standing reason** — the edge-to-edge fill, the toolbar-as-overlay look, and the X share card's image all need the user's own click-through.

**Fourth same-session follow-up round on Feature 53 — the user confirmed the editor page itself now works well and moved to the `/playground/battles` list page**, flagging its card as blurry with a reference screenshot (a project-card design from elsewhere) for the visual direction, "in our style."

1. **Root-caused the blur, not just re-exported a bigger image.** `BattleCard`'s `next/image` had `sizes="224px"` hardcoded from its only prior use (the hub's `w-56` scroll-row teaser) — reused as-is on the list page's much wider grid cards, telling the browser to fetch a lower-resolution image than it actually needed and upscale it via CSS. Confirmed via the file itself, not assumed: the source screenshot was also only 400×480px raw (1x), so even a correct `sizes` hint would've had no headroom left at the list page's actual card width. Fixed both: re-shot the pilot's target screenshot at `deviceScaleFactor: 2` (now 800×960px, same 400×480 CSS/logical size — `targetWidth`/`targetHeight` in the DB correctly stay unchanged, they describe logical not raw pixels), and gave the new list card a real responsive `sizes` value matching its actual grid breakpoints (`100vw`/`50vw`/`33vw` at the grid's own `sm`/`lg` breakpoints) instead of a stale fixed value.
2. **New `BattleListCard` component, not a modified `BattleCard`** — deliberate sibling, same precedent as `BattleCard`/`ExperimentCard`: the hub's compact teaser (thumbnail + title + difficulty pill, `w-56`) and the list page's richer card (cover art + badge + description + CTA) are different enough shapes that forcing one component to cover both would need prop-driven conditional rendering for no real reuse benefit. `BattleCard` itself is untouched, still used only by the hub's scroll row, still correctly `sizes="224px"` there.
3. **Matched the reference's structure, not its literal colors** — cover-art area (gradient `from-surface-secondary to-accent-muted/40` background, the screenshot shown `object-contain` with padding so it reads as a floating preview rather than a hard crop), the existing difficulty pill (reused `DIFFICULTY_STYLES`, not a new bare-text+icon treatment — kept consistent with every other difficulty badge already in this codebase rather than copying the reference's exact icon+text style), a real 2-line-clamped description (`getUiBattleCatalog()` extended to select `description` — it existed on the table already, just wasn't in the catalog query), and a pill CTA ("Start Battle", `ArrowRight` that nudges on `group-hover`, same pattern as `MoreInterviewPrepCta`) — rendered as a styled `<span>`, not a nested `<a>`/`<button>`, since the whole card is already one `Link` and a real nested interactive element would be invalid HTML.
4. **Explicitly did not add a "+100 rep"-style badge from the reference** — confirmed with the user first via `AskUserQuestion` rather than assumed: Playground has no XP/rep/scoring system at all in v1 (`build-plan.md` is explicit about this), and this codebase has a hard "never show fabricated data" precedent (every other honest-empty-state/real-data-only decision in this file). Dropped the second badge slot entirely rather than inventing a number or building a fake placeholder for it.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; a clean production `next build`; grepped every touched file for hardcoded colors, clean. **Not verified — same standing limitation** (no browser automation with a real login session in this environment): the actual sharpness of the re-shot image and the new card's visual layout both need the user's own look.

**Aside, same round:** the user separately reported a `/api/auth/get-session` 404 → hydration-mismatch error from their own dev server log, on the homepage (`(main)` route group — outside anything touched this session). Diagnosed as the exact Turbopack dev-server route-discovery quirk already documented in `AGENTS.md`'s "Known Dev-Server Quirks" section (a route silently failing to compile until an actual file save touches it) — applied the documented fix (a no-op double-save of `src/app/api/auth/[...all]/route.ts`) rather than treating it as a new bug. Confirmed `(main)/layout.tsx`/`UserDropdown.tsx` are real, existing files in this repo (not a different project pasted by mistake) before applying the fix.

**Fifth same-session follow-up round on Feature 53 — a small, direct fix to `BattleListCard`'s cover box, spotted by the user comparing their own screenshot side-by-side against the reference.** The cover art was still rendering tiny even after the description/blur pass above. Root cause: the cover box was a fixed `h-44` (short and wide), but the target is portrait (400×480) — `object-contain` inside a short-and-wide box is height-bound, so the rendered image shrank to a small fraction of the box's width, leaving mostly empty gradient around it. Fixed by changing the box from a fixed height to `aspect-[4/3]` (scales with the card's actual width instead of a pixel constant) with padding tightened from `p-6` to `p-4` — deliberately a middle-ground ratio, not tuned to this one portrait pilot, so a future landscape-shaped challenge (a page section, a grid) doesn't overcorrect into the opposite problem.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; a clean production `next build`; grepped the touched file for hardcoded colors, clean. **Not verified — same standing limitation.**

**Sixth same-session follow-up round on Feature 53 — the user confirmed the list page card now looks right and asked for the same visual quality on `BattleCard`** (the Playground hub's compact `w-56` scroll-row teaser, used on `/playground`, separate from `BattleListCard`). Brought it in line with the same treatment: cover box changed from a fixed `h-32` + `object-cover object-top` (cropped, flat `bg-surface-secondary`) to `aspect-[4/3]` + `object-contain` inside the same gradient (`from-surface-secondary to-accent-muted/40`) with a `rounded-lg shadow-md` floating-card wrapper — identical visual language to `BattleListCard`'s cover, just at `p-3` instead of `p-4` to suit the narrower card. Also reordered the footer (difficulty pill now above the title, matching `BattleListCard`'s rhythm, rather than title-then-pill) for visual consistency between the two cards. `BattleListCard` itself untouched — this was purely bringing its compact sibling up to the same standard, not a shared-component change.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; a clean production `next build`; grepped the touched file for hardcoded colors, clean. **Not verified — same standing limitation.**

**Two rounds of CodeRabbit PR review, each verified against current code before touching anything, per the project's standing CodeRabbit-findings rule:**

- **Round 1 (3 inline + 2 nitpicks):** fixed `key={activeFile}` on `CodeEditor` (was destroying undo history/view-state on every file switch — confirmed via `@monaco-editor/react`'s own docs that `path` + `keepCurrentModel` is the intended multi-model pattern, switched to that instead); fixed `FileTabs`' `aria-controls` (2 of 3 tabs pointed at nonexistent panel ids, since only one `tabpanel` is ever rendered — now all 3 point at one fixed `"battle-file-panel"` id); fixed empty `alt=""` on both `BattleCard`/`BattleListCard`'s target-image (real descriptive alt text now, e.g. "Target UI preview for the Login Card battle"); deduplicated a verbatim `FILE_LABEL` map between `FileTabs.tsx` and `BattleSolutionPanel.tsx` (now exported once from `FileTabs.tsx` as `FILE_META`). **Skipped, with reason:** a suggestion to switch `unstable_cache` to Next 16's `"use cache"` directive — grepped the entire `src/` tree and confirmed zero existing `"use cache"` usage anywhere in this codebase; `unstable_cache` is the sole, consistent pattern, and changing only this one file would introduce inconsistency rather than fix anything.
- **Round 2 (1 finding, found to be a real bug via reading the actual library source, not assumed):** Monaco model `path` values were bare `activeFile` ("html"/"css"/"js"), not scoped per challenge. Read `@monaco-editor/react`'s actual minified source (`node_modules/@monaco-editor/react/dist/index.js`) and confirmed both Monaco's model registry and the library's own view-state cache are global singletons keyed only by the `path` string, and `keepCurrentModel: true` deliberately skips disposing a model on unmount — so navigating from one battle challenge to another would silently reuse the *previous* battle's stale "html"/"css"/"js" models instead of the new challenge's real starter code. Fixed by prefixing the path with `challenge.slug` (`` `${challenge.slug}/${activeFile}` ``), which required threading `slug` into `BattleEditorWorkspace`'s `Props.challenge` type.

**Post-CodeRabbit browser regression pass (this session), using real login — not just typecheck/build:** no OAuth provider can be automated in this environment, so used Better-Auth's own official `testUtils` plugin (a separate, throwaway test-only `betterAuth` instance sharing the real DB + `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`) to mint a real, DB-backed signed session cookie for a throwaway user — same precedent as the Feature-31-completion-tracking fix's Playwright pass. Ran a 21-check Playwright suite covering both logged-out (`/playground/battles` browsable, direct editor URL redirects to `/login?callbackURL=...`, real alt text) and logged-in (editor loads, FileTabs/`aria-controls` correct, Monaco mounts and preserves per-file content and typed edits across tab switches, ComparePane renders both iframes, Diff/Slide toggle, non-premium user sees the real locked-solution message, Share button present) states — 21/21 passed, zero console/page errors. Test user/session deleted from the DB afterward; throwaway scripts removed before commit.

**Added a second real pilot challenge, "Profile Card" (`profile-card-recreate`), specifically to make the Round 2 Monaco fix testable end-to-end** — with only one challenge ever seeded, true cross-battle state bleed couldn't actually be exercised. Built from a user-supplied reference screenshot (a profile card: avatar, name/role, bio, "Contact me" button, 4 social icons) — used an initials avatar ("SD" on a colored circle, matching this app's own letter-avatar convention) rather than a real photo, and did **not** replicate the reference's "+100 rep" badge/"Starter" reward-style chrome, since this codebase has no scoring system in Playground v1 and never shows fabricated numbers (same rule already established earlier this feature). Target/starter/solution HTML/CSS/JS added to `scripts/seed/uiBattles.ts`, screenshot generated via the same Playwright-scratch approach as the pilot, seeded via `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts`.

**Ran a dedicated cross-battle isolation test against the two real challenges (the actual regression test for the Round 2 fix, not just a single-challenge smoke test):** loaded Battle A (Login Card) via a hard page load, typed a unique marker into its HTML file, then used real in-app `Link` clicks (client-side/soft navigation, not `page.goto` — a hard navigation would reset the whole JS realm and Monaco's registry with it, defeating the point of the test) to go A → list → Battle B (Profile Card) → list → back to A. Confirmed at every step: Battle B loads its own starter content with zero trace of Battle A's marker or content; typing a second marker into Battle B and returning to Battle A shows Battle A's own content plus its own marker, with zero trace of Battle B's. 8/8 checks passed — this is the first time the fix has been proven against genuine cross-battle navigation rather than a single challenge in isolation.

**Real bug found and fixed via a user-reported repro, not caught by any of the above:** the user copied Profile Card's official solution into their own editor and clicked its social icons — the output pane then showed the real app's `/login` page rendered *inside* the challenge's own output iframe, ghosted under the target overlay. Reproduced exactly via Playwright (frame-navigation events confirmed the *output iframe itself*, not the top-level tab, navigated to `http://localhost:3000/login?callbackURL=...`) and root-caused precisely: a `srcDoc` document's relative-URL base resolves against the *embedding page's* real URL, not the iframe's own address — so the placeholder `href="#"` on each social icon resolved to the real app's own same-origin URL. Since that URL differs from the iframe's actual loaded address (`about:srcdoc`), the browser treats the click as a genuine cross-document navigation, not a same-page scroll; the sandboxed iframe (`sandbox="allow-scripts"`, no `allow-same-origin`) has an opaque origin, so that navigation is cross-site for `SameSite=Lax` cookie purposes, the session cookie gets stripped, and the server correctly redirects the now-unauthenticated request to `/login` — which then renders for real inside the output iframe. Login Card never surfaced this because it has zero anchors; Profile Card was the first challenge with real in-page links. **Fixed two ways:** (1) the actual bug — added `onclick="event.preventDefault()"` to all 4 social anchors in both `targetHtml`/`solutionHtml` (`scripts/seed/uiBattles.ts`), stopping the browser's native navigation before it ever resolves anything; (2) systemic defense-in-depth — `buildBattleDoc()` now pins `<base href="about:blank" />` in the document `<head>`, so any relative href in *any* current or future challenge's content resolves somewhere inert instead of back into the real app, even if a future challenge author forgets the `preventDefault` guard. Re-verified via the same repro script (all 4 social links, fresh solution content pulled live from the DB post-fix): zero frame navigation of any kind, card renders identically before and after every click. Re-ran the full 21-check auth regression suite afterward to confirm Login Card (no anchors, unaffected by the `<base>` addition) still passes 21/21. `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` all clean.

**Follow-up, same session: real profile photo added, and a real regression in the fix above caught and corrected.** User supplied a Pexels photo URL to replace Profile Card's "SD" initials avatar. Flagged two real concerns before using it, confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` rather than assumed: (1) the photo is of a man, but the card's persona ("Sarah Dole") is written as a woman — an immediate content mismatch; (2) it reads as a genuine identifiable individual's real professional headshot, not generic stock/model photography — attaching a fabricated name/employer to a real-looking person's face is a different, more sensitive situation than a generic placeholder avatar. **User chose to keep the exact photo and rename the persona to match it instead** — the card's name changed from "Sarah Dole" to "David Chen" (all instances in `targetHtml`/`solutionHtml`), avatar markup changed from `<div class="avatar">SD</div>` to a real `<img>`, and `.avatar` CSS switched from the text-avatar treatment (flex-centered initials) to `object-fit: cover` + a light ring border. Photo cropped via `sharp` (`resize(400, 400, { fit: "cover", position: "top" })`) to a clean top-aligned square headshot crop, saved locally at `public/playground/battles/profile-card-avatar.jpg` (self-hosted, not hotlinked — matches every other asset in this feature) — the list-page/hub thumbnail screenshot was also regenerated to show the same photo.

**A real regression was caught and corrected during verification, not shipped blind:** the `<base href="about:blank">` defense-in-depth added for the earlier social-link fix also broke the new `<img src="/playground/battles/profile-card-avatar.jpg">` — since `<base>` affects *every* relative URL in the document, not just anchor navigation, the avatar rendered as a broken-image icon (caught via a Playwright screenshot before telling the user it was done, not after). Root-cause fix: removed `<base>` entirely and replaced it with a capture-phase `document.addEventListener("click", ..., true)` script inside `buildBattleDoc()` that calls `event.preventDefault()` specifically on any click that targets an anchor — this blocks the exact same navigation-leak bug for any current/future challenge without touching how any other relative URL (images, future assets) resolves. The per-anchor `onclick="event.preventDefault()"` attributes added to Profile Card's seed content in the round above were removed as redundant once the global guard existed. Re-verified via the same Playwright repro (paste solution, click all 4 social links): zero iframe navigation, avatar image loads correctly, and the full 21-check regression suite still passes 21/21.

**34 Roadmaps List Page — rescoped significantly before any code was written.** Ran the `architect` skill first. The original `build-plan.md` spec (a grid of FF-concept-only roadmaps) was pivoted, via explicit user direction across two rounds of discussion, into a roadmap.sh-style system: **Role-based** roadmaps (a full job-role path, e.g. "Frontend Developer", mixing internal FF Learn links with external article/video links where FF has no matching lesson) and **Skill-based** roadmaps (a single-technology deep dive mapping 1:1 onto one of FF's 8 concept categories, almost entirely internal-linked). A roadmap node can also link directly to Practice challenges and FF Collections interview questions, not just a Learn concept — explicit user request, since Practice/Interview content is mostly standalone (`challenges.conceptId`/`collection_questions` have no reliable concept link) so this can't be auto-derived and needs its own link table. Full visual format (an interactive pan/zoom node-graph canvas, roadmap.sh's actual style) is Feature 35's job — this feature ships the schema + list page only.

- **Schema replaced, not extended:** dropped `roadmap_steps` (never consumed by any shipped page) for a hierarchical model — `roadmaps` (+ `roadmap_type: 'role' | 'skill'`), `roadmap_nodes` (self-referencing `parent_id` for section grouping, hand-authored `position_x`/`position_y` canvas coordinates — same approach roadmap.sh's own source uses, no auto-layout algorithm/dependency), `roadmap_node_links` (polymorphic: `learn-concept` | `practice-challenge` | `interview-question` | `external-video` | `external-article`, one row per link so a node can carry several at once), and `user_roadmap_node_progress` (manual "mark as done" toggle, but only ever meant for external-linked nodes — Learn-linked nodes derive completion from real `user_concept_progress` instead of duplicating it; no XP awarded either way, deliberately avoiding the class of bug CodeRabbit caught on Feature 32's spaced-repetition XP). Migrations generated in two passes (`0022_drop_roadmap_steps.sql`, `0023_add_roadmap_node_graph.sql`) to keep drizzle-kit's interactive rename-detection prompt (no non-interactive flag exists) from misreading the drop+add as a rename — applied cleanly, confirmed via the generated SQL before running `drizzle-kit migrate`.
- **Seed rewritten** (`scripts/seed/roadmaps.ts`, `scripts/seed.ts`): the 2 old pre-pivot roadmaps (`frontend-foundations`, `react-expert-path`) were deleted outright (one-off cleanup script, run once, removed after) rather than migrated — they never fit the new role/skill split and no page had ever shipped rendering them. Replaced with 5 real roadmaps: **1 role-based** ("Frontend Developer" — a genuine, verified 2-section/9-node pilot slice: an "Internet & Web Basics" section with 3 real external-article links to MDN, confirmed live via `WebSearch` rather than guessed, plus an "HTML & CSS Fundamentals" section with 4 real internal Learn-concept links; the full topic run across every remaining section — Git, package managers, build tools, testing, framework choice, etc. — is Feature 35's dedicated content-authoring pass, same pilot-then-full-run precedent as Features 49/53/54) and **4 skill-based** (JavaScript/CSS/React/TypeScript — each built programmatically from `CONCEPTS`, wrapping that category's existing fully-authored concepts, in order, one `learn-concept` link per node — zero new lesson content, just structure, since Features 41/43/44/45 already wrote all of it). Roadmap nodes are upserted on their real `(roadmap_id, slug)` unique key inside a per-roadmap transaction, not deleted-and-reinserted (fixed in CodeRabbit review — see below); links (no stable key of their own) are still cleared and rebuilt, scoped to the roadmap's current node set. Verified idempotent (ran twice, byte-identical node ids on the second run).
- **UI** (`src/features/roadmaps/`, `/roadmaps`): `getRoadmapSummaries` mirrors `getCategorySummaries`' cache pattern exactly (`unstable_cache`-wrapped catalog query, tag `"roadmaps"`, then a `cache()`-wrapped per-user completion overlay computed in JS from `user_concept_progress` + `user_roadmap_node_progress`). List page groups roadmaps under "Role-based"/"Skill-based" headings, matching roadmap.sh's own homepage structure. **`RoadmapCard` went through 4 design rounds the same session, all direct user requests** (full `ChallengeCategoryCard`-style shell → title-only blurred/transparent card → a rejected hover-glow/arrow treatment, reverted → real devicon.dev brand marks left of the title, not a generic lucide icon or `CATEGORY_META` badge → the role roadmap's icon swapped from a devicon HTML5 mark to lucide's `Code2` ("</>"), since HTML5 read as "this is an HTML roadmap" — see `ui-registry.md`'s Roadmaps section for the full blow-by-blow and the 5 hand-inlined `components/shared/devicons/*` components this produced).
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean; live dev-server check via Playwright — `/roadmaps` screenshotted in both light and dark theme (theme forced via `localStorage`/class, since the `beforeInteractive` theme-init script doesn't re-run on a Playwright `page.reload()` the way a real browser navigation does — a test-harness quirk, not an app bug), zero console/page errors in either. Branch `feature/34-roadmaps-list-page` off `develop`, **pushed — PR #99 open against `develop`.**

**First CodeRabbit review round on PR #99 — 6 findings addressed, 2 skipped with reason:**

- **Real data-loss bug, not a style nitpick:** the seed script deleted every one of a roadmap's nodes and reinserted them fresh on every run, discarding `roadmap_nodes`' own real `(roadmap_id, slug)` unique key (which exists specifically to make upserting possible) and — because `roadmap_node_links`/`user_roadmap_node_progress` both cascade-delete off `roadmap_nodes.id` — silently wiping any real user's roadmap progress on the next content update, once this feature has real users. Fixed: nodes are now upserted on `(roadmap_id, slug)` (ids and any real progress survive), stale slugs are pruned with `notInArray`, links are cleared/rebuilt only for the roadmap's retained node ids, and the whole per-roadmap write now runs inside one `db.transaction` so a crash mid-run can't leave it half-updated. **A second, real bug was caught fixing the first one, not shipped blind:** the initial rewrite pruned stale nodes *before* backfilling `parent_id`, so a retained node whose parent had been renamed/restructured still pointed at the stale (about-to-be-pruned) parent at prune time — and because `parent_id` now has a real `ON DELETE CASCADE` (see below), pruning the stale parent collaterally cascade-deleted the retained child too, breaking the very next insert with a foreign-key violation. Caught by actually re-running the seed script against the dev DB, not just reading the diff — fixed by backfilling `parent_id` before pruning, not after. Verified: seed runs twice with byte-identical node ids, and a direct DB query confirms a node's id survives a re-seed.
- **`roadmap_nodes.parent_id` had no real foreign key at all** despite the schema comment calling it "self-reference" — a plain `uuid` column, no `.references()`. Added a self-referencing FK via the `AnyPgColumn` callback form (sidesteps referencing `roadmapNodes` before its own `const` finishes initializing), `ON DELETE CASCADE`.
- **`roadmap_node_links` had no DB-level guarantee that exactly one target column is set** — the schema comment said this was "enforced at the seed layer... same looseness as other mixed-shape tables," but `bookmarks_exactly_one_target_check` was already a real precedent for exactly this in the same file. Added `roadmap_node_links_exactly_one_target_check`, same pattern.
- **`RoadmapNodeLinkSeed` converted to a discriminated union on `linkType`** (was one flat type with every field optional) — a seed entry with `linkType: "learn-concept"` but no `conceptSlug` is now a compile error instead of a runtime throw. `seed.ts`'s link-resolution pass updated to direct property access per branch (no more `?.`/`??` guards for "is this field even provided," only the real "does this slug resolve to a DB row" check).
- **`roadmapType: r.roadmapType as RoadmapType` was an unchecked cast** on a plain DB `text` column — replaced with `parseRoadmapType()`, which validates against `ROADMAP_TYPES` and throws loudly on a data-integrity bug instead of silently mistyping it.
- **Devicon SVG fills were hardcoded hex, not CSS variables** — a real `AGENTS.md` rule-2 violation (unlike `components/shared/logos/*`'s company logos, which use `currentColor` and never violated it — the two aren't actually the same precedent, despite the original commit message claiming they were). Fixed: JS/TS icons now reference the existing `--color-js-badge-bg`/`--color-ts-badge-bg` tokens (same colors `categoryMeta.ts`'s letter badges already use elsewhere in the app); CSS3/React had no existing token to reuse, so two new theme-invariant tokens (`--color-css3-badge-bg`, `--color-react-badge-bg`) were added to `globals.css` alongside the existing pair.
- **Skipped, with reason — `RoadmapCard` not showing `completedCount`/`topicCount`:** CodeRabbit read this as `RoadmapSummary`'s completion data going unused. Not fixed — the title-only card is a direct, explicit, repeatedly-confirmed product decision (3 of the 4 design rounds above were the user rejecting progressively less-minimal versions of this exact card). It's also not an accessibility gap in the sense CodeRabbit implied: the card shows zero progress indication to sighted users either, so there's no sighted/screen-reader parity issue to fix — adding a screen-reader-only completion label would give blind users *more* information than everyone else gets, which isn't equal access, it's a different UI. Flagged to the user rather than silently overridden.
- **Skipped, with reason — `unstable_cache` vs. Next 16's `"use cache"`:** same nitpick already raised and rejected once before, on Feature 53 (see that entry above) — this codebase has zero existing `"use cache"` usage anywhere, so switching only this one file would trade a consistent pattern for an inconsistent one. Not a Feature-34-specific judgment call; re-litigating it per-feature would just re-answer the same question every time it comes up.

**Second CodeRabbit review round on PR #99 — 2 findings, both addressed:**

- **This round's heading itself was wrong** — the first round's summary line above said "1 skipped" while actually documenting 2 skipped bullets. Fixed to say "2 skipped."
- **`seed.ts` Pass 1's upsert never cleared a stale `parentId`:** the `onConflictDoUpdate` touched every other authored field but left `parentId` untouched, so a node that dropped its `parentSlug` in the seed data (promoted from a nested topic to a root) would keep pointing at its old parent forever — Pass 2 only ever *sets* `parentId` when `parentSlug` is present, it never clears it when absent. Fixed by resetting `parentId` to `null` in the Pass 1 `set`, with Pass 2 still responsible for reassigning it right after when `parentSlug` exists — same two-pass shape, just no longer one-directional.

**Merged to `develop` via PR #99 (merge commit `4fdd111`).**

**35 Roadmap Detail Page — the interactive node-graph canvas, built on Feature 34's schema.** Branch `feature/35-roadmap-detail-page` off `feature/34-roadmaps-list-page` (not `develop` — Feature 34 hadn't merged yet at the time, and 35 depends directly on its schema, so branching off stale `develop` would have meant rebuilding the roadmap tables from scratch; rebased onto `develop` after Feature 34's PR #99 merged, no conflicts outside this file and `queries.ts`'s barrel-split, both resolved by hand).

- **`getRoadmapDetail(slug, userId)`** (`features/roadmaps/lib/queries/detail.ts`, new — split out of `queries.ts` alongside the existing summaries query, same barrel pattern as Learn's `queries/*`) fetches one roadmap's full node graph: nodes, their links resolved against whichever table `link_type` calls for (`concepts`/`challenges`/`collection_questions` via `leftJoin`, external rows read straight off `roadmap_node_links`), and the current user's per-node completion (concept-linked nodes derived from `user_concept_progress`, external nodes from `user_roadmap_node_progress`). Not `unstable_cache`-wrapped like the list page's catalog — it reads per-user progress inline, and one roadmap's node count is small enough not to need a separate cached layer.
- **Canvas engine, built in-house, no new dependency:**
  - `useCanvasPanZoom` (`features/roadmaps/hooks/`) — pointer-drag pan + wheel zoom over a CSS `translate()/scale()` transform. Wheel zoom is bound as a *native* `{ passive: false }` listener via the container ref, not React's `onWheel` prop — React attaches synthetic wheel handlers as passive by default, so `preventDefault()` inside one is a silent no-op and the page scrolls along with the canvas.
  - A pointerdown that starts on a node button never begins a pan drag (checked via a `data-roadmap-node-interactive` attribute + `closest()`) — capturing the pointer at the container level would otherwise fight the button's own native click.
  - `canvasLayout.ts` — fixed node dimensions (topic 220×64, section 200×40) matching every authored `position_x`/`position_y`; `buildEdges()` connects every child to its parent (section → topics) plus chains root-level nodes *within the same authored column* (grouped by shared `positionX`, not one global chain — an earlier version chained all roots in one Y-sorted sequence and produced a stray diagonal line jumping between the two Frontend Developer columns whenever their section lengths drifted out of vertical sync; caught via the Playwright verification pass below, fixed before commit).
  - `RoadmapCanvas` (pan/zoom surface) / `RoadmapNode` (box) / `RoadmapConnectors` (plain SVG `<line>`s, no arrowheads) / `RoadmapCanvasControls` (zoom in/out/reset).
- **`RoadmapMobileList`** — a pan/zoom canvas is a poor fit under ~768px, so mobile renders the same node data as a grouped, scrollable list instead (CSS breakpoint, not JS viewport detection — avoids an SSR/hydration mismatch). Surfaced a second real ordering bug: `getRoadmapDetail`'s node query had no `ORDER BY` at first (arbitrary/insertion order), then a `positionY, positionX` row-major order that read fine on the 2D canvas but interleaved the mobile list's two columns out of their intended sequence (e.g. "JavaScript In Depth" before "JavaScript Fundamentals") whenever one column's sections ran shorter than the other's. Fixed by ordering `positionX, positionY` (column-major) — every column's nodes now stay grouped together in the flat list, matching how the content was actually authored.
- **`RoadmapDetailView`** — the stateful orchestrator: owns `nodes`/`selectedNodeId`/`isSaving` once and renders `RoadmapCanvas` + `RoadmapMobileList` (one hidden via CSS, not two independent state trees) plus one shared `RoadmapNodePanel`.
- **`RoadmapNodePanel`** — a Framer Motion slide-in drawer (not the shadcn `Dialog`, which is a centered modal — no side-drawer primitive exists in this codebase yet). Groups a node's links by type (Learn / Practice / Interview questions / Watch / Read) under icon-labeled sections; shows a read-only "Completed via Learn" badge for concept-linked nodes or a "Mark as done" toggle for external-only nodes — never both, and the toggle is disabled for concept-linked nodes (the API route rejects that write server-side too, not just hidden client-side).
- **`POST /api/roadmap-progress`** — same auth → rate-limit → validate → scoped-write shape as every other route (`code-standards.md`), mirroring `collection-questions/toggle-complete`'s exact insert-on-tick/delete-on-untick pattern. Rejects the write with a 400 if the target node has a `learn-concept` link (that node's completion isn't this route's to set). No XP — self-reported, unverifiable completion never earns a reward in this codebase (Feature 32's spaced-repetition XP-farming bug is exactly the class of mistake this avoids).
- **SEO/GEO pass** (`features/roadmaps/lib/roadmapSeoContent.ts` + `RoadmapFaq.tsx`): answer-first intro paragraph + FAQ per roadmap (all 5), always-visible (not a collapsed accordion — hidden-until-click text works against crawlability). `Course` + `BreadcrumbList` + `FAQPage` JSON-LD on the detail page, canonical/OG metadata via the shared `lib/seo.ts` helpers (same pattern as Feature 31's question pages).
- **Content — the full "Frontend Developer" roadmap authored this feature** (Feature 34 shipped only a 2-section, 9-node pilot): 13 sections / 52 topic nodes / 65 nodes total, laid out via a `layoutColumns()` helper in `scripts/seed/roadmaps.ts` (computes positions from the section/topic structure this file itself defines — not an imported auto-layout library, which the earlier architecture decision specifically ruled out) rather than 65 hand-typed coordinate pairs. Internal nodes (CSS, JS Runtime, React, TypeScript, Browser Internals, Performance, Accessibility — 7 of FF's 8 categories) reuse each linked concept's own title/description via a `conceptTopic()` lookup helper, so roadmap copy never drifts from the Learn page it points at. External nodes (Git/GitHub, npm, module bundlers, Sass, Jest, Playwright, MDN deployment guide, HTML fundamentals) link to **9 additional real resources, each verified live via `WebSearch` before being written** — no guessed URLs.
- **Verified via Playwright** (dev server, real seeded data — no mocked login was available, same standing limitation as every other feature in this environment; the logged-out "Log in to track progress" disabled-button state *was* confirmed): desktop canvas (pan, zoom in/out, node click → panel with real links) on both the Frontend Developer roadmap (2-column, sectioned) and a skill roadmap (single-column, no sections); mobile list (all 13 sections, correct topic order after the fix above); dark mode; zero console/page errors throughout. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean across the whole project at every step, not just the new files.

**Same-session redesign: the pan/zoom canvas was ripped out entirely and replaced with a static roadmap.sh-style flow diagram — direct user request against the real reference page (`roadmap.sh/frontend`), not a refinement of the canvas.** The user's framing: roadmap.sh itself has no zoom-in/zoom-out canvas at all, and the earlier build didn't look right regardless. `RoadmapCanvas`/`RoadmapNode`/`RoadmapConnectors`/`RoadmapCanvasControls`/`useCanvasPanZoom`/`canvasLayout.ts` were all deleted outright (confirmed unused via `grep` first), replaced with a page that just scrolls: a single vertical spine (every "section" node, or every parent-less "topic" node for the 4 section-less skill roadmaps) with each section's real topic children branching off to alternating sides on a dotted curve — a pure re-interpretation of the *existing* `parentId` hierarchy and node order (`features/roadmaps/lib/flowLayout.ts`'s `buildFlowRows`), no schema or query change. Connector curves use the same measured-DOM-position technique (`useLayoutEffect` + `getBoundingClientRect`, re-run on resize) the homepage's `PlatformGraph.tsx` established, needed here because a branch group's pill count (0–6) varies node to node with no fixed offset that fits every section. **Two real bugs caught during Playwright verification, not shipped blind:** (1) `buildFlowRows(nodes)` was called unmemoized on every render, so the measurement effect's `[rows]` dependency re-fired every render → state update → re-render → new array → repeat, tripping React's "Maximum update depth exceeded" — fixed with `useMemo`; (2) the vertical spine connector (one grid item spanning `grid-row: 1 / -1`) rendered at zero height because the grid's `items-center` shrinks a spanning item to its auto content height and centers it in the span instead of stretching it — fixed with `self-stretch`. Added `RoadmapSidebar.tsx` (Legend + a real Related Roadmaps list via the existing `getRoadmapSummaries`, not roadmap.sh's own "personal recommendation/alternative option" legend, which has no equivalent in this schema) to `/roadmaps/[slug]`'s layout. `RoadmapMobileList` untouched — the new diagram is still a poor fit under ~768px, same breakpoint split as before. Re-verified: Frontend Developer (13 sections, 52 branch pills) and JavaScript (flat spine, no branches) in light/dark, node-click → panel, no horizontal overflow, zero console errors after the two fixes above; `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean. Full narrative in `ui-registry.md`'s Roadmap Detail Page section.

**Same-session follow-up: sidebar moved to the right, and the "Frontend Developer" roadmap rebuilt at roadmap.sh's real full syllabus breadth — a second direct user request against the reference page, not a refinement of the flow-diagram redesign above.** The pilot/full-content builds up to this point (9-node pilot on Feature 34, then a 65-node expansion on Feature 35) had already outgrown roadmap.sh's real topic coverage in some places (a deep 6-concept "React" section) while missing huge swaths of it in others (no AI tooling, no bundlers/linters section, no deployment platforms, no GraphQL/Web Components/PWAs/desktop/mobile) — this pass closes that gap directly against the real `roadmap.sh/frontend` page, screenshot for screenshot, rather than a rough approximation.

- **`RoadmapSidebar` moved from the page's left column to the right** (`/roadmaps/[slug]/page.tsx`'s grid template flipped from `[240px_1fr]` to `[1fr_240px]`, `<aside>` moved after the main content div in JSX) — one-line layout fix, not a redesign.
- **`scripts/seed/roadmaps.ts`'s `layoutColumns` helper extended to accept bare topics, not just sections** (`RoadmapEntry = SectionSpec | TopicSpec`, discriminated via `isSection()`) — roadmap.sh's real page mixes branching groups (e.g. "Version Control" branching to Git/GitHub/GitLab) with plain, directly-clickable single nodes with no branches at all (e.g. "Auth Strategies," "PWAs," "Design Systems"); the old model only had section-with-branches, so a plain node had no honest representation. `conceptTopic(...)`/`externalTopic(...)` calls now drop straight into the top-level entries array with no section wrapper when that's what the real page shows.
- **The 2-column canvas layout is gone — one single column now** (`layoutColumns([FRONTEND_DEVELOPER_ENTRIES])`, not `[COLUMN_A, COLUMN_B]`): now that there's no pan/zoom canvas to fill with a 2D grid, `positionX`/`positionY` only need to produce the correct single-file spine reading order via `getRoadmapDetail`'s existing column-major sort, so splitting content across authoring "columns" for layout-balance reasons no longer serves any purpose.
- **Grew from 13 sections/65 nodes to 32 sections-or-standalone-topics/137 nodes, 110 links** — every new section maps to a real section on `roadmap.sh/frontend`, in the same reading order: Internet & Web Basics → HTML & Semantic Markup → CSS Fundamentals → JavaScript Fundamentals → **Version Control** (split out from Package Managers, +GitLab) → **Package Managers** (npm/Yarn/pnpm/Bun) → **Learn a Framework** (React/Vue/Angular/Svelte/Solid JS, all external — picking a framework, not FF's own deep-dive) → React Fundamentals (existing 6-concept deep dive, relocated here since it's the natural continuation once React is picked) → **CSS Frameworks** (Tailwind + the existing Sass topic) → **AI in Development** (AI-assisted coding, prompt engineering, agents/MCP) → **Module Bundlers** (Vite/esbuild/Rollup) → **Linters & Formatters** (ESLint/Prettier/Biome) → **Auth Strategies** (standalone, Better Auth docs — this project's own real stack) → CSS Beyond the Basics → JavaScript In Depth → **Testing** (Vitest/Playwright/Cypress/Jest) → **Web APIs** (internal: Storage APIs, Web Workers) → **Web Security** (CORS/HTTPS/CSP internal + OWASP Top 10 external) → **Type Checkers** (all 8 TypeScript concepts, not just 4) → **Web Frameworks** (Next.js/Nuxt/SvelteKit/Astro, the meta-framework layer — distinct from "Learn a Framework" above) → **Streaming SSR & Hydration** (standalone, internal) → **SSG** (standalone, external) → **Deployment** (GitHub Pages/Vercel/Netlify/Cloudflare Pages, replacing the old single generic MDN link) → Browser Internals (down to 3 concepts — CORS/security moved out to Web Security) → **Design Systems** (standalone, internal: component-driven-architecture) → Performance (5 concepts, up from 3) → **Web Components** (Custom Elements/Shadow DOM) → **GraphQL** (graphql.org + Apollo Client) → **Accessibility** (4 real concepts — previously 0 accessibility concepts were used anywhere in this roadmap) → **PWAs** (standalone, internal: service-workers-caching-strategies) → **Desktop Apps** (Electron/Tauri) → **Mobile Apps** (React Native/Flutter/Ionic).
- **Every one of the ~35 new external URLs verified live via `WebSearch` before being written**, same standing rule as the original pilot — nothing guessed, including URLs for well-known, seemingly-obvious sites (react.dev, tailwindcss.com, etc.), since a couple of real surprises turned up during verification (SolidJS's docs moved off `solidjs.com` to `docs.solidjs.com`; SvelteKit's docs live at `svelte.dev/docs/kit`, not the once-real `kit.svelte.dev`).
- **Internal FF content used wherever a real match exists, not just where it was already wired up**: Type Checkers now uses all 8 TypeScript concepts (was 4); Accessibility went from 0 concepts used anywhere in this roadmap to 4; Web APIs picked up 2 previously-unused Browser Internals concepts (`storage-apis`, `web-workers-concurrency`); Performance gained 2 more (`profiling-with-devtools`, `performance-budgets`); Design Systems/SSR/PWAs each got their own standalone node off a single well-fitting concept (`component-driven-architecture`, `streaming-ssr-hydration`, `service-workers-caching-strategies`) that had never been used in this roadmap before.
- **Verified:** re-seeded twice (137 nodes, byte-identical on the second run — the parentId-reset/CodeRabbit fix from PR #99 holds under a real large-scale content change, not just the small pilot it was originally tested against); `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean; Playwright — full page screenshot in light and dark, no horizontal overflow, zero console errors, a real external branch pill (React, under "Learn a Framework") clicked through to confirm its panel shows the right description and the verified `react.dev` link.

**Third same-session follow-up: the Frontend Developer roadmap was fully reverted back to a pure roadmap.sh mirror, and a second, brand-new role roadmap was built specifically to showcase the FF platform.** Direct user pushback on the syllabus-rebuild round above: "you've added our learn concepts there but it's not Frontend Development Roadmap" — mixing FF's own Learn content into a roadmap that's supposed to read exactly like roadmap.sh defeated the point. Explicit direction: keep Frontend Developer 100% external, and if platform content deserves its own roadmap, ship that as a **separate** roadmap instead. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before building: cover every platform feature area (not just Learn/Practice/Interview Prep), which meant adding a small new link type first.

- **Frontend Developer reverted to 100% external** — every remaining `conceptTopic()` call (CSS/JS fundamentals, HTML/ARIA, CSS-beyond-basics, JS-in-depth, Web APIs, Web Security's CORS/fundamentals, Type Checkers, Browser Internals, Design Systems, Performance, Accessibility, PWAs, SSR) replaced with a real, `WebSearch`-verified external resource (mostly MDN, plus web.dev for performance/rendering, typescriptlang.org for Type Checkers, WebAIM/Deque for accessibility, CSS-Tricks for debounce/throttle, Brad Frost's Atomic Design for Design Systems). The **"React Fundamentals" section was deleted outright** — it was FF's own 6-concept deep dive with no equivalent position on the real roadmap.sh page, the single clearest example of platform content leaking into what's supposed to be a faithful mirror. Type Checkers simplified from 8 TypeScript concepts down to the 4 branches roadmap.sh's own page actually shows (Basic Types/Interfaces/Generics/Narrowing), now linking to the real TypeScript Handbook instead of FF's Learn pages. Grew to 126 nodes (up from 137 in the previous round, net smaller despite adding a DNS branch, since dropping React Fundamentals removed more than the fixes added).
- **New `internal-page` roadmap-node-link type** (`ROADMAP_NODE_LINK_TYPES` in `constants.ts`) — needed because Playbook chapters, System Design guides, Study Plans, UI Battles, and Experiments are filesystem/registry content with no DB row, so none of the 3 existing internal link types (`learn-concept`/`practice-challenge`/`interview-question`) could reach them. Reuses the same `external_title`/`external_url` columns `external-video`/`external-article` already use — no new columns, no data migration, just a real DB migration (`0025_roadmap_node_links_internal_page_type.sql`) updating the `roadmap_node_links_link_type_check` CHECK constraint's allowed value list, since `linkType` is DB-CHECK-constrained, not just a TypeScript-level union. The only behavioral difference from `external-*`: `RoadmapNodePanel` doesn't open it in a new tab (`target="_blank"` is gated on `linkType.startsWith("external")`, which `"internal-page"` doesn't match) — it's a same-site page, not a foreign one.
- **New roadmap: "Frontend Interview Cracking Roadmap"** (`frontend-interview-cracking`, `roadmapType: "role"`) — a guided tour of the platform itself, 31 nodes across 6 sections, every node linking to a real, verified FF page: **Learn the Fundamentals** (8 branches, one flagship concept per Learn category, `learn-concept`), **Practice What You Learn** (5 branches, one real challenge per Practice category, `practice-challenge`), **Ace the Interview** (3 real FF Collections questions via `interview-question`, plus Company Guides and Study Plans via `internal-page`), **Master System Design** (3 real System Design guides, `internal-page`), **Read the Playbook** (2 real chapters from 2 different playbooks, `internal-page`), **Sharpen Your Skills in the Playground** (UI Battles + Experiments, `internal-page`). Every referenced slug (challenge, collection question, guide, chapter, battle, experiment) confirmed real by reading the actual seed/content files first, not assumed. Deliberately scoped to content with a real destination page — Leaderboard/Gamification (Phase 8) isn't shipped yet, so no node points at it.
- **A real, reproducible bug hit and fixed during verification, not glossed over:** right after seeding, the roadmaps list page kept showing only the old roadmap set — traced to `getRoadmapSummaries`'s `unstable_cache` (tag `"roadmaps"`, 1hr revalidate) serving a stale catalog from before the reseed, and confirmed to be Turbopack's *filesystem* cache specifically (surviving a plain dev-server process restart, only clearing on a full `.next` wipe with the server fully stopped first — same class of issue as the dev-mode image-cache quirk already documented in `AGENTS.md`). A second, separate instance of the same root cause 404'd every Playbook chapter route specifically (`/interview-prep/playbook/[playbookSlug]/[chapterSlug]`) while every other route worked — verified the underlying `getPlaybookChapter` file-resolution logic was correct by running it standalone outside Next entirely (it worked), which isolated the bug to Turbopack's cache rather than the app code; a second full stop → wipe `.next` → restart cycle fixed it. **Full verification after the fix:** all 19 real link targets across both roadmaps (Playbook chapters, System Design guides, Company Guides, Study Plans, FF Collections questions, Experiments, Practice challenges) hit directly and confirmed 200, not just clicked-and-assumed; `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean; re-seeded twice with stable node ids; zero console/page errors in Playwright light/dark passes on both roadmaps.

**Fourth same-session follow-up: rename + reorder + FF branding, narrow branch nodes broadened to category/index level, and Frontend Developer + all 4 skill roadmaps gated behind "Coming soon."** Three separate, direct user requests handled together.

- **"Frontend Interview Cracking Roadmap" renamed to "Frontend Forever Roadmap"** — title only, slug kept as `frontend-interview-cracking` (renaming the slug would orphan the old DB row: `seed.ts` upserts roadmaps by slug and only prunes stale rows at the node level within a roadmap, never at the roadmap level itself). `orderIndex` swapped with Frontend Developer's (1 ↔ 2) so it now sorts first in the Role-based section — `getRoadmapSummaries` orders by `(roadmapType, orderIndex)`, no page-level change needed. New `FFLogoIcon` (`components/shared/FFLogoIcon.tsx`) — the navbar's own "FF" wordmark redrawn as a scalable SVG `<text>` glyph so it drops into the same icon slot every other roadmap card uses (devicon marks / lucide's `Code2`), mapped in `roadmapMeta.ts`.
- **Three branch groups broadened from specific items to category/index level — direct user feedback against a real screenshot ("why people should go through our all Learn concepts... it could be our learn category there"):** "Learn the Fundamentals" (8 branches, one specific flagship concept per Learn category, `learn-concept` link) → still 8 branches but each is now the **category itself** (`learnCategoryTopic()`, new helper) — title/description come from `CATEGORY_META`, and the link type changed to `internal-page` (not `learn-concept`) targeting the category's real entry point (`/learn/{category}/{firstConcept}`, the same URL the `/learn` hub's own `CategoryCard` uses — there's no standalone category-index route) specifically so the node doesn't misleadingly auto-complete off finishing just that one entry concept. "Master System Design" (3 branches, each one specific guide) → 1 branch, to the real index page (`/interview-prep/ff-system-design`, which groups all 28 guides under 5 dynamic sections with no per-section deep link anyway, so 3 branches to the same URL would have been redundant). "Read the Playbook" (2 branches, each one specific chapter from 2 of the 6 real playbooks) → 6 branches, one per real playbook (`PLAYBOOK_SLUGS.map(...)`, driven directly off `PLAYBOOK_META` so it can't drift from what the site actually has), each linking to that playbook's own index/chapter-list page, not one chapter buried inside it. "Practice What You Learn" and "Ace the Interview" were left untouched — not flagged by the user, and already point at real specific challenges/questions rather than one overclaimed narrow item. Net: roadmap grew from 31 to 33 nodes (27 topics + 6 sections); the now-unused `conceptTopic()` helper and its backing `CONCEPT_BY_SLUG` map were deleted (dead code once "Learn the Fundamentals" stopped using them).
- **Frontend Developer + all 4 skill roadmaps (JavaScript/CSS/React/TypeScript) marked "Coming soon"** — direct user request, only the Frontend Forever Roadmap ships live for now. Deliberately non-destructive: their seeded node content stays exactly as-is in `scripts/seed/roadmaps.ts` and the DB (nothing deleted), so flipping the new `COMING_SOON_ROADMAP_SLUGS` list (`features/roadmaps/lib/comingSoonRoadmaps.ts`) is the only step needed to launch them later. `RoadmapCard` shows a "Coming soon" pill (stacked below the title, not beside it — an inline placement was tried first and truncated every skill-roadmap title, e.g. "JavaScr...", caught via a Playwright screenshot before shipping) but stays a clickable `Link`, same as the existing `CollectionRow`/`CompanyGuideCard` "coming soon" precedent elsewhere in the app. `RoadmapSidebar`'s Related Roadmaps list gained the same pill (shortened to "Soon" for the narrower sidebar width). The detail page (`/roadmaps/[slug]/page.tsx`) renders a new `RoadmapComingSoonState` (same dashed-border empty-state language as `ChallengeEmptyState`) in place of the flow diagram/sidebar/FAQ entirely when the slug is gated — header (icon/title/description) still renders, but `Course`/`BreadcrumbList`/`FAQPage` JSON-LD and `RoadmapFaq` are skipped, and `generateMetadata` sets `robots: { index: false }` plus a "(Coming Soon)" title suffix so a still-under-construction page doesn't get indexed.
- **A real, reproducible bug hit again and fixed the same way as before:** right after reseeding, the `/roadmaps` list page kept showing the *old* title ("Frontend Interview Cracking Roadmap") and the *old* order (Frontend Developer first) — this time not cache corruption but ordinary `unstable_cache` staleness (tag `"roadmaps"`, 1hr revalidate, and nothing in this flow calls `revalidateTag` after a manual `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` run). Fixed the same way as the two previous instances this session: full stop (also found and killed an orphaned, parentless `next dev` process left over from an earlier turn, still holding port 3001 and eating RAM on this machine's tight 4GB total) → `.next` wipe → restart. Also caught mid-verification: an orphaned duplicate dev server from a prior turn was independently consuming memory and contributing to repeated `tsc --noEmit` OOM crashes on this machine — killing it let `tsc` complete clean.
- **Verified:** re-seeded twice (33/126/14/10/12/8 nodes across the 6 roadmaps, stable both runs); `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean; Playwright — list page (role/skill grouping, ordering, FF icon, "Coming soon" pills, no truncation) and both detail-page states (Frontend Forever's full flow diagram; Frontend Developer's Coming Soon empty state) in light and dark, zero console errors; every one of the 8 new category-level Learn links plus the System Design index and all 6 playbook index pages hit directly and confirmed 200, not just clicked-and-assumed.

**Fifth same-session follow-up: FF icon polish, "Coming soon" pill moved back inline, and the FAQ section rebuilt as a real accordion — three more direct requests against the running build.**

- **FF icon bigger + the navbar's actual color, not accent teal.** `RoadmapMeta` gained a real `iconClassName` field (full size+color string, e.g. `"h-6 w-6 text-text-primary"`) instead of every caller hardcoding `"h-5 w-5 text-accent"` inline — `frontend-interview-cracking` overrides to a bigger size and `text-text-primary` (the same token the navbar's own "FF" wordmark uses, near-black in light mode, near-white in dark — not a literal hardcoded black, which would've gone invisible against the dark-mode icon chip). Both `RoadmapCard` and the detail page's header now read this instead of hardcoding their own icon className.
- **"Coming soon" pill moved back onto the title's row, undoing the same-session stacked-pill fix.** The stacked layout (title, then pill on its own line below) was built specifically to stop the pill from truncating skill-roadmap titles ("JavaScript Roadmap" → "JavaScr...") — direct user request reverted that anyway, wanting the pill back on the right of the title like the original design. Truncation is back as a known, accepted tradeoff (flagged to the user, not silently fixed a second time).
- **`RoadmapFaq.tsx` rebuilt as a real accordion, matching a screenshot of roadmap.sh's own FAQ section** — was deliberately an always-visible plain Q&A before this (comment cited `build-plan.md`'s Feature 31 GEO/SEO crawlability rule against hidden-until-click text). Rebuilt using a CSS `grid-template-rows` collapse (`grid-rows-[0fr]` ↔ `grid-rows-[1fr]` with `overflow-hidden` on the inner wrapper) specifically so every answer's full text **stays in the DOM at all times** — never conditionally unmounted — which keeps the original crawlability property intact under a genuinely different visual presentation. First question defaults open (matches the reference screenshot); each row is a real `<button aria-expanded>` toggling local `useState`, `ChevronDown` rotates 180° open/closed. Section header ("Frequently Asked Questions") is its own bordered box above the list, same visual language as each question card.
- **User then asked why the Frontend Forever Roadmap's FAQ only had 2 questions, compared to roadmap.sh's own ~10-question FAQ in the reference screenshot.** Flagged before writing anything: the reference screenshot's questions ("Is Frontend Developer a good career?", "What are Frontend Developer salaries?", etc.) are generic frontend-*career* questions, which thematically belong on the Frontend Developer roadmap (the literal roadmap.sh mirror), not on the Frontend Forever Roadmap (the platform tour) — copying them onto the wrong roadmap would repeat the exact mismatch the user had already corrected earlier this session. Confirmed scope via `AskUserQuestion`: write new platform-specific questions for the Frontend Forever Roadmap only (not roadmap.sh's questions, not the other 4 gated roadmaps). Added 6 new FAQs to `roadmapSeoContent.ts`'s `"frontend-interview-cracking"` entry (kept the original 2), for 8 total: premium-gating scope (verified real — `challenges`/`collectionQuestions`/`roadmaps`/`studyPlans` all carry a real `isPremium` column, so the "most content is free, a few items are premium" claim is checked against the schema, not assumed), section ordering, no-fixed-length framing, the relationship to Study Plans, how per-node completion tracking actually works (`learn-concept`-linked nodes auto-track off `user_concept_progress`; everything else is a manual toggle once logged in, confirmed by reading `RoadmapNodePanel`'s actual `hasConceptLink` branch rather than assumed), and whether the roadmap alone is sufficient prep on its own.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean on every touched file; Playwright — FF icon bigger/black in both light and dark, "Coming soon" pill back inline (with the truncation tradeoff visible and confirmed intentional), accordion open/collapse interaction (default-open first question, click toggles independently, chevron rotates), all 8 FAQ questions render with zero console errors.

**Sixth same-session follow-up: a full pre-merge review pass, then a CodeRabbit round on the resulting push — both requested directly, not routine.**

- **Pre-push review** ("review our code, test it, check edge cases, check code quality" — direct request before pushing): `tsc`/`eslint`/a full production build all clean; re-verified seed idempotency and all 26 real link targets; Playwright across desktop/mobile/light/dark, logged-out toggle state, and unknown-slug 404 handling. Two real issues found and fixed: (1) the "coming soon" branch of `/roadmaps/[slug]/page.tsx` was still calling `getRoadmapDetail` (the full node/link join — 126 rows for Frontend Developer) just to discard the result, since the header only needs `title`/`description` — restructured to use the already-fetched `getRoadmapSummaries` catalog instead, and only calls `getRoadmapDetail` on the path that actually renders it; (2) that fix initially duplicated the header JSX between the two return branches — deduped into one local component (later extracted to its own file in the CodeRabbit round below). One small polish: `FFLogoIcon` given a defensive default size (`cn("h-5 w-5", className)`), matching the sibling devicon components' convention. **Committed and pushed** (commit `6c0228d`) to `feature/35-roadmap-detail-page`.
- **CodeRabbit review on the push — 11 findings addressed, 3 skipped with reason, all verified against current code before touching anything (explicit instruction: fix only still-valid issues, keep changes minimal).**
  - Fixed: 3 stale doc references to the long-gone pan/zoom "node-graph canvas" (`progress-tracker.md`'s status lines + checklist entry, `ui-registry.md`'s section heading) updated to describe the shipped static flow diagram; `ui-registry.md`'s description of the coming-soon gate updated to match the pre-push review's actual restructure (`isRoadmapComingSoon(slug)` computed before, not after, `getRoadmapDetail`).
  - Fixed: a real content bug — the CSS section's Flexbox topic had slug `flexbox-vs-grid` left over from an earlier draft, but its title/content is Flexbox-only (no Grid comparison) — renamed to `flexbox`.
  - Fixed: `RoadmapPageHeader` (the dedup from the pre-push round) moved out of the route file into `features/roadmaps/components/RoadmapPageHeader.tsx`, matching where every sibling component actually lives.
  - Fixed: `/api/roadmap-progress` was the one write route in this codebase that took a client-supplied row id (`nodeId`, hitting a real `uuid` column) with no UUID-shape validation — every sibling route that does the same thing (`interview-rating`, `interview-review-rating`, `progress`, `practice/discussion`, `practice/submit`) already validates against a local `UUID_RE` before querying; added the same guard here.
  - Fixed: `RoadmapSpineNode`'s `ref` prop was typed `React.Ref<HTMLDivElement>` but force-cast to `React.Ref<HTMLButtonElement>` for its button branch — a real type-safety hole. Retyped as `React.RefCallback<HTMLElement>` (the only kind of ref any caller actually passes) — `RefCallback`'s parameter position is contravariant, so it safely satisfies both the `<div>` and `<button>` JSX slots with no cast; a plain `Ref<HTMLElement>` doesn't work here because `RefObject`'s `current` field isn't contravariant the same way (confirmed by trying it first and watching `tsc` reject it).
  - Fixed: `useFlowConnectors`'s `window.addEventListener("resize", measure)` called the real DOM-measurement function (a `getBoundingClientRect()` per branch pill plus 2 `setState` calls) directly on every native resize event — a live resize drag can fire that dozens of times a second. Now coalesced through `requestAnimationFrame` (cancel-and-reschedule on each event), so at most one measurement happens per frame.
  - Fixed: `queries/detail.ts` had two blind `as RoadmapType`/`as RoadmapNodeType` casts on DB text columns (both real CHECK-constrained columns) — `summaries.ts`'s existing `parseRoadmapType` exported and reused, plus a new analogous `parseRoadmapNodeType` for the node-type column (summaries.ts doesn't actually have one of its own to reuse, despite the finding's phrasing — it never returns a raw `node_type` field at all, so this one was authored fresh, same pattern).
  - Fixed: `getCompletedNodeIds` compared completed concepts by `slug`, requiring an `innerJoin` back through `concepts` purely to translate `conceptId → slug` so it could be compared against the *other* query's already-slug-joined data. Added `conceptId` to the main query's link-row select (the FK was already there, just not projected) and switched the comparison to raw ids — removes the join entirely, compares by the real foreign-key relationship instead of a secondary unique attribute.
  - Fixed: `RoadmapNodePanel` (the hand-rolled Framer Motion drawer — "no side-drawer primitive exists in this codebase yet") had zero dialog semantics: no `role`/`aria-modal`, no focus moved on open, no Escape-to-close, no focus trap. Added all four — `role="dialog"` + `aria-modal` + `aria-labelledby` pointing at the title, a `useEffect` that focuses the panel on open and restores focus to whatever triggered it on close, an `Escape` keydown handler, and a manual Tab/Shift+Tab trap (queries focusable descendants, wraps at the first/last). Keyed on `node?.id`, not the `node` object itself, and reads `onClose` through a ref — both deliberate, to stop the effect from re-firing (and re-stealing focus) on unrelated parent re-renders, e.g. clicking "Mark as done" itself re-renders `RoadmapDetailView` with a fresh `node` object and a fresh `onClose` closure.
  - **Skipped, with reason — wrap `roadmap-progress`'s two `SELECT` reads in try/catch too:** checked 5 sibling write routes (`interview-rating`, `practice/submit`, etc.) and every one leaves its reads unwrapped, only wrapping the write step — matching that would mean deviating from an established, repeated convention, not following one. The actual crash-causing input (a malformed UUID reaching a `uuid`-typed column) is what the UUID-validation fix above removes. **This turned out to be a real, documented gap, not just a nitpick** — `error-handling.md` states every async call to an external service should be wrapped, and none of the 6 routes actually do that consistently; tracked as a standing "Known gap, deferred" note in Current Status above rather than patched reactively on just this one route.
  - **Skipped, with reason — replace `RoadmapNodePanel`'s `bg-black/30` overlay with "the project's semantic overlay/scrim token":** no such token exists anywhere in `ui-tokens.md` or `globals.css` — checked directly, the finding's premise doesn't hold. This exact hardcoded-black-with-opacity pattern is also already what the codebase's own shadcn/base-ui `Dialog` primitive uses for its own backdrop (`bg-black/10`, `components/ui/dialog.tsx`).
  - **Skipped, with reason — surface `RoadmapDetailView`'s toggle-failure via toast/inline feedback:** no toast mechanism exists anywhere in this codebase, and `CollectionQuestionListClient.tsx` (the sibling optimistic-toggle-with-rollback component) uses the exact same silent-revert-on-failure pattern already — building new UI infrastructure to fix a nitpick on one component would be inconsistent with its own closest sibling.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean project-wide after every fix (including confirming the ref-typing fix's contravariance reasoning actually compiles, not just assumed); `eslint` clean; re-seeded twice (stable node counts, `flexbox` slug rename applied cleanly); a full production build.

**Merged:** PR #100 opened (no `gh` CLI in this environment, opened manually by the user) and merged into `develop`; local `develop` fast-forwarded to the merge commit. Post-merge smoke test done — `tsc --noEmit` clean, full production `next build` clean (133 routes, `/roadmaps` and `/roadmaps/[slug]` both present), and a live dev-server sweep of the list page, the Frontend Forever Roadmap, all 5 gated "coming soon" roadmaps, and an unknown-slug 404 — all correct with no error boundaries. `feature/35-roadmap-detail-page` kept, not deleted, per this project's never-delete-a-merged-branch convention.
