The feature turned out to be **two separate navbar components**, not one component with conditional slots — the user clarified: (1) the homepage (`(main)/` pages) keeps the existing pill-based `Navbar` but replaces "Log In" with the user's avatar/dropdown when signed in; (2) `/learn` and all other `(app)/` pages get a completely new flat full-width `AppNavbar`, matching `designs/Learn-design.png`. One routing decision resolved up front: create the `(app)` route group layout now (ready for Features 20+) rather than defer it — no root layout restructuring needed since `(main)/layout.tsx` already isolated `Navbar`/`Footer`.
- New files: `src/hooks/useUser.ts` (wraps `authClient.useSession()`, returns `{ user, isLoading }`), `src/components/shared/UserDropdown.tsx` (avatar + `@base-ui/react/menu`-based dropdown shared by both navbars, with Profile → `/settings/profile`, Settings → `/settings`, ThemeToggle, and Sign Out items), `src/components/layout/AppNavbar.tsx` (flat full-width sticky header, see below), `src/app/(app)/layout.tsx` (mounts `AppNavbar`, future `(app)` routes inherit it automatically), `src/app/(app)/learn/page.tsx` (stub — "Learn experience coming soon."). **`Navbar.tsx` updated** — added `useUser()` and a conditional right-side cluster: logged-in shows `ThemeToggle` + `UserDropdown` (Follow on X hidden for logged-in users), logged-out keeps existing Follow on X + ThemeToggle + Log In; mobile menu has matching conditional logic; file stayed within the 200-line limit.
- **Critical implementation detail — `DropdownMenuTrigger` has no `asChild` prop:** the project's `dropdown-menu.tsx` wraps `@base-ui/react/menu` (not Radix UI), and `DropdownMenuTrigger` renders a `<button>` natively with no slot/`asChild` mechanism. First draft hit `Property 'asChild' does not exist on type 'IntrinsicAttributes & Props<unknown>'`. Fixed by removing `asChild` and placing `<Avatar>` directly as the child of `<DropdownMenuTrigger>` — the button wraps it natively. Any future component using `DropdownMenuTrigger` as a custom-element trigger must follow this same pattern.
- **`AppNavbar` structure:** `sticky top-0 z-50 border-b border-border bg-surface dark:bg-background`, height `h-14` (shorter than `Navbar`'s `h-16`). Left: "FF" logo + "Frontend Forever" wordmark (hidden below `xl:`) + inline text-only nav links (Explore/Learn/Roadmaps/Practice/Interview Prep/Leaderboard) with no pill container. Center: search trigger button with ⌘K kbd chip. Right cluster (gated on `!isLoading && user` — hidden entirely when logged out or session pending): `<Flame className="size-4 fill-streak stroke-none" />` + "0 day streak" + Bell with static error dot + `UserDropdown`. Mobile: hamburger menu with icon + label nav links; mobile right cluster mirrors the desktop gate. Active link: `text-accent`; inactive: `text-text-secondary hover:bg-surface-secondary hover:text-text-primary`. Streak/isPremium are placeholder values (0/false) — real profile data deferred to Feature 27.
- **`fill-streak stroke-none` for solid SVG fills:** Tailwind v4 auto-generates `fill-*`/`stroke-*` utilities from custom CSS variables the same way `bg-*`/`text-*` already work — confirmed in Feature 06's `PlatformGraph`. `fill-streak` fills the flame solid orange; `stroke-none` prevents any inherited stroke from the icon's path. Use this pattern for any lucide icon intended as a solid fill rather than a stroked outline.
- **`DropdownUser` type fix caught by TypeScript:** `image` was initially required (`string | null | undefined`), but Better-Auth's session user type has it optional — changed to `image?: string | null` to align.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean on all new/modified files. Playwright screenshots at desktop (1440px) and mobile (390px) × light/dark confirmed both navbars render correctly in both themes, AppNavbar right cluster correctly hidden when logged out, zero console errors across all states.
- **Follow-up (live-testing bugs, same session, before any PR):** three defects found by actually using both navbars in the running app:
  1. **`Navbar`: Follow on X incorrectly absent when logged in.** The initial build removed it from the logged-in cluster entirely — wrong. Fixed by pulling Follow on X and `ThemeToggle` outside the conditional entirely so they always render, and only toggling the last slot (`UserDropdown` vs Log In). This also fixed the refresh flash (bug 2) as a side-effect.
  2. **`Navbar`: Flash of Log In button on refresh.** While the session was resolving (`isLoading: true`), `user` was null, so the full logged-out cluster (Follow on X + ThemeToggle + Log In) rendered briefly then swapped to the logged-in cluster. Root cause: the entire right cluster was inside a single `user ? ... : ...` branch, so every element changed at once. Fix: Follow on X and ThemeToggle are now rendered unconditionally; only the third slot (`{!isLoading && (user ? <UserDropdown /> : <Link>Log In</Link>)}`) changes — the stable anchor elements never flash, and the swap from nothing → UserDropdown is far less jarring than the full cluster swap.
  3. **`AppNavbar`: Items too crowded and ⌘K misaligned.** Container gap raised to `gap-5`; nav links got `ml-10` (extra separation from the logo) + `gap-2` between items; right cluster gap raised to `gap-5` with `shrink-0` added. For the ⌘K kbd: the `⌘` Unicode character has different vertical font metrics than `K` in monospace, causing misalignment that neither `leading-none` nor `inline-flex items-center` could fix at the text-node level. Replaced `⌘` with lucide-react's `Command` icon (`size-2.5`, SVG) — a proper glyph that sits exactly level with the `K` span regardless of font.
- **Follow-up 2 (loading delay + sign-out UX, same session, before any PR):** two more defects found via continued live testing:
  1. **Login button and profile picture slow to appear (both logged-out and logged-in).** Root cause: `authClient.useSession()` is a client-side-only hook — it fires a network request to `/api/auth/get-session` on every page load. Until that round-trip completes, `isLoading` is `true` and the auth-dependent slot renders nothing, causing a visible ~100–500ms pop-in. Fix: call `auth.api.getSession({ headers: await headers() })` in the server layouts (`(main)/layout.tsx` and `(app)/layout.tsx`), which reads the session cookie directly from the request with zero network round-trip, and pass the result as `initialUser` down to both navbars. `useUser()` now accepts an optional `initialUser?: SessionUser | null` parameter — when provided (even as `null` for logged-out), `isLoading` is always `false` from the very first render, so the correct state paints immediately with no pop-in. `SessionUser` type exported from `useUser.ts`. Both layouts are now `async` Server Components. A `try/catch` around `auth.api.getSession()` ensures a DB error falls back to client-side loading (the original behavior) without crashing the page.
  2. **Sign-out button: icon invisible on hover; no visible feedback after clicking.** Two separate bugs: (a) `DropdownMenuItem`'s base class includes `not-data-[variant=destructive]:focus:**:text-accent-foreground`, which on hover forces ALL descendant SVGs to `accent-foreground` color, overriding the icon's inherited `text-error` and making it disappear against the `bg-error-muted` hover background. Fixed by using `variant="destructive"` on the item (which exempts it from that rule) and overriding the resulting destructive-color classes with the project's own `text-error`/`focus:bg-error-muted`/`focus:text-error` tokens, plus `[&_svg]:!text-error` to pin the icon's color with `!important` against any remaining descendant selector. (b) The `Loader2` spinner was placed _inside_ the menu item, but `@base-ui/react/menu` closes the popup the instant you click any item — before React re-renders with `isSigningOut: true`, so the spinner was never visible. Fixed by moving the loading state to the `DropdownMenuTrigger` (the avatar button) itself: when `isSigningOut` is true, the avatar fades to 30% opacity, a `Loader2 animate-spin` appears centered over it, and the trigger is `disabled` so the menu can't be reopened while the sign-out request is in flight. The avatar is always visible in the navbar even after the dropdown closes, so the feedback is seen immediately and lasts until `router.push("/")` navigates away.
- **Follow-up (security hardening, after Feature 17 push, before PR):** addressed 5 security/quality gaps found during a post-push review of the auth feature as a whole — none were regressions introduced by Feature 17, all were pre-existing gaps now closed before the PR goes out: 1. **Login redirect guard** — `src/app/(auth)/login/page.tsx` is now an `async` Server Component that calls `auth.api.getSession()` in a try/catch and redirects logged-in users to `/` before rendering. Deferred from Feature 15 → Feature 17 scope; now done. Redirect target is `/` (homepage) by product decision — `/learn` is still a stub. 2. **`src/lib/env.ts` created** — typed wrapper for all env vars (`databaseUrl`, `betterAuthSecret`, `betterAuthUrl`, `googleClientId/Secret`, `githubClientId/Secret`, `upstashRedisUrl/Token`). Has `import "server-only"` at the top, which causes a build-time error if any Client Component ever accidentally imports it — secrets can't leak into the client bundle. `code-standards.md` always documented this file; it was never actually created until now. 3. **`src/lib/db.ts` and `src/lib/auth/server.ts` updated** — both now import `env` from `@/lib/env` and use `env.databaseUrl` / `env.googleClientId` / etc. instead of raw `process.env.*`. The explicit `if (!process.env.DATABASE_URL)` startup guard in `db.ts` was removed (ownership moved to `env.ts`). 4. **`src/lib/auth/client.ts` — added `"use client"` directive** — `createAuthClient()` uses browser APIs; without the directive a Server Component importing it would crash at runtime with no clear error. Now fails at build time instead. 5. **HTTP security headers added to `next.config.ts`** — all 6 headers from `security.md` now ship on every response: `X-DNS-Prefetch-Control`, `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` (clickjacking), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` (MIME sniffing), `Referrer-Policy`, `Permissions-Policy`, `Content-Security-Policy`. Also restored conditional `allowedDevOrigins` via `NEXT_ALLOWED_DEV_ORIGINS` env var (was manually reverted to a hardcoded IP locally). **Real bug hit during implementation:** the initial CSP used `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-eval'` without `'unsafe-inline'`, which blocked Next.js's inline hydration scripts — all sections using Framer Motion's `whileInView` (which starts at `opacity: 0`) became permanently invisible because React never hydrated. Fixed by adding `'unsafe-inline'` to `script-src`. The proper long-term fix is nonce-based CSP via `proxy.ts`; noted in a comment in `next.config.ts`. `frame-src 'none'` is correct for now and must be updated to `'self'` when the code-execution sandbox iframe is added (Features 24–25). - `context/architecture.md` updated: `env.ts` added to the `lib/` folder listing; `Auth + DB Client Patterns` code examples updated to show `env.googleClientId/Secret` and `env.databaseUrl` instead of the old raw `process.env.*` calls; `client.ts` note updated to mention `"use client"` directive.
  **Last completed:** 18 Database Tables — ran the `architect` skill before building. Six decisions resolved with the user up front: (1) `user_id` FK type is `text` (not `uuid`) on all user-owned tables — must match `profiles.id`'s physical Postgres column type for FK validation; (2) schema files split into two grouped files (`content.ts`: concepts, challenges, interview_questions, roadmaps, roadmap_steps; `user-data.ts`: user_concept_progress, xp_events, user_challenge_submissions, user_interview_reviews, bookmarks) rather than 10 individual files, mirroring the meaningful content/user-activity boundary; (3) seeding via a separate re-runnable `scripts/seed.ts` (not baked into migrations); (4) Drizzle relations defined for all 10 tables now, not deferred; (5) unique constraints added on `(user_id, concept_id)` for `user_concept_progress`, `(user_id, question_id)` for `user_interview_reviews`, and three partial unique indexes on `bookmarks` (one per entity type, with `WHERE col IS NOT NULL`); (6) all new timestamps use `{ withTimezone: true }` (architecture.md's `timestamptz`) — the existing `profiles.ts` timestamp columns were also migrated to `timestamptz` at the same time (safe: `premium_expires_at` was NULL for all users, `created_at`/`updated_at` values are UTC and convert cleanly).
- New files: `src/lib/schema/content.ts`, `src/lib/schema/user-data.ts`, `scripts/seed.ts`. Updated: `src/lib/schema/profiles.ts` (timestamp fix + `many()` back-references to user-data tables), `src/lib/schema/index.ts` (re-exports both new files).
- Migration `drizzle/0003_lively_swordsman.sql` generated and applied — 10 new tables created, `profiles` timestamps altered. Verified via `information_schema.tables`: all 10 tables present.
- Seed results (confirmed via direct `COUNT(*)` queries): 8 concepts (one per category: javascript-runtime/browser-internals/react/css/typescript/accessibility/performance/system-design), 3 challenges (debounce/virtual-list/specificity-calculator), 20 interview questions (5 per collection across ff-75/ff-javascript/ff-react/ff-system-design), 2 roadmaps + 8 roadmap steps. All seed content is original, specific to real platform concepts, and re-runnable via `onConflictDoNothing()`.
- Circular import pattern: `user-data.ts` imports from `profiles.ts`; `profiles.ts` imports from `user-data.ts`. Safe because Drizzle's `relations()` callbacks are arrow functions called lazily (after all modules are fully loaded), not at import time — the standard Drizzle cross-file relations pattern.
- **Follow-up (`/review`, then fixed all 4 findings, same session, before any PR):** ran the project's `review` skill against the finished feature. Found 4 issues, all fixed:
  1. **Important — seed not idempotent for `interview_questions`**: `onConflictDoNothing()` was called without a target, and the table had no unique constraint beyond the UUID PK — so duplicate questions would be inserted on every re-run. Fixed by: (a) adding `unique("interview_questions_collection_order_unique").on(table.collection, table.orderIndex)` to the table definition in `content.ts`; (b) changing the seed's insert to `onConflictDoNothing({ target: [interviewQuestions.collection, interviewQuestions.orderIndex] })`. Re-run confirmed 0 new questions on the second pass.
  2. **Important — no CHECK constraints**: all `text` columns acting as enums (`category`, `difficulty`, `collection`, `event_type`, `status`) had no DB-level validation — any out-of-range string would insert silently. Fixed by adding `check()` constraints to both schema files using `sql.join(ARRAY.map(v => sql.raw(\`'${v}'\`)), sql\`, \`)`to build`IN (...)`clauses dynamically from the typed constant arrays — so the SQL values stay in sync with the TypeScript types automatically. Migration`drizzle/0004_quiet_rafael_vega.sql` generated and applied (8 CHECK constraints + 1 UNIQUE).
  3. **Important — no shared constants file**: enum values were repeated as raw strings in the schema and seed, with no single source of truth. Created `src/lib/constants.ts` exporting typed `as const` arrays and union types for: `CONCEPT_TABS`, `CONCEPT_CATEGORIES`, `CONCEPT_DIFFICULTIES`, `CHALLENGE_DIFFICULTIES`, `INTERVIEW_COLLECTIONS`, `XP_EVENT_TYPES`, `CHALLENGE_STATUSES`. Schema files and seed now import from this file — one place to change, everything stays in sync.
  4. **Minor — seed used `as const` on data arrays, causing type-assertion workarounds**: `CONCEPTS`, `CHALLENGES`, `INTERVIEW_QUESTIONS`, `ROADMAPS` were declared `as const`, making every nested array readonly. The insert types expected mutable arrays, leading to a `[...ch.testCases]` spread cast — a disguised type assertion. Fixed by removing all `as const` from the seed arrays and replacing them with explicit type annotations (`ConceptSeed[]`, `ChallengeSeed[]`, etc.) — TypeScript infers the literal types from the definitions without needing casts, and all 22+ `difficulty: "easy" as const` suffixes were stripped as redundant. Typecheck (`npx tsc --noEmit`) clean after the fix.
- **Follow-up (CodeRabbit PR review findings, same session, after PR):** verified all 9 findings against current code; fixed 5, skipped 4:
  - Fixed: (1) `roadmap_steps` — added `unique("roadmap_steps_roadmap_order_unique").on(roadmapId, orderIndex)` to schema; migration `drizzle/0005_nosy_dormammu.sql` generated and applied (8 duplicate rows from pre-constraint seed runs were deduplicated before the ALTER); (2) `bookmarks` — added `bookmarks_exactly_one_target_check` CHECK enforcing exactly one of `concept_id`/`question_id`/`challenge_id` is non-null; (3) seed — `roadmapSteps` insert now uses `onConflictDoNothing({ target: [roadmapSteps.roadmapId, roadmapSteps.orderIndex] })` with an explicit target; (4) `UserDropdown` sign-out — timeout path now resets `isSigningOut` so the user can retry, rather than navigating away; (5) `useUser` — `initialUser` fallback is now only used while `isPending`; after session resolves, returns `null` if no live session (prevents stale-after-logout auth UI).
  - Skipped: (a) migration USING clauses — already applied, data was correct; (b) mojibake in seed.ts — confirmed only in comment decorators (`// ───`), not in seed data strings; (c) `DevAbortSuppressor` location — `components/providers/` is correct (same as `ThemeProvider`); (d) circular `profiles` ↔ `user-data` import — safe Drizzle pattern; all cross-references are inside lazy `relations()` callbacks and `.references(() => ...)` thunks, never accessed synchronously at module load time; no TDZ error has ever occurred.
- Pushed `feature/18-database-tables` to origin; PR #30 → `develop` merged (merge commit `7bdaae1`). Local `develop` fast-forwarded to match.
  **Last completed:** 19 Upstash Redis Setup — ran the `architect` skill (manually) before building. Resolved 3 decisions up front: (1) `lib/upstash.ts` exports both `redis` (`new Redis({ url: env.upstashRedisUrl, token: env.upstashRedisToken })`) and `ratelimit` (`slidingWindow(20, '10 s')`, `analytics: true`) — two exports so Better-Auth can consume the raw client while app routes call `ratelimit.limit()` directly, each hitting the same underlying Redis instance; (2) Better-Auth's deferred auth rate limiting from Feature 14 included in this feature — wired `secondaryStorage` (Upstash adapter with `get`/`set`/`delete`) + `rateLimit: { enabled: true, window: 60, max: 5, storage: "secondary-storage" }` matching `security.md`'s `/api/auth/*: 5 req / 60s` table, plus `session.storeSessionInDatabase: true` to keep sessions in Postgres as before rather than migrating to Redis-only; (3) created a minimal stub at `src/app/api/progress/route.ts` (POST: auth check → rate limit check → 501 Not Implemented) so the limiter has a real surface to test against rather than shipping untested infrastructure.
- New files: `src/lib/upstash.ts` (exports `redis` + `ratelimit`), `src/app/api/progress/route.ts` (stub — auth + rate limit + 501). Modified: `src/lib/auth/server.ts` (added `secondaryStorage`, `rateLimit`, `session.storeSessionInDatabase: true`).
- All Upstash packages (`@upstash/ratelimit ^2.0.8`, `@upstash/redis ^1.38.0`) and env vars (`env.upstashRedisUrl`/`env.upstashRedisToken` in `lib/env.ts`, CSP `connect-src https://*.upstash.io` in `next.config.ts`) were already in place from prior features — no new dependencies or config changes needed.
- Verified end-to-end: 22 parallel authenticated requests to `POST /api/progress` — 20 returned 501 (passed rate limit), 2 returned 429 (blocked). Rate limiter confirmed working against live Upstash instance.
- **Follow-up (`/review`, then fixed both findings, same session, before any PR):** ran the project's `review` skill against the finished feature. Layer 1 and Layer 3 passed clean. Layer 2 found 2 issues, both fixed: 1. **Important** — `Redis.fromEnv()` in `lib/upstash.ts` bypassed `lib/env.ts`, violating `code-standards.md`'s explicit rule ("Never access `process.env` directly in feature code — always import from `lib/env.ts`"). `env.upstashRedisUrl`/`env.upstashRedisToken` were already typed in `lib/env.ts` for exactly this purpose but were unused. Fixed by replacing `Redis.fromEnv()` with `new Redis({ url: env.upstashRedisUrl, token: env.upstashRedisToken })`. 2. **Minor** — `app/api/progress/route.ts` used `await headers()` from `next/headers` for the session check instead of `req.headers`, deviating from the pattern both `security.md` and `code-standards.md` establish. Left `req: Request` declared but unused. Fixed by switching to `req.headers` and removing the `next/headers` import. - Re-verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after both fixes.

Solved by wrapping `getCategorySummaries` in `React.cache()` — both calls hit the same cache entry, only one actual DB query fires per request.

- **Follow-up (multiple sidebar design iterations, same session, against `designs/Learn-design.png`):** user reviewed screenshots against the reference design and gave a series of feedback rounds. All changes landed in `LearnSidebar.tsx`, `CategoryAccordionItem.tsx`, `CategoryCard.tsx`, `categoryMeta.ts`, and `learn/layout.tsx`: 1. **Header:** removed concept count subtitle ("8 concepts across 8 topics") — header is now just the uppercase muted "LEARN" label (`text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-wider text-text-muted`), no border-b, increased top padding (`pt-5`). 2. **Bottom section added** (`border-t`): four nav links (Settings/Theme/Feedback/Changelog) + an Overall Progress block + profile row — all below the scrollable category list. Theme link is a `<button>` calling `useTheme().toggleTheme()` with Sun/Moon icon swap (real dark/light mode toggle, not a stub). "Overall Progress" label is `font-bold`. Progress bar uses `role="progressbar"` aria attrs. "View learning stats →" links to `/learn/stats`. 3. **Profile row with dropdown:** `learn/layout.tsx` now extracts `user: DropdownUser | null` from the session and passes it to `LearnSidebar`. Sidebar renders a full-width profile row (Avatar + name/email + ChevronDown) only when `user !== null`. Dropdown uses `DropdownMenu` with `side="top"` — items navigate via `onClick + router.push()` (no `asChild`, per the project's established `@base-ui/react` constraint). `DropdownUser` type imported from `UserDropdown.tsx` without touching that file. 4. **Badge/icon system for JS and TS** (`categoryMeta.ts`): added `badge?: string` and `badgeStyle?: { bg: string; text: string }` to `CategoryMeta`. JS: `badge: "JS"`, `badgeStyle: { bg: "bg-[#FFFF00]", text: "text-[#111827]" }` — pure bright yellow (`#FFFF00`), near-black text hardcoded (not theme-following, so text stays readable on bright yellow in both light and dark mode). TS: `badge: "TS"`, `badgeStyle: { bg: "bg-info", text: "text-accent-foreground" }` — solid blue, `accent-foreground` is theme-invariant `#FFFFFF`. **Bug class avoided:** `bg-info` is not overridden in `.dark {}` (stays solid blue), and `text-accent-foreground` is the one token never redefined in dark mode — white text stays readable in both themes by construction, same theme-invariant-foreground pattern established in Feature 08. 5. **Icon changes** (`categoryMeta.ts`): Accessibility icon changed from `Eye` to `Lock`. colorKey updated: javascript-runtime→`xp`, typescript→`info`, system-design→`premium`. 6. **Status circles** (`CategoryAccordionItem.tsx`): each concept link now has a status circle on the left (replaces the old right-side `<Check>`). Three states driven by `isActive` and `concept.isCompleted`: active = `bg-text-primary` + white Check, completed = `bg-success` + white Check, not started = `border border-border bg-transparent` (empty). Premium `<Lock>` icon remains on the right. 7. **`badgeStyle` propagated to `CategoryCard.tsx`** — same `badgeStyle.bg`/`badgeStyle.text` logic for the icon slot in the grid cards, so JS and TS cards match the sidebar. - All changes typecheck/lint clean throughout.

The left `LearnSidebar` comes for free from the existing `learn/layout.tsx` (Feature 20) since the concept route nests inside it.

  **Last completed:** 22 Understand Tab — ran the `architect` skill before building. Four decisions resolved with the user, all the recommended option: (1) author **Event Loop only** as the reference MDX + a "guide coming soon" empty state for unauthored concepts (authoring 8 guides is content work, not engineering); (2) implement a **minimal real `/api/progress`** understand-write now (Feature 27 layers XP/streak/bonus on top); (3) the 4 info cards come from **MDX frontmatter** fields; (4) the "Mark as understood" button is **visible logged-out and redirects to `/login`** on click.
- **Architecture decision — server MDX injected into the client switcher:** the Understand tab renders MDX via `next-mdx-remote/rsc`'s `<MDXRemote>` (server-only, needs `fs`), but the tab-switcher `ConceptInteractive` is a client component. Resolved with the same children-as-props pattern as the header/rail: `page.tsx` builds `<UnderstandTab>` on the server and passes it as an `understandContent: ReactNode` prop down through `ConceptPageShell` → `ConceptInteractive`, which renders it for the Understand tab (other tabs keep `TabPlaceholder`). Keeps MDX/`fs` off the client bundle. `TAB_BODY` retyped to `Record<Exclude<ConceptTab,"understand">, …>` so the placeholder map no longer carries a dead understand entry (the `=== "understand"` ternary narrows `activeTab` for the else branch).
- New files: `src/lib/mdx.ts` (`import "server-only"`; `getConceptContent(category, slug)` returns `ConceptContent | null` so a missing file yields the empty state instead of throwing; `getAllConceptSlugs()` for a future `generateStaticParams`), `content/concepts/javascript-runtime/event-loop.mdx` (frontmatter incl. `whatsHappening`/`keyInsight`/`memoryHook`/`inRealLife` + prose; frontmatter `slug`/`category`/`difficulty` match the seeded DB row), and under `concept/`: `mdxComponents.tsx` (token-styled MDX element map — no hardcoded colors), `InfoCards.tsx` (4 frontmatter-driven callouts, hue-token icons), `MarkUnderstoodButton.tsx` (client — logged-out→`/login`, POST `/api/progress`, 429/error/saved states), `UnderstandTab.tsx` (server — MDX + cards + button, or the empty state). New query `getUnderstoodState(userId, conceptId)` (live, per-user, not cached) for the button's initial state.
- `/api/progress/route.ts` — was a 501 stub (auth + rate-limit only). Implemented the minimal write: parse/validate `{ conceptId, tab }` (tab guarded against `CONCEPT_TABS`), then `onConflictDoUpdate` the matching `{tab}Completed` flag scoped to `session.user.id` via a type-safe `COMPLETED_SET: Record<ConceptTab, Partial<InferInsertModel>>` (no `as`, no dynamic-key insert). Still **no** XP/streak/fully-completed logic — that's Feature 27. Order is auth → rate-limit → validate → write, per `code-standards.md`.
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npx eslint` clean; event-loop Understand renders the full MDX guide + 4 info cards + button (light **and** dark — DOM-asserted "The single thread"/"What's Happening?"/button present, `[role=tabpanel]` innerHTML ~10.5KB, zero console errors); a concept without MDX (`css/css-specificity`) shows "Guide coming soon"; `/api/progress` returns **401** for an unauthenticated POST (warm 0.34s); a logged-out "Mark as understood" click navigates to `/login`. **Verification caught a false alarm:** a first dark-mode screenshot showed an empty body — re-checked with `networkidle` + a longer wait and the content was all present; it was a premature capture before `MDXRemote`'s RSC stream painted (same streaming/cold-compile artifact class as Feature 09), not a real bug.

  **Last completed:** 23 Simulate Tab — Full Simulators — ran the `architect` skill before building. Key reframe surfaced during homework: the 4 hero simulators (Features 09-12) already have the _full_ control set, so "full version" ≠ "more controls". The real deltas are (a) mount each simulator in the concept page's Simulate tab, (b) **scenario switching**, (c) **completion tracking** — matching Feature 09's own note that "Feature 23 adds scenario switching on top of the same engine." Built in two slices. Four decisions resolved with the user up front (all the recommended option): (1) **reuse the same components + props** (`SimulatorRootProps`: `showScenarioSwitcher?`, `onReachedEnd?`), not separate "full" components — the homepage Hero keeps its single-scenario, untracked behaviour by simply rendering `<XSimulator />` with no props; (2) **2 scenarios each** (existing + 1 new authored); (3) completion **auto-fires on the final frame, silent for logged-out** (no POST, no redirect), subtle "Simulator completed" indicator on success; (4) the 4 concepts without a simulator show a **"coming soon" empty state**. Scenario-2 content also confirmed up front per the project's simulator-semantics norm: Event Loop → **async/await**, React Rendering → **wasted child re-render** (see follow-up below — originally state batching), Browser Pipeline → **visibility:hidden** (contrast with scenario 1's display:none), CSS Specificity → **!important**.
- **Integration seam — the learn↔simulators boundary:** `features/learn` may **not** import `features/simulators` (the "features never import other features" invariant). Resolved exactly like the homepage Hero (which lives in `components/` and imports the simulators): new `src/components/simulators/ConceptSimulator.tsx` (a `components/`-layer client wrapper) owns the slug→simulator registry, the completion POST, and the empty state; the server `page.tsx` builds it and passes it down as a `simulateContent: ReactNode` prop through `ConceptPageShell` → `ConceptInteractive` (same children-as-props pattern Feature 22 used for `understandContent`). Simulators stay network-free (an architecture invariant) — they only expose a generic `onReachedEnd` lifecycle callback; all I/O (the `/api/progress` POST, fire-once via a ref, logged-out guard, 429/error handling mirroring `MarkUnderstoodButton`) lives in the host wrapper. `/api/progress` already accepted `tab:"simulate"` since Feature 22, so no API change was needed; added `getSimulateState(userId, conceptId)` (live, per-user) for the initial completed state.
- **Slice 1 (tab live):** added `onReachedEnd` to all 4 hooks (fired once via a ref-synced effect when `currentStep === totalSteps`; the naive in-render ref write tripped this repo's `react-hooks/refs` lint, so the sync moved into an effect) + the 4 roots; built `ConceptSimulator` + wired it through the page. This alone makes every Simulate tab functional with the existing scenario.
- **Slice 2 (scenario switching):** new shared `ScenarioSwitcher.tsx` (pill group matching `ConceptSwitcherTabs`, renders nothing for <2 scenarios). Each simulator's `data/scenarios.ts` became a `SCENARIOS`/`*_SCENARIOS` array (id/label/frames + the per-scenario static data); each hook gained `scenarios`/`activeScenarioId`/`setScenario` (resets to step 1, derives `totalSteps`/`frame` from the active scenario). The real work was that **each simulator bakes scenario-1 specifics into its visuals**, so authoring scenario 2 meant data-driving them, not just adding frames: Event Loop's hardcoded `CodePanel` JSX was tokenized into a `CodeLine[]`/`CodeToken` model + per-scenario `executionOrderByOutput` map + scenario-driven insight (its `consoleOutput` widened from the closed `ConsoleOutputToken` union to `string[]`, lookups guarded); CSS Specificity threaded `selectors`/`winner`/`elementHtml` through `StageCardsRow` to the 4 stage bodies + an `important?` flag rendering an `!important` badge (the lower-specificity `.primary` visibly winning over `#cta`); Browser Pipeline threaded `htmlSource`/`cssSource`/3 trees/`layoutBoxes`/`pageBoxes`/caption/insight through `ParseTracksRow` + `PipelineStagesRow` (added `hidden?` to `TreeNode`/`LayoutBox` + a `PageBox` model so the visibility:hidden element stays in the render tree / reserves a layout box / paints blank, the literal contrast with scenario 1); React Rendering started cheapest (no module-constant imports) — added `targetCount` + scenario `captionSubtexts`, threaded `count` into `VirtualDomBody`/`DiffingBody` (later reworked, see follow-up).
- Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npx eslint` clean across all touched files; Playwright (dev server, desktop 1440px): homepage hero still renders all 4 simulators with **no** scenario switcher (props omitted); all 4 concept Simulate tabs load (200) with a 2-pill switcher; switching to scenario 2 + stepping shows the correct authored frames (async/await "B executes", visibility:hidden "aside.ad is kept" with the node tagged `hidden` in the render tree, `!important` ".primary wins" with the badge and `.primary 0-1-0 ✓` beating `#cta 1-0-0`); a non-simulator concept (`typescript/type-narrowing`) shows the "coming soon" empty state. The only console error was a single Better-Auth `/api/auth/*` **429** after the test hammered ~7 protected pages in 10s (the documented 5/60s auth limit, not Feature 23 code — that page makes no progress POST and the run was logged-out). Caught and fixed one issue during visual review: the Browser Pipeline insight callout was still hardcoded to scenario 1 — made it scenario-driven like the others.
- **Follow-up (React scenario 2 redesign, same session, before any PR):** user reviewed the first React scenario 2 (**state batching**) and rejected it as too similar to scenario 1 — it walked the exact same 5 cards (Counter → Render → VDOM → Diffing → Real DOM) with the only on-screen difference being the count reading `2` instead of `1`; the "batching" idea lived entirely in caption text, so a learner couldn't _see_ the difference. Offered three genuinely-distinct replacements (with previews of each end state); user chose **"wasted child re-render"** — a Parent's state change re-runs both Parent and Child render functions, but diffing skips the unchanged Child and only Parent's DOM is patched (teaches render ≠ commit). This is the most visually distinct option because the lesson is about _components_ re-running, not HTML element diffing, so it needed a new visual vocabulary rather than reusing the counter trees. - Added a `kind: "dom-update" | "wasted-render"` discriminator + `componentLabel`/`stateLabel`/`clickLabel`/`childLabel`/`insight` to `RenderScenario`; scenario 1 keeps the counter `MiniTree` bodies untouched (zero regression risk), scenario 2 swaps the four downstream cards to a new `ComponentTreeView.tsx` (a Parent→Child node pair whose per-card `mode` — rerender/vdom/diff/commit — drives the emphasis: both nodes "re-rendered" in render/vdom, then the Child visibly falls away as "no change"/"untouched" in diff/commit). `StageCardsRow` branches its `bodies` map on `kind`; `ComponentStateBody` took label props (now "Parent Component"/"likes"/"Like"); the React `InsightCallout` + `ExecutionFlowBar`'s first pill ("Click Like") became scenario-driven. The earlier `targetCount`/`count`-into-trees threading from the batching pass stayed (scenario 1 still uses it). - Re-verified: `tsc`/`eslint` clean; Playwright (warmed dev route, `networkidle` to ensure hydration before clicking the tab) stepped scenario 2 to the diffing + commit frames — screenshots confirm Render Phase shows Parent **and** Avatar both "re-rendered", Diffing highlights only Parent as "changed" with Avatar faded "no change", Real DOM commit patches only Parent ("Only Parent patched") with Avatar "untouched", the insight reads "Re-rendering is not the same as updating the DOM", and the flow bar's first pill reads "Click Like"; zero console errors. (Note: the initial debug run used `domcontentloaded` and clicked the tab before hydration, so it read the still-mounted Understand panel — a test-timing artifact, fixed by waiting for `networkidle`, not an app bug.) - **Follow-up 2 (visual rework of the same scenario, same session):** user said the first `ComponentTreeView` pass (abstract labelled boxes + small text status badges) "is not good" and asked to make it genuinely visual. Reworked the two nodes into concrete UI: **Post** is a little card with a `Heart` icon + a "likes" count (the one thing that ever changes on screen), **Avatar** is a profile circle (`User` glyph) rendered **pixel-identical in every card** — only its emphasis ring/dim changes, never its content, which is the lesson made visible. Per-card visuals: Render Phase shows both nodes pulsing with a "⚡ render() ran" tag (both functions executed); Virtual DOM shows both as a fresh snapshot; **Diffing is now an explicit before→after diff** — `❤0 → ❤1 changed` (streak) on the Post row vs `👤 = 👤 ✓ same` (muted) on the Avatar row; Real DOM (commit) pops the Post's count to 1 ("DOM patched") while the Avatar sits faded ("DOM untouched"). Verified via the same Playwright flow — screenshots of the render/diff/commit frames confirm the concrete UI reads clearly at the card's narrow width; `tsc`/`eslint` clean, zero console errors.
  **Last completed:** 24 Challenge Tab — ran the `architect` skill before building. Homework surfaced two facts that shaped the whole feature: (a) the seeded `challenges.test_cases` are **descriptive labels, not executable code**, so a real "Run Tests" needs executable assertions that didn't exist; (b) **no challenge was linked to a concept** (`ChallengeSeed` had no `conceptId`), so the Challenge tab had nothing to render per concept. Three decisions resolved with the user up front: (1) **real iframe sandbox + real executable tests** (the engine is reused by Build/Practice later), not UI-first cosmetic; (2) **link all 3 seeded challenges to concepts + empty states elsewhere** (`implement-debounce`→event-loop, `virtual-list`→react-rendering, `specificity-calculator`→css-specificity), premium-gate seam stubbed for F38; (3) **JS-only sandbox now, re-scope `virtual-list`** to test the pure windowing function (`visibleRange(...)` — the real lesson) instead of mounting React, deferring the heavier React/JSX-in-iframe engine to F26. Test-spec storage (not asked, established-pattern default): DB `test_cases` stay display labels; executable assertions live in an in-code registry keyed by slug (off the client until needed), same precedent as F22's server-MDX / F23's slug→simulator registry.
- **Sandbox engine** — new self-contained `src/features/practice/sandbox/`: `buildSandboxDoc.ts` (builds the iframe `srcdoc` — a self-clearing timeout guard + `error` listener, the user code in its own `<script>` so a syntax error there can't stop the harness and its declarations become globals, then an authored-tests runner that posts one `RESULT`; injects `assert`/`assertEqual`/deep-`eq`/`delay` helpers; escapes `</script>` in user code; every message carries a per-run `nonce`), `runInSandbox.ts` (creates a hidden `sandbox="allow-scripts"` iframe — never `allow-same-origin` — listens on `message` filtered by nonce, 6s parent timeout > the inner 5s guard, always tears down before resolving, resolves once and never rejects), `useSandbox.ts` (React wrapper: `idle|running|done` status, last result, `run()` returns the result + ignores re-entrant runs), `testSpecs.ts` (`getTestSpec(slug)` → `SandboxTest[]`, labels matched 1:1 to the seeded `test_cases[].label`), `types.ts`, `index.ts`. Follows `security.md` → Code Execution Security exactly (allow-scripts only, postMessage only, timeout). Known limitation (inherent to the iframe approach `security.md` prescribes, not a Worker): a synchronous infinite loop in user code can't be interrupted by the inner `setTimeout` guard — acceptable for short challenge code; the parent timeout still resolves the UI.
- **Challenge UI** — `src/features/practice/components/`: `ChallengeEditor` (Monaco per library-docs — `'use client'`, always `theme="vs-dark"`, minimap off, explicit pixel height, dark pulsing skeleton via the Editor `loading` prop), `TestResultsPanel` (spec rows with idle/running/pass/fail status icons, `X/Y passing` summary, run-level error banner, per-test error text), `HintsPanel` (collapsible `AnimatePresence` height animation + **progressive** one-at-a-time reveal from `hints[]`), `SolutionPanel` (locked → reveal button → read-only dark code block), `ChallengePrompt` (title + difficulty badge + description with a tiny inline-`code` renderer — splits on backticks, no HTML injection), `ChallengeStates` (`ChallengeEmptyState` "coming soon" + `ChallengePremiumLocked` seam). Integration host `src/components/challenge/ConceptChallenge.tsx` lives in `components/` (not `features/learn`) so it can import `features/practice` — same `components/` → `features/` boundary the Hero and `ConceptSimulator` already use; owns editor state, attempt count, the fire-once completion `POST /api/progress { tab:"challenge" }` (ref-guarded, logged-out-silent — mirrors `ConceptSimulator`), and the solution gate (unlocks on pass or 3 attempts, attempts tracked client-side).
- **Seed** (`scripts/seed.ts`): added `conceptSlug?` to `ChallengeSeed`, resolved it to `conceptId` at insert time, and switched the challenge insert from `onConflictDoNothing` to `onConflictDoUpdate` (via `excluded.*`) so a re-run **backfills** `conceptId` onto rows seeded before they were concept-linked while staying idempotent on slug. Re-authored `virtual-list` from a JSX `VirtualList` component to a pure `visibleRange(scrollTop, rowHeight, containerHeight, totalRows, overscan)` function (starter/solution/`test_cases`/hints all updated). Re-ran the seed (3 upserted, no missing-concept warnings).
- **Wiring:** `page.tsx` fetches `getChallengeByConceptId` (cached) + `getChallengeState` (live per-user) + `getIsPremiumUser` (premium seam), computes `isPremiumLocked = challenge.isPremium && !isPremiumUser` (always false today), builds `challengeContent` and threads it through `ConceptPageShell` → `ConceptInteractive` (same children-as-props pattern as F22/F23); the Challenge entry was removed from `ConceptInteractive`'s placeholder `TAB_BODY` map.
- **Required CSP change:** `next.config.ts` had `frame-src 'none'` with a comment literally reserving it for "Features 24-25". Updated to `frame-src 'self'` (allows the srcdoc sandbox frame). No `script-src` change needed — it already carries `'unsafe-inline'` (Next.js hydration), which the srcdoc's inline harness inherits. Headers are production-only (dev disables them), so this didn't affect dev verification.
- **Verification (real Chrome via CDP — no Playwright installed; drove the cached `chromium-1228` `chrome.exe` over the DevTools Protocol using Node 22's built-in `WebSocket`):** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean. Test specs validated against the seeded solutions in Node first (11/11 pass) before any browser run. Real `buildSandboxDoc` output run in-browser: the debounce solution passes 3/3 with correct nonce/`origin:"null"`. Full UI happy path on `/learn/javascript-runtime/event-loop`: typed the solution into Monaco via real keystrokes (`Input.insertText`, auto-closing-brackets disabled for the test), Run Tests → **3/3 passing**, green "challenge complete" banner shown, View Solution unlocked (screenshot confirms). Starter code → **0/3** with the correct per-test "fn is not a function" errors; a deliberately broken paste → the harness's `error` listener surfaced "Uncaught SyntaxError" in the panel (error path works). Empty state confirmed on `typescript/type-narrowing` ("Challenge coming soon", no editor). Dark mode renders correctly (`css-specificity`). Zero console errors across runs.
- **Two real findings during verification, both diagnosed not guessed:** (1) the first full-UI run timed out — isolating with a CDP probe proved the iframe+postMessage mechanism and the real generated doc both work, so the timeout was my test harness driving the run before the solution reached React state, not an engine bug. (2) `@monaco-editor/react` does **not** fire `onChange` for a programmatic `editor.setValue()` (the controlled `value` stays stale, so the editor showed the solution while `code` state stayed at the starter → a misleading 0/3) — real user typing does fire it, confirmed by switching the test to `Input.insertText`. Both were test-harness artifacts, not app defects.
- **Follow-up (post-merge user feedback — banner bug + interactive playground):** user reported the green "All tests passing — challenge complete" banner showing even when the current run was 0/3 failing. Root cause: the banner was driven by `succeeded = completed || passed`, so a _previously_-completed challenge (persisted `challengeCompleted` in the DB, or a pass earlier in the session) kept the green banner regardless of the current code. Fixed: the green banner is now driven by the **current run only** (`passed`); a previously-completed challenge instead shows a distinct neutral "you've completed this — run again any time" pill that disappears the moment a new run starts, so it can never contradict a failing panel. (`react-hooks/static-components` also forced the demo lookup into a `ChallengePlayground` switch component rather than a `const Demo = getX()` variable.)
- **Then (same feedback, larger ask): richer description + a neal.fun-style interactive playground that runs the user's real code.** Two decisions taken with the user up front: (1) the live demo **runs the user's actual code** (not a canned illustration); (2) ship the **richer description + the debounce demo together** as the flagship. Built:
  - `ChallengeDescription.tsx` — a dependency-free Markdown-subset renderer (headings, paragraphs, bullet lists, fenced code blocks, blockquotes, inline `code`, `**bold**`, `*italic*`), token-styled, authored content only (no HTML injection). Replaced `ChallengePrompt`'s old inline-code-only paragraph; `ChallengePrompt` is now just title + difficulty badge. The seed's `implement-debounce` description was rewritten into a proper teaching brief (the search-box problem, the idea, the task, a code example, a debounce-vs-throttle aside).
  - **Live, bidirectional sandbox** (new, alongside the one-shot test runner): `buildLiveDoc.ts` (a persistent `allow-scripts` srcdoc that runs the user code + a challenge-specific _driver_ in a private closure, exchanging messages both ways — nonce stays private, same anti-spoof posture as the test harness), `liveDrivers.ts` (`getLiveDriver(slug)` → driver source; the debounce driver wraps the user's `debounce` around a "fire request" reporter and falls back to firing on every key when the debounce is missing/broken), `useLiveSandbox.ts` (manages one session, rebuilds on debounced code change, and **queues events until the iframe signals READY then flushes** so the demo works from the very first keystroke — fixed a real startup race where early keys were dropped).
  - `DebounceDemo.tsx` + `RequestLane.tsx` — the neal.fun visual: a search box where every keystroke fires a "request"; two lanes ("Without debounce" naive, counted parent-side, vs "With your debounce", driven live by the sandbox) each with an animated packet flying to a 📡 server, an API-call counter, and a $0.002/call cost; a "wasted calls saved" line when the user's debounce starts reducing calls. `ChallengePlayground.tsx` (slug switch) renders it for `implement-debounce` and nothing for other challenges. Wired into `ConceptChallenge` between the description and the editor.
  - **Verification:** `tsc`/`eslint` clean throughout. The live session logic was proven deterministically in Node's `vm` (a correct debounce coalesces 5 rapid KEYs → **1** CALL; the empty starter → **5** CALLs via the naive fallback; READY handshake fires). In-browser (CDP): the rich description renders (headings/code-example/blockquote present), and typing in the demo drives the "your debounce" lane live (5 keys → 5 CALLs end-to-end; lane counter updates). **Dev-cache gotcha confirmed and worked around:** `getChallengeByConceptId` is `unstable_cache`d (disk-backed under `.next/cache`), so the rewritten description stayed stale across a plain process restart — required clearing `.next` to surface it in dev (production rebuilds fetch fresh). The earlier transient "your-debounce lane shows 0" was a cold-compile timing artifact (READY not yet received in the test window), not a logic bug — confirmed by the message-flow probe and the settled-page run.
- **Follow-up (neal.fun aesthetic rollout — debounce redesign + 2 more playgrounds):** user asked to lift the playground look-and-feel to neal.fun quality and extend it to the other two existing challenges. Studied neal.fun's principles first (the page _is_ the idea; one strong loop; restraint + lavish feedback on the one thing; concrete/absurd real-world framing; satisfying accumulation; a payoff moment). Added shared primitives `CountUp` (eased ticking number) and `Celebration` (render-pure confetti burst, token-colored). Rebuilt `DebounceDemo` from the clinical two-lane version into **"The Server Bill"** — a search box where each keystroke pings a server with a live load meter + mood emoji, flying coins, a running bill, absurd-scale framing ("$X/day at 1M users"), and a confetti payoff. Built two new bespoke playgrounds in the same language: **`VirtualListDemo`** ("10,000 Products" — scroll drives the user's `visibleRange`, DOM-node counter collapses 10,000→~14) and **`SpecificityDemo`** ("Selector Showdown" — four selectors scored live by the user's `specificity()`, the winner's color snaps onto a real button). New per-challenge drivers (`virtual-list`, `specificity-calculator`) added to `liveDrivers.ts`; all three wired through `ChallengePlayground`'s slug switch.
  - **Verified:** `tsc`/`eslint` clean (fixed a few `react-hooks` lint rules along the way — set-state-in-effect, render purity, created-during-render). The debounce loop confirmed end-to-end in-browser (starter → "server on fire $30k/day"; a correct debounce → 15 keys collapse to **1 request**, 93% saved, "$28k/day saved", confetti). The two new drivers proven deterministically in Node `vm` (virtual-list solution @scrollTop 4000 → window {121,137} = 17 nodes vs starter ok:false; specificity solution scores `button=[0,0,1] / .btn=[0,1,0] / .primary.btn=[0,2,0] / #cta=[1,0,0]` → #cta wins). Both new demos confirmed rendering correctly in-browser in their starter "problem" state (the solved-state browser capture was blocked only by dev compile-churn during Monaco typing, not logic). **Caught + fixed a real correctness bug:** the demo showed a false "saving 100% 🎉" when `yours === 0` (no calls had fired yet) — guarded so savings/celebration require `yours > 0`, since a real debounce always fires at least once.

  **Last completed:** 25 Interview Tab — ran the `architect` skill before building. Homework surfaced the same data gap Feature 24 hit: **interview questions were seeded by collection only, never linked to a concept** (`InterviewQuestionSeed` had no `conceptSlug`; the `concept_id` column existed but was always null), so a concept page's Interview tab had nothing to render. Four decisions resolved with the user up front (all the recommended option): (1) **link existing seeded questions to concepts, mirror F24** — add `conceptSlug`, backfill `concept_id`, empty state elsewhere — not author fresh sets or fall back to the category collection; (2) **ratings are local-only this feature** — "I knew this"/"Need to review" drive the progress indicator + completion trigger; SM-2 spaced repetition into `user_interview_reviews` stays Feature 32's scope; (3) **completion = all questions rated** (fire-once POST, silent for logged-out — same contract as Simulate/Challenge); (4) **promote the small Markdown renderer to `components/shared`** (3rd consumer: MDX, challenge descriptions, now interview answers → rule of three) rather than a cross-feature import. One assumption flagged and accepted: linking existing questions gave react-rendering 5 (all of `ff-react`) but the other three built concepts only 1 each, so I **topped each up with ~4 new concept-specific seed questions** to reach 5 apiece.
- **Seed** (`scripts/seed.ts`): added `conceptSlug?` to `InterviewQuestionSeed`; linked the 3 naturally-matching `ff-75` questions (event-loop / css-specificity / browser-rendering-pipeline) + all 5 `ff-react` questions (react-rendering); authored 12 new questions (4 event-loop in `ff-javascript`, 4 css-specificity + 4 browser-rendering-pipeline in `ff-75`) — each with markdown answers deliberately using ordered lists / fenced code to exercise the renderer. Switched the interview insert from `onConflictDoNothing` to `onConflictDoUpdate` with a `COALESCE(excluded.concept_id, …)` backfill, exactly mirroring the challenges insert. Verified in the DB: each of the 4 built concepts has exactly 5 concept-linked questions; the other 4 concepts have 0 (empty state).
- **Queries** (`features/learn/lib/queries.ts`): `InterviewQuestionData` type + `getInterviewQuestionsByConceptId` (cached, `"concepts"` tag, static content) + `getInterviewState` (live per-user, `interviewCompleted`).
- **Shared renderer**: moved `ChallengeDescription`'s dependency-free Markdown-subset renderer to `src/components/shared/Markdown.tsx` and **added ordered-list (`1.`) support** (the interview answers rely on numbered lists, which the original only-`-`/`*` renderer dropped to plain paragraphs). `ChallengeDescription` is now a thin wrapper delegating to it — practice call sites unchanged.
- **Feature components** (`src/features/interview-prep/components/`): `QuestionCard.tsx` (expandable disclosure — click header to reveal the markdown answer, difficulty + company badges, `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`, `AnimatePresence` height reveal gated by `useSafeReducedMotion`, "I knew this" / "Need to review" toggle buttons using the `success`/`warning` tokens). `QuestionCard` exports its own local `InterviewQuestionView` view-type (`question`/`answer`/`difficulty`/`companies`) so the feature never imports the DB row type from `features/learn`. The host `ConceptInterview.tsx` (local ratings state, a `role="progressbar"` "X of Y answered" bar, fire-once completion POST silent for logged-out, and the "coming soon" empty state) lives in **`src/components/interview/`**, matching its two sibling concept-tab hosts `ConceptChallenge` (`components/challenge/`) and `ConceptSimulator` (`components/simulators/`) — the host is the `components/` → `features/` bridge (references the `features/learn` query type + composes the `features/interview-prep` pieces), a crossing a feature itself may not make. **(Corrected in the `/review` follow-up below — the host was first placed in `features/interview-prep`, which made it the codebase's only `features/ → features/` import.)**
- **Wiring**: `page.tsx` fetches questions (cached) alongside the challenge and `getInterviewState` in the per-user `Promise.all`, builds `interviewContent`, threads it through `ConceptPageShell` → `ConceptInteractive` (same children-as-props pattern as F22–24); removed `interview` from `ConceptInteractive`'s placeholder `TAB_BODY` map (only `build` remains).
- **Real completion-trigger decision (not an effect):** the completion POST fires from the rating handler the moment the last unrated question is rated (computed from the pre-update ratings snapshot + this id), not a `useEffect` on the answered count — the project's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule forbids the effect approach, and the fire-once ref keeps re-rates from double-posting.
- **Pre-existing mojibake caught + fixed because this feature is the first to surface interview answers in the UI:** `scripts/seed.ts` stored em dashes as `â€"` and arrows as `â†'` (classic double-encoded UTF-8, present in the repo before this feature). The Interview tab rendering the answers verbatim made it user-visible, so I fixed the corrupt sequences file-wide (em dash / arrow / curly-quote in content, plus the decorative box-drawing `─` in comment separators) — verified the DB now has **0 rows** containing the mojibake marker and the seed file has no remaining `â`/`€` bytes (only legitimate `—`/`→`/`…`/`─`). This also cleaned the already-shipped challenge descriptions as a side benefit.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean. Playwright (dev, real DOM assertions not just screenshots): event-loop Interview tab renders exactly 5 question cards; progress bar `aria-valuenow` goes 0 → 5 as questions are rated; expanding a card shows the answer + both rating buttons; ordered lists render (`ol.list-decimal` present); the "Completed" pill stays hidden for the logged-out session (correct — no POST fired); `typescript/type-narrowing` shows the empty state with 0 cards; no horizontal overflow at 1440px or 390px in light or dark. The only console noise was `429` on `GET /api/auth/get-session` — Better-Auth's own 5-req/60s limiter (Feature 19) tripped by six rapid headless page loads, unrelated to this feature. A dev tab-switch quirk (clicks landing before React hydration attaches handlers) was handled in the test harness with a retry, not a code change — real users don't click within the hydration window. **Note:** because `getInterviewQuestionsByConceptId` is `unstable_cache`d (same disk-backed `.next/cache` gotcha documented for F24's challenge description), the re-seeded/cleaned answers required a dev server restart to surface — production rebuilds fetch fresh.
- **Follow-up (same session, user request): "more questions" CTA → Interview Prep.** Added `MoreInterviewPrepCta.tsx` — a `next/link` banner ("Want more interview questions?" naming the full FF 75 / JavaScript / React / System Design collections) rendered by `ConceptInterview` below the question list and inside the empty state. Points at `/interview-prep`, the Feature 30 hub — a deliberate **forward-link that 404s today**, matching the Navbar/Footer which already link there ahead of Phase 6; it lights up automatically when the hub ships. Verified present in both the populated tab and the empty state (`css-specificity` + `type-narrowing`); `tsc`/`eslint` clean.
- **Follow-up (`/review` skill, then fixed the one Important finding):** ran the project's `review` skill. Layer 1 (plan alignment) and most of Layer 2/3 passed; the review found **1 Important issue** and 2 minor ones. The Important one, confirmed by grepping the whole tree: `ConceptInterview.tsx` and `QuestionCard.tsx` were the **only `features/ → features/` imports in the codebase** (`features/interview-prep` importing `InterviewQuestionData` from `features/learn`), violating `architecture.md`'s "features never import other features" invariant — the two sibling concept-tab hosts (`ConceptChallenge`, `ConceptSimulator`) both live in `components/` precisely to legalize this crossing, and this host had broken from that pattern. **Fixed** by (a) moving the host to `src/components/interview/ConceptInterview.tsx` (so its `features/learn` type reference + `features/interview-prep` composition is the accepted `components/` → `features/` bridge) and (b) giving `QuestionCard` its own local `InterviewQuestionView` type so `features/interview-prep` no longer reaches into `features/learn`. Re-verified: a tree-wide grep now reports **zero** cross-feature imports; `tsc`/`eslint` clean; the tab still renders (css-specificity route 200 + content present). The 2 minor findings were left as accepted: `Markdown.tsx` at 201 lines (1 over the split rule) and the CTA's intentional `/interview-prep` forward-link 404.
- **Follow-up (CodeRabbit PR findings, verified then fixed the real ones):** worked through the PR's inline + nitpick comments, checking each against current code rather than applying blind.
  - **Real, fixed — premium content leak (Important, matches `security.md`'s "Premium Data Must Not Leak in API Responses" almost verbatim):** neither `page.tsx` nor `ConceptInterview` gated `interviewQuestions.answer` on `isPremium`, unlike the Challenge tab's `clientChallenge` redaction — a concept-linked premium question's full answer would have shipped to every client regardless of entitlement. Currently unreachable (no concept-linked question is premium — only `ff-system-design` questions are, and none of those are linked), but the same class of bug the Challenge tab already guards against, so fixed rather than left as a note. **Fix:** `page.tsx` now computes `isLocked = q.isPremium && !isPremiumUser` per question and strips `answer` to `""` server-side when locked (never serialized to a non-premium client — same "server-side only, client UI is cosmetic" posture as `clientChallenge`); `QuestionCard` renders a `Lock`-badged teaser + "Upgrade to Premium to unlock this answer" in place of the markdown + rating buttons for locked cards; `ConceptInterview`'s `total`/progress/completion denominator now excludes locked questions (you can't rate an answer you can't see) via `questions.filter(q => !q.isLocked).length`. **Verified live, not just by inspection:** temporarily flipped one real `css-specificity` question to `isPremium: true` via direct SQL (dev DB only), confirmed the locked card shows the Premium badge + upgrade message + zero rating buttons, and the progress denominator correctly read "0 of 4 answered" (5 questions, 1 locked) — then reverted via `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` (the upsert's `isPremium: sql\`excluded.is_premium\`` resets it) and confirmed 0 premium concept-linked questions remain in the DB.
  - **Real, fixed — "Completed" pill contradicted "0 of N answered" on a revisit:** ratings are local-only (session state) per this feature's own architect decision, but `initialCompleted` (DB-persisted) could be `true` on page load while `ratings` starts empty — showing a green "Completed" pill next to a 0% progress bar read as contradictory. **Fix:** `showCompletedPill` now requires `answeredCount === total` in the _current_ session (not just the persisted flag), and a new `showPreviouslyCompletedNote` (muted text: "You've completed this tab before — rate the questions again to refresh your memory") fills the gap when `completed` is true but nothing's been rated yet this session — same "current state, not stale flag" precedent as the Challenge tab's `passed`-driven banner fix. Re-verified the normal 0→5 rating flow still works unchanged (event-loop: 5 cards, `valuenow` 0→5, no console errors).
  - **Real, fixed — two more mojibake leftovers + one stale/inaccurate comment, all in `scripts/seed.ts`:** a `ff-system-design` answer had `5“30s` (should be `5–30s`, a different double-encoding artifact than the em-dash/arrow ones fixed in this feature's first pass); the `useEffect`/`useLayoutEffect` answer overclaimed that `useLayoutEffect` "emits a warning" on the server — softened to "does not run at all"; the `// 5 per collection` header comment was stale once concept top-ups were added. Fixed all three; re-scanned the full file for any remaining `â`/`€` mojibake bytes (zero found) before re-seeding.
  - **Nitpick, fixed — merged `page.tsx`'s two sequential `Promise.all` calls into one:** the challenge/interview content fetch and the per-user progress-state fetch had no data dependency on each other, so they now run in a single `Promise.all` (per-user calls short-circuited to `Promise.resolve(false)` when logged out, preserving the exact same fallback values) instead of two round-trips.
  - **Confirmed-but-skipped (documented, not fixed) — `interview_questions` upsert identity is `(collection, order_index)` only, not a stable per-row key:** reordering questions within a collection (not just appending, which is all any seed edit has done so far) can upsert onto the wrong existing row and leave a stale duplicate behind. A correct fix needs a schema migration (a stable `slug`/key column, unique per row) — out of scope for this pass; left a comment at the upsert site documenting the caveat and the real fix.
  - Re-verified after all fixes: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean on every touched file; zero console/page errors across all Playwright checks (normal flow, empty state, locked-question flow).
- **Follow-up (user-reported, same session): the premium-lock teaser's dashed border looked broken — top edge invisible in a screenshot.** Diagnosed rather than guessed: `getComputedStyle` confirmed all 4 sides of the `border-dashed border-premium/40` box had identical width/style/color — not a broken/missing border in code. The real cause was legibility: a 1px dashed line at 40% opacity is thin enough that at normal (1x) screen resolution the top edge — sitting right below the card's own header divider, the lowest-contrast zone on the card — visually disappeared while the other 3 sides (bordered by more contrast from the box's own fill/text) stayed legible; a 3x-zoomed crop showed the border technically present, but a normal-resolution screenshot reproduced the same "invisible top edge" the user saw. **Fix:** bumped `border-premium/40` → `border-premium/70`. Verified via normal-resolution screenshots (not zoomed crops) that all 4 sides are now evenly visible in both light and dark mode. Also caught and fixed a stale-cache side effect from the prior round's temporary DB flip (a dev-only `unstable_cache` entry was still serving the old locked state after a restart that didn't wipe `.next`) — full `.next` wipe + restart confirmed the real state: 0 concept-linked questions are actually premium.

Applied + verified `project_briefs` exists via a direct query.
- **Seed:** added `ProjectBriefSeed` type + `PROJECT_BRIEFS` (4 entries) to `scripts/seed.ts`, same `conceptSlug`-link-resolved-to-`concept_id`-at-insert-time + `onConflictDoUpdate` pattern as `CHALLENGES`/`INTERVIEW_QUESTIONS`. Each brief is designed around **one exported pure function** so Run Tests stays meaningful despite being informational-only: `kanban-board` → `moveCard` (react-rendering, free), `async-task-runner` → `runWithConcurrency` (event-loop, premium), `render-blocking-analyzer` → `getRenderBlockingResources` (browser-rendering-pipeline, premium), `specificity-conflict-finder` → `findOverriddenRules`/`specificity` (css-specificity, premium). **All 4 reference solutions verified passing their own 16 tests in a scratch Node script before being committed to `features/build/data/testSpecs.ts`** (same "prove it in Node first" precedent as Feature 24's challenge tests) — script deleted after use.   - **Two real verification-harness bugs found and fixed, not app bugs:** (1) ~15 leaked headless Chrome processes from earlier crashed test scripts (each missing a `try/finally` around `browser.close()`) were silently choking the machine, making the dev server itself take 10-37s per request and producing false "editor never rendered" failures — killed all leaked `chrome.exe` processes and wrapped the final script in `try/finally`, after which response times dropped back to ~1s. (2) Typing a multi-line solution via `page.keyboard.insertText` triggered Monaco's live auto-closing-brackets feature, duplicating closing braces into a real `SyntaxError` inside the sandbox — the exact harness artifact already documented in Feature 24's own verification notes ("auto-closing-brackets disabled for the test"). Fixed by disabling `autoClosingBrackets`/`autoClosingQuotes`/`autoIndent`/`autoSurround` on the live editor instance via `window.monaco` before typing (test-only, no app config change). Also hit and correctly dismissed as expected-not-a-bug: the Challenge tab's starter code legitimately shows "0/4 passing" when no solution has been typed in — not a regression.
