Resolved as a deliberate forward-link (same precedent as `MoreInterviewPrepCta` → `/interview-prep` ahead of Phase 6), not by building the real pricing/Stripe page now (that's Phase 9's scope, not requested here).
  - **`BuildPremiumLocked` gained an `isLoggedIn` prop** and a `bg-premium`/`text-premium-foreground` CTA button (`ui-tokens.md`'s already-documented "Premium CTA" style — first real usage of that spec). Logged-in → links straight to `/pricing`. Logged-out → links to `/login?callbackURL=/pricing`. `ConceptBuild.tsx` threads its existing `isLoggedIn` prop through on the early-return branch.
  - **Real gap found while wiring the logged-out path, not just cosmetic:** `OAuthButton`'s `callbackURL` was hardcoded to `"/learn"`, so a logged-out user clicking through from a locked Build project would always land back on `/learn` after signing in, losing the "they were trying to reach pricing" intent entirely. Fixed properly, not with a one-off special case: `OAuthButton` now accepts an optional `callbackURL` prop (default `"/learn"`, so every existing call site is unchanged); the login page reads a `?callbackURL=` search param and threads the validated value to both `OAuthButton` and the already-logged-in bounce (`redirect(...)`, previously hardcoded to `"/"`).
  - **Security consideration surfaced by this change, handled before shipping, not after:** `?callbackURL=` is an untrusted query param controlling a redirect destination — the textbook open-redirect shape. Added `getSafeRedirectPath(path, fallback)` (`features/auth/lib/getSafeRedirectPath.ts`, same file-placement precedent as `getLoginErrorMessage`): rejects anything not starting with a single `/` (absolute URLs, protocol-relative `//host`, and the `/\host` backslash variant some browsers normalize the same way) and falls back to the caller's own existing default instead of ever passing an unsafe value through.
  - **Verified live, not just by reading the code:** real browser (Playwright, network-intercepted `signIn.social()` calls rather than trusting the UI alone): clicking the locked state's CTA while logged out lands on `/login?callbackURL=%2Fpricing`; the real outgoing Better-Auth request carries `callbackURL: "/pricing"`; visiting `/login?callbackURL=https://evil.example.com` directly and clicking "Continue with Google" sends `callbackURL: "/learn"` to Better-Auth — the malicious value is discarded, not forwarded. Typecheck/lint clean project-wide. **Two more leaked-Chrome-process rounds hit during this verification** (same root cause as the main entry's Follow-up 2 note — crashed scripts missing `try/finally`) — killed them each time; a `page.waitForURL()` race (reading `page.url()` immediately after a client-side navigation click, before the route actually changed) was the one real test-script bug, fixed by awaiting the URL change instead of a fixed timeout.
  - **Docs updated:** `ui-registry.md` (`BuildPremiumLocked`'s CTA, the `callbackURL`/`getSafeRedirectPath` mechanism under both `OAuthButton` and the Build Components section), this entry.
- **Follow-up 4 (user-reported bug, same session): logged-in premium account intermittently showed the logged-out Navbar for 1-2 minutes after navigating through the new login→pricing flow.** Investigated rather than assumed — diagnosed as a real, pre-existing auth-infrastructure bug this feature's new flow just made easy to trigger, not something the CTA/redirect code itself did wrong. - **Root cause, confirmed empirically, not guessed:** Better-Auth's own internal rate limiter (`lib/auth/server.ts`, set up in Feature 19: global `{ window: 60, max: 5 }`) applies to _every_ Better-Auth endpoint including `/get-session` — a read-only check of the caller's own already-issued cookie, not a brute-forceable action like sign-in. `curl` in a tight loop confirmed the 6th request within 60s reliably returned `429`. Read Better-Auth's actual client-side session-store source (`node_modules/better-auth/dist/client/session-atom.mjs`) rather than assuming: on a non-401 error it correctly _preserves_ the last-known session data — but a fresh full-page navigation (Build tab → CTA → `/login` → homepage is exactly this: several real page loads in a row, each remounting the session store from scratch) has no prior data to preserve. If that page's very first `get-session` call happens to land on the tripped limiter, the Navbar shows logged-out until the 60s window resets (sometimes closer to 2 minutes if the user's own refreshes re-trip it). **This exact 429 was already visible once before and dismissed as harmless** — Feature 25's progress-tracker note mentions "six rapid headless page loads" tripping it during automated verification — but a real user hitting it through ordinary browsing turns it from test noise into a visible product bug. - **Fix:** added `rateLimit.customRules: { "/get-session": { window: 60, max: 100 } }` in `lib/auth/server.ts` — confirmed via Better-Auth's own source (`node_modules/@better-auth/core/src/types/init-options.ts` for the option shape, `node_modules/better-auth/dist/api/rate-limiter/index.mjs` for how `customRules` resolves per-path, and that Better-Auth's own built-in "special rules" already give sign-in/sign-up a separate tighter window — `/get-session` isn't one of them, so it was silently falling back to the app's blanket default). Left the global `max: 5` untouched — it still protects sign-in/sign-up. - **Verified live:** before the fix, a `curl` loop 429'd on request 6; after, 12 rapid requests all returned `200`, and a longer 90-request burst confirmed the new ceiling still caps out (around the 89th/90th request, consistent with `max: 100`) — so this is a real, working override, not just a config value that looks right on paper. - **Docs updated:** `architecture.md` (new "Better-Auth's Own Rate Limiter" subsection under Rate Limiting Pattern — this mechanism was previously undocumented entirely, a real gap, not just this fix), this entry.
  **Last completed:** 27 Progress API + XP System (finishes Phase 4) — ran the `architect` skill before building. Three decisions resolved with the user up front, all the recommended option: (1) tab-completion XP is only awarded on a false→true transition of that tab's flag (fetch-then-compare, not blind upsert) — repeat POSTs stay a no-op; (2) the streak bonus fires once per UTC calendar day (first activity that day), not on every POST; (3) all writes (progress upsert, xp_events inserts, profiles update) run inside a single `db.transaction()` — the first transaction in the codebase, so a mid-request failure can't leave XP awarded without the progress flag set or vice versa. Two more gaps surfaced during homework, also resolved with the user: `architecture.md`'s "+50 first-time concept completion bonus" had no matching `xp_events.event_type` — added `concept_completed` to `XP_EVENT_TYPES` (required a migration widening the `xp_events` CHECK constraint); and `streak_last_activity` day comparisons use the server's UTC calendar day (no per-user timezone system exists).
- **`lib/constants.ts`:** added `concept_completed` to `XP_EVENT_TYPES`; new `TAB_XP_REWARDS`/`TAB_XP_EVENT_TYPE` lookup maps (10/15/25/20/30 XP for understand/simulate/challenge/interview/build), `CONCEPT_COMPLETED_BONUS_XP = 50`, `STREAK_BONUS_XP = 5` — matching `architecture.md`'s XP System table exactly.
- **Migration `0008_lean_triathlon.sql`:** widens `xp_events_event_type_check`. Applied via the same workaround as Feature 26 (`drizzle-kit migrate` hangs on this machine's TLS-intercepted connection) — ran `drizzle-orm`'s own `migrate()` over a relaxed-TLS `pg.Pool`, in a throwaway script, deleted after use. Confirmed live via `pg_get_constraintdef` that the DB constraint now includes `concept_completed`.
- **New `src/lib/progress/applyProgressUpdate.ts`:** the transactional write, extracted out of `route.ts` to stay under `code-standards.md`'s 200-line file limit and to be directly testable (a route handler can't easily be unit-tested; the extracted function can be imported and called against a real DB). Takes the transaction handle + `userId`/`conceptId`/`tab`: `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`s the existing `user_concept_progress` row (locks it for the transaction's duration so two concurrent completions of the same tab can't both observe "not yet completed" and double-award), upserts the tab's flag, awards that tab's XP only if the flag was previously false, sets `fullyCompleted`/`completedAt` + awards the +50 bonus the first time all 5 flags are true, then `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`s the `profiles` row and runs the streak math (same-day → no change; yesterday → `streakCurrent + 1`; older/null → reset to 1; `streakLongest = max(...)`) awarding +5 XP only when the day actually changed, and finally increments `profiles.xp` by the request's total.
- **`route.ts` simplified to orchestration only** (51 lines): auth → rate-limit → parse/validate (all unchanged from Feature 22) → `db.transaction((tx) => applyProgressUpdate(tx, ...))` → `{ success: true }` or a 500 on any failure (transaction rolls back everything).
- **Verified against the real dev DB** (no UI to drive — build-plan.md scopes this feature as Logic only), via a throwaway script (deleted after use) that inserted a disposable `user`/`profiles` row, called the real `applyProgressUpdate` directly (not a re-implementation), and asserted: first `understand` completion → 15 XP (10 + 5 streak bonus) + 2 xp_events; a repeat `understand` POST the same day → 0 additional XP, 0 new events (idempotent); completing simulate/challenge/interview the same day → correct XP each, no extra streak bonus; completing `build` (5th tab) → +30 XP + the +50 `concept_completed` bonus, `fullyCompleted`/`completedAt` set; a repeat `build` POST → no re-award; backdating `streak_last_activity` to yesterday → `streakCurrent` increments and `streakLongest` tracks it; backdating to 3 days ago → `streakCurrent` resets to 1, `streakLongest` unchanged. All 7 scenarios passed. Also confirmed (real dev server, `curl`) that an unauthenticated POST still returns 401. `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean throughout. Test user deleted after (FK cascade from `user` cleaned `profiles`/`user_concept_progress`/`xp_events` automatically).
- **Follow-up (surfaced during homework, resolved with the user before starting): the Navbar's streak was still a hardcoded "0 day streak."** A Feature 17 `ui-registry.md` note had explicitly deferred wiring the Navbar's real `streakCurrent`/`isPremium` to "Feature 27." Confirmed `isPremium` isn't actually read anywhere in `AppNavbar`/`UserDropdown` today (no premium pill exists yet — that UI is Feature 38's scope per `build-plan.md`), so only the streak half was a real stale placeholder. **Wired it:** new `src/lib/profile.ts` → `getProfileStreak(userId)` (`cache()`-wrapped, same precedent as `lib/auth/server.ts`'s `getCachedSession`); `(app)/layout.tsx` fetches it alongside the existing session call and passes `initialStreak` to `AppNavbar` (same SSR-then-prop pattern as `initialUser`); `AppNavbar` renders `"{initialStreak} day streak"` in both the desktop and mobile clusters instead of the hardcoded `0`. No client-side polling — reflects the value as of the last full navigation, matching `getIsPremiumUser`'s existing once-per-page-load precedent. Verified the query against the real DB (disposable profile row, `streakCurrent: 7` read back correctly) and that a logged-out `/learn` request still renders 200 with the new code path in place; a live-browser check of the actual rendered number wasn't done (would require forging a real Better-Auth session, out of proportion for a one-line prop-display fix) — `tsc`/`eslint` clean.
- **Follow-up 2 (user did a real end-to-end test in their own browser, same session):** confirmed live on a real account (`superpower-india`) that the whole chain works — completing all 5 tabs on Event Loop produced exactly the expected 7 `xp_events` (10/5/15/25/20/30/50 = 155 XP), `fully_completed` flipped true, and the Navbar showed "1 day streak". One real pre-existing-data wrinkle surfaced during this: that account had completed all 5 Event Loop tabs **before** this feature existed, so `xp_events` was empty and `fully_completed` was still `false` for a fully-done row — a stale artifact of the old flag-only system, not a bug in the new logic (confirmed no XP was double-counted; the account's `profiles.xp`/streak fields were still untouched `0`/`null`, consistent with "nothing was ever awarded before"). I can't see it, progress bar shows 13%."** Clarified the 13% is `LearnSidebar`'s unrelated `completedConcepts/totalConcepts` metric, and that there was genuinely **no XP display anywhere in the UI** — `architecture.md`'s folder-structure diagram lists an aspirational `XPBadge.tsx` that was never actually built, and `build-plan.md` only ever plans an XP display on the Leaderboard (Feature 36, not built). Same gap class as the streak number before this feature's Navbar fix. User chose to add it now rather than wait for Feature 36. **Extended `src/lib/profile.ts`:** `getProfileStreak` → `getProfileSummary(userId)` returning `{ xp, streakCurrent }` in one query (was already fetching the row; no extra round-trip). `(app)/layout.tsx` destructures both into `initialXp`/`initialStreak` props. `AppNavbar` renders `"{initialXp} XP"` next to the streak (`Zap` icon, `fill-xp stroke-none` + `text-xp` — the documented XP token pair from `ui-tokens.md`, same treatment `Flame`/`fill-streak` already gets for the streak). **Real bug caught in the process, not by design:** the mobile hamburger menu's streak line had been missed entirely in the original Feature 27 Navbar fix — it still read the hardcoded `"0 day streak"` (only the desktop cluster got fixed). Caught only because adding the mobile XP line sat right next to it; fixed both in the same edit. - **Follow-up 4 (user-reported bug, same session): a correct Build-tab solution ("Execution timed out (5s limit)") failed Run Tests.** Diagnosed rather than assumed — not a Feature 27 change (untouched `src/lib/sandbox/`, Feature 24/26 code), but surfaced by the user actively exercising the app during this session. Two-step verification: (1) re-ran the exact `async-task-runner` reference solution against its exact test specs in plain Node — 344ms, all 4 pass (same "prove it in Node first" precedent as Features 24/26); (2) rebuilt the identical sandbox mechanism (hidden `sandbox="allow-scripts"` iframe, `srcdoc`, nonce'd `postMessage` handshake) in an isolated real browser outside the Next app entirely (headless Chrome via CDP, no Next.js/React/Monaco in the page) — all 4 tests passed, but took **~2.1s**, not near-instant. Confirmed separately that this dev environment is measurably slow under real load: a live request to an already-running dev server took **15+ seconds** just to compile/hydrate one route. Conclusion: the code and the harness mechanism are both correct, but the original 5s inner / 6s parent timeout budget left too little headroom over the ~2s real baseline once genuine dev-mode main-thread contention (Turbopack HMR, React, Monaco all sharing one thread with the sandboxed iframe) is added — a margin problem, not a logic bug. **Fix:** widened `buildSandboxDoc`'s inner guard 5000ms → 12000ms and `runInSandbox`'s parent cap 6000ms → 13000ms (`src/lib/sandbox/`) — affects both Practice's Challenge tab and Build tab, the two consumers of this shared engine. Verified `tsc`/`eslint` clean. (Housekeeping note: several throwaway CDP/headless-Chrome repro scripts and profile dirs were created and fully cleaned up during this investigation — zero leftover automation processes or files.)
- **Follow-up 5 (user-reported, same session): completing all 5 Interview-tab questions, switching tabs, and coming back showed "0 of 5 answered" again.** Confirmed via direct DB check that nothing was actually lost — `interviewCompleted`/XP were intact — but traced the visible reset to a real mechanism: `ConceptInteractive.tsx` keeps only the active tab's content **mounted** (`AnimatePresence mode="wait"` + `key={activeTab}` swaps in a fresh element), so switching away from Interview fully unmounts `ConceptInterview` and destroys its local `ratings` state; switching back mounts a brand-new instance starting empty. This was Feature 25's original, deliberate "ratings are local-only" scope decision, just more aggressive in practice than that framing implied (resets on every tab switch, not just page reloads). Asked the user how far to take the fix — offered lift-state-up (session-only, no DB) vs. full DB persistence vs. leave-as-is; **user chose full DB persistence.**
  - **Reused `user_interview_reviews` rather than inventing a new table** — it already exists for exactly this relationship (Feature 32's SM-2 spaced-repetition schema, not yet built), has a `(user_id, question_id)` unique constraint ready for upserts, and Feature 25's own notes already anticipated mapping the tab's 2 buttons onto SM-2 quality. This pass only writes `quality` (`knew→5`, `review→2`), increments `repetitions`, and stamps `last_reviewed_at` — `ease_factor`/`interval_days`/`next_review_at` are left at their column defaults for Feature 32 to compute for real once actual spaced-repetition scheduling is built; no migration needed, the table's shape already supported this.
  - **New `POST /api/interview-rating`** (`src/app/api/interview-rating/route.ts`): auth → rate-limit (reuses the shared `ratelimit` instance from `lib/upstash.ts`, same as `/api/progress`) → validate `{ questionId, rating: "knew"|"review" }` → `onConflictDoUpdate` upsert. Not fire-once like the completion POST — re-rating an already-rated question is a deliberate user action and the route is a safe upsert either way.
  - **New query `getInterviewRatings(userId, conceptId)`** (`features/learn/lib/queries.ts`): joins `user_interview_reviews` → `interview_questions` scoped to both the user and the concept, derives `quality >= 4 ? "knew" : "review"`, returns a plain `Record<string, "knew"|"review">` rather than importing `features/interview-prep`'s `QuestionRating` type (the same "features never import features" boundary Feature 25 already had to fix once).
  - **`ConceptInterview`** now takes an `initialRatings` prop (seeds the `ratings` `useState` instead of always starting at `{}`) and `handleRate` also fires the new rating POST (best-effort, silent no-op when logged out, matching every other tab's contract). `page.tsx` fetches `getInterviewRatings` in the same per-user `Promise.all` and threads it through.
  - **Verified against the real DB** (disposable test user + real event-loop interview questions, deleted after): rated 2 questions, confirmed both read back correctly via `getInterviewRatings`'s exact join logic; re-rated one from "knew" to "review", confirmed the upsert flipped it, `repetitions` incremented 1→2, and `ease_factor` stayed untouched at its 2.5 default (correctly left for Feature 32). Confirmed live on the running dev server that an unauthenticated `POST /api/interview-rating` returns 401. `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean throughout.
- **Docs updated:** `architecture.md` (Progress Tracking data-flow diagram now shows the transaction/locking/idempotency/streak-bonus-once-per-day detail; `xp_events.event_type` column now lists `concept_completed`; folder structure shows `lib/progress/applyProgressUpdate.ts` and the new `api/interview-rating/route.ts`), `ui-registry.md` (`POST /api/progress` entry updated for the transaction; `AppNavbar` entry updated for the real streak + XP wiring and the mobile-streak-bug fix; Concept Interview Tab section updated for ratings persistence), this entry.
- **Follow-up 6 (`/review` skill, then fixed Critical + Important):** ran the project's `review` skill against the whole branch. Found 1 Critical, 3 Important, 2 Minor. User asked for Critical + Important fixed; Minor left as-is.
  - **Critical, fixed — silently swallowed error in `ConceptInterview.tsx`'s rating POST:** `void fetch(...).catch(() => {})` did nothing on failure — no log, no user feedback, no retry — directly violating `error-handling.md`'s "errors are never silently swallowed" invariant, and meaning a concept could show "fully completed" while a specific rating silently never saved. **Fix:** extracted the fetch into `postRating()`, which `console.error`s on a non-OK response or network error and tracks failed question ids in a new `failedRatingIds` state; `QuestionCard` gained an optional `saveFailed` prop rendering a small inline `text-error` note ("Couldn't save this rating — pick a rating again to retry") next to the rating buttons — re-picking a rating naturally retries since the route is a safe upsert, not fire-once.
  - **Important, fixed — `code-standards.md`'s `XP_EVENT_TYPES` example was stale**, still showing the pre-Feature-27 8 values under "never use any other string." Added `concept_completed` with a comment noting when/why.
  - **Important, fixed — `AppNavbar.tsx` (221 lines) and `queries.ts` (360 lines) both exceeded the 200-line file rule** (`AppNavbar.tsx` was already 7 lines over pre-Feature-27; both grew further this feature without being split). **`AppNavbar.tsx` → 144 lines:** extracted `AppNavbarStats.tsx` (the XP+streak pair, previously duplicated between the desktop and mobile clusters) and `AppNavLinks.tsx` (`AppNavLink`/`AppMobileNavLink`, previously bottom-of-file helpers). **`queries.ts` → a 10-line barrel** re-exporting from a new `queries/` folder split by concern: `concepts.ts` (catalog/summary/detail, 134 lines), `tabState.ts` (the 5 per-tab completion checks + `getIsPremiumUser`, 94 lines), `challenge.ts` (46 lines), `interview.ts` (questions + ratings, 55 lines), `build.ts` (42 lines) — every existing `from "@/features/learn/lib/queries"` import elsewhere in the codebase kept working unchanged (same "promote + barrel re-export, zero import-path churn" precedent as Feature 26's sandbox-engine promotion).
  - **Important, fixed — both `/api/progress` and `/api/interview-rating`'s catch-all 500 handlers never logged the underlying error** (`catch { return Response.json(...) }`), contradicting `error-handling.md`'s documented pattern and Logging Strategy — a DB write failure in production would have been completely unobserved server-side. **Fixed properly, not naively:** rather than logging the raw `error` object (which is what `error-handling.md`'s own example does), added a shared `getPostgresErrorCode(error)` helper (`src/lib/dbErrors.ts`) that extracts only the Postgres error code from `error.cause` — mirroring the _safer_ precedent already established in `lib/auth/provisionProfile.ts` (Feature 16), where logging a raw `DrizzleQueryError` was found to leak the full insert payload (name/email/avatar URL) into logs. Both routes now log `[route] DB write failed: <code>` before returning the generic 500.
  - **Re-verified after all fixes:** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean across every touched file. Real dev server: `/learn` and a concept page both still 200 after the `queries.ts`/`AppNavbar.tsx` splits (import paths intact); a disposable-user DB script re-confirmed `getInterviewRatings` still round-trips correctly through the split query module; unauthenticated `POST`s to both `/api/progress` and `/api/interview-rating` still return 401.
  - **Left as-is (Minor, not requested):** `/api/interview-rating` doesn't verify a rated question is one the user can actually see (no premium check) before writing — no content leak, just permits rating a locked question; `security.md`'s own "5s limit" example text and its Rate Limiting Reference table are now stale relative to the actual 12s/13s sandbox timeout and the new route (covered by that table's generic "all other write routes" row, just not named).
- **Follow-up 7 (CodeRabbit review on PR #44, verified each finding against current code before touching anything):** 2 inline + 1 real correctness bug fixed, 1 nitpick fixed, 2 findings skipped with a verified reason.
  - **Fixed — `/api/progress` and `/api/interview-rating` shared one rate-limit bucket.** Both called `ratelimit.limit(session.user.id)` with the bare user id as the key — heavy use of one endpoint could exhaust the other's quota. Namespaced each: `` `progress:${session.user.id}` `` / `` `interview-rating:${session.user.id}` ``.
  - **Fixed — AppNavbar's XP/streak went stale after a completion, only updating on the next full navigation** (by original design — no client polling — but a real point of confusion, matching what the user hit this session going from 155→185 XP with no visible change until refresh). Added `router.refresh()` after every successful `/api/progress` completion POST, across all 5 tab hosts (`MarkUnderstoodButton`, `ConceptSimulator`, `ConceptChallenge`, `ConceptBuild`, `ConceptInterview`) — re-fetches `(app)/layout.tsx`'s server data (including the Navbar's `initialXp`/`initialStreak`) without losing client state.
  - **Fixed — real race condition in `applyProgressUpdate`, confirmed by reproducing it, not just by reading the code.** `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` only locks rows that already exist — a user's very first-ever action on a concept has no row yet, so two truly concurrent first completions could both read "no row" before either lock applied, and both award XP. **Fix:** an `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` now runs before the `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, guaranteeing the row exists by the time it's locked — the insert itself is the synchronization point, since Postgres serializes concurrent inserts targeting the same unique `(userId, conceptId)` key. **Verified against the real DB with genuine concurrency** (two separate pool connections, two full `db.transaction()` calls fired via `Promise.all` at the same instant, same brand-new concept/tab pair neither had ever touched): before trusting the fix, confirmed the scenario was reachable; after the fix, exactly 1 `xp_events` row was written, not 2. Re-ran the standard 7-scenario regression suite too — all still pass.
  - **Fixed (nitpick) — the 5 tab-state getters (`getUnderstoodState`/`getSimulateState`/`getChallengeState`/`getInterviewState`/`getBuildState`) each queried `user_concept_progress` separately**, even though `page.tsx` always calls all 5 with the identical `(userId, conceptId)` in one `Promise.all` — 5 round trips for 1 row. Consolidated behind a `cache()`-wrapped `getTabProgressRow(userId, conceptId)` (`queries/tabState.ts`) that fetches all 5 completion columns once; each getter now just plucks its own boolean. Every existing call site/signature unchanged. Verified the consolidated query returns the correct 5 flags against real data (Event Loop, all true).
  - **Skipped, verified not a bug — "streak date mixing" (Date vs YYYY-MM-DD string).** Checked Drizzle's actual behavior rather than assuming: `profiles.streakLastActivity` is declared as a bare `date("streak_last_activity")` with no `{ mode: "date" }` override, and Drizzle's own `date()` overloads default to **string** mode when no mode is given (confirmed both by reading `drizzle-orm/pg-core/columns/date.d.ts` and by a `tsc` type probe: assigning a string literal to `profiles.streakLastActivity`'s inferred type compiles clean). So the column is genuinely `string | null` at both the type and runtime level, matching `todayUtc()`/`yesterdayUtc()`'s string returns exactly — the comparison in `applyProgressUpdate` was already correct; there was no Date/string mismatch to fix.
  - **Skipped, not worth it at this stage — migration `0008`'s `ADD CONSTRAINT ... CHECK` should use `NOT VALID` + a separate `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` to avoid a full-table scan/lock.** Correct general Postgres advice, but: (1) this migration is already applied to the dev DB and committed — rewriting it after the fact violates migration immutability (the right move, if ever needed, is a _new_ migration, not editing 0008); (2) `xp_events` currently has a handful of dev-seed rows, so the lock/scan this guards against doesn't exist yet at this pre-launch stage. Left as-is; worth revisiting as a real migration if/when this table has production scale.
  - **Re-verified after all fixes:** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean project-wide. Real dev server: homepage and both API routes' 401 paths still correct with the new namespaced rate-limit keys.

Needed because this feature renumbers the 8 pre-Phase-10 concepts' `orderIndex` to their new position in the final teaching order (e.g. `event-loop` moves from position 1 → 9 within `javascript-runtime`), which only takes effect on re-seed with an update-capable upsert.

Slugs unchanged (no URL/FK impact).
- Authored descriptions for all 68 new concepts (build-plan.md only specified titles) and a slug for each (lowercase kebab-case, filler words dropped, "&" spelled out as "and"), matching the existing 8 entries' one-sentence tone.
- Ran `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` against the dev DB: 76 concepts upserted. Verified via a disposable script (deleted after) that every category's `orderIndex` sequence is exactly `1..N` with no gaps/dupes, matching build-plan.md's per-category ordering.
- **Verified live on the dev server** (after clearing a stale `.next` cache that was serving a pre-rename `unstable_cache` snapshot from an earlier dev-server instance still running on port 3000 — same class of issue as the Feature 01 stale-Turbopack-cache note; killed the stale process, cleared `.next`, restarted clean): Learn index shows the correct per-category concept counts (14/9/12/10/8/8/8/7); the renamed `react-rendering` and `aria-roles-and-semantic-html` concepts render their new titles correctly; a brand-new concept (`hoisting-temporal-dead-zone`) shows the existing "coming soon" empty state correctly on all of Simulate/Challenge/Interview/Build, with no console errors; the JS Runtime sidebar accordion (now 14 items) has no overflow. `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean.
- **Observed, not fixed (out of scope for this feature):** `YourTurnRail.tsx` (Feature 21) is still the original hardcoded Event-Loop-specific placeholder ("Promise.then()" quiz options, "Call Stack"/"Microtasks" related topics) regardless of which concept is open — it says so in its own comment ("Feature 22+ wires the real quiz and per-concept data"), but that wiring never happened. Was cosmetic-only on 8 concepts; now visibly wrong on 76. Worth a dedicated pass, not part of Feature 40's data-only scope.
- **Follow-up (`/review` skill, 1 Important finding, fixed):** the `concepts` `onConflictDoUpdate` set its `isPremium` column from the proposed insert row's value, but `ConceptSeed` never exposed an `isPremium` field, so every reseed silently forced `is_premium` back to its column default (`false`) for all 76 rows — confirmed live-facing, not dead, since `CategoryAccordionItem.tsx` already renders a `Lock` icon off `concept.isPremium`. Harmless today (no concept has ever been premium, and Phase 10 doesn't need one to be), but would have silently clobbered any future out-of-band premium flag the moment someone reran the seed. **Fixed:** added `isPremium?: boolean` to `ConceptSeed`, mirroring `ChallengeSeed`/`ProjectBriefSeed`'s existing optional-if-free convention. Re-ran the seed to confirm it's stable (no concept's `isPremium` changed). `tsc`/`eslint` clean.
- **Follow-up (user-reported after merge, on a dev server that had been running continuously across this feature's several reseeds):** a brand-new concept page 404'd, plus an unrelated-looking auth glitch (appeared logged out, logging back in bounced to the homepage still showing "Log In"). Diagnosed rather than assumed: `curl` to the 404'ing route reproduced it live while a direct DB query confirmed the row existed correctly (right slug, right category) — a stale-cache-read problem, not a data problem. Root cause: `getConceptBySlug`/`getConceptCatalog` are `unstable_cache`-wrapped (`tags: ["concepts"], revalidate: 3600`), and `scripts/seed.ts` is a standalone process with no way to call `revalidateTag("concepts")` on an already-running dev server — a slug queried before the seed added/renamed it stays cached as a miss for up to an hour. Restarting the dev server (clears Next's in-memory Data Cache) resolved both the 404 and the auth glitch in one step. Documented under Notes below so a future reseed-while-running doesn't get mis-diagnosed as a real regression.

- **Follow-up (`/review` skill, 1 Important finding, fixed):** the `auto-bound-event-emitter` project brief (`this-binding-execution-context`) was titled and framed around guaranteeing correct `this` binding, but its solution never actually touched `this` — `emit` called every handler as a bare `handler(payload)`, and none of the 3 test cases exercised `this` either. **Fixed:** `on(event, handler, context)` now takes an optional `context`, and `emit` always invokes via `handler.call(context, payload)` — a bare method reference (`dash.handleLogin`) genuinely keeps the right `this` with no manual `.bind()` by the caller, which is what the brief always claimed. Test cases rewritten to actually verify it (independent `this` per handler, plus the no-context fallback). Re-verified: `tsc`/`eslint` clean, seed idempotent, fix confirmed live in the DB.
- **Follow-up (user-reported after reviewing the live page):** `memory-management-leaks` originally had no Challenge — a deliberate planning decision ("memory leak detection isn't unit-testable in a browser sandbox"). User asked for it to have one anyway, same as every other concept. **Added `find-leaked-listeners`** — reframed the exercise around the one leak pattern that _is_ cleanly testable without a real heap: given a chronological log of `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` calls, find the ids added more times than removed (a bookkeeping problem, not a GC-observation problem). `medium` difficulty, premium (matches the advanced-concept-challenges-are-premium rule). This brings `CHALLENGES` to **15 total (3 pre-existing + 12 new: the 11 above plus this one)**, re-confirmed via `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` ("15 challenge(s) upserted"). `esm-vs-commonjs` remains the one concept in this category with no Challenge — revisit if the same request comes up for it.
- **Follow-up (user-reported: "Run Tests" not working for any concept's Challenge or Build tab) — a real architectural gap this feature's planning missed entirely.** `getTestSpec`/`getBuildTestSpec` (`src/features/practice/sandbox/testSpecs.ts`, `src/features/build/data/testSpecs.ts`) are hardcoded, code-side `Record<slug, SandboxTest[]>` maps — the DB's `test_cases` column is display-labels only (documented in both files' own header comments, missed during this feature's research/planning). None of the 12 new Challenges or 13 new Project briefs had an entry, so every one fell back to "Automated tests for this challenge are coming soon," regardless of concept. **This means Challenge/Build content is not actually fully data-driven the way Understand/Interview are** — a correction to carry into Features 42–48: each new Challenge/Project brief needs a matching hand-written `SandboxTest[]` entry (real executable `assert`/`assertEqual`/`delay` source, keyed by slug), not just a seed.ts row. **Fixed:** wrote real test specs for all 25 new items. Verified for real, not just by inspection — a standalone Node harness (reproducing the sandbox's exact `eq`/`assert`/`assertEqual`/`delay` helpers) ran all 79 assertions against the actual `solutionCode` for every one (all passed), and a deliberately wrong solution was confirmed to fail (ruling out a vacuous/always-passing harness). `tsc`/`eslint` clean on both files. Confirmed live: the previously-missing test labels now appear in `hoisting-temporal-dead-zone`'s page data and the "coming soon" fallback is gone.
- **Follow-up (CodeRabbit review on PR #48, 2 findings, both fixed):** `array-object-methods-immutability.mdx`'s `whatsHappening` grouped `Object.entries`/`Object.keys` with spread as if all three produce a non-mutating object copy — `Object.entries`/`Object.keys` actually return arrays, not object copies, only spread does. Reworded so only spread is described that way. Separately, `progress-tracker.md`'s own Challenge-count arithmetic didn't reconcile: "11 new + event-loop's replacement" double-counted the replacement (already one of the 11), and the `find-leaked-listeners` follow-up above never updated the running total. (2) Of this feature's 3 brand-new `ff-system-design` flagship interview questions, 2 (`api-design-data-fetching-strategy`, `frontend-architecture-patterns`) were marked `isPremium: true` but the 3rd (`designing-real-time-updates`) wasn't, despite all three backing concepts being equally premium — an inconsistency introduced within this feature's own new rows, distinct from the already-known, already-deferred platform-wide gap (Feature 43) of interview questions generally not carrying `isPremium`, which stays out of scope. **Fixed:** added `isPremium: true` to the `designing-real-time-updates` flagship. Re-ran `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` (counts unchanged — 74/395/76, both fixes applied via update-on-slug/order_index, confirmed directly against the live rows), re-ran the standalone real-file verification harness (36/36 still pass), and re-ran `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` (clean; also cleared a stale `.next` artifact left by the earlier live-server spot-check, unrelated to source).
- **Follow-up (inline review comments, 2 findings, both fixed):** `designing-real-time-updates.mdx`'s `keyInsight` claimed SSE "gets full-duplex's benefits," which is backwards — SSE is one-way server-to-client push only; full-duplex describes WebSockets, not SSE. **Fixed:** reworded to describe SSE accurately (one-way push over a single plain-HTTP connection) while keeping the direction-vs-frequency guidance and the WebSocket-full-duplex contrast intact. Separately, `dedupe-and-cache-fetcher`'s `createFetcher` only deleted a key from `inflight` inside the success (`.then`) callback — a rejected `fetchFn(key)` left that key stuck in `inflight` permanently, so every subsequent `get(key)` call would return the same stale rejected promise forever instead of retrying. **Fixed:** moved the `inflight.delete(key)` cleanup into `.finally()` so it runs on both the resolve and reject paths. Verified directly against the real extracted `solutionCode` (not just inspection): a fetcher whose `fetchFn` rejects on the first call and succeeds on the second now correctly rejects the first `get()`, then successfully retries and resolves the second — confirming the fix, not just the absence of a crash. Re-ran the existing 36-assertion real-file harness (all still pass), re-seeded (counts unchanged), `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean.

  **Currently building:** 28 Practice Hub + Category List Pages, on branch `feature/28-practice-hub`. **This entry documents the feature's full build order, oldest-first — read top to bottom.** It started as a UI-first pass with mock data (per Rule 1, stopping for user visual sign-off before wiring real data); the logic pass (real DB queries), the standalone-content rework, and full content-authoring across all 5 categories all followed later in this same arc — see the "Logic pass" and later follow-ups below for the completed state. Ran the `architect` skill (manually) first; see Pre-Feature-28 decision above for the Hub→List restructure this feature builds against.
- Built `/practice` (category grid, mirrors Learn Index's `CategoryCard` pattern via new `ChallengeCategoryCard`, plus a logged-in-only stats strip and `ContinueChallengeCard`) and `/practice/[category]` (breadcrumb + client-side-filterable `ChallengeCard` grid via `ChallengeListClient` — search/difficulty/solved-status filters run purely over the local array, no data-fetching yet). New files under `src/features/practice/{lib/mockPracticeData.ts,components/{ChallengeCategoryCard,ContinueChallengeCard,ChallengeCard,ChallengeListClient,PracticeBreadcrumb}.tsx}` + `src/app/(app)/practice/{page.tsx,[category]/page.tsx}`. All data is mock (`mockPracticeData.ts`) — 22 hand-picked challenges across all 8 categories using real Phase-10 concept titles for authenticity, not generic placeholders.
- **Decision made mid-build:** `ChallengeCategoryCard` is a deliberate sibling of Learn's `CategoryCard`, not a shared/generalized component — different data source (challenge counts vs. concept counts) for the same visual shape; reuses `CATEGORY_META`/`COLOR_CLASSES` directly rather than re-deriving them. No shared `Breadcrumb` primitive existed, so `PracticeBreadcrumb` was built Practice-local (single consumer) rather than promoted to `components/shared/`.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; Playwright screenshots (desktop 1440px + mobile 390px, light and dark) of both pages confirm the category grid, stats strip, continue card (structure verified by code review — no logged-in session was driven through OAuth for this check), filter bar, difficulty badges, and completion checkmarks all render correctly with no layout breakage; a headless console-error check across all three routes (`/practice`, `/practice/react`, `/practice/javascript-runtime`) came back clean (0 errors).
- `context/build-plan.md` and `context/ui-registry.md` already updated (see Pre-Feature-28 decision + `ui-registry.md` → Practice Components → "Practice Hub + Category List (Feature 28)"). At this point in the build: not yet pushed — waiting on user sign-off on the UI before the logic pass (real `challenges`/`concepts`/`user_challenge_submissions` queries) and before opening the PR. (Superseded — see "Logic pass" below: real queries landed, the feature was later pushed, and PR #67 is now open.)
- **Follow-up (user visual review of the UI-first pass, same session, before any push/PR):** 4 changes requested against the running dev server (real screenshots, not mockups). Resolved a real scope question first via a clarifying question (per the "genuinely the user's decision" bar) rather than guessing: BFE's own Practice section mixes coding challenges, quizzes, and general interview Q&A under one "Practice" nav item — user confirmed Practice should stay scoped to coding challenges only; quizzes (a content type FF doesn't have) and interview questions (already Interview Prep's, Feature 30-32) stay out, avoiding both new schema work and duplicating a section that isn't built yet.
  1. **Category set narrowed from 8 to 5** — dropped Browser Internals/Accessibility/Performance per explicit request (their concepts remain fully covered in Learn, just not surfaced as a Practice category). New `features/practice/lib/practiceCategories.ts` exports `PRACTICE_CATEGORIES` (the 5 kept) and `PRACTICE_CATEGORY_LABELS`, a Practice-only BFE-style rename ("JavaScript Coding Questions", "React Coding Questions", "CSS Questions", "TypeScript Puzzles", "Front-End System Design Questions") — deliberately **not** applied to `CATEGORY_META`, which Learn still uses unchanged, since renaming the shared meta would have relabeled Learn's own cards too. `mockPracticeData.ts`'s 7 challenges belonging to the 3 dropped categories were removed (22 → 15 mock challenges); the Hub grid and both pages' category validation now key off `PRACTICE_CATEGORIES`, not the full `CONCEPT_CATEGORIES`.
  2. **Category card restyled** — challenge count moved from bottom-of-card text into a top-right pill badge (BFE's visual convention), freeing the bottom row for just the logged-in solved-progress/`Start →` indicator.
  3. **Category List page: added a Company filter, switched grid → list.** New `companies: string[]` field on `MockChallenge` (1-2 real company names per challenge, e.g. "Google, Uber" — picked from the user's attached BFE company-logos screenshot, text-only, no logo assets sourced for this pass, deliberately, given the effort that would take for ~14 trademarked marks in a mock-data pass). Company filter is a multi-select `DropdownMenuCheckboxItem` list (base-ui's dropdown-menu primitive, already installed) whose options are derived from the _actual_ companies present in that category's challenges, not a fixed master list, so no filter option is ever a dead end. The old grid `ChallengeCard` was deleted and replaced by `ChallengeListRow` — a dense single-line-per-challenge layout (completion icon, title + concept tag + company chips, trailing difficulty pill), closer to BFE's numbered-list density than the original card grid.

Investigated rather than assuming the code was wrong: `netstat -ano` showed **two zombie dev server processes** squatting on ports 3000 and 3001, left over from earlier in this session — `pkill -f "next dev"` had silently failed to match them (Windows/Git Bash spawns `npm run dev` as a bare `node` process; the pattern never matched its argv), so the user's browser had been hitting a pre-fix server the entire time. Killed both by their actual port-bound PID (`netstat` → PID → `taskkill //F //PID`, not by name pattern) and started one clean instance; confirmed via fresh screenshot that the current code already had everything from the prior round.
  2. **Numbering + row redesign:** once looking at the real current state, user still didn't like the row design and asked for numbering. Added a numbered circle badge (same shape as `QuestionCard`'s index circle from Interview Prep) that swaps to a green `Check` once solved, and moved the difficulty pill onto the title's own line (previously a 3rd column spanning the full row height) so the row reads as one tight primary line + one metadata line instead of a 3-column spread.
  3. **Real mobile overflow bug, caught by testing the redesign at 390px before shipping it (not by the user reporting it):** the difficulty badge rendered partially cut off at the viewport edge ("Ea", "Ha", "Medi"). Root-caused with `getBoundingClientRect()` walked up the full DOM chain from the row to `<html>` rather than guessed at — a classic flexbox gap: a flex item's default `min-width` is its content's natural width, not `0`, so the title never actually shrank low enough for `truncate` to engage, even with `min-w-0 flex-1` present on intermediate wrappers. The overflow surfaced 3 levels up at the page's own `mx-auto max-w-4xl px-6` container (rendering wider than the 390px viewport) and was invisible to a plain `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` check because `<body>` has `overflow-x: clip`, silently absorbing it. **Fixed properly, not by re-guessing more `min-w-0` placements:** switched the row's two split points to CSS Grid with explicit `grid-cols-[auto_minmax(0,1fr)]` / `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto]` — a `minmax(0, 1fr)` track has no min-content floor the way a flex item does, so this is a structural fix, not a workaround. Re-verified via the same DOM-chain measurement (every ancestor now reports exactly 390px, no silent overflow) and a fresh screenshot (full difficulty text visible, long titles truncate with an ellipsis instead of pushing content off-screen).
  - Re-verified after this follow-up: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; desktop + mobile screenshots confirm the numbered rows and the overflow fix; a full console-error + overflow check across `/practice`, `/practice/javascript-runtime`, `/practice/react` came back clean (0 errors, `scrollWidth === clientWidth`). Dev server stopped cleanly via its real PID this time (not by name pattern) to avoid leaving another zombie. Still not pushed — pending user sign-off.

- **Follow-up 5 (real width bug, found while chasing what looked like a simple "make it wider" request, same session, before any push/PR):** user compared the same reference screenshot again and correctly flagged the search bar/rows as noticeably narrower than the reference, calling the page "very compact."
  1. **First pass (wrong fix, caught before declaring done):** read `ui-rules.md` → Layout and found both pages were using Learn Index's narrow `max-w-4xl` (~900px reading-column exception) instead of the platform's actual general page standard, `max-w-7xl` (1280px, documented in `ui-rules.md`). Bumped both pages' container to `max-w-7xl px-6 lg:px-8`. **Screenshotted before reporting it fixed — it looked identical to before, not wider at all.**
  2. **Root-caused rather than re-guessing a bigger number:** measured every ancestor's width via `getBoundingClientRect()` (same technique as the earlier row-overflow bug) and found the page's own content div rendered at ~613px — completely ignoring both the old and new `max-width` values. Cause: the div is a direct child of `AppLayout`'s `<main className="flex flex-1 flex-col">`, making it a flex item in a column flex container. A flex item with `mx-auto` (auto cross-axis margins) but no explicit `width` doesn't stretch under the default `align-items: stretch` — auto margins disable stretch, so the item shrinks to its own content's width instead, and `max-width` never gets a chance to apply. Confirmed precisely: raising `max-w-4xl` → `max-w-7xl` alone produced _zero_ measured change. **Fixed by adding `w-full`** (`mx-auto w-full max-w-7xl ...`) — re-measured and the div correctly rendered at exactly 1280px this time.
  3. **Checked whether this was a wider, pre-existing bug before closing it out:** Learn's Index page uses the visually-identical `mx-auto max-w-4xl` pattern _without_ `w-full` — worth checking rather than assuming it shares the bug. It doesn't: Learn's content div sits inside `LearnLayout`'s sidebar wrapper (`<div className="min-w-0 flex-1">`), which itself is a plain block element (no `flex` class), not a flex container — so the stretch/auto-margin interaction never triggers there. Confirmed via the same `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement (Learn's div correctly renders at its full 896px). No other page affected; nothing else to fix.
  - Re-verified after the real fix: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean; screenshots at 1920px, 1440px, and 390px (mobile unaffected, as expected — `max-width` only caps the upper bound) all confirm the page now genuinely uses the wider container, matching the reference's proportions; console-error check across `/practice`, `/practice/javascript-runtime`, `/practice/react` clean. Documented the `w-full`-inside-a-flex-`<main>` gotcha in `ui-registry.md` so a future page built directly under `AppLayout` doesn't repeat it. Dev server stopped via PID, `.next` cleared preemptively. Still not pushed.

- **Follow-up (React Custom Hooks & Patterns Roadmap content authored, same session, before any push/PR):** user supplied a second roadmap document — 7 stages (R1–R7), 38 questions covering React fundamentals through custom hooks, DOM/timing hooks, data/form hooks, data-fetching/state-management patterns, and hook-internals/component-pattern capstones. Investigated the sandbox first rather than assuming it could run this content as-is: confirmed (via a research pass over `src/lib/sandbox/`) that the sandbox has **no React/ReactDOM loaded and no JSX transform** — user code is injected as a literal `<script>` tag, so real component rendering isn't possible without building substantial new sandbox capability (a JSX transform + bundled React + mount/act helpers), which was out of scope for a content-authoring pass. Also found the existing 12 `"react"`-category entries in `scripts/seed.ts` are Learn **concepts** (prose only, no code), not `CHALLENGES` — so Practice's `"react"` category had zero challenge content before this, consistent with the note above.
  - **Resolved the sandbox gap by following this codebase's own established precedent**, not inventing a new pattern: the existing standalone JS roadmap already has entries (`mini-react-hooks-runtime`, `proxy-reactive-store`) that model React internals as plain, pure-JS-testable functions instead of real JSX. Extended that same approach to all 38 hook questions — each hook becomes a factory function returning the same shape the real hook would (`{ getValue(), toggle(), ... }`), driven by explicit calls instead of a React render cycle. Where a hook is fundamentally about the DOM (`useClickOutside`, `useEventListener`, `useHover`, `useFocus`), used real DOM APIs and dispatched real events inside the sandbox (`document.createElement`, `dispatchEvent(new MouseEvent(...))`) — the sandbox's iframe genuinely supports this, confirmed already working for the JS roadmap's own DOM-API stage. Where a hook depends on a browser API that's unreliable/unavailable in a bare iframe (`useOnScreen`'s `IntersectionObserver`, `useWindowSize`'s real viewport), the hook takes an **injectable dependency** (a fake observer factory, a fake window-like object) instead — the same technique real test suites use to test these hooks, not a workaround unique to this platform.
  - **Two topics overlapped with existing `javascript-runtime` content** (`mini-react-hooks-runtime` covers useState+useEffect together; `proxy-reactive-store` is a Vue-style auto-tracking reactive system) — rather than duplicating them, this roadmap's versions were deliberately scoped/angled differently: `usestate-from-scratch` is useState-only with its own distinct test scenarios (functional updaters, multi-slot ordering), `usereducer-from-scratch` and `usememo-usecallback-from-scratch` are separate hooks entirely, and `valtio-style-proxy-store` uses Valtio's actual API shape (a directly-mutable `store` object + coarse-grained `subscribe`, no per-property dependency tracking) rather than re-implementing Vue's `effect`-based tracking.
  - All 38 entries added to `scripts/seed.ts`'s `CHALLENGES` array (`category: "react"`, `orderIndex` 1190–1227, stage-ordered) with full descriptions, starter/solution code, DB-display `testCases`, hints, and roadmap-sourced `companies` attributions; matching `SandboxTest` blocks (3–5 executable assertions each) added to `testSpecs.ts`'s `TEST_SPECS` map.

Designed all 37 CSS challenges as JS functions that build/style real DOM elements, tested via genuine `getComputedStyle()`/`getBoundingClientRect()`/pseudo-element (`getComputedStyle(el, "::before")`) assertions against the sandbox's real layout engine — meaningfully higher-fidelity than reducing every question to abstract arithmetic, since these questions are fundamentally about real rendered CSS behavior (centering, Flexbox/Grid geometry, cascade/specificity, pseudo-elements), not just numbers.

Two real, useful findings came out of this: (1) a CSS `transition` was still mid-animation when a test read `getComputedStyle().transform` synchronously right after triggering it — fixed by awaiting past the transition's duration first, the same discipline already used for the JS/React batches' timers; (2) Grid/Flex row-wrapping detection via comparing `getBoundingClientRect().top` needs items with real (non-zero) height, or empty rows collapse to the same y-coordinate and produce a false "didn't wrap" result — confirmed the underlying Grid `auto-fit` behavior was actually correct (visually verified 3-then-2 row wrapping) once real content/height was given to the test items. Also confirmed real matrix-transform parsing, `:checked`-driven pseudo-class re-evaluation on a programmatic `.checked` set, and `::selection` computed-style queries all work as expected in this environment — none of these had ever been exercised in this codebase before. Adopted plain string `split`/`replace`/`indexOf` for parsing computed values (transform matrices, CSS text) instead of regex throughout, to sidestep the nested-template-literal backslash-escaping risk entirely rather than manage it.

- **Follow-up (TypeScript Type-Challenges Roadmap content authored + new real type-checking infrastructure built, same session, before any push/PR):** user supplied a fourth roadmap document — 8 stages, 68 "implement X from scratch" type-level questions (`Pick`, `Omit`, `infer`-based introspection, template-literal string manipulation, tuple/array recursion, type-level logic and arithmetic, and capstone "trick" types like `UnionToIntersection`). This one is categorically different from JS/React/CSS: a TypeScript type's correctness is a **compile-time-only property** — types are erased at compile time, so there is no runtime behavior to execute or assert against at all, unlike CSS (which genuinely renders) or React hooks (whose state logic genuinely runs as JS). Investigated first: confirmed Monaco (`@monaco-editor/react`, already a dependency) is used purely for cosmetic syntax highlighting with no live TS language-service diagnostics wired in anywhere, `typescript` itself was a devDependency only (never shipped to the browser), and there was zero prior precedent for type-level grading anywhere in the codebase.

User chose the most ambitious option: **build real TypeScript type-checking**, not degrade the questions into disguised runtime-function exercises.

  - **A real, non-obvious TypeScript compiler bug found and fixed mid-batch, not glossed over:** the `assertNever(value: never): never` exhaustiveness-checking question originally tested `Parameters<>[0]` and `ReturnType<>` separately via `Equal<>`. The `ReturnType` check failed even against the objectively-correct solution — isolated via a dedicated diagnostic script to a genuine, documented TypeScript quirk: `infer`-based extraction (`ReturnType<T>`'s internal `T extends (...args: any) => infer R ? R : any`) resolves unreliably to `any` specifically when the function's _parameter_ type is `never`, confirmed empirically (`Equal<ReturnType<typeof assertNever>, any>` evaluated `true`, and `ReturnType<typeof assertNever> extends never ? true : false` produced the union `boolean` — the exact signature of `any` silently distributing through a conditional). Fixed by testing the whole function signature at once (`Equal<typeof assertNever, (value: never) => never>`, already empirically confirmed reliable) instead of decomposing it — sidesteps the quirk entirely rather than working around it.
  - **Mass-validated all 68 solutions against the real grading engine, not just type-checked the surrounding TS file:** maintained a byte-identical mirror of `gradeTypeChallenge.ts`'s logic (minus the `server-only` guard, which correctly blocks execution outside Next's bundler — confirmed by deliberately trying to run the real file directly and watching it fail exactly as intended) as a `tsx`-run validation harness, re-run after every stage.
