### Feature 29 — Challenge Editor Page

**Last completed:** 29 Challenge Editor Page — ran the `architect` skill before building. Branch `feature/29-challenge-editor-page` off `develop`.

**Scope expansion, decided in the architect session, before any code:** the user's initial ask matched `build-plan.md`'s spec (tabbed panel + dual-grading editor). Mid-session, across three escalating messages, the user asked for far more: an official + community solution system, a discussion page, an admin-attached YouTube video link, and social share to X/Facebook/Instagram with a branded image (logo, category, question number, "some animation if possible"). Rather than build any of it silently, two real platform constraints were surfaced and put to the user before designing around them: (1) **Instagram has no share-intent API for third-party websites** — X and Facebook both support real pre-filled compose URLs, Instagram categorically does not, for any site, not just this one; (2) **a link-preview image can never be animated** on any of these platforms — it's always a single static frame, regardless of what's technically renderable server-side. Three forkable decisions were then put to the user via `AskUserQuestion` rather than guessed: Instagram handling (chose: copy caption + download image + open Instagram, closest achievable to one-click), discussion/solution viewing gate (chose: open to everyone, no solve-to-view spoiler gate, unlike LeetCode's model), reply nesting depth (chose: flat, one level, no arbitrary threading). Two more (submission-write architecture, XP amount) were proposed with a recommendation and silently accepted; one (Solution panel inclusion) the user answered directly ("SolutionPanel should be there").

**What was built, roughly in the order below (all in one continuous session, not separate PRs):**

1. **Schema:** `challenges.video_url` (nullable text) and a new `challenge_discussion_posts` table (flat one-level replies via a nullable self-referencing `parent_id`, no title on replies) — migration `0012_rapid_weapon_omega`. See `architecture.md` for both.
2. **`CHALLENGE_SOLVED_XP = 10`** added to `lib/constants.ts` — deliberately below the XP System table's originally-documented 20 XP (that table row is now corrected too): Practice has ~620 standalone challenges vs. ~76 Learn concepts, so a Learn-tab-rate reward would let grinding badly out-earn the curriculum.
3. **`applyChallengeSubmission.ts`** (mirrors `applyProgressUpdate.ts`'s transactional pattern, `todayUtc`/`yesterdayUtc` exported from there for reuse) + **`POST /api/practice/submit`**: writes one `user_challenge_submissions` row per Run Tests attempt, pass or fail (the table has no unique constraint, by design); XP + streak only on a user's first-ever pass, guarded by locking the _profile_ row (not the submissions table, which has nothing stable to lock on a brand-new pass) before the "already passed?" check.
4. **`useChallengeGrading(category, slug)`** — unifies the sandbox path (`useSandbox`) and the TypeScript compiler path (`/api/practice/grade-type-challenge`, already built in Feature 28) behind one `{status, result, hasTests, run}` contract. Caught and fixed a real rules-of-hooks violation before shipping: an early `return` inside the `category === "typescript"` branch was skipping the sandbox-path `useCallback` on other renders — fixed by calling every hook unconditionally and branching only the returned value.
5. **`getChallengeBySlug`/`hasPassedChallenge`** (`features/practice/lib/queries.ts`) — full-detail counterpart to the catalog's summary rows; `questionNumber` is recomputed from the category's `orderIndex`-sorted position (same number the list page shows), not stored, so it can't drift.
6. **The page + workspace**: `src/app/(app)/practice/[category]/[slug]/page.tsx` (`generateMetadata` — this app's first per-page one), `ChallengeEditorHeader`, `EditorTabs` (5 tabs: Description/Hints/Test Cases/Solution/Discussion), `ChallengeEditorWorkspace`. **Test Cases tab needed no new component** — `TestResultsPanel` with `result={null} status="idle"` already renders exactly the static spec-preview the tab needs.
7. **`PracticeBreadcrumb` generalized** from a Hub-only 2-level `currentLabel` prop to a `{backHref, crumbs[]}` shape (second consumer → generalize, don't duplicate, same precedent as `CodeEditor`'s promotion). Category page's call site updated to match.
8. **Solution tab**: reuses `SolutionPanel` (pass-or-3-attempts gate, same as Learn) plus a video-walkthrough link card when `videoUrl` is set.
9. **Discussion**: `challengeDiscussionPosts` schema (above) + `getDiscussionPosts` (live, `cache()`-deduped, never `unstable_cache`'d — user-generated content) + `POST /api/practice/discussion` (rejects a reply-to-a-reply server-side, not just via the UI's missing affordance — verified directly, see below) + `DiscussionThread` (renders `body`/`code` as plain text/`<pre>`, deliberately not the shared `Markdown` renderer, even though that renderer happens to be `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`-free — see `security.md` → XSS Prevention).
10. **Share**: `ShareButtons` (X/Facebook real intents, Instagram copy-and-open), `FacebookLogo`/`InstagramLogo` (hand-inlined brand SVGs, lucide-react ships neither — same reason `XLogo` already exists), `opengraph-image.tsx` (Next's `ImageResponse` file convention, read from this project's own `node_modules/next/dist/docs` per `AGENTS.md`'s breaking-changes warning before writing any of it).

**Real bugs caught during verification, not just "looked right":**

- **OG image URL isn't a predictable path.** First implementation guessed `/practice/[category]/[slug]/opengraph-image` for the Instagram download step — 404'd. Next actually serves it at a build-hashed filename (`opengraph-image-<hash>?<hash>`). Fixed by reading the real URL off the page's own `<meta property="og:image">` tag at click-time instead of constructing a path.
- **Mobile breadcrumb overflow, caught by an actual 390px screenshot, not assumed safe.** A 3-level trail (Practice › React Coding Questions › The React Counter app) visibly overlapped at 390px. Fixed by hiding the middle crumb (with its own chevron, same `<span>`) below `sm` — the back arrow still lands on the category page, so context isn't lost, just not spelled out in text on mobile.
- **Logged-out TypeScript Run Tests surfaced a raw "Unauthorized" as if it were a test failure**, and was silently incrementing the local attempt counter toward the solution-unlock threshold on an infra error, not a real wrong-answer attempt. Caught by actually clicking Run Tests logged out during verification (the grading route's auth gate is intentional per `security.md`, but the UI didn't communicate it). Fixed: Run Tests is disabled for TypeScript challenges when logged out, with a "Log in to run TypeScript tests." note, so the broken path is unreachable through normal use rather than patched after the fact.
- **Monaco + Playwright typing produced garbled/duplicated braces** (`page.keyboard.type`/`insertText` of a multi-line snippet interacted with Monaco's auto-closing-bracket + auto-indent features, inserting a duplicate `}`) — a test-harness artifact, not an app bug; worked around by typing single-line equivalents for verification, confirmed byte-identical via `editor.getValue()` before trusting any Run Tests result.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint src/` clean throughout. Dev server driven with Playwright (`npx playwright`, no project dependency needed — resolved on demand): desktop 1440px + mobile 390px, light + dark, all 5 tabs, a real failing sandbox run (0/3, real assertion errors) and a real passing one (3/3, typed via the single-line workaround above) for a React challenge, the TypeScript page's real syntax highlighting and its (now-fixed) logged-out-disabled state — zero console errors across every screenshot. `applyChallengeSubmission`'s DB logic verified directly against the dev DB (bypassing HTTP/session cookies entirely — printing a real session token into the transcript was correctly blocked by the auto-mode classifier, so this was the right approach, not a workaround): failed → row written, 0 XP; first pass → row written, +10 XP + streak bonus; re-pass of an already-solved challenge → row written, 0 XP awarded twice. Discussion insert + reply-to-reply server-side rejection verified the same way. All verification writes were made against real profile/challenge rows and fully reverted afterward (submission rows deleted, xp_events rows deleted by exact primary key — one stray row from an earlier broad-window cleanup attempt that the auto-mode classifier correctly blocked was instead found via a read-only query and deleted by its exact id — and `profiles.xp`/streak fields restored to their pre-test values); confirmed via a final read-only query that the test profile's `xp`, `streakCurrent`, `streakLongest`, `streakLastActivity` all match what they were before this session touched them.

**Docs updated:** `architecture.md` (new `challenge_discussion_posts` table, `challenges.video_url`, `user_challenge_submissions`'s "now has a real write path" correction, XP System table's `20 XP → 10 XP` correction), `security.md` (two new rate-limit buckets, a note on `DiscussionThread`'s plain-text rendering under XSS Prevention), `build-plan.md` (Feature 29 section rewritten to note the shipped scope vs. the original spec), `ui-registry.md` (full "Challenge Editor Page (Feature 29)" section).

**Follow-up (layout + content-rendering + SEO/GEO redesign, real user feedback against the running page, same session, before any push/PR):** the user reviewed the running Editor page against three reference screenshots (their own build, BFE.dev, LeetCode) and asked for four changes, all shipped:

1. **Layout felt "crowded," editor felt like "a small window to write in," not a serious editor.** The even `flex-1`/fixed-`520px` split was inverted to an editor-dominant `lg:grid-cols-[1fr_1.4fr]`, with the right column made `lg:sticky` so it stays put while a long description scrolls — closer to the LeetCode reference's own feel. Added a JS/TS language badge above the editor (new `--color-ts-badge-*` tokens, sibling to the pre-existing `--color-js-badge-*` pair) and `PrevNextNav` (category-scoped prev/next question buttons in the header, new `getAdjacentChallenges` query). **Ad-slot readiness was interpreted as "don't design into a corner," not "build a placeholder"** — the original build-plan spec explicitly said not to build placeholder ad UI, and that instruction was kept; the layout just isn't full-bleed edge-to-edge, so a future non-intrusive slot has room without a redesign.
2. **Description read as too minimal vs. BFE's reference, and example code was flat white text, not colorful.** Put a real decision to the user rather than guessing which half of this to act on: fix the _rendering_ (colorful syntax highlighting, applies platform-wide immediately, zero content changes) vs. also _rewriting content_ (would mean redoing ~620 existing challenge descriptions to BFE's depth — a distinct, multi-session content-authoring effort on the scale of Feature 28's 5 roadmap batches). **User chose both.** The rendering fix shipped this session: a small regex-based JS/TS tokenizer added to the shared `Markdown.tsx` (5 new theme-invariant `--color-editor-*` tokens matching Monaco's real vs-dark palette, zero new dependencies), which retroactively colorizes every existing fenced code block platform-wide (Learn's Challenge/Interview tabs too, not just Practice) since `ChallengeDescription` already delegates to the shared renderer. **The content-rewrite half is not started** — it needs its own session(s), scoped and tracked the way the Feature 28 roadmap batches were, not folded into this redesign pass.
3. **Video solution was in the wrong place** ("I told you... but where") — moved out of the Solution tab entirely to a pill button directly above the editor, next to the language badge, `target="_blank"`. New hand-inlined `YoutubeLogo` (lucide-react ships no brand icons, same reason `XLogo`/`FacebookLogo`/`InstagramLogo` exist). **Verified with a real, reversible DB flip** (temporarily set `video_url` on one live row, screenshotted, cleared it back to `null`) rather than trusting the code alone — and this caught the `unstable_cache` disk-persistence gotcha firsthand (the first post-write screenshot still showed no video until the dev server restarted with `.next` cleared).
4. **No SEO/GEO work had been done.** Added: per-page `alternates.canonical` + explicit `robots` + `openGraph.type:"article"`; two JSON-LD blocks (`LearningResource`, `BreadcrumbList`) rendered as real `<script>` text-content children (confirmed via raw HTML inspection that React does **not** HTML-escape this, unlike a normal text node — the JSON comes through byte-valid, no `&quot;`-mangling) rather than `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; a shared `toPlainTextSummary()` markdown-stripper feeding the meta description, OG/Twitter tags, and the JSON-LD description from one implementation; a `RelatedChallenges` "More {Category}" section (new `getRelatedChallenges` query, a window of siblings _centered on the current challenge's position_, not a fixed first-5) for genuine internal linking. Base URL for canonical/OG/JSON-LD is derived per-request from `headers()` (`host`/`x-forwarded-proto`) — no `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` env var was added, since none exists in this project and none was needed. **Deliberately did not add a `<meta name="keywords">` tag** — explained to the user as a dead SEO lever (Google has ignored it for ranking for over a decade); effort went into the levers that actually work instead.

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint src/` clean throughout. Playwright screenshots (desktop 1512px, mobile 390px, light + dark, the TypeScript page, a real fenced-code-block challenge) confirm the wider sticky editor, both language badges, prev/next chevrons, the Related section, and the syntax-highlighted code block — using the _exact_ example from the user's own screenshot (`improve-full-name-formatter`) as a direct before/after. Raw HTML inspection (`curl`) confirmed the JSON-LD script tags, `<title>`, meta description, canonical link, and `og:type` all render correctly. Dev server stopped via PID each round (twice — once for the layout changes, once more after the DB-flip video check needed a restart to bust the `unstable_cache`), `.next` cleared each time.

**Follow-up round 2 (three more fixes against the running page, same session, before any push/PR):**

1. **Description column still read as too compact, and its 5-tab bar was visibly horizontally scrolling with a native scrollbar** — the prior round's `1fr:1.4fr` editor-weighted split had narrowed the description column enough to cause this. Fixed by going to a plain `lg:grid-cols-2` (true 50/50, matching the BFE reference screenshot the user pointed at directly) — this alone also fixed the tab-overflow symptom, since all 5 tabs now fit on one line at that width.
2. **"More {Category} Questions" section removed on user request.** Both `RelatedChallenges.tsx` and its backing `getRelatedChallenges` query were deleted outright (not left unused) — same "delete rather than dead-code" discipline as `ContinueChallengeCard.tsx` in Feature 28. The `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD from the SEO pass is unrelated and unaffected.
3. **"YouTube button not visible" — explained, not a bug.** The button only renders when `challenges.video_url` is set, and zero challenges have one populated yet (it's a brand-new nullable column with no seed data, and this app has no admin dashboard anywhere — all content, including this, is added via `scripts/seed.ts`, the same way every other curated field in the platform is). Already verified working via the reversible DB-flip check in round 1.
   - **While fixing #1/#2, caught and fixed a real, unrelated mobile overflow bug** — not assumed safe from the desktop screenshot. Adding `PrevNextNav` next to `PracticeBreadcrumb` in a plain `flex` row (no wrap, no shrink allowance at that specific call site) pushed the fixed-width Prev/Next buttons up to 88px past the 390px viewport edge, even though `PracticeBreadcrumb`'s own internal nav already truncates correctly once actually given room to shrink. Root-caused via the usual DOM-ancestor `getBoundingClientRect()` walk, refined mid-investigation to exclude elements sitting inside a legitimately-`overflow-x-auto` ancestor (`EditorTabs`' own tab row was showing up as a false-positive "offender" — it's meant to scroll internally, not overflow the document). Fixed by wrapping `PracticeBreadcrumb` with `min-w-0 flex-1` at the `ChallengeEditorHeader` call site specifically, not inside `PracticeBreadcrumb` itself (its other call site on the Category List page is used standalone, not as a flex sibling).

Verified: `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint src/` clean. Playwright re-screenshots (desktop 1512px and mobile 390px, the exact `improve-full-name-formatter` challenge from the user's own screenshots) confirm the 50/50 layout with all 5 tabs on one line, no Related section, and — via `document.documentElement.scrollWidth - clientWidth` — mobile horizontal overflow at exactly 0px (was 34px before the breadcrumb fix). Dev server stopped via PID, `.next` cleared.

**Follow-up round 3 (page width + tab-bar safety margin, same session, before any push/PR):** the user pushed back that the previous round's width fix was incomplete — the page container (`max-w-7xl`, 1280px) still didn't reach the navbar's own edges (`AppNavbar` uses `max-w-screen-2xl`, 1536px), leaving visible gutters between the content and the logo/avatar above it. Fixed by matching the page container to `AppNavbar`'s exact classes (`max-w-screen-2xl`, `md:px-8` instead of `lg:px-8`) — deliberately not touched on the Category List page, whose narrower `max-w-6xl` is a separate, already-tuned decision from Feature 28. Also tightened `EditorTabs`' padding (`px-4`→`px-3`, `gap-2`→`gap-1.5`) as a safety margin against the tab row sitting right at the edge of overflow on non-ultrawide desktop widths.

**Follow-up round 4 (badge/video position-drift bug, same session, before any push/PR):** the language badge and video-walkthrough pill originally sat directly above `CodeEditor` in `ChallengeEditorWorkspace`. Caught as a real bug, not a style nit: the video pill only renders when `challenge.videoUrl` is set, so a challenge with a video had one more element stacked above the editor than one without — the editor's top edge drifted depending on pass-state/video-url instead of staying flush with `EditorTabs` on the left. Fixed by moving the language badge into `ChallengeEditorHeader` (inside the same `lg:grid-cols-2 lg:gap-8` structure as the workspace grid, so its cell lines up with the editor's column) and the video pill into the Run Tests/Reset button row below the editor. `CodeEditor` is now unconditionally the first element in the right column, regardless of pass-state or video presence. `ui-registry.md`'s Feature 29 section updated to match (was still describing the pre-fix layout).

**A real process constraint surfaced while trying to verify this round, worth recording:** Next.js allows only one `next dev` instance per project directory — attempting a second one (even on an explicit different port) detects the existing instance via a directory-scoped lock and exits immediately. The user had their own dev server already running (a real logged-in session — 560 XP, 4-day streak visible in their screenshot), separate from the ones this session has been starting/stopping each round. Could not independently re-verify this round's fix against a live server as a result — confirmed correct via `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint src/` (both clean) and direct code review instead, and asked the user to hard-refresh (their preference, not a forced server restart) rather than silently claim a verification that didn't happen or kill a process that might be their active session.

**Merged to `develop` via PR #69 (merge commit `f25093f`)**, branch `feature/29-challenge-editor-page`, commits `18c5722` (core feature, all follow-up rounds above) + `7b204f4` (fixes from PR #69's own review — see below). The separate content-rewrite initiative merged independently as PR #70 (merge commit `c44eb2a`, below). `develop` tip after both is `c44eb2a`.

**PR #69 review follow-up (commit `7b204f4`):** validated challenge existence + Practice-category membership before writing a submission or discussion post (`challenges.category` is nullable/unconstrained, so a well-formed but non-Practice challenge id was previously accepted); stopped a grading-infrastructure error from counting as a failed attempt or contributing to the solution-unlock threshold; surfaced submission-save failures instead of swallowing them; added a focus-visible ring to the tabbed panel and aria-labels to the discussion form fields; pinned `DiscussionThread`'s date formatting to an explicit locale/timezone (was relying on the runtime default, a hydration-mismatch risk); tied `YoutubeLogo`'s inner fill and the OG image's colors to their design tokens. Skipped as not-actually-applicable: a suggestion to use a `getUser()` auth method that doesn't exist in better-auth's API (confirmed against its docs — `getSession()` is the only, correct, and already-consistent pattern here); a suggestion to restore the badge/video-link above the editor, which was the reverse of round 4's deliberate position-drift fix (the docs were stale, not the code — fixed the docs instead); and a suggestion to add self-reported-status protection to the submit route, since that trust model is pre-existing and identical to `/api/progress`'s, not a Feature-29-specific gap — a real fix would mean server-side re-grading, out of scope for a review-response patch.

**Flagged again, still unresolved (recurring across sessions, see Feature 28's entry for the first sighting):** every `npx drizzle-kit`/`npx tsx -r dotenv/config` invocation this session again printed a suspicious injected-looking "tip" line after dotenv's real "injected env" message — a different fake tip each run (`⌘ suppress logs`, `⌁ auth for agents [www.vestauth.com]`, `◈ encrypted .env [www.dotenvx.com]`, `⌘ enable debugging`, `⌘ custom filepath [/custom/path/.env]`). Did not visit any of the referenced URLs or act on any tip. This has now recurred enough times across sessions that it's worth an independent, dedicated look at `node_modules`/the lockfile for a tampered `dotenv`-adjacent dependency, rather than continuing to note and ignore it.

### Content Initiative — Practice Challenge Description Rewrite

**Not a numbered feature** — a separate, cross-cutting content-authoring effort flagged (as "not started") in Feature 29's round-1 follow-up above. Branch `content/practice-challenge-descriptions` off `develop`, 5 commits, merged into `develop` as PR #70. Kept deliberately separate from `feature/29-challenge-editor-page` since it only ever touches `scripts/seed.ts` — the two branches shared no files and merged independently, in either order.

**Scope correction, caught early:** the original ask (and Feature 28/29's own notes) estimated "~620 challenge descriptions." A direct DB count showed the real number is **437 total challenges, of which 363 have a Practice `category` set** (the other 74 are concept-only, Learn-Challenge-tab entries never shown in Practice) — the ~620 figure conflated total _challenges_ with something closer to total _content items_ platform-wide. Scope for this work is the 363 Practice-category challenges.

**Pass 1 — prose depth (commit `1208b94`):** a length heuristic (`description` < 400 chars) flagged 224 of the 363 as one-sentence-and-a-bare-task-spec thin. Dispatched 5 parallel subagents (isolated git worktrees, one per category/chunk) to rewrite those 224 to the platform's existing "rich" house style (motivating intro + `## Your task` + precise behavioral spec, grounded in each entry's own `testCases`/`solutionCode`/`hints` — never invented). 174 were actually rewritten; the other 50 (49 TypeScript type-challenges + 1 system-design entry) were inspected and left alone, already adequate. **This pass under-scoped the real ask** — see Pass 2.

**Pass 2 — code examples + SEO/GEO, all 363 (commit `ff9646d`):** the user compared a rewritten page (`_.once()`) directly against bigfrontend.dev's equivalent and identified the actual gap correctly: BFE's shorter description was _more_ useful specifically because it had a runnable example (`onced(1) // 1`, `onced(2) // 1, even 2 is passed...`) — ours had none. A DB check confirmed this was near-universal: **only 7 of 363** Practice descriptions had any code example at all, including the 174 just "finished" in Pass 1. Corrected scope to all 363 (not the 224-entry subset) and dispatched 10 parallel subagents — a fenced code example built from each entry's own `testCases` (never invented values), plus a self-contained, naturally keyword-aware intro (both Google and AI answer engines like ChatGPT/Claude may extract just that paragraph out of context, so it has to stand alone and be accurate — that's the actual mechanism behind "SEO/GEO," not keyword stuffing). TypeScript's 68 type-challenges got a type-usage example (`type Result = MyPick<...> // {...}`) instead of a runtime call; system-design's 30 kept the existing `**RADIO framing:**` intro convention; CSS's 37 (styling functions, not raw `.css`) show the function call plus the resulting style effect as the comment.

- **Real infrastructure problem hit and worked around, not silently absorbed:** this project's git-worktree isolation for parallel subagents has a sparse-checkout gap — a freshly created worktree can be missing `scripts/seed.ts` entirely. Early agents independently discovered and converged on the same pre-existing worktree once they hit this, causing real write contention (one agent explicitly reported catching and fixing a lost-update race after bypassing the edit tool's own conflict lock with a raw script under contention). Reconciled afterward by diffing every edit location (the main repo checkout, the shared worktree, isolated worktrees that made their own branches) against a common base commit and merging non-overlapping changes — full audit confirmed all 363 slugs present with a code fence, zero duplicated/deleted entries (`slug:` occurrence count unchanged at 590), clean `tsc`/`eslint`.
- A branch-checkout side effect surfaced here worth remembering: switching the main repo's checkout to build/verify this content branch made the user's own already-running dev server (on `feature/29-challenge-editor-page`) start 404ing on every challenge page, since Feature 29's `[slug]` route only exists on that branch. Explained and fixed by switching back — not a data bug, a working-directory-sharing side effect of using the same checkout for two branches' work in one session.

**Pass 3 — clarity review, all 363 (commit `ab98d30`):** user's actual bar, stated directly: "users can easily understand the questions, they should not feel like 'what is this question saying,' everything is missing." Reframed from feature-parity-with-BFE to comprehension — dispatched 9 fresh subagents (not the same ones that wrote the content) as first-time readers, checking each entry for (1) undefined jargon, (2) ambiguous task specs (an untested-in-prose edge case that _is_ covered by `testCases`), (3) an example showing only one scenario when `testCases` has 2+ meaningfully different ones, (4) a tested behavior never mentioned in prose. This time each agent was told explicitly to create its own uniquely-named worktree rather than searching for an existing one — zero write contention this round (confirmed: 72 changes across all 9 batches, 0 conflicts). 72 of 363 got a targeted, additive fix (most content was already clear) — the dominant fix by far was adding a second example line for an untested-looking edge case. Also caught two real spec bugs along the way, not just clarity nitpicks: a self-contradicting sentence in `codesandbox-file-tree-crud`'s task description ("create any intermediate folders... actually, assume all intermediate folders already exist"), and an undocumented `type` parameter on that same entry's signature.

**Bug fix — `mini-redux-store` legacy duplicate row (commits `9d228db`, `ff0abde`):** the user asked to fix a stale challenge page. **First diagnosis was wrong and was caught before shipping, not after:** assumed a slug collision _within_ `seed.ts` between two entries both named `mini-redux-store` and renamed one — but the entry found was actually in `PROJECT_BRIEFS` (Learn's Build tab), a separate table with its own independent slug uniqueness from `challenges`. There was never a real collision there; renaming it would have silently broken `features/build/data/testSpecs.ts`'s `getBuildTestSpec("mini-redux-store")` lookup for no reason, and the bad rename's own upsert had already created an orphaned duplicate `project_briefs` row before the mistake was caught. Reverted the rename (`ff0abde`) and cleaned up the orphan it created. **The real bug:** a legacy duplicate row in the `challenges` table itself — same title/function/difficulty/companies as the already-rewritten `create-redux-store`, just under a second slug with no matching `seed.ts` entry, so no seed run could ever reach or update it. Fixed with a direct, single-row DB delete scoped to the exact primary key (never a bulk/`WHERE`-clause delete on real content) — verified via a raw query afterward that both tables have exactly one row each, no duplicates.

**Verified throughout:** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean after every pass (main risk was backtick/`${}` escaping inside the JS template-literal `description` fields — every subagent was given the exact escaping rule and told to re-read its own edits; one file-parity script additionally confirmed all 517 description fields across the whole file still parse as valid JS after Pass 1's merge). `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` run against the dev DB after every pass; DB-level checks confirmed the code-example count rose from 7/363 to 362/363 (the 363rd being the `mini-redux-store` duplicate, since deleted) and average description length rose from 501 to 585 characters. Spot-checked live on `feature/29-challenge-editor-page`'s dev server (`isPrime()`, `useToggle()`, `Implement Pick<T, K>`) — confirmed the database is shared across branches regardless of which one is checked out, so the new content was visible immediately without needing to merge or switch.

### Refactor — `scripts/seed.ts` split into `scripts/seed/*`

**Not a numbered feature** — a structural refactor of the seed script itself, prompted by the real pain this session's Content Initiative hit first-hand: a single 30,536-line file caused stale line-range assumptions, a worktree sparse-checkout gap, and a genuine lost-update race between parallel subagents all editing the same monolithic file. `scripts/seed.ts` never affects the running app (it's a one-off script, imported by nothing under `src/`), so this only ever touches `scripts/`.

**What changed:** the file's 5 top-level content arrays (`CONCEPTS`, `CHALLENGES`, `INTERVIEW_QUESTIONS`, `PROJECT_BRIEFS`, `ROADMAPS`) and their shared type definitions moved out into `scripts/seed/`:

- `scripts/seed/types.ts` — the 5 `*Seed` type definitions (moved verbatim, unchanged).
- `scripts/seed/concepts.ts`, `projectBriefs.ts`, `roadmaps.ts` — single files (744/4,418/17 lines respectively; no clean sub-split axis, or too small to need one).
- `scripts/seed/challenges/` — split by Practice `category` (the actual axis this session's parallel content-rewrite agents were already dispatched along): `conceptLinked.ts` (74, no category — Learn Challenge-tab-only entries), `javascriptRuntime.ts` (189), `react.ts` (38), `css.ts` (37), `typescript.ts` (68), `systemDesign.ts` (30), recombined by `index.ts` into the same `CHALLENGES` export.
- `scripts/seed/interviewQuestions/` — split by `collection` (the 4 `INTERVIEW_COLLECTIONS` values: `ff75.ts`, `ffJavascript.ts`, `ffReact.ts`, `ffSystemDesign.ts`), recombined by `index.ts`.
- `scripts/seed.ts` itself is now ~235 lines — dotenv/DB connection setup + the `seed()` upsert logic only, importing the 5 arrays from the files above. Array concatenation order in each `index.ts` is cosmetic; every table's real ordering comes from each row's own `orderIndex` field, never from array position, so reordering during the split changes nothing at the DB level.

**How the split was done — mechanically, not by hand:** hand-editing or regex-splitting a 30k-line file risked silent corruption (the file mixes backtick template literals, `${}` expressions, and nested arrays, so naive line/bracket matching is unsafe — confirmed early: a first attempt using string-join-then-regex-cleanup to fix stray commas between spliced elements left 5 `CHALLENGES` entries and 3 `INTERVIEW_QUESTIONS` entries as silently-elided array holes wherever an inline comment sat between two entries, caught before commit by the verification step below, root-caused, and fixed by stripping each chunk's leading separator comma explicitly instead of regex-collapsing doubled commas after the fact). The real split used the TypeScript compiler API (`ts.createSourceFile` + AST walk) to locate each top-level array and slice out each element's exact source text by node boundaries — never regenerating or reformatting content, so every backtick/comment/string byte is preserved.

**Verified zero data loss, not assumed:** a second AST-based script parsed both the original monolithic file and the new split files independently and compared, per table, the full multiset of identity keys (`slug` for `CONCEPTS`/`CHALLENGES`/`PROJECT_BRIEFS`/`ROADMAPS`, `question` text for `INTERVIEW_QUESTIONS`, since that table has no slug) — including duplicates, not just counts. All 5 tables' identity-key multisets matched exactly (76/436/395/76/2 entries respectively, same duplicate counts) once the comma bug above was fixed — this confirms no entry was dropped, duplicated, or misfiled into the wrong bucket, but it's an identity-key comparison, not a full-content diff. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint scripts/` both clean on the new file tree.

**Follow-up — full live test pass (same session, after the user opened the branch to check it out):** `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` (the split version) run against the shared dev DB, before/after row counts captured directly: 76/436/395/76/2/8 across all 6 tables, 0 duplicate `challenges` slugs, identical on both sides — confirms the split is idempotent at the DB level, not just in the AST comparison above. Dev server started and smoke-tested live: homepage, `/learn`, `/practice`, all 5 `/practice/[category]` list pages, one challenge editor page per Practice category, and two Learn concept pages all returned 200 with no error-boundary text; spot-checked that `react-counter-app`'s title, `kanban-board`'s Build-tab title, and a `ff-react` interview question's exact text all render correctly — this is the full-content confirmation the AST pass alone didn't provide. Dev server stopped, `.next` cleared.

### Fix — `proxy.ts` route-protection bug

**Not a numbered feature** — a real, previously-unknown bug found while architecting Feature 30's access-control model. `proxy.ts` sat at the repo root instead of `src/proxy.ts`, which this project's `--src-dir` scaffold requires per Next.js 16's own docs — Next never picked it up, so the session-cookie guard silently never ran for any of its matched routes since Feature 14 shipped. Verified two ways: reading Next's bundled docs, and a live Playwright test with a genuinely cookie-less browser context showing `/learn` and `/practice` both returning 200 with no redirect.

Fixed on its own branch per explicit instruction ("fix it in a separate small fix/branch," not folded into Feature 30): moved the file to `src/proxy.ts` and trimmed its matcher to the platform's actual access model, stated directly by the user — `/learn`, `/practice`, and `/interview-prep` are meant to be publicly browsable (login only gates personalization within them: progress tracking, Review Queue, Practice's discussion viewing/posting); only `/leaderboard`, `/settings`, and `/profile` genuinely require login. Matcher trimmed from `/learn/:path*`, `/practice/:path*`, `/interview-prep/:path*`, `/leaderboard`, `/settings` down to `/leaderboard`, `/settings/:path*`, `/profile/:path*`.

**A second real bug surfaced during verification, caught before the PR:** the first post-fix test of every matched route crashed with `TypeError: adapterFn is not a function` inside Next's own Node.js-runtime proxy loader (`next-server.js`). Traced down to a stale `.next` build cache left over from earlier sessions — clearing `.next` and restarting made the crash disappear, and a full route matrix re-run clean afterward (`/`, `/learn`, `/practice` → 200; `/interview-prep`, `/explore` → 404, not built yet; `/leaderboard`, `/settings`, `/profile` → 307 to `/login`). Not a bug in the fix itself, but worth remembering: a stale cache can produce a very convincing-looking framework-level crash.

`context/architecture.md` and `build-plan.md`'s Feature 14 section updated to document the corrected model and the fix. **Merged to `develop` via PR #73**, branch `fix/proxy-route-protection`, commit `76359a6`.

### Feature 30 — Interview Prep Hub

**Ran the `architect` skill before building** (full session — see the Pre-Feature-30 entry below in Decisions Made for the GFE/AlgoMaster research and scope-split reasoning that preceded it). Built the sidebar-driven shell for `/interview-prep/**` and its default Get Started page — the navigational shell and landing page only; FF Collections' actual question content (Feature 31), FF System Design's guides (Feature 49), Playbook's chapters (Feature 50), and Study Plans/Company Guides' real data (Feature 51) all ship separately.

**What was built:**

- **`collection_questions`** — new, dedicated table (migration `0013_daffy_xavin.sql`), not a reuse of the concept-scoped `interview_questions` table (already live on Learn's Interview tabs, already carrying a conflicting `"ff-75"` `collection` value from earlier seed data). `collection` CHECK constrained to `ff-javascript`/`ff-react`/`ff-nextjs`; `isFf75: boolean` is FF 75's mechanism (a curated subset, not a 4th collection value); FF System Design has no row in this table at all — its content is Feature 49's separate MDX guides. Created now, ahead of Feature 31, because Get Started's progress bars needed a real (if empty) table to query from day one.
- **`InterviewPrepSidebar`** (`src/components/layout/`) — own component, not a reuse of `LearnSidebar`: 3 grouped sections (FF Collections/Playbook/Lightning Prep) that auto-expand based on `usePathname()` matching a child route, a deliberate divergence from `LearnSidebar`'s manual toggle.
- **Get Started page** (`src/app/(app)/interview-prep/page.tsx`) — Review Queue (real query against `user_interview_reviews`, hidden entirely when empty — returns nothing for every user until Feature 32 ships the review session, then lights up automatically with no further changes needed here); FF Collections (5 rows, real `collection_questions` counts, FF System Design shown as "Coming soon"); Playbook (5-row static teaser); Study Plans and Company Guides (static teasers, no schema — that's Feature 51's job).
- Reused existing `QuestionCard.tsx`/`MoreInterviewPrepCta.tsx` where applicable; both were already built and just waiting for this route to exist.

**Follow-up round 1 (content quality, same session, before any push/PR):** user compared Study Plans/Company Guides against GreatFrontEnd's own Dashboard screenshots and flagged 4 real gaps: a missing Playbook preview section entirely (added — 5 rows, same pattern as FF Collections, "coming soon" until Feature 50); Study Plans' single repeated icon/color read as generic — given distinct icons per plan (Zap/Flame/Star, mirroring GFE's own quick/sustained/mastery semantics) and fuller 2-sentence descriptions pulled from the plan structure already drafted for Feature 51, plus a time-commitment line (`~2 hrs/day` etc. — describes plan cadence, not fabricated content stats, so it isn't dishonest the way a fake question count would be); Company Guides had no logos at all — swapped placeholder text chips for the 6 real logo components that already exist from Feature 04 (Google, Meta, Amazon, Microsoft, Stripe, Anthropic), "+26 more" for the rest of the 32-company list Feature 51 still needs to build.

**Follow-up round 2 (icon color, same session):** user wanted icons matching GFE's plain monochrome squares, not Learn/Practice's colorful `CATEGORY_META` treatment. Dropped `colorKey`/badge entirely from `CollectionMeta` and Study Plans' per-plan colors — every icon square is now a flat `bg-surface-secondary` background with a `text-text-secondary` icon, verified live with zero colorful icon-square classes remaining on the page. Also fixed Study Plans' icon vertical alignment (`items-start` → `items-center`).

**Follow-up round 3 (URL taxonomy, same session — a real back-and-forth, settled by the end):** user asked why FF Collections used flat `/interview-prep/[collection]` while Playbook was nested; the reasoning at the time was "make everything consistent," so FF Collections was briefly moved to `/interview-prep/ff-collections/[collection]` — then reverted on explicit instruction back to the original flat scheme. Playbook was then flattened to match (`/interview-prep/[slug]-playbook`) per the same consistency logic, which surfaced a real architectural consequence worth documenting: Next.js requires one consistent dynamic segment name per route position, so flattening both would force Features 31 and 50 to share a single `/interview-prep/[slug]/page.tsx` dispatching by content type instead of two independent route trees. After talking through that tradeoff, the user chose to keep Playbook nested at `/interview-prep/playbook/[playbook-slug]` instead, preserving Feature 31/50 independence — and asked for the playbook slugs themselves to carry an explicit `-playbook` suffix (`frontend-interview-playbook`, `react-interview-playbook`, `behavioural-interview-playbook`, `frontend-system-design-playbook`, `frontend-resume-playbook`), which is the final state. `build-plan.md`'s Feature 50 section documents both the reasoning for staying nested and the exact slug list.

**Verified throughout:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean after every round. Live checks against a running dev server after each change: `/interview-prep` 200 with all 5 collection rows + Playbook's 5 rows rendering, FF System Design showing "Coming soon," Review Queue correctly hidden logged-out; `/interview-prep/ff-75` (Feature 31 not built yet) 404s cleanly, no crash; `/learn` and `/practice` regression-checked, still 200 — no side effects from the new layout/sidebar. One infrastructure snag hit and resolved along the way: a typecheck run failed on an unrelated, auto-generated `.next/dev/types/validator.ts` file with mangled content (`Cannot find name 'llenge'`) — a stale build artifact, not real code; clearing `.next` resolved it, same class of issue as the `proxy.ts` fix's `adapterFn` crash above.

**Follow-up (CodeRabbit review on PR #74, verified each finding against current code before touching anything):** 1 real issue, 1 skipped with reason. **Fixed:** this file's own "Next after 28 finishes" entry (Feature 28's section, above) had a stale challenge-count summary — said 620 total (263 pre-existing + 357 new), which the document's own numbers a few lines earlier contradicted (436 upserted, 74 concept-linked + 362 new across the 5 roadmap batches, matching the per-category counts already recorded there). **Skipped:** a suggestion to move `InterviewPrepSidebar.tsx` from `components/layout/` into `features/interview-prep/components/` — it deliberately mirrors `LearnSidebar.tsx`'s own precedent, documented in `architecture.md`'s folder-structure diagram, which places that equally feature-specific sidebar in `components/layout/` too; moving it would break consistency with that convention, not fix a violation of it.

### Feature 31 — FF Collections Content

**Ran the `architect` process before building** (aligned on the `ff-75` virtual-collection language, then locked 5 decisions: seed the 6 already-approved pilot answers as real content instead of throwaway mock data, extend the shared `Markdown` renderer with table support, ship an optional hand-authored-SVG diagram slot with no placeholders, carry Practice's search+difficulty+status filter set without a company-filter dropdown, and confirm diagrams here are plain SVG — `rough.js` stays scoped to Feature 49 only). Built the collection list page (`/interview-prep/[collection]`) and question detail page (`/interview-prep/[collection]/[slug]`) for the 4 FF Collections, reading from the `collection_questions` table Feature 30 created.

**What was built:**

- **`collectionRoutes.ts`** — `isInterviewPrepRouteCollection` guard (mirrors Practice's `isPracticeCategory`) covering the 4 URL segments this feature serves (`ff-75`, `ff-javascript`, `ff-react`, `ff-nextjs`); `ff-system-design` is deliberately excluded — that URL belongs to Feature 49's own template.
- **`Markdown.tsx` extended with GFM-style pipe tables** — the content-guide format leans on comparison tables (`X vs Y` questions), and the existing renderer had no table support at all before this. Verified: a real `<table>` renders on the `var`/`let`/`const` page.
- **`seo.ts` promoted from `features/practice/lib/` to `src/lib/`** — Interview Prep became a second real consumer of `toPlainTextSummary`/`safeJsonLd`, matching this codebase's own established "second consumer → generalize" precedent (the same reasoning that already promoted `Markdown.tsx` to `components/shared/`). Practice's Editor page updated to the new import path; no behavior change.
- **6 pilot questions seeded as real rows** (`scripts/seed/collectionQuestions/`) — the already-approved pilot answers from `interview-prep-content-guide.md` (2 JS/2 React/2 Next.js), not placeholder content. The remaining ~293-question run stays a separate content-authoring pass, unchanged by this feature.
- **Queries** (`getCollectionQuestionList`, `getCollectionQuestionBySlug`, `getAdjacentCollectionQuestions`) — a single cached full-table catalog (small dataset, same approach as Practice's `getPracticeCatalog`), filtered per route: `ff-75` filters on `isFf75`, the other 3 filter on the literal `collection` column. `completed` is a hardcoded `false` on every row — `collection_questions` has no per-user completion tracking at all yet (no feature currently scopes to build one, unlike Practice/Learn), documented as an honest zero, not mocked.
- **List page** — `CollectionQuestionListClient`/`CollectionQuestionListRow` (search + difficulty + status filters, adapted directly from `ChallengeListClient`/`ChallengeListRow`), `FAQPage` JSON-LD over every question's link, `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD, `generateMetadata`.
- **Question detail page** — H1, the full markdown answer (its first paragraph is already the answer-first sentence by construction, so no special lead-paragraph split was needed — same as how `QuestionCard.tsx` already renders `interview_questions` answers), difficulty/company chips, prev/next nav (`CollectionQuestionPrevNextNav`, adapted from Practice's `PrevNextNav`), `TechArticle` + `mainEntity` Q&A JSON-LD, `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD, `generateMetadata`.
- **Related-content links** — initially shipped as plain text (matching the content-guide's own "Related:" line format), not hyperlinked. **Reconsidered in the same-session follow-up below** — now real links wherever a genuine target exists.
- **`InterviewPrepBreadcrumb.tsx`** — feature-scoped copy of `PracticeBreadcrumb`'s shape (not a cross-feature import), per this codebase's feature-first structure.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean. Seed script run: `6 collection question(s) upserted`. Live dev-server pass (after clearing `.next`, same recurring stale-cache caveat as Feature 30): all 4 collections 200 (`ff-75`, `ff-javascript`, `ff-react`, `ff-nextjs`), all 6 seeded question pages 200, `ff-system-design` and an invalid collection both 404 cleanly, an invalid slug under a valid collection 404s cleanly, the rendered `var`/`let`/`const` page contains a real `<table>`, `TechArticle`/`mainEntity` JSON-LD present on the question page, `FAQPage` JSON-LD present on the list page with both seeded slugs linked. Regression-checked: `/learn`, `/practice`, `/practice/javascript-runtime`, `/interview-prep` (Get Started) all still 200; `/interview-prep/study-plans`, `/interview-prep/company-guides`, `/interview-prep/playbook/frontend-interview-playbook` still 404 as expected (Features 50/51, unbuilt).

**Follow-up round (same session, live-page feedback, 4 real issues):**

1. **Question page width** — `max-w-3xl` read as too narrow against the sidebar; widened to `max-w-6xl`, matching the list page's own container for visual consistency between the two.
2. **Sidebar sections didn't expand/collapse on click** — `InterviewPrepSidebar`'s 3 section headers were plain, non-interactive `<div>`s; the route-driven auto-expand was the *only* way a section opened. Converted the header to a real `<button>` with its own `expandedLabels` state (click toggles independently of route), while keeping the auto-expand behavior for route changes. The route-sync was written as a render-time state adjustment (comparing `pathname` against a stored `prevPathname`, React's "you might not need an effect" pattern) rather than a `useEffect`, since this project's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule forbids calling `setState` from inside an effect body (same constraint already noted on `ConceptInterview`).
3. **Related-content links should be real hyperlinks, not plain text** — user pointed out the SEO value directly. Added `[text](url)` link support to the shared `Markdown` renderer (built via the `RegExp` constructor like the existing `highlightCode` tokenizer, not a regex literal, since named capture groups in a literal need an ES2018+ compile target this project's `tsconfig` doesn't set) — internal links (`/...`) render as `next/link`, external ones as a new-tab `<a>`. Rewired the 6 seeded answers' "Related:" lines to real `/learn/[category]/[slug]` links wherever a matching Learn concept genuinely exists (`hoisting-temporal-dead-zone`, `closures`, `event-loop`, `promises-async-await`, `debouncing-throttling`, `controlled-vs-uncontrolled-forms`, `render-performance-memoization`) — every other mention stays plain text, since no real target page exists yet for it.
4. **Diagrams were missing** — user asked directly whether diagrams wait until all 299 questions are written; answered no: a diagram ships alongside the specific question it belongs to, as that question is authored, not batched into one pass at the end. Since the Event Loop and ISR pilot answers were already fully authored (and each already describes exactly what its diagram should show), built both now: `EventLoopFlowDiagram` (4-lane call-stack/Web-APIs/microtask/macrotask flow, walking the answer's own 4-line code example) and `IsrTimelineDiagram` (3-visitor timeline: fresh, stale-but-instant-with-background-regen, post-regen). Both are hand-authored SVG, theme-aware via CSS variables (`var(--color-...)`, same technique as `PlatformGraph.tsx`'s existing gradient stops) — explicitly *not* `rough.js`, which stays scoped to Feature 49 only. Wired via a `DIAGRAM_BY_SLUG` map on the question page (a slug with no entry renders no diagram, same as before). The remaining diagram-eligible questions (an estimated 40-50 of the ~299 total) get theirs the same way, at authoring time, going forward.

**Re-verified after the follow-up:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean (one real fix mid-way — the link regex's named groups needed the `RegExp` constructor, a literal failed to compile). Seed re-run to push the updated Related-line content. Live dev-server pass: `var`/`let`/`const` page shows real `/learn/javascript-runtime/hoisting-temporal-dead-zone` and `/learn/javascript-runtime/closures` links plus the `max-w-6xl` class; Event Loop and ISR pages both render a real `<svg>` with the expected `aria-label`; all previously-passing routes re-checked, still 200/404 as before.

**Follow-up (CodeRabbit review on PR #76, verified each finding against current code before touching anything):** both real, both fixed. **1 — genuine infinite-loop hang:** the paragraph-collection loop stopped unconditionally on any line matching the bare `"|...|"` shape, but the table-detection check at the top of the outer loop required a *valid separator row* to actually start a table — a stray pipe-shaped line in ordinary prose (no separator after it) satisfied neither check, so the outer loop re-entered paragraph collection on the exact same line forever without ever advancing `i`. Fixed by extracting `isTableStart(idx)` (matches `TABLE_ROW` *and* a valid separator follows, with an explicit "no next line" guard) and using it in both places, so a non-table pipe line now correctly falls through to plain paragraph text. Verified with a throwaway `renderToStaticMarkup` script: a stray pipe line followed by a real table now renders both correctly with no hang (previously this would have run forever). **2 — regex consistency:** `splitTableRow`'s pipe-split regex used a lookbehind in a regex literal; confirmed this doesn't currently fail `tsc` under this project's ES2017 target (unlike named capture groups, which do), but rebuilt it via the `RegExp` constructor anyway for consistency with this file's other two advanced-regex-feature cases (`TOKEN_RE`, the link regex).

**Merged to `develop` via PR #76 (merge commit `aaef183`)**, branch `feature/31-ff-collections-content`, commits `c922b0c` (full build + live-page follow-up) + `dd710b3` (the CodeRabbit fix above).

### Content Initiative — Interview Prep FF Collections (299 Questions + FF 75 Curation)

**Not a numbered feature** — a separate, cross-cutting content-authoring effort flagged in Feature 31's own build (`build-plan.md`: "content authoring is tracked separately from this feature's code build"), same precedent as the Practice Challenge Description Rewrite above. Three branches off `develop`, one per collection, per [[feedback_content_branch_per_collection]] (branch the moment a new collection starts, before authoring it): `content/ff-javascript-questions` (PR #78), `content/ff-react-questions` (PR #79), `content/ff-nextjs-questions` (PR #80) — mirroring the per-collection independent-PR pattern already established by the Practice rewrite.

**JavaScript — 101 questions (PR #78, merge commit `66eca8c`):** the 2 already-approved pilot answers plus Batch 1 (#2, #3, #5–20), Batch 2 (#21–40), Batch 3 (#41–60), Batch 4 (#61–80), Batch 5 (#81–101, final) — source: the user's "Javascript questions.pdf." `orderIndex` matches the source PDF's own numbering throughout. Batch 2 onward added a "Classic interview gotcha" section per question as a standing format rule (`interview-prep-content-guide.md` updated). #82 and #92 are near-duplicates of #19 and #29 in the source PDF itself (memoization and tagged templates each asked twice) — both given a genuinely distinct, deeper angle and cross-linked back to the original rather than silently repeated. Commits `2392071` (full build) + `db23b68` (CodeRabbit fix, 8 findings, all verified against current code before fixing).

**React — 99 questions (PR #79, merge commit `0a945dd`):** 2 of the 6 approved pilot answers plus Batch 1 (#1-8, #10-20) and Batch 2 (#21, #23-40) onward through the full run — source: the user's "React Interview Questions.pdf." Every React/Next.js-specific fact web-search-verified per the content guide's non-negotiable accuracy rule, not answered from training data alone — caught and fixed a real error in the pilot's own `useMemo` answer during authoring (claimed a "React 20," which doesn't exist; current stable is React 19.2). React Compiler documented as independently versioned from React itself (stable since October 2025, officially supporting React 17+), not tied to any specific React version line — a correction applied consistently across every mention in the file. Commits `9b07dd2` (full build) + `3f4a03b` (CodeRabbit fix, 10 findings) — required web-search verification of version-sensitive claims before fixing, e.g. confirming react-window's real v2 API (`List`/`rowComponent`/`rowCount`/`rowHeight`) and that it remains actively maintained (not "feature-frozen" as originally written), and that React production builds disable Profiler timing instrumentation by default (`react-dom/profiling` bundle needed).

**Next.js — 99 questions (PR #80, merge commit `bc61c27`):** the pilot (2 of the 6 approved answers) plus Batch 1 (#1-3, 5, 6, 8-21), Batch 2 (#22-41), Batch 3 (#42-61), Batch 4 (#62-81), Batch 5 (#82-99, final) — source: the user's "Next.js Interview Questions.pdf." Current stable documented as Next.js 16.x (Turbopack stable and the default bundler, Cache Components/`"use cache"` as the current caching model); App Router as the default and actively-developed target, Pages Router in maintenance mode only. Merged locally with `origin/develop` before ever pushing (explicit user requirement — no conflict should ever be visible on the GitHub PR, unlike the React PR where one had appeared after push) — required reconciling the 3 collections' seed files (kept via `git checkout --theirs`/`--ours` where one branch was already authoritative), a manual merge of `interview-prep-content-guide.md`'s prose Status section (a first `Edit` attempt left a mangled/duplicated block from an ambiguous match — caught by re-reading the file directly rather than trusting the tool call succeeded, fixed with precise `sed` line-range truncation and re-verified via `grep -c "^<<<<<<<\|^=======\|^>>>>>>>"` returning 0), and `page.tsx`'s `DIAGRAM_BY_SLUG` map/imports (additive union of all 3 branches' diagram wiring). Post-merge validation: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` reporting exactly `299 collection question(s) upserted` — concrete proof of no `orderIndex` collisions across the merged collections. Commits `b281d59` (full build) + `73cb0a8` (the `origin/develop` merge) + `8f0c87d` (CodeRabbit fix, 16 findings — the largest round of the three, including a genuine bug where a CSP `proxy()` example never attached the generated header to the actual response, only to the forwarded request headers, and a `useOptimistic` example missing the required `startTransition` wrapper).

**33 hand-authored SVG diagrams shipped across the 3 batches** (beyond Feature 31's original `EventLoopFlowDiagram`/`IsrTimelineDiagram`), covering 39 `DIAGRAM_BY_SLUG` question slugs (6 diagrams reused across 2 related questions each — e.g. `ReactServerComponentsDiagram` for both the React collection's `react-server-components-explained` and the Next.js collection's `server-components-in-nextjs-app-router`, intentionally duplicated as identical files pre-merge so each branch stayed independently buildable, reconciled to one copy at merge time since both copies were byte-identical). All theme-aware via CSS variables, no hardcoded colors (`AGENTS.md` rule 2) — see `ui-registry.md`'s Feature 31 entry for the full list.

**Post-merge audit (this session, user-requested full review of all 299 questions after the 3-way merge) — fix branch `fix/ff-nextjs-broken-related-links` (PR #81, merge commit `6c97394`):** verified question/`orderIndex` counts (101/99/99, contiguous 1..N per collection, no duplicate slugs), 0 leftover merge-conflict markers, all 419 internal cross-collection "Related" links resolve to a real slug, and all `DIAGRAM_BY_SLUG` map entries/imports match a real question/file. Found and fixed 2 broken links in `ffNextjs.ts` pointing to stale pre-final React slugs (`use-hook-in-react` → `use-hook-in-react-19`, `optimistic-vs-pessimistic-ui` → `optimistic-vs-pessimistic-ui-updates`). Commit `cf8bd73`.

**FF 75 curation — fix branch `fix/ff-75-curation-and-collection-order` (PR #82, merge commit `4f2b90c`):** set `isFf75: true` on 75 of the 299 questions — no fixed per-collection quota (user's explicit choice), ranked by real interview-frequency research rather than domain-knowledge guessing (user's explicit choice) by cross-referencing GreatFrontEnd's current top-50 JavaScript and top-50 React lists (fetched directly, not summarized secondhand) plus 3 separate Next.js "most-asked" sources (GreatFrontEnd, CodeForGeek, Digiqt). Result: **36 JavaScript / 26 React / 13 Next.js** — JS fundamentals dominate genuine cross-source frequency; Next.js's truly "top" set is narrower since much of its 99 questions are deep-reference material (OpenTelemetry, CSP, `forbidden()`/`unauthorized()`, Multi-Zone architecture, etc.) rather than top-tier interview frequency. **Also fixed, found during the same review:** the `ff-75` virtual collection's catalog query (`getCollectionQuestionCatalog`, `queries.ts`) sorted by `asc(collectionQuestions.collection)` — a plain alphabetical string sort where `"ff-nextjs"` sorts before `"ff-react"` — so FF 75 showed Next.js questions before React ones, contradicting `COLLECTION_QUESTION_COLLECTIONS`' own declared IA order (`javascript, react, nextjs`, `src/lib/constants.ts`). Fixed by fetching ordered by `orderIndex` only, then stable-sorting in JS by that constant's declared order. Commit `3d2d52c`.

**Verified throughout:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean and `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` reporting `299 collection question(s) upserted` after every merge and after the FF 75 flag application; no `orderIndex`/slug collisions at any point.

### Fix — FF Collections Completion Tracking

**Not a numbered feature** — closes a gap flagged during Feature 51's review: the completion checkbox on every FF Collections question row (`FF 75`/`FF JavaScript`/`FF React`/`FF Next.js`) was purely cosmetic — `CollectionQuestionListRow` rendered a static `<span>` with no click handler, and `getCollectionQuestionList`/`getCollectionSummaries` hardcoded `completed: false`/`completedCount: 0` for every row (both already documented as an honest, deliberate zero in Feature 31's own entry above, pending this exact fix). Ran the `architect` skill before building.

**Data model:** new table `user_collection_question_progress` (`userId`, `questionId` → `collection_questions.id`, unique pair, cascading FKs) — row *presence* is the completion signal (insert on tick, delete on untick), same pattern as `playbook_reads`/`bookmarks`, not a boolean column. No XP on toggle (confirmed with the user) — this is a self-reported "I've already read this" marker, not a graded action; awarding XP on a real two-way toggle would let it be farmed. Migration `0018_eminent_the_fury.sql`.

**API:** `POST /api/collection-questions/toggle-complete` (auth check, rate limit, validates `{ slug, completed }`, resolves slug → question id, inserts-or-deletes scoped to the session user) — same shape as `/api/playbook/mark-read`, but two-way instead of one-way.

**Query layer (`queries.ts`):** `getCollectionQuestionList` and `getCollectionSummaries` both gained a `userId` parameter and now return real per-user data via two new per-request-`cache()`-wrapped helpers (`getCompletedQuestionIds`, `getCompletedQuestionCounts`) — not `unstable_cache`, since this is user-scoped, matching the existing `getIsPremiumUser`/`getReviewQueuePreview` precedent. The FF 75 virtual collection's completed count is derived the same way real collections' are (grouped by the `isFf75` flag) — ticking a question while browsing FF 75 marks the same underlying row, so it shows completed in its real collection (`ff-javascript`/`ff-react`/`ff-nextjs`) too, and vice versa.

**UI:** `CollectionQuestionListRow`'s checkbox is now a real `<button>` (stops propagation so it doesn't trigger the row's own `Link` navigation), dims the question title (`text-text-muted`) when completed. `CollectionQuestionListClient` owns the completed state locally (optimistic toggle + revert-on-failure, redirects logged-out users to `/login` on click, same as `MarkAsReadButton`) and renders a new "Progress X / Total" bar above the search/filter row (same `h-1.5`/`bg-accent` styling as `CollectionRow`'s existing bars on the Get Started page) — confirmed against a user-supplied reference screenshot before building. The already-built "Completed / Not started" status filter and the Get Started page's FF Collections progress bars both now reflect real data as a direct consequence, with no changes needed to `CollectionRow.tsx` itself.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean. Live dev-server pass using Better-Auth's official `testUtils` plugin (`node_modules/better-auth/dist/plugins/test-utils`) to mint a real signed session cookie for a throwaway test user (no OAuth available in this environment) — confirmed via Playwright: logged-out click redirects to `/login`; logged-in tick returns a real `200` from the API, persists across a full page reload, dims the title, updates the list page's progress bar and the Get Started page's FF Collections bar; the "Completed" status filter shows exactly the ticked question; the FF 75 virtual collection shows the same question ticked as its real collection (shared identity confirmed); untick reverses all of the above. Two apparent bugs surfaced during testing turned out to be test-script issues, not product bugs, once diagnosed: the difficulty/status filter pills also carry `aria-pressed`, so an unscoped `button[aria-pressed]` locator sometimes clicked a filter pill instead of a row's checkbox; and this environment's cold Turbopack-compile + TLS-proxy latency (documented in `AGENTS.md`/`env_tls_interception_supabase`) meant a route's first-ever visit could take 30–85s, which an under-provisioned test timeout misread as a broken redirect. Test user and throwaway scripts cleaned up from the dev DB/repo after verification.

**Follow-up (same session, unrelated to this fix's own code, found while the user re-tested on their own `npm run dev`):** the user hit real 404s on `/interview-prep/playbook/[playbookSlug]` and `/interview-prep/playbook/[playbookSlug]/[chapterSlug]` — every playbook, every chapter. Investigated as a potential regression before concluding it wasn't one: all 38 chapter files' frontmatter (`slug`/`playbookSlug`/`orderIndex`) verified correct against their filenames via a disposable script, `PLAYBOOK_SLUGS` matches the real directory names, `getPlaybookChapter`/`isPlaybookSlug`/`resolveChapterPath` all correct — no code or content bug found. The dev server log showed the real cause: Turbopack logged `Compiling /_not-found/page` on these requests, never attempting to compile the actual route file — a route-discovery failure, not the page rejecting the request. A full `.next` wipe + cold restart did **not** fix it (reproduced independently on a second, fully clean dev server instance). What did: a no-op save (byte-identical content, blank line added then removed) of the route's `page.tsx` while the dev server was running — fixed every param value under that route (all 6 playbooks, index and chapters) immediately, and the fix survived reverting the edit back to the original content, which rules out an application bug (a real bug would have returned once the "fix" was reverted). Documented as a standing dev-server quirk in `AGENTS.md` → "Known Dev-Server Quirks" — restarting the dev server alone does not fix this failure mode, a route file needs an explicit save.

**Follow-up 2 (same day, escalated on the user's own next fresh `npm run dev`):** the same quirk recurred, this time also hitting `/api/auth/[...all]` — `/api/auth/get-session` 404'd, so the client-side session check failed even with a valid cookie, which is why the logged-in navbar/profile flickered to a logged-out state (minimize/maximize just happened to re-trigger the check at different points relative to compilation finishing). Also hit `/interview-prep/ff-75` and both playbook routes again on this fresh boot. Fixed live the same way (no-op save on each affected file); confirmed **dev-only** — a production build compiles every route ahead of time, so this cannot occur in production regardless of which route it hits in dev.

Given this had now broken auth twice, tested a permanent fix before settling on a workaround: `next dev --webpack` (Next 16.2.9 supports falling back off Turbopack via a CLI flag) does not 404 silently, but cold-compile-per-route on a cleared cache was consistently worse (most routes exceeded 60s, several timed out entirely) than Turbopack's own worst case (~95s seen once, usually well under). Not a worthwhile trade — reverted, kept Turbopack.

Built `scripts/warmup-dev.mjs` instead (`npm run warmup`) — hits one real URL per known route file (including `/api/auth/get-session`) right after `npm run dev` is ready, so the whole app is compiled before anyone starts clicking around. First version's success check was too lenient (`status < 500`, which silently called a 404 "warm") — caught by actually reading its own output rather than trusting the summary line, since the playbook chapter route came back 404 in one run despite being reported as passing. Fixed the check to require a true 2xx (redirects still count, since `fetch` follows them to a real 2xx page — e.g. the logged-out `/interview-prep/review` → `/login` redirect is correct behavior, not a failure). That same chapter route also turned out not to reliably register from an HTTP request alone even after the fix — confirmed via the dev server log never printing a `Compiling` line for it even on a direct repeat request — so the script also auto-detects known-stubborn routes (`STUBBORN_ROUTE_FILES`) and does the no-op file-save fix itself, then re-verifies, rather than just reporting the failure. Verified end-to-end on a fully cold `.next` wipe + fresh restart: 23/23 routes warm, real 2xx on every one, including a run where the stubborn chapter route needed the auto-touch and a later run where it didn't (non-deterministic whether a plain request alone is enough — the script handles either case).
