## Decisions Made During Build

- **Pre-Feature-52 (dropping Feature 33, adding Phase 12 — Playground):** Ran the `architect` skill before this pivot, prompted by the user rejecting Feature 33 (Explore Page) outright — "I don't want explore nav and nav section" — in favor of a new idea: a CSSBattle + CodePen/neal.fun mashup. Two reference screenshots supplied (a CSSBattle editor: HTML/CSS source → live output vs. target, with Slide & Compare/Diff; and CSSBattle's horizontal-scroll daily-challenge card row). Multiple rounds of `AskUserQuestion` resolved the decisions that actually change direction: (1) **editor tech** — not React components; a real multi-file editor (file tree with `index.html`/`style.css`/`script.js`) rather than CSSBattle's single combined-source pane, confirmed after the user's first answer ("I have changed my mind" to all three initial questions) turned out to mean rejecting Explore entirely, not indecision on Explore's own details; (2) **scoring** — visual compare only in v1 (Slide & Compare/Diff toggle), no CSSBattle-style pixel-diff/character-count score, deferred as a fast-follow; (3) **Experiments delivery** — pre-built, first-party code with a read-only "View Code" panel, not a live-editable CodePen-style sandbox (avoids needing an in-browser bundler for arbitrary imports like three.js/CV libraries); (4) **target format** — a static reference screenshot per challenge (fixed width×height), not a second live-rendered sandbox pane, to keep v1 simple and match the no-scoring scope. **Scoped as a new Phase 12 — Playground** (Features 52–54, append-only numbering, same constraint Phases 6/10 hit) rather than reusing the 33 slot, scheduled to run immediately ahead of Phase 7's Roadmaps features. See `build-plan.md` → Phase 12 for the full feature specs. No code has been touched yet — this entry and the `build-plan.md`/checklist updates were made first, before any implementation, per the project's git-workflow rules.
- **00 Project Setup:** `create-next-app@latest` installed **Next.js 16.2.9** (React 19.2), not Next 15 as named in `architecture.md`. Followed the build-plan's literal `@latest` instruction rather than pinning to 15. Stack decision in `architecture.md` should be read as "Next.js 16" going forward.
  - Breaking change to plan for in **Feature 14 (Supabase Setup)**: Next 16 renames `middleware.ts`/`middleware()` to `proxy.ts`/`proxy()`. The `edge` runtime is not supported in `proxy` (runtime is fixed to `nodejs`). `architecture.md` and `build-plan.md` still say `middleware.ts` — use `proxy.ts` instead when implementing session validation.
  - Breaking change to plan for across all dynamic routes: `params`/`searchParams` (and `cookies()`/`headers()`) are async-only in Next 16 — always `await` them, no synchronous fallback.
  - Turbopack is on by default in 16; `next.config.ts` sets `turbopack.root` explicitly because a stray `package-lock.json` exists at `C:\Users\DEEPSHIKHA\` (outside this repo) which Next's workspace-root inference was picking up.
- **00 Project Setup:** The project folder name `Frontend-Forever` has capital letters, which `create-next-app` rejects as an invalid npm package name for the target directory. Worked around by scaffolding into a throwaway temp directory (lowercase name), moving the generated files into this root, and deleting the temp directory. `package.json` name is set to `frontend-forever`. No separate folder was kept — final project lives directly in this root as intended.
- **01 Design System:** `globals.css` defines the full `ui-tokens.md` token set verbatim in `@theme`, plus a small block of shadcn/ui primitive aliases (`--color-primary`, `--color-card`, `--color-muted`, `--color-ring`, etc., and `--radius-4xl` for `badge.tsx`'s pill shape) that point at our semantic tokens instead of shadcn's default oklch palette. This keeps installed primitives on-brand without ever editing files in `components/ui/`. If a newly-added shadcn component references a semantic class not yet aliased, add the alias in `globals.css` rather than hardcoding a color in the component.
- **01 Design System:** Theme persistence uses `useSyncExternalStore` in `ThemeProvider`, not `useState` + `useEffect`. An effect that calls `setState` synchronously to "correct" SSR state trips the `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` ESLint rule and is the wrong tool for syncing with an external mutable source (DOM class / localStorage) that can legitimately differ between server and client snapshots. `useSyncExternalStore`'s `getServerSnapshot` (`"light"`) vs `getSnapshot` (real DOM class) split avoids both the lint error and any hydration mismatch — the anti-FOUC script in `layout.tsx` (via `next/script`, not a raw `<script>` tag — raw tags inside a Server Component log a console error) sets the real class before hydration, and a `MutationObserver` notifies React of the change.
- **Pre-Phase-2 decision (after Phase 1 finished, before Feature 14 started): Auth provider switched from Supabase Auth to Better-Auth, "only for the authentication."** User-requested architecture change, not a bug fix. Supabase remains the hosted Postgres database (and Storage, if a future feature needs it) — only the auth/session/OAuth layer moves to Better-Auth. Resolved 3 open questions with the user before editing any docs:
  1. **Scope:** update every context doc that described Supabase as the auth provider, not just this file — `build-plan.md` (Phase 0 deps/env, Phase 2 features 14–17, Phase 3's RLS line), `architecture.md` (stack table, folder structure, OAuth/progress data flows, schema, Authentication, client patterns, invariants), `security.md`, `library-docs.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `error-handling.md`, `code-standards.md`, `project-overview.md` — all updated in this same pass so a future session reading any one of them doesn't get instructions that contradict this one.
  2. **DB adapter:** Better-Auth connects via a direct Postgres connection (`DATABASE_URL`, `pg`), not through `supabase-js`/PostgREST — it manages its own `user`/`session`/`account`/`verification` tables in the same Supabase-hosted Postgres instance. **Follow-up, same session:** user asked whether an ORM (Drizzle/Prisma) was in use — it wasn't (Kysely, a query builder, was the original pick, reusing the same library Better-Auth's own Postgres adapter is built on internally). Offered Kysely/Drizzle/Prisma as options; user chose **Drizzle**, so this was redone before any code existed: Better-Auth now uses `drizzleAdapter(db, { provider: 'pg' })` instead of a raw `Pool`, app queries go through Drizzle's query API (`db.query.*.findFirst()`, `db.insert().values().onConflictDoUpdate()`, etc.) instead of Kysely's `selectFrom`/`executeTakeFirst` builder syntax, and schema lives as TypeScript table definitions in `lib/schema/` migrated via `drizzle-kit` rather than Kysely's hand-written-SQL-plus-types approach. Every file touched in the first pass (`architecture.md`, `build-plan.md`, `security.md`, `library-docs.md`, `error-handling.md`, `code-standards.md`, this file) was re-edited in this same follow-up so nothing still shows Kysely syntax.
  3. **`profiles` linkage:** `profiles.id` now references Better-Auth's `user.id` instead of `auth.users` — Better-Auth is configured (`advanced.database.generateId`) to generate uuids so this PK stays consistent with every other table's uuid PKs, rather than Better-Auth's string-id default.
  - **The bigger architectural consequence, surfaced and resolved during this same pass, not left implicit:** RLS policies like `USING (user_id = auth.uid())` only work through Supabase's PostgREST layer with a Supabase-issued JWT. Since app data now also goes through a direct Postgres connection (reusing the same `lib/db.ts` pattern Better-Auth itself needs) rather than `supabase-js`, there is no per-request Supabase JWT for `auth.uid()` to read, and RLS doesn't apply to a direct connection anyway. Decided: **drop RLS as the authorization boundary entirely** — every query against a user-owned table must filter `WHERE user_id = session.user.id` in application code (`security.md`'s existing "defense in depth" language is now the _only_ layer, not a second one on top of RLS). `DATABASE_URL` is documented everywhere as the new equivalent of the old `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` in sensitivity — full table access, no policy layer underneath.
  - **Why this was safe to do as a docs-only pass:** confirmed via `Glob` that no auth or DB code exists yet (`src/lib/` only has `utils.ts`, no `middleware.ts`/`proxy.ts`, no `app/(auth)/`, no `app/api/`) — Phase 1 was UI-only and Phase 2 hadn't started, so there was nothing to migrate or delete, only planning docs to correct before the first line of auth code gets written.
  - **Done in Feature 14** (was: "left for whoever builds Feature 14"): `@supabase/ssr`/`@supabase/supabase-js` uninstalled, `better-auth`/`drizzle-orm`/`pg` (+ `drizzle-kit`/`@types/pg` dev deps) installed. `.env.local` updated to the new var list (`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` removed).
- **01 Design System:** Hit a stale Turbopack dev cache during verification — `globals.css` was fully rewritten but the served CSS still reflected the old shadcn token mapping (`.text-accent { color: var(--accent) }` instead of `var(--color-accent)`). Fixed by killing the dev server and deleting `.next` before restarting. Worth trying first if styles look wrong after an unusually large `globals.css` change and the file content looks correct on disk.
- **Pre-Phase-10 decision (after Feature 25 shipped, before Feature 26 started): added Phase 10 — Concept Curriculum Expansion to `build-plan.md`.** User observation that started it: every category has exactly 1 built concept (JS Runtime = Event Loop only), and even the concepts with a full 4-tab experience (React Rendering, CSS Specificity) are missing a written guide — the catalog looks far thinner than the platform's own Interview Prep collections imply. Four decisions resolved with the user up front:
  1. **Priority:** run this phase next, ahead of resuming Phase 5+ (Practice/Interview Prep/Roadmaps/Leaderboard/Premium) — those all lean on a real concept catalog behind them. **Refinement, same session, in two steps:** first, Feature 26 (Build Tab) — already "Next" before this decision — still runs _first_, since it's the last missing tab _system_ and Phase 10 is about filling systems that already exist, not building new ones. **Second correction (user caught this, not initially planned for):** Feature 27 (Progress API + XP System) — the actual last feature of Phase 4, not Feature 26 — also has to run before Phase 10. `/api/progress` was deliberately built minimal in Feature 22 (writes only the tab-completion flag; Feature 27 layers on `xp_events`, `profiles.xp`, and streak updates) — every tab completion across every concept, including all of Phase 10's new ones, routes through it, so it needs to be complete before Phase 10 floods the catalog with content that would otherwise complete without earning XP. So the actual order is: 26 → 27 → Phase 10 → Phase 5+.
  2. **Simulate tab scope:** user chose "every concept eventually gets one" over the recommended "flagship concepts only." This makes Simulate open-ended — each simulator is a multi-day bespoke Framer Motion build (Features 09–12's precedent), so it is **not** one of Phase 10's numbered features; it's an ongoing backlog pulled from over time (prioritization rule: every category reaches 2 simulators before any category reaches 3), independent of the Understand/Challenge/Interview/Build content passes.
  3. **Scale:** decided category-by-category rather than one target count — JS Runtime and React ended up deepest (14 and 12 concepts) matching how real interview platforms weight those two; System Design stayed shortest (7) since there's little genuinely "beginner" content in frontend system design.
  4. **Concept list authorship:** user asked for a drafted proposal to react to, not a from-scratch brainstorm. Delivered as a reviewable artifact (not written into the repo until approved), then revised twice before being written into `build-plan.md`:
     - **Senior-engineer review pass** (explicit user ask: "review it like a senior engineer, don't miss important concepts"): caught 2 real teaching-order bugs (Event Loop was sequenced after Promises & Async/Await, backwards — promises are scheduled _by_ the event loop; CSS Specificity was sequenced before Cascade & Inheritance, its own prerequisite) and ~15 real content gaps, the most significant being TypeScript jumping straight to Interfaces with no beginner on-ramp, React having no Forms concept despite it being one of the most common practical React interview tasks, JS Runtime never covering `==`/`===` coercion despite it already being a seeded interview question, and Browser Internals having nothing on CORS or web security (XSS/CSRF/CSP) despite this project's own `security.md` leaning on exactly that knowledge.
     - **Follow-up catch (user-reported):** Callbacks & Higher-Order Functions was missing from JS Runtime entirely — not a minor gap, since it's the prerequisite for Array & Object Methods (map/filter/reduce _are_ the callback pattern) and the motivating problem Promises exist to solve. Inserted after Closures, which also reordered Array & Object Methods to come after it rather than before.
  - **Final approved curriculum: 76 concepts (8 existing + 68 proposed)** across all 8 categories, transcribed in full into `build-plan.md`'s Phase 10 section (Features 41–48, one per category) — that section, not any external draft, is now the durable source of truth.
  - **Numbering constraint surfaced and resolved:** feature numbers are tied to git branch names (`feature/[number]-[name]`) throughout this file's history, so a new phase can only be _numbered_ at the end (40+) even though it's _scheduled_ to run before Phase 5 — build-plan.md's top-level "Build order" line and the Phase 10 section both carry a note explaining this so a future session doesn't assume numbering order means execution order.
- **41 JavaScript Runtime Concepts: a platform-wide premium-gating proposal was raised and deliberately deferred to Feature 38, not built here.** The user proposed a broader rule mid-feature — beginner concepts fully free across all 5 tabs, intermediate/advanced concepts fully locked across all 5 tabs, platform-wide, for every category. Surfaced back to the user rather than folded into this feature: Feature 41 was scoped as content-only (zero code changes), the rule contradicts the currently-documented gate list in `project-overview.md`/`security.md` (which gates specific things — FF System Design questions, Build tab, Study Plans, Company Collections — not "every tab of every intermediate+ concept"), and only Build tab actually has premium lock/unlock UI today — Understand/Simulate/Challenge/Interview have no concept-level gate in the code at all. **Decision: keep Feature 41 content-only, using only the existing per-item `isPremium` mechanism (already exercised in `PROJECT_BRIEFS`/`CHALLENGES`, though only Build's was ever seen by a real user before this feature); Feature 38 (Premium Content Gating) should implement the user's rule — new gating code across the 4 ungated tabs, applied platform-wide by difficulty — when that feature comes up.** `build-plan.md`'s Feature 38 section should get a note reflecting this the next time it's touched.
- **42 Browser Internals Concepts: two of Feature 41's per-tab content rules were overridden, effective from this feature forward through Features 43–48 (not retroactive to Features 40/41's already-shipped content).**
  1. **Challenge is now mandatory for every concept, not selective.** Feature 41 skipped a Challenge for 2 of its 13 concepts ("not a natural executable-code exercise"). The user rejected that precedent outright — every concept gets one, in this feature and every remaining category. Where a concept isn't naturally a "write a function" exercise (CORS, web security, the network stack, service workers, Web Workers), the Challenge reframes the browser mechanic as a pure-logic problem the sandbox can assert against — the same technique Feature 41's `render-blocking-analyzer` project brief already used, just applied to Challenge too.
  2. **Premium is now concept-difficulty-driven, uniformly across every tab** (beginner → free everywhere, intermediate/advanced → premium everywhere), replacing Feature 41's per-tab rules (Challenge: only-advanced-premium; Interview: always-free regardless of difficulty). This is effectively pulling forward the _data_ half of the platform-wide rule Feature 38 will eventually enforce with real gating UI — `concepts.isPremium` is used for real for the first time (previously `false` on every concept ever seeded). **Explicitly forward-only, per the user:** the original 8 pre-Phase-10 concepts and all 13 Feature 41 JS Runtime concepts are untouched, still `isPremium: false` at the concept level — "we'll take care of it in the future" if a retroactive pass is ever wanted.
  - **`browser-rendering-pipeline` (this feature's one pre-existing concept) was treated like Feature 41 treated `event-loop`:** already shipped with `isPremium: false`, left untouched rather than forced premium by its `intermediate` difficulty; its one new addition (a Challenge, `classify-style-change`) matches that at `isPremium: false` too, so the concept doesn't end up free with one premium-gated tab.
  - **Scope correction found mid-feature:** `browser-rendering-pipeline` turned out to have **no Understand MDX guide at all** — only `javascript-runtime` had any content on disk before this feature; the "existing concept, just add the missing Challenge" assumption from planning was wrong. This feature wrote all 9 Understand guides, not 8.
  - **Verified for real, not just by inspection**, following Feature 41's `find-leaked-listeners` precedent: a standalone Node harness mirroring `buildSandboxDoc.ts`'s exact `assert`/`assertEqual`/`delay` semantics ran all 72 real test assertions against the 17 new Challenge/Build `solutionCode`s (all passed), plus a deliberately wrong solution confirmed to fail (ruling out a vacuous harness). `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` — 76 concepts (unchanged), 24 challenges (15 → 24), 144 interview questions (104 → 144), 25 project briefs (17 → 25); re-ran to confirm idempotency (identical counts). `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint` clean. Live dev server (cleared `.next` first, per Feature 40's documented stale-cache gotcha): spot-checked a free concept (`dom-vs-bom`) and a premium one (`web-workers-concurrency`) — all 4 tabs render real content on both, no "coming soon" fallback, no console/server errors.
- **Pre-Feature-28 decision (2026-07-10): restructured Feature 28 (Practice) from a single flat filterable list into a two-level Hub → Category List flow, before any Phase 5 code was written.** User shared BFE.dev screenshots (its Practice mega-dropdown of 9 categories with counts, a per-category filtered question list, and a split-pane problem/editor page) as a structural reference, explicitly critiquing its visual execution — dense gray text rows, ad slots crammed into the editor page — as "not looking good," while confirming the underlying flow (browse by category → filtered list → editor) was worth keeping. User then explicitly delegated the remaining design/engineering judgment calls ("you know better than me what is good for the platform and for the users") rather than specifying every detail.
  - **Decisions made under that delegated judgment:** (1) drive the hub off FF's own existing 8 concept categories (the same taxonomy already powering Learn) rather than inventing a parallel one — category cards reuse Feature 20 Learn Index's visual pattern (icon + name + count + completion %); (2) challenges render as cards (title, difficulty badge, concept tag, completion check), not BFE's plain numbered link rows; (3) the editor page's left panel (Feature 29) becomes tabbed (Description/Hints/Test Cases) instead of BFE's always-visible cluttered icon-button row.
  - **Ad slot:** user's own idea — in the future, once real traffic exists, add a single Google AdSense placement styled as one tasteful premium/sponsor banner, explicitly not BFE's scattered "ads via Carbon" look. Recorded as a deferred future note directly in Feature 29's spec; no placeholder ad UI is built now.
  - `build-plan.md`'s Feature 28 section rewritten to spec both the Hub (`/practice`) and Category List (`/practice/[category]`) pages under one feature (no new feature number needed — both are browse/list UI, same scope class as other multi-page features like Feature 21's shell+sidebar); Feature 29 updated with the tabbed left panel and the future ad-slot note. No code written yet — planning-only pass, done ahead of Feature 28 actually starting.
- **Post-Feature-47 decision (2026-07-09): added Phase 11 — Simulator Reimagining to `build-plan.md`, explicitly long-term/unscheduled, not a redirect of current work.** Started from the user's observation that the platform has drifted toward a text-guide + LeetCode-style-Challenge + Interview-Q&A model, away from the original "see concepts working, playful, game-like" founding idea. Confirmed the gap is real, not just a feeling: only 4 of 76 concepts have any Simulate tab at all (Features 09–12), and even those 4 are scripted walkthroughs (Play/Step/Restart/Speed over one fixed scenario) rather than something manipulable. Compared against paperdraw.dev (a system-design tool where users build/break their own architecture and watch live metrics react) and directly against `designs/hero-section-1-react-rendering.png` — the shipped hero simulator is a clean SaaS-dashboard diagram (thin-border cards, dotted arrows), its "Real DOM" panel is a static text mockup, and there's no code editor in it anywhere. The vision the user described: a real code editor as input, the internal pipeline animated interactively (game/video-like, pausable, pokeable) rather than steps highlighting in sequence, and a genuine rendered-output canvas showing the real page live — all in an illustrated/cartoonish "white paper canvas" style, not the current SaaS-panel look. **Explicitly confirmed as long-term**, not something to act on now — Phase 10 and the Simulator Backlog continue unchanged. Written into `build-plan.md` as Phase 11 with no numbered features yet, so the direction isn't lost but also doesn't block or reorder anything currently in flight.
- **Pre-Feature-30 decision (2026-07-14): Phase 6 — Interview Prep restructured from 3 features to 6 (30–32 kept, 49–51 added) during pre-build planning, before any Feature 30 code was written.** Started from the original `build-plan.md` spec (a simple 4-collection hub + collection pages + spaced repetition), then went through several full pivots across one long planning session — recorded here in full so a future session sees the reasoning, not just the final shape.
  1. **GreatFrontEnd deep-dive.** User asked for a real analysis of greatfrontend.com, not a guess from training data — fetched and screenshotted the live homepage and `/prepare` page (Playwright, via the `C:\tmp_pw` scratch environment already set up in this project for design verification). Found `/prepare` is far richer than a 4-card hub: a persistent sidebar (Get Started/Practice Questions/Recommended Strategy/Time-Savers/Guides), a numbered "Recommended preparation" pathway, time-boxed Study Plans, a ~30-company Company Guides grid, and a Question Formats/Frameworks breakdown — all using one consistent horizontal row pattern (icon → title → description → count + progress bar → arrow), not portrait cards. Cross-checked against this project's own `project-overview.md` and found **Study Plans and Company Collections were already promised in the Monetization section but had never been given a `build-plan.md` feature number** — the GFE reference wasn't just a style swap, it closed a real planning gap.
  2. **First locked scope (via `AskUserQuestion`):** adopt GFE's row-list pattern over FF's existing portrait `CategoryCard` style; expand Feature 30 to absorb Company Guides + Study Plans now rather than defer them; add an Interview-Prep-scoped sidebar. Premium gating for the new sections deferred to Feature 38, matching how every other tab already works (no gate until that feature exists).

Must validate `profiles.username`'s existing unique constraint server-side (surface a real "username taken" error, not a raw Postgres constraint failure).

Lists real linked providers from the `account` table (`authClient.listAccounts()`), allows linking the other of Google/GitHub (`authClient.linkSocial()`) and unlinking (`authClient.unlinkAccount()`) — **must block unlinking down to zero linked providers**, since `auth.ts`'s `socialProviders` config has no `emailAndPassword` fallback (confirmed: OAuth-only, no password auth exists in this codebase) and a zero-provider account would be permanently unreachable. Danger-zone account deletion (`authClient.deleteUser()`, cascades via `profiles.id`'s existing `onDelete: "cascade"` FK) uses **type-to-confirm** (user must type their username/email into a field before the delete button enables) — chosen over a plain confirm-dialog for the extra deliberateness a destructive, irreversible action warrants, same reasoning as this project's own git-safety rules around destructive commands.
  3. **58 — Security (active sessions).** New "Security" sidebar section. Real session list from the existing `session` table (`ipAddress`, `userAgent`, `createdAt`/`updatedAt` columns already exist, unused by any UI today) via `authClient.listSessions()` — no new schema. Device/browser label (e.g. "Windows · Chrome") via a **small local regex-based parsing helper reading `userAgent` for OS + browser name only** — user chose this over adding a UA-parsing dependency (e.g. `ua-parser-js`) as overkill for what's a fairly small feature. Tags the caller's own current session, per-session revoke (`authClient.revokeSession()`), and a "sign out of all other devices" action (`authClient.revokeOtherSessions()`).

Adds a reduced-motion **manual override** toggle layered on top of the existing `useSafeReducedMotion` OS-level (`prefers-reduced-motion`) detection — same `useSyncExternalStore` pattern as the theme system and `useUser.ts`, not a `useState`+`useEffect` flag (would trip the same `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule already hit and fixed twice this project).

- **58 Settings — Security (Active Sessions): two real findings, one from planning and one from testing, both driven by reading Better Auth's own source rather than assuming its documented client API behaves as a black box.**
  1. **Planning-time finding, resolved via `architect` + `AskUserQuestion` before any code was written:** `authClient.listSessions()` / `auth.api.listSessions()` runs behind Better Auth's `freshSessionMiddleware` (`session.mjs`), which requires the session's `createdAt` to be within `freshAge` (defaults to 1 day, unset in `auth.ts`). Sessions themselves last 7 days (`expiresIn` default) and get silently extended on activity (`updateAge`), but `createdAt` never resets — so any user who signed in more than a day ago, i.e. most returning users, would hit `SESSION_NOT_FRESH` just trying to *view* their own session list, not only when revoking one. **Decision (user-confirmed over the alternative of showing a re-auth wall on every stale visit):** `getActiveSessions` in `queries.ts` reads the `session` table directly via Drizzle, scoped `WHERE user_id = session.user.id` — the same app-level authorization pattern every other user-owned-table query in this codebase already uses, safe here specifically because it's a read. The freshness gate stays exactly where it belongs: on the real mutations (`authClient.revokeSession()`/individual revoke), which correctly can ask a stale-session user to re-auth, matching Feature 57's `isSessionNotFreshError`/`REAUTH_MESSAGE` precedent.
  2. **Testing-time finding, not caught during planning — surfaced by a disposable-fake-session testing technique built for this feature:** with only 1 real active session on the test account, the real "sign out of all other devices" mutation had nothing genuine to revoke against. Rather than touch the account's real other devices (against this project's standing testing rules) or skip verifying the mutation entirely, two throwaway `session` rows were inserted directly into Postgres via a scratch script (`scripts/_f58_fake_sessions.ts`, deleted after use), scoped to the real account's own `userId`, with fake tokens/IPs/user-agents — then the real (unmocked) revoke flow was run against them and cleaned up. This is a DB write, so it went through explicit `AskUserQuestion` confirmation (Auto Mode's safety classifier correctly blocked the first unconfirmed attempt) with the exact SQL shown before running. Testing this way caught a real bug that pure error-path mocking would have missed: `authClient.revokeOtherSessions()` returned `{status:true}` but never actually deleted the fake rows. Root cause, confirmed by reading `internal-adapter.mjs` directly: with `secondaryStorage` (Redis) configured, `revokeOtherSessions()`'s internal `listSessions()` call reads **only** Redis's `active-sessions-{userId}` index — no Postgres fallback exists in that code path — while `revokeSession({token})` (the single-session endpoint) *does* fall back to Postgres per-token when `storeSessionInDatabase: true` is set (confirmed true in this project's `auth.ts`). Since this feature's viewable list is sourced from Postgres (decision 1 above), a Redis/Postgres index drift would let "sign out of all other devices" silently under-revoke relative to what the user sees on screen. **Fixed by not calling `revokeOtherSessions()` at all** — `SecuritySection.tsx`'s `handleRevokeOthers` instead loops `authClient.revokeSession({token})` over every session already in the component's own `sessions` prop (excluding the current one), guaranteeing it revokes exactly what's rendered. Verified for real: DB-confirmed 2→0 fake sessions revoked, real session left untouched, in both the individual-revoke and sign-out-others paths.
  - **Also verified:** unauthenticated access redirects to `/login?callbackURL=...` (same pattern as `profile`/`account`); device/browser label parsing (`parseUserAgent.ts`, small local regex, no `ua-parser-js` dependency per the Pre-Feature-56 scope) correctly read a real `Windows · Chrome` UA; client-side sidebar navigation into Security works (confirmed slower under this dev environment's known Turbopack/filesystem-cache latency — up to 55s+ compile-cache writes logged for unrelated routes in the same window — not a real app bug); `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean throughout.
  - **User-reported suspected bug, investigated and confirmed as expected behavior, not a defect:** testing with two real browsers signed into the same account, clicking "sign out of all other devices" in Browser 1 appeared not to log Browser 2 out — Browser 2's already-open tab kept rendering as signed in. A direct read-only Postgres check confirmed the revoke *had* actually deleted Browser 2's session row immediately — the DB layer was correct. The apparent bug was Browser 2's idle tab simply never having made a new request since the revoke; there's no websocket/push channel telling an open tab to re-check its session. Confirmed by having the user navigate within Browser 2 (no reload needed) — it was immediately redirected to `/login?callbackURL=...`, proving enforcement is real-time on the server side, just not proactively pushed to an idle client. **Decided not to build a polling/websocket layer to force-close idle other-device tabs in real time** — meaningfully bigger infra for a narrow UX gain, not in Feature 58's scope. Explicitly re-confirmed with the user after they asked directly whether Browser 2 should auto-redirect like "other apps" do: presented three options (leave as-is / app-wide lightweight polling every 30–60s / full WebSocket-SSE push), user chose to leave as-is — next-request enforcement (already proven immediate and correct) is enough, no new background infrastructure. Also worth noting for future sessions: `auth.ts`'s `cookieCache` (`maxAge: 5*60`) could in principle let a revoked session validate from its signed cookie without hitting the DB for up to 5 minutes (a caveat Better Auth's own docs call out directly) — not what was observed here (enforcement was immediate), but worth remembering if a similar report ever surfaces stale-auth behavior that *survives* a real reload, not just an idle tab.
  - **Follow-up, same day: the "worth remembering" caveat above turned out to be real, not hypothetical.** A second live test (still two real browsers, same account) reported Browser 1 staying fully functional — including surviving a hard reload — for 5-10 minutes after Browser 2 revoked it. This is a materially different, more serious report than the one above: idle-tab staleness is expected and harmless; a *fresh reload* still succeeding for minutes after a real revoke defeats the entire purpose of a "revoke this device" security control (e.g. a stolen laptop stays usable). A direct Postgres check confirmed the account had exactly one non-expired session at the time — the DB layer was correct again, isolating the cause to the auth/session-validation layer, not the revoke logic itself. Root cause: `auth.ts`'s `cookieCache` (`maxAge: 5 * 60`) — Better Auth's own docs state directly that "revoked sessions may remain active on other devices until the cookie cache expires." **Fix, confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` given it's a global auth-performance tradeoff, not scoped to this one page:** presented three options (shrink `maxAge` to ~30s / disable `cookieCache` entirely / scope an uncached check to `/settings/**` only); user chose shrinking to 30s — closes the exposure window from minutes to seconds while still avoiding a DB hit on every single click during ordinary fast browsing. **Verified for real, not just by config inspection:** with explicit per-action user approval (this deletes a real, current session row — the same effect a real revoke has, fully reversible by logging back in), ran a tightly-sequenced live test — fetch a fresh Better Auth cache cookie via the real `/api/auth/get-session` endpoint, delete that exact session's Postgres row (simulating another device's revoke), then immediately recheck `/settings/security` using the cached cookie. Result: rejected (redirected to `/login`) only ~9 seconds after the revoke and ~16 seconds after the cache cookie was minted — well inside the new 30s ceiling and nowhere near the old 5-10 minute observation. **One nuance surfaced during this test, worth remembering:** a raw page-route request (`getCachedSession()` → `auth.api.getSession()` called directly from a Server Component) never even triggered Better Auth to *set* a `session_data` cache cookie in the response — only hitting the real `/api/auth/get-session` HTTP endpoint directly did. This suggests the original 5-10 minute report may have been compounded by this dev environment's already-documented Turbopack/filesystem-cache latency under load (visible as a "Compiling..." badge in the user's own screenshot) rather than being explained by cookie-cache masking alone — the fix is still correct and directly targets Better Auth's documented mechanism, but the full original delay likely wasn't from one single cause.
  - **Final confirmation, a genuine live two-real-browser run (not curl) after a clean dev-server restart:** both browsers loaded `/settings/security` simultaneously, each correctly self-identified as "This device" and the other as revocable, and clicking "sign out of all other devices" in Browser 1 correctly dropped its own list to one row. A same-moment reload of Browser 2 (~18s after the click) *did* still succeed — this is not a bug, it's the intentionally-bounded residual window the 30s `maxAge` still allows (down from minutes to under 30s, the exact tradeoff approved above). Re-checking Browser 2 again after the window had genuinely elapsed correctly redirected it to `/login?callbackURL=...`. Postgres confirmed the revoked session row was already gone throughout — the residual window is purely about how long a *already-cached* signed cookie is still honored, not about the revoke itself being delayed or unreliable.
  - **Unrelated, same investigation session: a spurious "useThemeContext must be used within a ThemeProvider" + hydration-mismatch error and a severe (10-15 minute) `/login` compile stall both turned out to be dev-environment issues, not code regressions.** The theme error was stale Turbopack dev state after the large `auth.ts` edit (which is imported by nearly every route) — fixed via this project's documented pattern (kill dev server, `rm -rf .next`, restart), confirmed via an automated extension-free Playwright check showing zero console errors afterward. A second, separate hydration warning (`__processed_<uuid>__` injected onto `<body>`) was a browser-extension DOM-modification artifact, not app code — confirmed by the same clean automated check finding nothing wrong. The severe `/login` compile stall (dev log showed individual "Finished writing to filesystem cache" steps taking 3-5 *minutes* each, versus 10-58 seconds earlier the same session) was traced to likely Windows Defender real-time protection scanning every file Turbopack's persistent cache writes to `.next/` — a well-documented Windows dev-environment problem. No supported Next.js 16.2.9/Turbopack config flag was found to disable the persistent filesystem cache outright; the recommended fix (communicated to the user, not applied by the assistant since it requires admin elevation — a real system-security decision) is a Windows Defender exclusion for the project folder (`Add-MpPreference -ExclusionPath ...`).
  - **Full pre-merge regression pass, requested explicitly ("test the app every functionality... like a senior software tester or SDET") before opening the PR — every route in the app, not just Feature 58's own.** Fresh dev server, real slugs pulled from Postgres/content files for every dynamic route template. Public/static routes (34+, spanning Learn/Practice/Interview Prep/Roadmaps/Playground/Homepage/Login/Changelog/Feedback): all correct (200 for real content, 307 redirect-to-login for auth-gated routes when logged out, 404 for genuinely-invalid slugs). Authenticated routes (Dashboard, Leaderboard, Interview Prep Review, all 3 Settings sections) with a fresh cookie: all correct, zero console errors, sidebar client-side navigation between Settings sections confirmed working. One real, reproducible-at-the-time bug surfaced: every `/interview-prep/playbook/[playbookSlug]/[chapterSlug]` route (Feature 50, unrelated to Feature 58) returned a fast, non-timeout 404 — investigated down to the filesystem-read layer (`playbookGuides.ts`), confirmed via a standalone script that the exact same path-resolution logic correctly finds the file when run outside the dev server, and confirmed via the dev log that Turbopack had never even attempted to compile that specific nested route (no "Compiling" log entry despite repeated real requests) — diagnosed as dev-server route-table staleness on that specific running instance, not a code defect. Resolved by a full clean restart (`rm -rf .next`); re-verified on the fresh instance across every playbook (all 6) and multiple chapters — all 200. This dev machine's variable, sometimes-severe resource contention (Chrome + VS Code + this session's own heavy Playwright/curl/tsx usage all competing) produced a large batch of misleading Playwright navigation timeouts mid-pass on routes already proven working seconds earlier via lightweight curl — each was individually re-verified via curl (the lighter-weight, more reliable tool under contention) rather than accepted at face value, and none were real. **No code changes were needed as a result of this regression pass** — Feature 58's own code (from the two earlier review rounds) held up, and the one non-Feature-58 bug found was environmental, not a defect requiring a fix.
  - **Committed (`9ce8068`) and pushed to `origin/feature/58-settings-security`; PR not yet opened.** A CodeRabbit review against the pushed branch then found one real, meaningful gap the prior two review rounds missed: `getActiveSessions`/`ActiveSession` selected and returned the raw `session.token` column to the client so `SecuritySection.tsx` could call `authClient.revokeSession({token})` directly — a session token is a bearer credential, the exact value Better Auth signs into the auth cookie, so shipping every listed session's token (not just the caller's own) to client-side JS for every page view was a real exposure, not a style nit. **Fixed by removing tokens from the client entirely**, not just minimizing exposure: `ActiveSession` no longer has a `token` field; `SecuritySection` now identifies sessions and tags "This device" by `id` (`currentSessionId`, sourced from `getCachedSession()`'s own `session.session.id` — no token needed there either); two new server-only routes, `POST /api/settings/security/revoke` (body `{sessionId}`) and `POST /api/settings/security/revoke-others` (no body — computes "every other session" fresh from Postgres server-side rather than trusting a client-supplied list), look the target token(s) up scoped to `WHERE id = ... AND user_id = caller`, then hand them to `auth.api.revokeSession()` server-side, which still enforces its own freshness gate. Same pass fixed two smaller CodeRabbit findings: `revoke-others`'s loop now uses `Promise.allSettled` (matches the Feature 57 @better-fetch finding — a thrown rejection from one session's revoke must not skip the others or skip reporting what did succeed) instead of `Promise.all`; `getActiveSessions` now caps at 50 rows. Verified for real against the pushed code, not just re-reviewed: confirmed zero token substrings anywhere in the Security page's rendered HTML/RSC payload, then re-ran the same disposable-fake-session revoke test (individual + "sign out of others") end-to-end through the two new routes — both real, DB-confirmed successes, with the one apparent failure on the first attempt traced to this dev machine's already-documented severe latency (a `router.refresh()` that took 65s under load, not a broken route — confirmed via a direct curl call to the same route returning `{"success":true}` in 4.5s). `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean throughout.
- **59 Settings — Appearance: rescoped from its original two-part spec (theme + reduced motion) down to reduced motion only, via `architect` before any code was written.** The Pre-Feature-56 decision entry's original plan was to surface the existing light/dark/system theme mechanism in a proper settings home, plus a reduced-motion override. Before building, the user pushed back on the theme half: a direct grep confirmed theme switching already exists in **three** separate places — the navbar `ThemeToggle`, `UserDropdown`, and `LearnSidebar` — all sharing the exact same 2-way light/dark toggle. Agreed this was real duplication, not a gap: the only thing theoretically missing anywhere (an explicit "System" option, since today the only way back to following the OS is manually clearing `localStorage`) was minor and unrequested, and would have required extending `ThemeProvider`'s context/API for near-zero real benefit. Rather than invent alternative scope to fill the slot, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` that Reduced Motion alone — a real, non-duplicated accessibility gap — justified keeping a slimmed-down Feature 59 over dropping it entirely. **As built:** `useSafeReducedMotion.ts` (previously OS-query-only) now also reads a `reduced-motion-preference` `localStorage` key (`"system" | "on" | "off"`), with an explicit override always winning over the OS `prefers-reduced-motion` query — merged into the existing hook itself, so all 21 pre-existing call sites (simulators, streak celebration, card animations) respect it automatically with zero changes to any of them. A module-level listener set (notified by the new `setReducedMotionPreference`) feeds the same `useSyncExternalStore` `subscribe` the hook already had, so a Settings → Appearance toggle takes effect immediately in any simulator mounted at the same time (e.g. behind the Settings modal), not just after a reload — same live-update guarantee `ThemeProvider` already provides for theme. New `AppearanceSection.tsx` reuses `LeaderboardRangeTabs`' existing segmented-pill visual pattern (no new dependency, no shadcn `radio-group`) for a 3-way System/On/Off control. Wired in exactly like Security: `"appearance"` added to `SETTINGS_SECTIONS`/`SETTINGS_SECTION_META` (icon: `Wind` from `lucide-react` — swapped in from an initial `Accessibility` pick after live testing, since that read as a broader accessibility-settings claim than one motion toggle actually is), a branch in `SettingsSectionContent.tsx` — the only section with no server data fetch, since the setting is entirely client-side/`localStorage`-scoped by design (no schema, no API route, no cross-device sync, matching the existing theme mechanism's own scope). Verified: `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean; unauthenticated `/settings/appearance` correctly 307-redirects to `/login?callbackURL=%2Fsettings%2Fappearance`, same as the other 3 sections.

---

## Notes

- **Parallel-subagent worktree isolation has a sparse-checkout gap in this project — a freshly created worktree can be missing `scripts/seed.ts` entirely (found during the Content Initiative's large-scale rewrite, see above; not specific to that work).** When a worktree lacks a file it needs, agents fall back to working directly wherever they can find it — which, left unguided, causes multiple agents to independently discover and converge on the same pre-existing worktree/file, producing real write contention (a lost-update race was hit and self-corrected once during that initiative).
  - **What worked:** telling every agent up front, explicitly, to create its own uniquely-named worktree (`git worktree add .claude/worktrees/<unique-name> <branch>`) if its assigned one is missing the file, rather than searching for/reusing an existing one. Zero contention across 9 agents doing the same class of work once this instruction was added, versus real contention across 10 agents doing similar work without it.
  - **Reconciling multiple worktrees' independent edits back into one file:** diff each worktree's final file against the common base commit they all branched from, confirm zero of the same slug/entry was changed differently by two agents (a real conflict, not just two agents touching the same file), then apply all non-conflicting changes onto one base. A plain per-line diff is noisy across large files with many small scattered edits — extracting and comparing by logical unit (e.g. one JS object entry's field) rather than by raw line range makes the "did two agents touch the _same thing_" check reliable.
  - **If this resurfaces:** don't assume a second agent's "worktree" is actually isolated — verify `git worktree list` and each worktree's actual branch before trusting that concurrent agents can't collide.

- **`scripts/seed.ts` doesn't invalidate a running dev server's Next.js Data Cache — a freshly-seeded (or renamed) concept can 404 or show stale data until the server restarts (found during Feature 40's rollout, not specific to that feature's content).** User reported a brand-new concept page 404ing right after Feature 40 seeded it, plus an unrelated-looking auth glitch (appeared logged out, logging back in bounced to the homepage still showing "Log In"), both on a dev server that had been running continuously across several reseeds.
  - **Root cause (confirmed, not assumed):** `getConceptBySlug`/`getConceptCatalog` (`features/learn/lib/queries/concepts.ts`) are wrapped in `unstable_cache(..., { tags: ["concepts"], revalidate: 3600 })`. `scripts/seed.ts` is a standalone Node process that writes straight to Postgres via its own `pg` pool — it has no request/server-action context, so it has no way to call Next's `revalidateTag("concepts")` on an already-running dev server. If a slug was queried (e.g. by anyone clicking around) _before_ the seed added/renamed it, that miss (or stale row) is cached for up to an hour. Verified directly: `curl` to the 404ing route reproduced it live while a fresh DB query confirmed the row existed correctly (right slug, right category) — so this was a stale-cache-read problem, not a data problem.
  - **Fix:** restart the dev server after running `scripts/seed.ts` while it's already running — this clears Next's in-memory Data Cache (a `.next` clear isn't required for this specific issue, that's the separate Turbopack filesystem-cache problem documented elsewhere in this file, though clearing it too is harmless). Restarting resolved both the concept 404 and the auth glitch in one step, consistent with both being downstream of the same long-running, increasingly-stale server process rather than two separate bugs.
  - **If this resurfaces:** after any `npx tsx scripts/seed.ts` run, restart whatever `next dev` process is already running before testing the new/changed content — don't assume a real regression until you've done that.

- **User avatar sometimes shows fallback initials instead of the real OAuth photo, dev-only, not a code bug (found during Feature 26's verification, unrelated to that feature's own code):** user reported the Navbar showing initials ("SI") instead of their real Google profile photo after logging in. Diagnosed via the user's own DevTools Network tab screenshots rather than guessing — two requests fired to the _identical_ `lh3.googleusercontent.com` avatar URL: the first `200 OK`, the second `429 Too Many Requests` — and the 429 came from Google's own image-serving infra (`Server: fife`), not this app.
  - **Root cause:** `@base-ui/react`'s `Avatar` (`useImageLoadingStatus`) detects load status by creating a hidden `new Image()` and setting `.src` inside a `useLayoutEffect`. React Strict Mode (on by default, dev-only) intentionally double-invokes every effect (mount → cleanup → mount again) to surface bugs — this creates that probe _twice_ in quick succession for the same URL, firing two near-simultaneous real network requests. Google's CDN rejects the second one with a 429. Whichever of the two `Image()` instances React ends up keeping determines the final status shown; if that one lost the race, the Avatar renders its fallback initials instead of the photo.
  - **Confirmed dev-only, not a real app bug:** Strict Mode's double-effect-invocation doesn't happen in a production build (`next build && next start`), so this specific race can't occur once deployed. Verified the underlying DB/session data was correct throughout (`profiles.avatar_url` and Better-Auth's `user.image` both held a real, working Google photo URL) — this was purely a client-side dev-mode timing artifact, never a data problem.
  - **Why a plain refresh didn't immediately fix it:** the user had DevTools' Network tab "Disable cache" checkbox on (common while actively debugging) — that forces every reload to bypass the HTTP cache entirely, so the same race replayed on every single reload instead of the first successful response's `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400` ever getting a chance to serve the second request from cache. Unchecking it and reloading normally resolved it immediately.
  - **If this resurfaces:** it's cosmetic-only (fallback initials, not a crash or missing data) and specific to `next dev` with DevTools cache disabled — don't assume a data/schema regression; check the Network tab for the same 200-then-429-from-`fife` pattern first.

- **Cross-cutting responsive breakpoint audit (same session as Feature 09, before starting Feature 10):** user tested the live site on real devices (14" laptop, iPad portrait/landscape, iPhone) via the local network and reported several layout problems with screenshots from each. Rather than guessing from the screenshots, measured every claim directly with Playwright at matching viewport sizes (1366×768/1512×982 laptop, 768×1024 tablet portrait, 1024×768 tablet landscape, 390×844 mobile) before changing anything — found real, precise root causes, not vague impressions:
  1. **Tablet landscape (1024px) page-level overflow + cramped navbar:** `Navbar.tsx`'s full desktop nav (pill + Follow-on-X/theme-toggle/Log-In group) switches on at `lg:` (1024px), but doesn't actually have room until well past that — measured the right-side button group alone extending 70px past the viewport edge at exactly 1024px. Fixed by raising all 4 of `Navbar.tsx`'s `lg:flex`/`lg:hidden` breakpoints to `xl:` (1280px), so the hamburger menu now covers the 768–1279px range instead of a cramped full nav.
  2. **Tablet portrait (768px) page-level overflow from company logos:** `CompanyLogosStrip.tsx`'s "no-scroll, centered" mode switched on at `sm:` (640px), but measured that 7 logos need ~960px to actually fit without overflowing — at 768px the row was 54px wider than its own box with `overflow-visible` set, bleeding into the page's real scroll width. Fixed by raising every `sm:` class in that row to `lg:` (1024px), matching where the row's own `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` were empirically confirmed equal.
  3. **Mobile (390px) — the page could be scrolled horizontally, full stop.** Traced to `ConceptSwitcherTabs.tsx`, not the previously-assumed `CompanyLogosStrip` (past sessions' notes had misattributed a similar-looking number to the logos strip without re-verifying which element was actually responsible each time — worth remembering before citing an old "known baseline" as an explanation again). The tab group had zero overflow handling (`inline-flex`, no wrap) — measured its box at `left: -50, right: 440`, and `440` was the _exact_ number matching `document.documentElement.scrollWidth`. Fixed by changing the container to `flex flex-wrap justify-center` with a `max-w-full`, and shrinking the pills' padding/font/icon size at the base breakpoint (`sm:` and up keeps the original sizing) — now wraps cleanly to 2 rows of 2 instead of forcing the page wider.
  4. **Laptop (1366×768 and similar shorter-viewport laptops) — the Event Loop simulator card (796px tall) was taller than the viewport (768px),** so no scroll position could show both the top of the card and the Play button at once. Fixed by trimming the simulator's own vertical footprint, not by hiding anything: `SimulatorPanel.tsx`'s 4 panels `min-h-[180px] p-4` → `min-h-[130px] p-3`, `CodePanel.tsx`/`OutputPanel.tsx` `p-4 leading-6` → `p-3 leading-5` (and `OutputPanel`'s `min-h-[120px]` → `min-h-[100px]`), `EventLoopSimulator.tsx`'s body `gap-4 p-5` → `gap-3 p-4`, `SimulatorControls.tsx` `gap-3 px-6 py-4` → `gap-2 px-4 py-3`. New card height: 678px — verified via `getBoundingClientRect()` (not just a screenshot) that it now fits inside a 768px viewport with 90px to spare, and confirmed visually that scrolling the card's top to y=20 leaves the Play button fully visible in the same frame.
  5. **Headline wrapping to 3 lines on mobile / an awkward 2-vs-3-line split on tablet portrait:** `Hero.tsx`'s `<h1>` was `text-4xl sm:text-5xl lg:text-5xl`. Changed the base size to `text-3xl` (mobile now wraps to a clean 2 lines instead of 3) and the `sm:` step to `text-4xl` instead of jumping straight to `text-5xl` (since `sm:` and `lg:` were previously identical, the headline got the full desktop size all the way down to 640px, including the entire 640–1023px tablet range where it didn't actually fit on one line) — `lg:text-5xl` (1024px+, true desktop) is unchanged from the original, already-approved Feature 03 sizing.
  - **Found but deliberately not touched, per explicit user instruction to only touch files relevant to the reported issues:** a small (10px) residual page overflow remains at exactly 768px tablet-portrait width, traced (via a refined check that excludes elements clipped by an ancestor's own `overflow-x-auto`/`hidden`, since a naive "widest element" search kept finding false positives inside already-correctly-scrolling containers) to a date span — `"Jun 2025"` — inside a `TestimonialsSection` card, an already-merged, unrelated Feature 07 component the user never mentioned or screenshotted. Flagged here for a future pass rather than fixed now.
  - **Two intermittent false alarms during verification, not real bugs:** (1) several screenshots came back fully blank below the navbar — confirmed as the same already-documented "first-hit Next.js dev cold-compile delay" / "instant JS-driven scroll gives the compositor no frames to repaint before the screenshot" artifacts noted elsewhere in this file, not application defects (re-screenshotting with a longer wait or a real `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded()` call always produced the correct result). (2) mobile's overflow check fluctuated between clean (390px) and as high as 719px across consecutive runs on the same unchanged code, mid-session, with no edits in between — matching this project's own previously-documented Turbopack-HMR-timing flakiness after a long run of rapid edits. Resolved by killing the dev server, clearing `.next`, and restarting clean (the same fix already used twice elsewhere in this file) — re-ran the exact same check immediately after and got a clean, repeatable 390=390 every time.
  - Re-verified after all fixes: typecheck/lint clean across every touched file; Playwright confirmed zero page-level horizontal overflow at laptop/tablet-landscape/mobile (tablet-portrait's only remaining 10px is the unrelated, out-of-scope `TestimonialsSection` item above); visually confirmed via screenshots at all 4 breakpoints that the navbar, headline, company logos, concept switcher, and simulator all read correctly.
  - These fixes touch `Navbar.tsx` (Feature 02), `Hero.tsx` and `ConceptSwitcherTabs.tsx` (Feature 03), and `CompanyLogosStrip.tsx` (Feature 04) — all already merged to `develop` — alongside `EventLoopSimulator`'s own files (Feature 09, still on the open `feature/09-event-loop-simulator` branch/PR). Committed and pushed together on that same branch/PR rather than split out, since the user is managing merges manually.
  - **Standing instruction from the user, going forward:** "every standard screen size" should be checked for every upcoming feature from here on — not just desktop and one mobile width as before.

- **Follow-up to item 4 above (later session, same feature branch):** the user manually increased `SimulatorPanel.tsx`'s 4 panels from `min-h-[130px]` to `min-h-[170px]` for more breathing room. Re-verified the laptop-viewport-fit constraint still holds: card height is now 718px (up from 678px), still fits inside a 768px-tall laptop viewport with the Play button visible at the bottom — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` and a screenshot. No overflow introduced on mobile (390px). If this value needs to grow further, re-check against a 768px-tall viewport before assuming it still fits.

- **Follow-up, reversing item 1 above (later session, same feature branch):** the user explicitly rejected hiding the full nav behind a hamburger through the whole 1024–1279px tablet-landscape range and asked for the real nav, compacted to fit, instead. Reverted the show/hide breakpoint in `Navbar.tsx` back to `lg:` (1024px) and compacted everything that renders from `lg:` up (pill links, "Follow on X", Log In, logo) with `xl:` overrides restoring full desktop sizing at 1280px+ — see `ui-rules.md` → Responsive Design and `ui-registry.md` → Navbar for the exact values. Also fixed two issues caught by `/review`: (1) the icon-only "Follow on X" link had no accessible name at all between `lg:` and `xl:` (label text was `display:none`, icon was `aria-hidden`) — added `aria-label="Follow Frontend Forever on X"` to match the mobile menu's already-correct pattern; (2) the wordmark's visibility logic (`min-[1024px]:hidden min-[1100px]:inline`) only works because both bounds are arbitrary breakpoints — mixing in a named one (`lg:hidden`) silently fails to cascade by pixel order, confirmed by testing both ways — documented with an inline comment since this isn't discoverable by reading the class names alone. Verified zero overflow/clipping at 1024/1100/1279/1280px via Playwright screenshots, typecheck/lint clean, no console errors.

Used the generic `XIcon` glyph for "Follow on X" in both Navbar and Footer since it's the only icon that visually doubles as the X (Twitter) mark. Check `Object.keys(require('lucide-react'))` before assuming a brand icon name exists.

Verified by pixel-sampling the reference PNG directly (Python/PIL, sampling darkest pixel per region) rather than eyeballing — confirmed nav link text/icons and the "Follow on X" text are near-black (`text-text-primary`), not the muted `text-text-secondary` gray the component originally used. Pill border-radius (`rounded-2xl`), icon choices, and button styles were already correct against the design. Fixed in `Navbar.tsx` (`NavbarPillLink`, `NavbarMobileLink`, "Follow on X" anchor) and corrected the same wrong claim in `ui-rules.md`/`ui-registry.md`. Verified visually via a Playwright screenshot of the running dev server cropped to the navbar, compared side-by-side against the design crop.
- **02 Navbar + Footer (round 2):** Same design, second pass — user pointed out the pill lacked Y padding, the wordmark was too big relative to `FF`, and the pill's shadow/border were too sharp/solid. Pixel-measured the design: `FF`'s cap-height is ~3x "Frontend Forever"'s. Changed logo to `text-3xl`/`text-sm` (was `text-2xl`/`text-lg`), pill container to `px-1.5 py-2.5` (was `p-1.5`) with `border-border-light` + `shadow-xl` (was `border-border` + `shadow-md`) for a softer, more diffused look. `border-border-light` (`#F3F4F6`) is an existing `ui-tokens.md` token, not a new one. Re-verified with the same Playwright-screenshot-vs-design-crop method. - **02 Navbar + Footer (round 3):** User pointed out the landing page background and navbar background read as the same color. Re-checked the design pixel-by-pixel along the header's bottom edge (away from the pill) and found no border line or color step anywhere — the design's header has no chrome of its own; only the floating pill has a visible surface/border/shadow. Removed `border-b border-border` and changed the header from `bg-surface` to `bg-background` to match, so the page and header are intentionally the same color, exactly like the reference. (The header also already had `sticky top-5`, not `top-0` — navbar floats with a gap from the viewport edge — which is what made the previously-identical-but-technically-different `bg-surface`/`bg-background` colors visible as a seam in the first place.)
- **02 Navbar + Footer (round 4):** Round 3's fix used `--color-background` (#FAFAF9, off-white) on the assumption that the page and header should match each other. Pixel-sampling the full reference PNG (PowerShell `System.Drawing`, not just the header edge this time) showed the _entire_ canvas — page margins, gaps between sections, everywhere — samples as pure `#FFFFFF`, identical to the pill's `bg-surface`. So `--color-background` was never actually used in the delivered design; the whole light-mode page is `bg-surface`. Changed `Navbar.tsx`'s header and `page.tsx`'s outer container from `bg-background` to `bg-surface` (scoped to these two files only — `--color-background`/`ui-tokens.md` left untouched, since other future pages may still legitimately want the off-white token; user chose this scoped fix over a global token change). Verified via Playwright screenshot of the running dev server — navbar, header, and page now read as one continuous white, matching the design.
  - Follow-up bug from the same round: `header` uses `sticky top-5`, and `position: sticky` immediately offsets an element away from the viewport edge to satisfy its `top` value — even at scroll position 0, with no scrolling needed. That offset leaves an empty gap in the document flow above the header, which is painted by whatever is behind it: `<body>`'s own background, still `bg-background` at the time. Fixed by changing `body { @apply bg-background ...}` to `bg-surface` in `globals.css`'s `@layer base` block — `<body>` is shared root chrome like the navbar, not per-page content, so this is consistent with the round-4 scoping (not a `--color-background` token edit). Needed another dev-server restart + `.next` cache clear to see the change (same stale-Turbopack-cache issue as the note in Feature 01) — re-verified via pixel sampling the new screenshot: gap and header both read `255,255,255`.
- **03 Hero Section (Static):** Ran the `architect` skill before building — aligned on "static" (full visual fidelity, zero interactive logic; entrance motion only) and resolved two scope decisions with the user: (1) the simulator panel placeholder is a full static replica of the design's Event Loop panel, not the build-plan's literal minimal "card with label + badge" wording, since design screenshots are the source of truth and Feature 09 will later swap in the real interactive engine on the same layout; (2) the 4 concept switcher tabs have no `onClick` in this feature — only Event Loop has any content, so making the other 3 "clickable" with nothing to switch to would be misleading. Real switching is Feature 13.
  - New component location: `src/components/homepage/` (sibling to `layout/`/`shared/`), not `src/features/` — homepage sections are page-level UI, not self-contained features like the simulators. `Hero.tsx`, `ConceptSwitcherTabs.tsx`, `HeroSimulatorPreview.tsx`. See `ui-registry.md` for full details.
  - Headline was first built at the build-plan's literal 56–72px and wrapped onto 2 lines — wrong against the design, which fits it on one line. Diagnosed by resizing the full-resolution design PNG (2731×4096) down to the screenshot's working width (1440) and comparing cropped regions directly, then iterating Tailwind size classes against fresh Playwright screenshots until it matched (down to `text-4xl sm:text-5xl lg:text-5xl` + `max-w-6xl`) — same iterative pixel-compare method as the Navbar fixes, not a one-shot calculation. `ui-rules.md` corrected.

Caught by cropping the full-resolution design rather than relying on the downscaled thumbnail, which made the per-column color-coding and status icons (checkmark vs. dot) clearly visible. Since `ui-tokens.md` has no generic "purple"/"blue" token, reused the closest existing semantic tokens (`premium`, `success`, `info`, `streak`) outside their original gamification meaning, scoped to this one component — documented in `ui-registry.md` so it isn't mistaken for those tokens' usual semantics elsewhere.

Also hit a stale dev-server-on-port-3000 from a previous session returning HTTP 500; killed it and started fresh (same category of issue as Feature 01's stale Turbopack cache — when a previously-started dev server misbehaves, kill and restart rather than debugging the stale instance).

Reverted `ConceptSwitcherTabs.tsx` to the pill-group style and corrected `ui-rules.md`/`ui-registry.md` to document this as a deliberate user-requested deviation from `designs/hero-section-1-event-loop.png`, not a mistake — the design's actual tab bar is still the continuous divided style, on record in case a future pass wants to revisit it.
