Documented in `ui-registry.md`.
- **02 Navbar + Footer (round 5):** User flagged dark mode as flat/low-contrast and asked for a true black backdrop that's still easy on the eyes, with the navbar/buttons reading more visibly. Root cause: round 4 made `body`/`Navbar` header/`page.tsx` use plain `bg-surface` to pixel-match the light-mode reference (where the whole canvas is one white) — but that same single-token treatment carries into dark mode, where `--color-surface` (`#1A1A1A`) was the _only_ tone in play, so the page, header, pill, and card all rendered identically with zero hierarchy (the light-mode design relies on `shadow-xl` for separation, which barely registers on a dark background). Light mode has a real reference screenshot proving "everything is one white" is correct; dark mode has no such reference, and the user's direct feedback is that dark mode specifically needs the opposite — a darker backdrop than its elevated surfaces, which is standard dark-theme practice (e.g. Material's elevation system) since shadows don't carry the separation job in the dark.
  - Fix: kept `bg-surface` in light mode but added `dark:bg-background` on `body` (`globals.css`), `Navbar.tsx`'s header, and `page.tsx`'s outer container — these three now diverge from the pill/card's `bg-surface` only in dark mode. Darkened `--color-background` (dark) from `#111111` to `#0A0A0A` for a true near-black that's still a hair off pure `#000000` (avoids the harsher max-contrast halation against light text that reads as "hurts the eyes"). Lightened the dark border tokens for visibility against the new darker backdrop: `--color-border` `#2A2A2A → #333333`, `--color-border-light` `#222222 → #292929`, `--color-border-muted` `#333333 → #404040`. `--color-surface`/`secondary`/`tertiary`/`elevated` untouched — they didn't need to change since contrast comes from the backdrop getting darker, not from them getting lighter. Mirrored all dark-token changes in `ui-tokens.md` and corrected `ui-rules.md`'s navbar section (still described the old single-token light+dark `bg-background` header from before round 4). Verified via Playwright screenshot of the dev server with `localStorage.theme = 'dark'` + pixel-sampling: header/page sample `#0A0A0A`, pill/card/footer sample `#1A1A1A` — a clear, deliberate two-tone split instead of one flat color.
  - Follow-up: user wanted the footer specifically to match the body/header backdrop in dark mode instead of standing out as a `bg-surface` block — added `dark:bg-background` to `Footer.tsx` alongside its existing light-mode `bg-surface`, same pattern as the header/body/page-container fix above. The `border-t border-border` is now the only thing separating footer from page content in dark mode (as in light mode). Re-verified via pixel sampling: footer samples `#0A0A0A`, matching body/header exactly.
- **04 Company Logos Strip:** Ran the `architect` skill before building. Two decisions resolved with the user up front: (1) pixel-inspecting `designs/hero-section-1-event-loop.png` showed the logo strip actually sits _inside_ the hero flow, between the CTAs and the concept switcher tabs — not as a separate page-level section below the whole hero block, which is what the build-plan's feature-by-feature numbering implies. Built it that way (editing `Hero.tsx` from Feature 03 to insert `<CompanyLogosStrip />` and shifting the existing motion stagger for tabs/simulator/scroll-indicator back by ~0.4s to make room), matching the design over the literal build-plan structure — same precedent as Feature 03's own headline-size and tab-style corrections. (2) Feature 03 isn't merged to `develop` yet (no PR opened), so `feature/04-company-logos-strip` branches off `feature/03-hero-section` instead of `develop`, since `Hero.tsx` only exists there.
  - The 6 logos (build-plan listed Google, Meta, Amazon, Microsoft, Stripe, Airbnb) are pixel-sampled as **monochrome** in the design (near-black, `text-text-primary`), not the companies' real brand colors — built as simplified inline-SVG/text components in `src/components/shared/CompanyLogos.tsx` using `currentColor`, not static files in `public/logos/` as build-plan literally says, so they correctly invert in dark mode. Same reasoning as `XLogo` from Feature 02.
  - Caught during visual verification (not by typecheck/lint, which both passed clean before this fix): on mobile the logo row silently overflowed the _page_ instead of scrolling internally. Root cause — `Hero.tsx`'s section is `flex flex-col items-center`, and the wrapping `<div>` around `CompanyLogosStrip` (and the component's own root `<div>`) lacked `w-full`; without it, a flex item's cross-axis size shrinks to its content's intrinsic width instead of the viewport's, so the scrollable row never actually became wider than its own box and nothing scrolled. Fixed by adding `w-full` at both levels, matching the pattern `HeroSimulatorPreview`'s wrapper already used. Verified via Playwright screenshots (desktop light/dark, mobile 390px) — confirmed via direct DOM measurement (`scrollWidth` 671 vs `clientWidth` 342 on mobile) before and after the fix, not just visually. Any new full-bleed child added to `Hero.tsx` needs the same `w-full` treatment or it will hit this same silent-overflow bug.
  - **Follow-up:** user supplied real brand SVGs one at a time post-build and asked for each placeholder logo to be swapped in (recolored to `currentColor` to stay monochrome): Google, Meta's icon, Amazon, Stripe all replaced with real path data. One supplied "Meta wordmark" SVG was caught as garbled/mismatched before integration — rendering it standalone first showed it actually spelled "heton" with an unrelated two-circle icon, not "Meta" — so it was rejected rather than wired in; render any future supplied SVG standalone before trusting it. Airbnb was then removed entirely and replaced with Anthropic (real SVG, `fillRule="evenodd"`) per explicit user request, and Cursor was added as a 7th logo (icon + wordmark, two real SVGs) on top of the build-plan's original 6 — the row is now Google, Meta, Amazon, Microsoft, Stripe, Anthropic, Cursor, diverging significantly from build-plan's original 6-company list. All 7 still fit on one line on desktop and the mobile scroll/mask treatment still holds — re-verified visually after each addition.
  - **Follow-up (sizing bug):** user asked if there was an alignment issue. Measured via `getBoundingClientRect()` in the live DOM rather than eyeballing — all 7 logos were already correctly vertically centered (`items-center` working as intended, centers within 2px of each other), but rendered heights ranged from 16px (Anthropic, Cursor, both `h-4`) to 20px (Google, Amazon, Stripe, already `h-5`) to 28px (Meta, Microsoft — driven by Tailwind's `text-xl` default 28px line-height on their text labels, not their icon size). The 28px outliers were fixed by adding `leading-none` to Meta's and Microsoft's text spans, collapsing them back to ~20px to match the rest of the row. Anthropic and Cursor were left at 16px in the final pass — smaller than the other 5 by deliberate choice during cleanup, not an unresolved bug. See `ui-registry.md` for current per-logo sizing and how to bump those two to match if a future pass wants full uniformity.
  - **Follow-up (`/review`):** ran the review skill before PR. Found 3 issues, all fixed:
    1. `CompanyLogos.tsx` bundled 7 unrelated components in one file, against `code-standards.md`'s "one component per file" rule and the project's own `XLogo.tsx` precedent. Split into `src/components/shared/logos/` — one file per logo (`GoogleLogo.tsx`, `MetaLogo.tsx`, `AmazonLogo.tsx`, `MicrosoftLogo.tsx`, `StripeLogo.tsx`, `AnthropicLogo.tsx`, `CursorLogo.tsx`).
    2. `AmazonLogo`'s hardcoded `id="amazon-logo-a"` was a latent DOM-id-collision risk if the shared component were ever rendered twice on one page. Replaced with React's `useId()` so each render gets a guaranteed-unique id.
    3. The logo row was almost entirely invisible to screen readers — verified by reading the live accessibility tree, not just visually: 5 of 7 logos had zero accessible name (pure decorative SVGs with no fallback), and the other 2 leaked their visible text inconsistently. Fixed by making every logo's own markup fully `aria-hidden` and adding one `<span className="sr-only">{name}</span>` per logo in `CompanyLogosStrip.tsx`, so every brand name is announced exactly once regardless of how that logo is built internally. Re-verified via DOM inspection (each wrapper has exactly one un-hidden `sr-only` name + one `aria-hidden` logo root) and confirmed zero visual regression via screenshot diff.
- **05 How It Works Section:** Ran the `architect` skill before building. User explicitly asked whether this section's motion was inspired by Notion/Linear's interactive marketing sections — it wasn't (every existing homepage section just uses `whileInView` + manual delay stagger) — and chose to elevate it: scroll-linked motion via Framer Motion's `useScroll`/`useTransform`, the first use of that pattern in this codebase, over the alternative of adding hover micro-interactions on top of the existing simple stagger (explicitly deferred, to keep scope contained). Two other decisions resolved with the user up front: (1) keep the section's height natural/compact like other sections rather than artificially tall to stretch out the scroll-scrubbed reveal; (2) keep a connector between steps on the mobile vertical stack (reoriented vertically) rather than dropping it, since the connecting motif is what signals "this is a sequence."
  - No design screenshot exists for this section (`designs/` only has hero variants + Learn page) — built directly from `build-plan.md`'s text spec: 5 steps (Understand/Simulate/Challenge/Interview/Build), each `icon + title + 1-line description`, no card background, horizontal-with-arrows on desktop / vertical-stack on mobile.
  - **Scroll-offset bug caught during verification, not by typecheck/lint:** the first `useScroll({ offset: ["start 0.8", "end 0.35"] })` attempt made the last step (and its connector) permanently stuck at `opacity: 0` no matter how far down the page was scrolled. Root cause: this is currently the last homepage section before a short `Footer` (Features 06–08 don't exist yet), so the page's total scrollable distance ran out before the section's bottom edge could physically reach 35% from the top of the viewport — the math demanded more scroll room than the page had. Diagnosed by reading each step's live inline `style.opacity`/`style.transform` via Playwright at a series of `window.scrollTo()` positions (not by eyeballing screenshots), which showed steps revealing correctly up to a hard ceiling that matched the page's actual max scroll position. Fixed by loosening the offset to `["start 0.9", "end 0.7"]`, which completes well within the currently-available scroll room with margin to spare. If a future section is added between this one and the footer, this has more headroom than it needs today — but if this `useScroll` pattern is reused on another section that also happens to sit near the bottom of a short page, re-verify the same way rather than assuming the same offset values transfer.
  - Confirmed via Playwright DOM inspection that this feature introduces no overflow: the only `scrollWidth > clientWidth` offenders on mobile belong to Feature 04's `CompanyLogosStrip` (its intentional internal `overflow-x-auto` scroll row), not to anything in `HowItWorksSection`.
  - **Follow-up (full redesign, same session, before any PR):** after the v1 layout above was built and verified, the user shared a screenshot of a Notion marketing-site section ("Keep work moving 24/7") — a left vertical list of selectable items (one expanded with a description) paired with a right-side live-preview panel that swaps content per selection — and asked to build something like that instead. Three scope decisions resolved with the user before rebuilding: (1) replace the v1 row/stack layout entirely rather than keep both (the row and the switcher cover the same ground; keeping both had no clear justification); (2) the right-side preview content per step is a simple static placeholder mockup for each step (no real simulator/challenge/interview UI exists yet — those are Features 09–26), not a reuse of `HeroSimulatorPreview`'s specific visual frame; (3) no autoplay/pause control for this pass — click-to-select only, matching "UI first" and keeping this PR's scope contained; autoplay is a possible later fast-follow.

  - **Follow-up 2 (second full redesign, same session, before any PR — v2 → v3):** user said v2 still wasn't it, then described the actual target in detail: 5 separate full sections (not one switcher card), alternating left/right per step, colorful animated SVG visuals, and "texts moving doing something" — specifically per-letter kinetic typography where a word's two repeated letters (e.g. "Understand"'s two `d`s) fly past each other in a parabolic arc and swap places, with other letters flipping/rotating in. Ran the `architect` skill's question-by-question style directly (no separate subagent) since the bulk of remaining unknowns were creative/scope decisions only the user could resolve, not codebase unknowns. Resolved up front: (1) **no GSAP** — `architecture.md` commits to Framer Motion only, and the user's own "shouldn't hang, lightweight" requirement argues against a second animation runtime when Framer Motion already covers everything described; (2) **no bespoke character illustrations** — Notion's actual illustrations are commissioned art, not something reproducible via hand-written SVG; built simple custom shape/icon compositions in brand colors instead (upgraded v2's preview cards rather than starting over); (3) **one generic letter-swap algorithm, not 5 manually curated word choreographies** — among a word's repeated letters, swap the pair with the largest gap between occurrences (tie-broken by whichever starts later) — verified by hand-tracing all 5 words that this mechanically reproduces the user's own "Understand" example (the two `d`s) with zero special-casing; (4) one-shot `whileInView` entrances per section, not continuous scroll-linked `useScroll` — directly answers "shouldn't hang" and avoids re-hitting the exact class of scroll-offset fragility already learned the hard way in v1.
    - v2's entire two-panel switcher shell (`activeId` state, `StepListItem`, the `AnimatePresence mode="wait"` crossfade) was deleted — same "no value preserving code that doesn't match the chosen interaction model" reasoning as the v1→v2 transition. The 5 `*Preview` components were kept and upgraded (enlarged, given one motion touch each) since their actual visual content was already approved at small scale, not thrown away.
    - **Real bug caught during mobile verification, not visually obvious from most screenshots:** the first letter-animation implementation gave each individual letter its own `whileInView`/`viewport` trigger. On a mobile screenshot, "Simulate" rendered as "ulate" — its first 3 letters ("S", "i", "m") were permanently invisible. Direct DOM inspection (`getComputedStyle`/inline `style.opacity`/`getBoundingClientRect`, the same technique used for the v1 scroll-offset bug) showed those letters frozen at `opacity: 0` with `transform: rotateX(-90deg)` and, critically, a **rendered bounding-box height of exactly 0px** — `rotateX(-90deg)` with no `perspective` set fully foreshortens the element's projected height to zero. A zero-area element can fail to ever register as "intersecting" with an IntersectionObserver, so `whileInView` never fired for those specific letters — non-deterministically, since sibling letters on the exact same line (`u`,`l`,`a`,`t`,`e`) happened to trigger fine. This is the kind of bug that "looks fine" in most screenshots (other words/breakpoints didn't happen to hit it) and only showed up reliably via direct state inspection. Fixed by restructuring `AnimatedHeading` to put the `whileInView`/`viewport` trigger **only on the parent container** (always a healthy, non-degenerate bounding box) and propagating the reveal to letters via Framer Motion `variants` + `transition: { staggerChildren }` — the idiomatic pattern for staggering a group of children, and one that never depends on a tiny/transformable child correctly self-reporting its own visibility. Also softened the flip angle from `-90deg` to `-60deg` as a second safety margin. Re-verified via the same direct-DOM-inspection method (not just re-screenshotting) that all letters in all 5 words settle to `opacity: 1` on both mobile and desktop. **Lesson for any future per-letter/per-item entrance animation in this codebase: observe the parent, never each tiny child.**
    - Re-verified: typecheck/lint clean; Playwright screenshots desktop/mobile × light/dark for all 5 sections confirming correct alternation (text/visual flips every other step) and settled letter animations; repeated the mobile `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` overflow check (one transient 719px reading turned out to be a hot-reload artifact caught mid-recompile — re-running the identical check immediately after gave a clean, consistent 440px, the same pre-existing `CompanyLogosStrip` baseline as v1/v2, confirming no real regression).

Fix: assigned each of the 5 steps one of the existing semantic tokens purely for hue — Understand=`accent`, Simulate=`info`, Challenge=`premium`, Interview=`success`, Build=`streak` — same "reuse a semantic token outside its literal meaning, scoped to one component" precedent already established by `HeroSimulatorPreview`'s 4-color column treatment in Feature 03. Applied that theme color to: a new large rounded icon badge per section (`size-16 rounded-2xl`, theme `bg-{color}-light/muted` + `text-{color}`), each preview card's outer shell (swapped flat `border + bg-surface-secondary` for borderless `bg-{color}-light/muted` + `shadow-sm`, dropping borders project-wide in this section in favor of tint+elevation), a new decorative blurred color blob (`blur-3xl opacity-20`) behind each visual panel for depth, and the heading's swap-letters (now rendered in the step's theme color instead of plain black, tying the motion and color systems together). Semantic status colors inside cards (pass/fail green/red, "Marked as understood" check) were deliberately left alone — only the card shell, badge, blob, and swap-letters take the theme color.

  - **Follow-up 4 (second polish pass on v3, same session, before any PR; Auto Mode active — proceeded on reasonable judgment without re-asking):** user gave three pieces of feedback at once: (1) section-to-section alignment looked inconsistent; (2) still needed more "creative and illustration," not just colored cards; (3) wanted continuous animation "every 2 second" instead of one-shot scroll reveals, for a more attractive/interactive feel.
    - **(1) Alignment root cause:** `StepSection`'s root flex container used `items-center`, vertically centering the text column against the visual column. Since `SimulatePreview` is taller than the other 4 preview cards (extra status row + controls row), centering shifted that section's icon badge/heading to a different vertical start point than the other 4 sections, which all centered against shorter, more similar-height cards — reading as "randomly" inconsistent even though every section uses the identical structure. Fixed by changing to `lg:items-start` so every section's text and visual columns share a top edge regardless of content-height differences on either side. Confirmed via screenshot comparison across all 5 steps that the icon badge and the top of each preview card now align consistently.
    - **(2) More illustration:** added two small `aria-hidden` floating decorative shapes per section (a `Sparkles` icon top-right and a `Plus` icon bottom-left of each visual panel, both in the step's theme color) — a deliberately lightweight way to add "illustrated" visual interest without bespoke art (still no GSAP, no external assets, consistent with constraints already settled in the v2→v3 redesign).
    - **(3) Continuous motion:** added the first **infinite-loop** (not one-shot `whileInView`) Framer Motion animations in this codebase — `animate` (not `whileInView`) with `transition: { duration: 2, repeat: Infinity }` on: each icon badge's icon (gentle scale+rotate), the two floating decorations (float/rotate), and one signature accent per preview card (Understand's lightbulb pulse, Simulate's dot now loops back-and-forth forever instead of animating once, Challenge's code block gained a blinking text cursor + pulsing pass/fail badges, Interview's thumbs-up wiggles, Build's terminal icon wiggles + button pulses). Kept deliberately small in count (1–3 looping elements per section, simple transform/opacity tweens only) specifically because of the project's own "shouldn't hang, lightweight" requirement from earlier in this same redesign — infinite loops are more expensive than one-shot animations by definition (they never stop), so scope was kept tight rather than animating everything continuously.

User opted to generate all 5 icons externally themselves (lightbulb, sync arrows, trophy, chat bubble, wrench) using an AI image tool, using color specs I gave matching the existing per-step theme tokens (e.g. `#0D9488` teal for Understand, `#2563EB` blue for Simulate, etc.), and placed the resulting files in `public/icons/how-it-works/`.

Inspecting unique fill counts (82 for the bulb vs. 900–1100+ for the other 4) showed why: the bulb is pure grayscale (no color at all, inconsistent with the brief), and the other 4 lean toward their correct theme hue but have hundreds-to-thousands of near-duplicate fill shades — an artifact of however the source was vectorized/traced, not deliberate shading. Flagged both findings to the user rather than silently working around them: asked whether to accept the monochrome bulb as-is or have it regenerated (kept as-is, reads fine against the teal badge) — the hue-but-not-flat-color issue on the other 4 wasn't blocking enough to need a decision, just noted.

Removed the lucide `Lightbulb`/`ArrowLeftRight`/`Trophy`/`MessageCircle`/`Wrench` imports and the `icon: LucideIcon` field from `STEPS` entirely — replaced with `iconSrc`/`iconWidth`/`iconHeight` per step (the floating decorative `Sparkles`/`Plus` from the previous follow-up stayed as lucide icons, untouched).

Got 86–92% reduction per file, ~108 KB total. Verified via before/after Playwright screenshots at the actual rendered badge size (`h-9 w-auto`, ~24–36px) that nothing visibly changed — re-ran the full 5-icon × desktop/dark screenshot sweep, zero console errors, typecheck/lint clean.

Replaced the `size-16 rounded-2xl bg-{color}-light` badge with: an `absolute size-28 rounded-full opacity-40 blur-2xl` glow (same recipe as the visual panel's blob, just smaller) sitting behind a much larger bare icon (`h-20 md:h-24`, up from `h-9`). `BADGE_CLASSES` stayed in the file (still used by the floating `Sparkles`/`Plus` decorations) — only the per-step icon's own container changed.

Isolated by elimination, in order: (1) checked the sibling description paragraph two lines below, using the identical `whileInView`/`viewport={{ once: true, margin: "-80px" }}` pattern — it triggered fine, ruling out a general viewport-trigger breakdown; (2) suspected the nested continuously-animating icon (`motion.span` with `animate` + `repeat: Infinity`) was somehow interfering with the parent's own trigger — temporarily stripped it entirely, bug persisted, ruling that out too; (3) suspected the `margin: "-80px"` itself, since this is a notably smaller/shorter element than anything else in this codebase using that same convention — removed it (now just `viewport={{ once: true }}`), bug fixed immediately, confirmed across all 5 steps via the same inline-style check. Root cause inside Framer Motion/the browser's IntersectionObserver is unconfirmed (most likely an edge case where a -80px-inset root margin interacts badly with a small/short element on a short viewport), but the fix is verified and isolated to just this one element — every other `whileInView` in the codebase keeps its `margin: "-80px"` unchanged. **If a small, short, or low-on-the-page element ever silently fails to animate in on mobile only, try dropping its `margin` before assuming the bug is elsewhere in the component.**

- **`src/lib/sandbox/buildSandboxDoc.ts` (Practice/Build's code-execution sandbox, Feature 24, already merged) has the same unescaped-terminator/case-normalizing bug CodeRabbit caught in Feature 53's `buildBattleDoc.ts` — found incidentally while merge-checking Feature 53, not yet fixed, out of scope for that branch.** Its own `escapeForScript` (`code.replace(/<\/script>/gi, "<\\/script>")`) only matches an exact `</script>` — the HTML tokenizer actually ends a script block on `</script` followed by whitespace, `/`, or `>` (case-insensitive), so a Practice/Build submission containing `</script >` or `</SCRIPT\n>` would slip through and truncate the harness document early. It also hardcodes the replacement to lowercase `<\/script>`, silently changing the case of whatever was matched (e.g. a string literal `"</SCRIPT>"` in the user's own submitted code would read back as `"</script>"` after the browser unescapes `\/` — altering the actual runtime value their code produces, not just its escaped source form). Feature 53's fix (`buildBattleDoc.ts`) is the reference implementation — same regex (`/<\/script(?=[\s/>])/gi`), replaced via a matcher-based function that reuses the matched text and only inserts the escaping backslash, instead of a hardcoded lowercase replacement string. Same pattern applies directly here, minus the click-navigation-guard script (irrelevant to this file). **If this resurfaces or gets picked up as its own fix:** it's a one-line change in `buildSandboxDoc.ts`, same regex, no other files affected; verify with the same edge-case set (`</script>`, `</script >`, `</script\n>`, `</SCRIPT>`, `</Script/>`, `</scriptfoo>` as a deliberate non-match, and a case-preserving string-literal case) before considering it done.
